# Cicada — active backlog

**Code-backed priority review: 2026-10-06 (`dev` `3efba432`).** Every original G address is retained. Completed scopes moved to [DONE.md](DONE.md); aliases and settled choices remain below. There was no G174 row in the source backlog; that gap is preserved. G187 adds Windows feasibility; G188 adds ingestion modularity. G189 adds the Home revamp (painted banner off for now); G190 asks what exports carry beyond chats (2026-10-06). Provider/source/benchmark/release follow-ups were checked at `dev` `ba7a4957`.

[TODO.md](TODO.md) is the execution order and binding rulings. This file holds reasoning and remaining scope. [BACKLOG_CONTEXT.md](BACKLOG_CONTEXT.md) holds older cross-cutting context and original owner prompts; [working-method.md](working-method.md) defines the implementation bar. Dated progress inside a row is history: its **Remaining scope / priority review** and the TODO execution view take precedence over stale “not merged” or “next” text.

Priority means sequence, not a mandate to finish every row before a small trial. **P0:** actual write/commit/revision correctness and the reported regression. **P1:** useful-memory trial, automatic project continuity across sessions/agents, source refresh, video through Sleep, selected iMessage capture, replacement whole-system benchmarks, lifecycle/provenance and targeted quality work. **Today, 2026-10-06:** working GitHub releases and release/main/website synchronization (G182), with applicable integrity and integration checks. **P2:** CLI, headless packaging, provider/source modularity, reusable start prompts and portable procedures. **P3:** design/mascot and remaining app/Reader improvements. **P4:** replication and sharing after the primary-writer topology. Research follows its trigger. G148 is planned P1 work; full harness completion and new embeddings do not gate a small hands-on trial.

Every row now has an **Implementation review** and a current status; older progress is retained as history. [The per-ID implementation ledger](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md) distinguishes DONE, PARTIAL, UNBUILT, VERIFY, UNVERIFIED, DECISION, CONDITIONAL and REDIRECT, with code evidence and acceptance bounds. G1/G23/G50 were restored from DONE because their original scopes have unfinished slices. A principle or declined choice is not an automatic build task. The paid-run and binding ruling boundaries are unchanged.

The Cognition comparison is saved in [2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md), with implementation work on G180, G186, G55/G112, G76, G16, G132/G181/G92 and G148. Cicada keeps its capture, temporal validity, human verdict and provenance rails while borrowing a simpler portable format. The owner’s follow-up on unfinished source refresh and video consolidation is saved in [source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md) and applied to G61/G22/G162/G166.

## Current decisions

- Markdown and git remain authoritative; sqlite-vec/FTS are derived and disposable. No storage migration is scheduled.
- Keep the closed entity types, promotion gate and structured claims. No broad peer ontology rewrite is scheduled.
- Both grounded Ask and direct file traversal are implemented.
- Named banks are implemented. **Owner 2026-10-06 amendment:** selective node/project sharing and multiple contributors are requested design under G16; they are no longer indefinitely parked. A general human/agent/robot peer network remains research-only.
- One primary git writer per bank remains binding for server mode, sync and sharing. A private push-only backup is separate from replication or collaboration.
- The first memory trial is user-started; scheduled runs retain TODO ruling 4. No consolidation, upload or publication was performed by this documentation review.

## North star (moved here from G79, 2026-09-01)

**Cicada is worth having only if it learns from lived experience AND curation, and gives it
back at the right moment** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the best version of cicada is one that
smartly gives and learns from all my experience as a human + the things that i've curated and
might be relevant to me"). Not a feature — the sentence every other row is scored against,
recorded here so it can arbitrate scope. **Read literally it names two intake streams and one
output discipline.** *Experience* = what actually happened to the user: conversations (MCP
capture, imports), decisions and their reversals, work done in repos, places, people, the
passage of time itself (the decay layer is the only part of Cicada that models experience as
something that *fades*). *Curation* = what the user deliberately chose to keep: saves with
reasons (G71), bookmarks, papers, design references (G70), prompts (G73), skills (G72) —
signal the user paid attention to select, which is categorically different from conversational
exhaust and should probably carry different trust and different decay (already true
structurally: media/bookmarks are `evergreen`, G66). *Smartly gives* = the output discipline,
and the part Cicada is furthest from: **relevance at the right moment, unprompted, without
noise.** Today Cicada answers when asked (`/ask`, `cicada_recall`) and interrupts on a schedule
(inbox); it does not yet notice "you are about to do X and three months ago you curated the
exact thing that helps." **The measurable claim hiding in the sentence** (worth stating as a
thesis-grade goal): the system should surface a *curated* item inside an *experiential* context
the user never explicitly linked — i.e. cross-stream retrieval, not within-stream search. That
is a testable capability (does a Pinterest save from March ever legitimately reach a September
conversation about a project?) and today the honest answer is "only if the user searches for
it." **Use as a filter:** a row that only adds intake (more capture channels) advances half the
sentence; a row that only adds surfaces advances none of it; rows that connect curated
artifacts into live context (G70's design identity, G60's timed questions, G75's handshake
priming, G53's state dictionary, G52's Ask) advance the whole of it — score accordingly.
**Tension to keep honest:** "gives smartly" and "never intrusive" (UX principle 4) pull against
each other; the resolution so far has been pull-not-push, and if that stays fixed, the ceiling
on "smartly gives" is set by whatever the agent loads at conversation start — which makes
**G75** (handshake) and **G53** (state dictionary) load-bearing for the north star, not
niceties. → the scoring lens over the whole table; most directly served by **G53**, **G75**,
**G52**, **G70**, **G66**, **G71**; the reason **G74** is the top blocker (nothing learns while
Sleep is dead).

---

**Current application of that lens:** the plan engine exists; the immediate test is whether the record improves ordinary conversations (G10), followed by cross-stream relevance (G93), skills (G112) and design memory (G70/G151). The historical engine-blocker sentence above is not the current queue.

First artifact, fresh download/update acceptance, signing/notarization and website link remain. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g182). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Release bundling, versioning, signed-manifest update plumbing and CI are merged. A read-only GitHub release listing on 2026-10-06 returned no releases. Finish clean downloaded-artifact install, first-run/capture/recall, update verification and website link; publish only through the established manual main-promotion/release process. Record actual Apple Silicon/Intel support and Gatekeeper/signing behavior from the artifact rather than promising it. Developer ID/notarization and the desired drag-to-install distribution stay explicit open scope. This backlog edit does not publish/tag/promote anything. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Today** `main` is promoted by hand (a merge of `dev`), with no tag, no version and no build (the repo has no git tags and no GitHub releases, checked 2026-10-05); this Mac updates from `dev` through `scripts/dev/auto-update.sh`; a new person installs from source (`install.sh`, `install.md`). The website's macOS install should always hand out what `main` is. **Shape — a promotion to `main` is a release:** a version tag (`v0.x.y`) → CI builds the release app → signed with a Developer ID and notarized (needs the Apple Developer account) → a `.dmg` on a GitHub Release with notes from the merged PRs → the website's download button points at the latest release's asset, so a release needs no website edit → installed apps update themselves (a Sparkle appcast, or a check against the latest release) → the backend and its Python runtime ship inside the app (today's packaging blocker: no embedded Python) or the first run sets them up. **Two channels:** `dev` stays the developer channel (the auto-updater); releases from `main` are what people get. **Each release:** suites green on `main`, release notes, bank migration notes, the version in the app's `Info.plist` and the API, a smoke test of the `.dmg` on a clean macOS user. **To decide:** the version scheme; Sparkle or our own updater; whether the website lives in this repo; where signing runs (CI secrets vs this Mac); a Homebrew cask for the app beside G181's formula. Relates **G76**, **G88**, **G92**, **G181**. **Owner 2026-10-05: no App Store — people download a `.dmg` from the website and drag it into Applications like any other app.** That is exactly what a Developer ID is for: it is Apple's signing for apps distributed *outside* the App Store, and it is the only thing that removes Gatekeeper's block. Without it a browser-downloaded app (quarantined) opens on macOS 15/26 only through System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway (Control-click → Open no longer bypasses it since macOS 15), or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine` in Terminal; a signature that doesn't match its bundle reads as "damaged". The Apple Developer Program is 99 USD/yr and an individual can enrol (the legal name shows as developer; only the Account Holder creates the Developer ID Application certificate). A Homebrew cask is no way around it: current Homebrew quarantines every cask download and has dropped `--no-quarantine`. The `curl … \| bash` install on the website works only because curl never sets the quarantine attribute — it stays as the from-source path. **Research 2026-10-05 (codebase + Apple + CI + Python packaging, then two adversarial fact-checks) — why the app can't simply be zipped today:** (1) `install_app.sh:105-108` signs ad hoc or with a self-signed local certificate using `--deep`, `--timestamp=none` and no `--options runtime` — notarization rejects all three. (2) The `.app` holds no backend: `bundle.sh` copies the Swift binary, icon and resource bundle and stamps the build machine's checkout path as `CicadaRepoRoot` (`bundle.sh:126`); `BackendProcess.installRoot()` (`BackendProcess.swift:164-176`) falls back to `~/cicada`; the launchd plist, MCP registration and Claude Code/Codex hooks all run `<checkout>/api/.venv/bin/python`, whose interpreter is a symlink into uv's cache. `resolveMemoryPath()` derives the bank from `installRoot()/memory`, so a bundled app would point the bank inside the `.app` — the runtime seam must cut memory-root resolution away from the install root (split-brain class). (3) Size: `sentence-transformers` is a base dependency in `api/pyproject.toml` (it also sits in the `local` extra), so torch comes with every install and the venv measures **906 MB**; the default local embedder is a gated Hugging Face model needing a token. Search already degrades to words when the embedder can't import; the index-build path (`vector_index.rebuild_pending_index` via `entity_resolver.py:385`, `link_recon.py:394`) needs the same, with a reason the person can see. `install.sh:279` still hints `uv sync --extra local` although torch is already in the base install. (4) The backend runs bare `git` for every bank commit (`git_service.py`, `bank_registry.py`), which on a Mac without the Command Line Tools pops Apple's install dialog. (5) No `.github/`, no tags, no releases; the version is hardcoded (0.2 in `bundle.sh:89-90`, 0.1.0 in pyproject and `main.py`). (The 2026-10-05 code audit found the same split at `3d28cd0c` — `bundle.sh:89` vs `api/pyproject.toml:3` — and left it to this row; #182's one `VERSION` file closed it.) (6) `api/.env` has three readers (BackendProcess's overlay, `install-backend-agent.sh`'s memory-path read, `BackendAgentService.swift:119`) and pydantic loads it only when the working directory is `api/`, so today's launchd backend already ignores it apart from the memory path — a latent bug of its own. **Proposed shape (owner asked to record it, not to plan or build yet):** a `.dmg` built in CI on each promotion to `main` that carries its own CPython 3.12 (python-build-standalone, arm64 first) and the locked core dependencies, no venv or shebangs, about 110 MB *estimated* without torch; Mach-O code placed where Apple expects code (`Contents/Frameworks` or `Contents/Helpers`, not `Contents/Resources`); every `.so`/`.dylib` re-signed inside-out with the Developer ID and hardened runtime (library validation only loads same-Team-ID code), never `--deep`; the app stapled as well as the DMG so an offline first launch works; published as a GitHub Release so the website button is a plain link to `https://github.com/rorosaga/cicada/releases/latest/download/Cicada.dmg` (free hosting; a hotfix published after a newer release must use `--latest=false` or it becomes "latest"). The backend registered with `SMAppService.agent` from a plist inside the bundle, re-registered when the app's path or version changes (registration is not reliably kept across updates/moves), never while running from the DMG or translocated. Hooks and MCP point at stable links under `~/.cicada/bin` that the app re-points on launch, so capture survives app moves and updates (G105). Torch and the on-device model become an optional in-app download (bundled uv into Application Support). Sparkle 2 or a latest-release check for updates; a Homebrew cask only after signing. **Gaps the fact-checks found, to settle before building:** CLAUDE.md names `install-backend-agent.sh` the one source of the `com.cicada.backend` plist — a bundled plist changes that rail, and an existing legacy plist with the same label must be booted out or the label changed (migration); under `SMAppService` the app becomes the backend's responsible process, so permissions granted to the app may extend to the backend, against the rail that the launchd backend has no Full Disk Access; deleting the app leaves hooks/MCP pointing at dangling links, so capture would fail silently — needs an uninstall/repair story and a hook that fails loudly; a dev checkout and a released app on one Mac must not fight over port 8000 or re-point hooks away from the checkout; a bundled backend must detect and migrate an older bank on first launch after an update; an update check is a network call the app starts on its own and needs a named gate. Unconfirmed from official sources: the exact Gatekeeper dialog wording and whether Open Anyway asks for an admin password, and the minimum hardened-runtime entitlements for embedded Python (start from `cs.allow-unsigned-executable-memory`; `cs.disable-library-validation` only if a runtime-installed extra needs it). **To decide (added):** pay for the Developer ID or ship an unsigned `.dmg` with Open Anyway instructions (free but every first launch hits the block); embedded runtime vs a signed thin app that installs the backend with uv on first launch (smaller, needs the network, Python code not notarized); torch in the download (~250 MB+, estimate) or as an in-app download; bundle a signed `git` or have onboarding ask once for the Command Line Tools; the default embedder for a fresh install (ungated model, words-only, or the gated one with a token); arm64 only or an Intel build too (no x86_64 torch wheels). **Built 2026-10-05, phase 1 — one version:** a `VERSION` file at the repo root (`0.3.0`) is the only place the version is written; `bundle.sh` stamps it as `CFBundleShortVersionString`, `api/version.py` reads it for FastAPI's `app.version` (so `/healthz`) and the MCP server's `serverInfo`, and `api/pyproject.toml` carries the same string (`test_version.py` holds them together). `CFBundleVersion` is the build number: the commit count, or `CICADA_BUILD_NUMBER` when a build passes one, so it only grows along `main`. Settings → General shows "Version x.y.z (build)" and says so when the background service reports a different version. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 2 — the self-contained app:** `bundle.sh --release --with-backend` carries CPython 3.12, the torch-free release dependency set, Cicada's code, a portable git and the int8 bge-small ONNX model (347 MB unzipped, 138 MB zipped), signed ad hoc inside out; agents, hooks and the launchd plist run `~/.cicada/bin` launchers the app rewrites on launch; `CICADA_PORT` everywhere; `scripts/release/smoke-test.sh` proves a build in a temp folder. Plan and rulings: `docs/plans/2026-10-05-g182-releases.md`. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 4 — the release workflow:** `make release VERSION=x.y.z` bumps, merges dev into main, tags and pushes atomically from a temporary worktree; the tag runs `.github/workflows/release.yml` on macos-26, which builds, smoke-tests, zips, signs the zip with Ed25519 and publishes a GitHub Release with `latest.json`; a `ci/release-dry-run` push does all but publish. **Built 2026-10-05, phase 5 — install and update:** testers run `curl -fsSL …/main/scripts/install-release.sh \| bash`; a release app checks GitHub's latest release on launch and every 6 hours (Settings → General → *Install updates automatically*, on by default), verifies sha256 + Ed25519 before unzipping, and installs on quit or *Restart to update* through a detached helper that keeps the old copy on any failure. **Built 2026-10-06, phase 3 — search models:** each bank is built with its own model (the person's choice → what it records → the default), so a default change never re-embeds a bank; Settings → Memory → Search model offers the built-in model and EmbeddingGemma as an optional download (runtime into `~/.cicada/extras` with the bundled pip, weights with the person's own Hugging Face token, used once). **Built 2026-10-06, phase 6 — docs:** `docs/RELEASING.md` (cut a release, what testers run, the signing key, adding a Developer ID and notarization), the tester line in `install.md` and the README. **Owner decisions taken 2026-10-05:** ship ad hoc-signed (no Developer ID yet), a zip on a GitHub Release (no `.dmg` yet), our own updater (CryptoKit Ed25519, no Sparkle), arm64 only, the small ONNX model bundled with EmbeddingGemma optional, git bundled. **2026-10-06 — the bundled search model is multilingual** (owner: "switch to a multilingual one"): `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` (int8 ONNX, 113 MB + 16 MB tokenizer, MIT, ungated, 384 dims, `query: ` / `passage: ` prompts, mean pooling) replaces the English-only `bge-small-en-v1.5`, which missed every Spanish query against English notes in a spot check where e5 found all six. Banks keep the model they were built with. **2026-10-09 — EmbeddingGemma 2 on the Neural Engine is the search model wherever it runs** (Rodrigo 2026-10-09: "cant we just run everything with embedding gemma 2?"; TODO "Embeddings" ruling): `google/embeddinggemma-2:768` through Core ML (`coreml_embedder`, macOS 15+ on Apple silicon; `coremltools` now in both dependency sets, sympy/mpmath kept in the bundle, ≈ +28 MB unzipped), downloaded once on request into `~/.cicada/models` from one pinned, sha256-checked revision (`model_fetch`; Settings' *Neural Engine* pick is one click with no token, `make embedding-model-gemma2` in a checkout), never bundled (≈ +340 MB zipped for a model macOS 14 can't run); e5-small stays bundled as the floor. A model change is a background re-embed outside Sleep that never blocks recall (`embedding_models.start_reindex_if_needed`); numbers in `storage.md` (sqlite-vec). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · release infrastructure built; first publication and install acceptance open. | PARTIAL · Today **Status 2026-10-07:** releases are live. Ruling 19 (a release is a dev→main merge) is implemented (#202: CI on `main` tags `VERSION`, owns its draft by id, publishes with the tag spelled out and verified, only `main`'s tip may publish, per-commit concurrency; `release-check.yml`; `make release`/`make release-pr`). **v0.3.0** published (PR #203) and **v0.3.1** (PR #207; installer UTF-8 hotfix #205: `$REPO…` broke `curl | bash` on every Mac — now linted). Install acceptance PASS on v0.3.1 (real curl line under UTF-8 into a temp dir; `smoke-test.sh` all checks). Stable asset `Cicada-macos-arm64.zip` resolves. Open: website button PR (cicada-website #18), real update acceptance 0.3.0→0.3.1 (needs an isolated home: a release app rewrites `~/.cicada/bin`), the integration matrix after the trial's consolidation, Developer ID/notarization, owner GitHub settings (required check + PR-only `main`). |

## P0 — record integrity and reproducible regressions

| ID | Goal | Reasoning and remaining implementation | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|
| G225 | <a id="g225"></a>**A fresh-bank re-consolidation and a full retest before Cicada goes to people** (owner 2026-10-26, with the Sources and Projects notes) | **Why.** The owner's banks carry what earlier builds wrote (detected projects that are not his among them, G222); judging today's Cicada on them judges old code. **What:** one clean fresh-bank run of the owner's exports and captured conversations on the current `dev` (the Claude plan, the default engine), then a full retest across the app, the MCP, the CLI and the remote door with the full suites (the once-per-release run), and a written list of what is wrong — before any tester gets a build. **Rails:** a bulk write to a real bank only with the owner's go (old banks are throwaway, a new bank is the run); inspect per-item before any bank write. Relates G10. | APPLY · P0 |
| G183 | <a id="g183"></a>**Write-safety gaps the two October audits left open — page-lock coverage, a commit that misses a file, a lock held across a scan, and a bank switch that runs ahead of the server** (found 2026-10-05 by the audits' own reviews; the owner asked 2026-10-06 that every disclosed gap live in the backlog) | **2026-10-07/08 — dev auto-updater:** #226 defers while Sleep runs and restarts only for runtime code; #239 treats a slow or failing backend as busy (a 3 s timeout under load let one restart through mid-drain). Imports still leave episodes untracked until the first Sleep commit (attribution smear — open). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Audit fixes landed, but page-lock coverage, follow-up owned-path manifest, scan lock duration, manual dedup commit and optimistic bank activation remain. Implement from current reproductions, preserve true writer attribution. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g183). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Each is disclosed in an audit status doc and none is fixed. **(a) Page-lock coverage.** #181 (audit P1-2) added `page_lock.page_lock(bank)`, a cross-process `flock` held across a page's read → reconcile → write and its commit (lock order page → git → episode; `docs/architecture/storage.md`). Claim writes, retracts and the follow-up resolver take it; **Sleep's own page writes and the inbox's other resolvers do not**, so a Sleep batch and an agent's `cicada_write_claim`, or two resolutions, can still race on one page — the exact lost update P1-2 reproduced (both report `written`, one survives). Fix: route every entity-page writer through the lock (Sleep per page, not per batch, so capture never waits a batch out). **(b) A follow-up that merges into an open conflict item** writes that item but leaves it out of the files it commits, so the item rides along in the next writer's commit under the wrong author — the class #189 (P1-3) fixed for resolutions; this case predates it. Fix: list the merged item in the manifest. **(c) The MCP save's dedup scan holds `episode_lock`** (audit 2026-10-02, K01): `cicada_save_episode` hashes every episode while holding the lock that capture, the stager and Sleep's retirement share, so a save on a large bank makes the Stop hook and the stager wait for the whole scan. Fix: scan unlocked, then take the lock and re-check only the candidates (or keep a hash index). **(d) `ActivateBank` runs ahead of the server**: the optimistic apply sets `store.bank` and hydrates the new bank's cache before `POST /banks/activate` lands (`Sync/Mutations.swift`, `ActivateBank.optimistic`), so for about one round trip a refresh or a write leaves for the server's still-active old bank while the app files it under the new one — the split-brain class (`cicada-mcp-split-brain`; A03 closed it for entity reads only). Fix: send the switch first and hydrate on success, or tag every request with the bank it meant and drop answers for another. **(e)** `POST /maintenance/dedup-sweep` leaves its merges uncommitted — tracked on **G21**. **Rails:** one git writer per bank; a write commits alone, under its true author; capture never waits on Sleep longer than one episode (`capture-and-sleep.md`). Evidence: `docs/goals/audit-2026-10-02/STATUS.md`, `docs/goals/audit-2026-10-05/STATUS.md`. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 APPLY. | PARTIAL · P0 **Status 2026-10-07:** (a)/(e)/G21 shipped #204 — dedup sweep refuses in the window and commits only its pre-computed footprint (refuses dirty/unmerged/symlinked pages, exact rollback); decay/repos/inbox-resolve guarded. (b) not reproducible on any current path and (c) fixed (save scan outside `episode_lock`, lock hold −85%) — #201. (d) shipped #209: one serialized bank switch, `X-Cicada-Bank` + `409 bank_mismatch`, and every request pinned to one bank (ContextVar); scheduler jobs unpinned. Open: the write-admission lock closing the Sleep-transition race (branch `fix/g183-window-admission`, in review); **new (trial):** imported episodes are not committed by the import, so Sleep's first batch commit swept all 1,520 under the model's author. |
| G177 | <a id="g177"></a>**Narrow the "Sleep is running" refusals to the write window — a drain holds them for hours** (found 2026-09-29 building G163's drain) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Backend is_writing guards and paused-run behavior exist. ProjectWriteGate still keys off running, disabling app edits between drain batches; wire the writing contract through the Store. In-window claim attribution remains open, coordinated with G183. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g177). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** A person-started drain keeps `SleepState.status == "running"` for the whole run — many minutes to hours on a first import — and roughly fourteen guards key off that one word: the 409s on projects, entities, backlog, banks export/delete, local sources, memory, maintenance and the remote connector, MCP's `BACKLOG_SLEEPING` refusal, and `mcp_tools._backend_sleep_running`, which makes a stdio agent's claim stand **uncommitted** while Sleep runs so it rides Sleep's next commit (`test_agent_write_commits.py`). Under a drain that means (a) the app's writes are refused for hours, and (b) an agent's claim written between batches is swept by the next batch's `git add -A` under the Sleep author — the G85-class smear, at drain scale. Awake capture is not gated, so its episodes wait safely for the next run. **Design constraint for a fix:** the refusals only need to cover the window in which Stage 5 is writing to the commit, plus the tail's own commits; between batches the tree is clean and a writer can commit safely under its own author. That wants one predicate (`sleep_cycle.is_writing()`?) that every guard and the MCP probe share — never a per-route patch — and the SSE/`GET /sleep/status` contract unchanged. Bank switching is already refused by name for a drain (`SleepState.drain_run`). → follows **G163**, **G85**, **G135**. Triage: APPLY, S/M. **Built 2026-09-29 (branch `feat/consolidate-reads-everything`):** `sleep_cycle.is_writing()` — a plain or scheduled cycle holds the bank for its whole run; a drain only from a batch's Stage 2 (the pages Stage 5 rewrites are read there) through its commit, plus the run's start and tail. There is no continuation-only extraction checkpoint. Repeated consolidation of a growing thread repeats prefix input; quantify tokens/calls/latency rather than asserting a measured bill. [Incremental-consolidation research brief](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md): compare append-only new turns plus bounded prior context, stable turn/chunk reuse and targeted revision-aware reprocessing with full fallback against today's baseline. G163 owns accepted-reading retry reuse, G171 indexes, G110 pre-Sleep continuity. **Priority split:** P0 actual revision/integrity correctness; P1 RESEARCH efficiency and retention. The optimization does not gate the first small G10 trial. **Previous title (historical):** **A resumed conversation is consolidated twice, as if it were two** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "if i was working on a claude code conversation yesterday > slept > then continued in that conversation, is important that the system knows it is continuing"). **Earlier implementation review (before the efficiency follow-up):** One episode per session and changed-body requeue are implemented. Verify/reconcile removed or changed facts derived from previous revisions so grown sessions do not retain obsolete claims as current; new-file dedup is insufficient. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g104). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Verified: the mechanics exist, nothing uses them.** `session_id` is stamped at capture (G48) and `sleep_cycle._collect_session_ids` gathers them into `Cicada-Session:` trailers — but episode *selection* is purely `processed: false` (`sleep_cycle.py`), so Sleep never asks whether a conversation is still open. The failure sequence: work Monday → Sleep consolidates Monday's episodes and marks them processed → continue the **same** session Tuesday → Sleep consolidates the second half **with no awareness it already drew conclusions from the first**. Two concrete harms: (i) the **promotion threshold** is "referenced in 2+ separate conversations", and one split conversation can satisfy it, promoting an entity that was mentioned twice in a single sitting; (ii) a belief formed from partial context is never revisited when the rest of the conversation arrives — the extraction that saw half the story stands as if it saw all of it. **The tension is that `CLAUDE.md` already anticipated this** ("Conversations don't have clean endings. Batch sees patterns across full day") — but that reasoning assumed a conversation is *over* by nightfall, and G48's resumable sessions break the assumption. **REFINED 2026-09-01 (Rodrigo: "should see that either the first chunk produced something or it didnt, if it did re-check and add or remove or keep the same… or just reconsolidate everything?"). The incremental version is probably right, because most of the reconciliation ALREADY EXISTS:** `claim_reconciler` stamps `valid_to`/`superseded_by` when a new claim replaces an old one (`claim_reconciler.py:138-139`), and Wave-1's predicate work decides whether a second value contradicts or accumulates. So claims extracted from the second half already flow through add/supersede/reinforce **without re-reading the first half**. That narrows the gap to three things: (1) the **promotion threshold miscounts** — "2+ separate conversations" is satisfied by one split conversation; count distinct `session_id`s, not episodes (cheap); (2) nothing marks a belief as **formed from partial context**, so a claim from an open conversation is indistinguishable from one from a finished conversation — a provisional flag would let the later extraction supersede rather than merely contradict; (3) **summaries do not reconcile, only claims do** — the entity body is synthesized prose (`conflict_resolver._synthesize_entity_update:642`), so a summary written from half a conversation is NOT repaired by adding claims and must be regenerated. **Working split: claims incremental, summaries regenerated**, with full reconsolidation reserved for the case where the second half contradicts the first — which claim supersession already detects, so it can trigger itself. Cost argues the same way: with the CLI engine Stage 2 is sequential and dominates, so "re-extract everything nightly" is the wrong default. **Candidate solutions, cheapest first:** (a) **defer** — skip episodes whose session is still resumable and idle less than N hours, letting the conversation settle before consolidating (cheap, no new machinery, delays value by up to a day); (b) **treat a session as the promotion unit** — count distinct `session_id`s rather than episodes when evaluating the 2+-conversations threshold (very cheap, fixes harm (i) only); (c) **reconsolidation — the likeliest right answer**: when a conversation grows after having been consolidated, re-run extraction over the *whole* session and supersede the earlier claims rather than appending to them. The claim layer already supports this exactly — `valid_to` + `superseded_by` is precisely "this belief was replaced by a better-informed one" — so reconsolidation is less new machinery than it sounds, and it generalises beyond resumed sessions to *any* case where better evidence arrives later. Cost: extraction is re-run over already-processed episodes, so it needs a bound (re-consolidate a session at most once more, or only while it stays resumable). → depends on **G48** (session identity, already shipped); touches the promotion model and Stage 1/2; relates **G101** (the raw layer that would make a re-run cheap) and **G74** (the engine that must exist for any re-extraction to be possible). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P0 integrity; P1 RESEARCH incremental processing |

Expose actual checkpoint/capture freshness and missing turns; avoid a second authoritative context store. [G104 research](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md). **Start-prompts linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** G73 saves reusable objective briefs that can initiate work; link the chosen version and inputs to the resulting project/session. This row then continues that work automatically using current state, including before Sleep.
