Missing source/workspace/tool context is explicit, never a false promise of a byte-identical thread. Native transcript forking remains a separate optional research slice. [Required behavior and acceptance](../specs/2026-10-06-project-continuity.md). **Previous title (historical):** **Forking and handing off a live conversation — literal transcript transfer vs. memory handoff** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "an ability to include in cicada (forking ongoing conversations and such...)"). **Earlier implementation review (before the owner’s portability clarification):** Memory recall is portable; literal live-transcript fork/handoff is not implemented. Decide whether scope is a memory packet or transcript transfer, with separate consent and attribution. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g110). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The trigger:** Superset ships a button that transfers a whole Claude Code thread to "Codex, OpenCode, Pi, or any other agent", plus a separate "Fork session…". The stated pain is billing, not memory — hit a model's usage limit mid-session and be forced to downgrade or restart the work elsewhere. **Second prior-art data point, NOT yet researched — Cursor ships "Import from Claude Code"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, screenshot; flagged explicitly as "research how it works later"). Three things are visible in the dialog and each one matters to this row. **(i) It is a bulk *sync*, not a live handoff** — the button says `Sync`, it enumerates what it found (`Chats (2)`), and the copy is "Bring over your chats, and send a follow-up to fork it", i.e. **import-then-continue**: the thread is replayed into a new Cursor thread and forking happens by *sending the next message*, not by branching a live session. That is a materially different mechanism from Superset's per-thread transfer button and from `--fork-session`, and it is the cheapest of the three to implement — which is itself evidence about where the real difficulty is. **(ii) It necessarily reads the Claude Code transcript store** (`~/.claude/projects/*.jsonl`), so it is a live existence proof that the read is practical — and a direct instance of the rail this row is bounded by: reading is fine, *landing content in a bank* is not. **(iii) The part neither Superset nor this row had considered: it syncs `Plugins & skills (12)` by default, alongside the chats.** A conversation is not self-contained — it presupposes the tools, skills and plugins that were available when it happened, and a thread replayed into an agent lacking them produces confident nonsense about capabilities it does not have. For Cicada this opens a **third packet dimension beyond conversation content and consolidated memory: capability context** — which is, notably, the one dimension Cicada is worst placed to supply and the harnesses are best placed to, since Cicada does not model the receiving agent's toolset at all. **Research questions for later, none answered here:** does Cursor read the transcript files directly or drive a Claude Code CLI/export path; does "send a follow-up to fork it" preserve any lineage id or start a genuinely fresh thread; what is the plugins/skills sync actually copying (files, a manifest, a marketplace reference); and does any of it survive when the source session is still live. Settle these before assuming Cicada should build any of the three modes — a mechanism two vendors have independently chosen is a stronger signal than either one alone. **Cicada is unusually well placed for the memory half, because G48 already shipped the hard part:** a conversation has an identity (`resolve_session_identity`, `mcp/server.py:62-89`), it is stamped onto every episode (`_session_frontmatter`, `:96-108`), it is credited transitively to entities through `source_episodes` (`session_stats._group`), it is written into git as `Cicada-Session:` trailers (`git_service.py:35-62`, capped 50), it is listed by `/conversations/recent` and reopened by `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` (`conversations.py:164-207`). Cicada already knows what a conversation *is* and what it *produced*. What it has never had is any relation **between** conversations. **The design fork, and it must be picked before anything is built. (a) Literal transfer** needs one reader per harness and there is no shared format: Claude Code transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<uuid>.jsonl`) are a parent/child-UUID DAG of Anthropic-Messages turns (`content[].type == "text"`), Codex rollouts (`~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`) are a flat sequential log of OpenAI-Responses turns (`content[].type == "input_text"`) carrying `sandbox_policy`/`permission_profile`/git metadata with no Claude analogue — and the codebase's own precedent (`conversations.py:213` `detect_source()` dispatching to one `parse_*` per platform) already answers this: **one translator per harness, not one interchange format**. Both vendors also already ship the primitive (`claude --resume` / `--fork-session`; `codex resume [SESSION_ID\|thread_name]` / `codex fork [SESSION_ID]`, both defaulting to a cwd-filtered picker), so a literal transfer is Cicada re-implementing two CLIs' internals cross-vendor. **State the rail precisely, because the loose version is false:** it is *not* "no code opens a session log" — `harness_stats.codex_rate_limits` (`api/services/harness_stats.py:63-88`, live behind `GET /consumption/harness`, `routers/consumption.py:112`) already `read_text()`s and `json.loads()`es whole Codex rollout files today. The rail is **no transcript content enters a bank**, and G76 measured why: 741 MB `~/.claude/projects` and 73 MB `~/.codex/sessions` against a 2.4 MB curated bank. A translator that lands turns in `memory/` crosses it; one that pipes them CLI-to-CLI does not. **(b) Memory handoff** — mint the child its own identity and seed it with a small packet assembled from what the parent already got consolidated: the entity ids touched (`session_stats.find_conversation`, read **uncapped** — the summary projection truncates at `MAX_CONVERSATION_ENTITIES=12`, `session_stats.py:40`), the claims it wrote (`parse_claims`, `claims.py:175`, filtered to claims whose `source_episodes` intersect this conversation's episodes or whose `all_session_ids()` contains it), and the open questions on those entities — the last an **approximation only**, since inbox items key on `entity_id` and carry no session id anywhere (`inbox_service.py:81,333-334,636-638`; `InboxItem`, `schemas.py:795-819`). **(c) Continue from compact — Rodrigo's third framing, and it reorders the other two** (2026-09-01: "we could have the option to continue from compact (handoff kind of), on another agent (could be another harness), or continue with the same stream (which is what fork is probably)"). **First, a vocabulary correction that swaps which mode is hard:** in Claude Code's own terms `--fork-session` branches to a **new** id, while plain `--resume` continues the **same** id — so "continue with the same stream" is *not* fork, it is resume, and `POST /conversations/{id}/resume` **already does it today**; true forking is a one-flag change. **Second, the compaction boundary is a real and better-shaped seam than a transcript read, but the hook Cicada has written down is the wrong one.** `PreCompact` fires *before* the summary exists — verified structurally against a live transcript: the `compact_boundary` event carries only metadata (`preTokens`/`postTokens`/`trigger`, measured 905,931 → 26,812 tokens, ~30x) and the ~12.6-18.2k-char prose summary is the *next* line, a synthetic `user` turn. The hook that carries content is **`PostCompact`**, which is real and **appears nowhere in Cicada's docs or backlog**. **Third, and this is the trap:** G49 does list a "PreCompact flush" and G76 specs that hook precisely — as a **content-free pointer** (`{session_id, transcript_path, cwd, reason}`, "a pointer, NO content"). So carrying a compaction summary is **not** a second consumer of an already-designed capture point; it deliberately cuts against what G76 chose, and needs its own ruling rather than being smuggled in as an extension. No hooks are installed in the repo today at all (no `hooks` key in `.claude/settings.json`). **Why the framing is still the strongest of the three:** a compaction summary is already lossy, already prose, already stripped of harness structure — i.e. already the packet shape (b) has to construct — and it exists at exactly the moment the user hits the limit the tweet complains about, which is the one moment (b)'s packet is emptiest. **The tempting parallel, checked and only half-true:** a harness compacting because its window filled is structurally the same operation as Sleep — episodic turns compressed into semantic summary under capacity pressure — and Cicada's provenance/claims apparatus is a genuine edge over bare summary prose. But it does **not** cash out as a shortcut: Sleep is nightly batch over *closed* episodes while a hook has seconds mid-task, and compaction content is mid-task working state — the exact granularity G76 already excluded as "volume without judgment". The parallel justifies caring, not reusing the pipeline. **Fourth objection, specific to this half:** the hook surface is a moving target — the live hooks doc lists **33 event names**, most (`Setup`, `PostToolBatch`, `WorktreeCreate`, `PreModelSwitch`, …) absent from Cicada's own inventory — so anything built tightly against it dates fast. **What it does to provenance.** No conversation-to-conversation relation exists: zero hits for `parent_session`/`forked_from`/`thread_id` across every `*.py` and `*.swift`; `ConversationSummary` (`schemas.py:263-296`) and its Swift twin carry a flat `conversation_id`, a closed `kind ∈ {mcp, import}`, and the entities touched — nothing pointing at another conversation. An optional `parent_session_id` threads through exactly the four places `session_id` already flows (episode frontmatter, the 3-field frozen `SessionIdentity` at `mcp/server.py:55-59`, `_group`/`project_conversation`'s "first non-empty across the group" collection, one optional field on `ConversationSummary` + its Swift twin); trailers need no change (already a dedup'd flat list) but **degrade semantically** — a cycle spanning a parent and its forks reads as N unrelated conversations. **Memory stays shared, never branched:** the only branch-memory primitive is `POST /banks/{name}/duplicate`, the wrong grain (a deliberate life-partition, not a per-session artifact), and a working tree per fork would force all 20 routers to become branch-aware and break filesystem-as-single-source-of-truth. **One thing to NOT claim:** sibling forks that contradict each other are not silently archived — `claim_reconciler.trust_decision` (`claim_reconciler.py:170-236`) is trust-gated, returns `SUPERSEDE` for two `agent_extracted` claims only `if newer` and otherwise `CONFLICT_NUDGE` (`:210-212`), and since `valid_from` is always a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` two same-day forks tie and **already raise an inbox card**. The narrow real gap is that the card's option description (`_claim_option`, `:248-259` — age plus source episode) cannot say "this was a deliberate branch point". **Strongest objection, and it is serious: the packet is emptiest exactly when it is wanted.** Sleep is batch by ruling ("Batch over real-time consolidation"), and `_get_unprocessed_episodes` / `POST /sleep/trigger` run over the whole bank's `processed: false` backlog — there is no scoped-to-one-conversation sleep — so a session forked twenty minutes in has episodes and near-zero consolidated claims, and populating its packet would mean a synchronous bank-wide Sleep (slow, real spend, touches unrelated entities). The feature's value therefore skews to forking **old, already-slept** conversations, not the live "I hit my limit, hand off now" case the tweet is actually about. Second objection: a brand-new session already inherits everything Sleep consolidated, for free, via `cicada_recall` ("use this at the start of conversations", `mcp/server.py:137`) — lineage metadata improves provenance *presentation*, it unblocks no capability. **NOT DECIDED — do not build a half on assumption.** (i) literal mid-session branch vs. linear handoff (Superset ships both as separate buttons; only the handoff half is satisfiable inside the bank rail); (ii) whether a same-harness fork should get a NEW session id at all, or keep the parent's — keeping it sidesteps **G104** entirely for the common case, while a fork otherwise makes G104 strictly worse (three fragments of one shared prefix, and the "2+ separate conversations" promotion threshold reading siblings as independent corroboration); (iii) who calls the packet — a new MCP tool the parent invokes pre-handoff, or the user pasting; (iv) whether cross-harness lineage is admissible at all, since no signal crosses harnesses (a Codex rollout has no Claude UUID concept), so `parent_session_id` would be a **declared** fact self-reported by a third-party tool — an unauthenticated provenance channel Cicada has never accepted; (v) **UNVERIFIED** whether `claude --fork-session` exposes a parent-session env var to the child's MCP process, and under what name — only a structurally analogous pair was observed live (`CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` beside `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION=1`, for subagent spawns), and `resolve_session_identity` reads neither; settle it with an actual `--fork-session` env dump. Note that a *minting* path is not the gap — tier 2 is documented as an "explicit override for any MCP client" with `CICADA_SESSION_HARNESS` naming the harness (`mcp/server.py:42-43`) and tier 3 mints a `ses_` id for any caller (`:82-86`); what is missing is a Codex-side auto-detect branch. **Triage: RESEARCH, not committed work — and the cheap half is already filed.** G53's state dictionary plus G75's handshake hand any connecting agent a curated cursor with no transcript read at all; a fork packet is largely that payload plus a `from_conversation_id`, so build those first and see whether the fork want survives. → extends **G48** (conversation identity, the only reason this is addressable at all), reuses **G53**/**G75** rather than duplicating them; bounded by **G76**'s no-transcript-content-in-a-bank rail and its 741 MB / 73 MB / 2.4 MB measurement, and **contradicting G76's content-free-pointer choice** for the compact half, which needs its own ruling; extends **G49** (whose plugin design names a PreCompact flush but not `PostCompact`, the hook this actually needs); blocked on a **G104** ruling (a fork multiplies its double-consolidation harm); relates **G105** (the per-harness transcript stores and the absence of an interchange format) and **G106** (the two-way conversation↔entity loop a packet would render). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 **Status 2026-10-07:** slice 1a shipped #210 — same-folder rollover before Sleep (SessionStart startup/clear, fail-closed source, whole-context budget, `cicada_continue` pager; registry + disposable index in a guarded home outside every bank; no git in the hook path). Owner gates pending: A (PreCompact/SessionEnd flush vs ruling 7), B (capture-cap head+tail retention), C (G149 strip only harness-marked spans). Acceptance 5 recorded as a strict behavioural xfail (T10). Live acceptance deferred until the trial's bank is no longer active. |
| G61 | <a id="g61"></a>**Update sources for facts — "where to look this up" as a first-class reference on entities & claims** | **2026-10-28 — a saved link is where to check the page it is about, and a read's facts land on their pages (vision item 1; Rodrigo 2026-10-28: "Sources of update for entity pages properly getting saved and written from consolidation, conversation, research … Reading the saved websites and if there was a reason written when saved take that as signal for specifics"; branch `feat/vision-sources-reason`).** Gap measured on a copy of the owner's bank (real values redacted): a saved article about a tool never became a source on the tool's page, and a read page's facts never left the saved-link page. Built: Sleep's where-to-check step proposes every saved link a project/company/concept/tool/place page's live claim rests on, or whose `about` claim (link recon) names it — the saved URL, `entity:` the saved-link page, the person's reason as its evidence when they gave one; never a person page, a walled, workspace, secret-bearing, video, paper or search-results link (`reading_hosts.classify`). Dry run on the copy: **133 proposals on 113 pages** (55 company, 31 tool, 25 project, 15 concept, 7 place; 6 carry the person's reason), ≤ 4 a page, all rows of the one "Confirm N links" review; the step's `plan()` 1.5–1.7 s → 1.9–2.2 s on 8,032 pages. `cicada_record_read(facts=[{subject, predicate, object, quote}])` writes each fact as a claim on its subject's existing page with the quote's `page` span and the link as the agent's untrusted source (every door, G199/R12). **Coordinator calls, 2026-10-28:** saves with no reason join the review too; a read's facts never land on a person's page. Research citations as proposed sources (amends rulings 27/44) is written up, not built; a save's page description counting as the person's words for promotion is a separate fix. **2026-10-28 — sources coverage, measured (vision item 1's report, branch `feat/vision-measure-pictures`).** `cicada coverage sources` / `GET /diagnostics/coverage/sources` say which write path put each source on a page (`coverage_report.writer_of`, inferred from the fields each writer stamps). On a copy of the owner's bank: 293 of 5,683 live pages (5 %) list a source, 303 in all — Stage 1's official site 178 pages (113 trusted), the site backfill 59 (47), ruling 40's org sites 10, Sleep's proposals 49 (2 taken), cited URLs 2; **no source from the person, an agent tool, a remote app, the LinkedIn import or Contacts**; 171 tracked for a ruling-26 re-check; web citations stay claims (web-context 9, web-check 1). No media page lists its own saved URL as a source (4,027) — the gap vision item 1's "saved link becomes where-to-check" slice closes. **2026-10-26 — quick add in Sources (Rodrigo 2026-10-26: "In Sources, the ones that are reading in my browser — I should be able to quickly add more, like manually add a link. An agent can also do this ('add this source'); I told the agent and they added it here, and we can check there. This has a lot to do with sources of update for entity pages.").** Built in PR `feat/traces-sources-projects`: the "Reading in your browser" sheet opens on *Where to check* — paste a link, optionally pick the page (a name search over live pages, never creating one), Add: with a page it is that page's source as the person's own (`POST /entities/{id}/sources`, trusted); with no page it is saved as a link for Sleep (`POST /sources/save`). Below, every page's sources the person and their agents added (`GET /sources/where-to-check`, `source_roster`, engine-free, ETag over entities, not a Store domain), each with who and when (an agent by its harness name), each removable as the person (`/sources/change` remove — the `sources_removed` mark keeps it out). Cicada's own finds, a Sleep model's citations and the engine-free proposals stay on their pages and in the inbox review. *Next:* G223 (any source by its URL, the agent looks). **2026-10-26 — ruling 40: an organisation's own website, found during consolidation (Rodrigo: "a university has a website. Why wasn't this detected? … from it we also infer the picture if we are 100% sure"; branch `feat/org-website-sources`).** Cause, measured on a copy of the owner's bank (real values redacted): a `website` source came only from Stage 1 at page creation (`conflict_resolver.py:780`) or a backfill needing a 3+-letter own-name host label (`site_sources.py:123,155`); `location` was never eligible; 120 Stage-1 proposals sat "not confirmed" outside the D5 review. `org_sites` adds the record (an own-name origin in the person's line or on the page) and one structured lookup (Wikidata P856 for exactly one type-agreeing item, country not in conflict), trusted only when the homepage names the entity — else a proposal in "Confirm N links". Dry run: 483 live in-scope pages, 55 trusted, 114 proposals, 29 left to another writer, 285 nothing; zero model calls. Design constraint: a search hit is never trust; a removal is never re-added; nothing personal is sent. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Source CRUD, purposes, trust, removals and optional existing-entity links exist. Missing: general answer-time source lookup/check recording outside pending inbox questions, usable purpose/freshness UI, and bounded Sleep prechecks. S3 remains shadow-only. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g61). **2026-10-14 — one place for where a page's knowledge comes from (Rodrigo: "improve the view of source links that are used to update knowledge about an entity"; branch `feat/sources-view`).** Measured on the owner's bank (real values redacted): 251 pages carry exactly one source, every one a `website`; 227 added by an agent, 24 by Cicada; 75 verified, 60 with a `checked` time; no page has a source for any other fact — while the rest of "where this came from" sat in four other places (`## Links`, claim evidence, article provenance, the conversations list). Built: `GET /entities/{id}/sources/overview` (`page_sources`, engine-free, bounded: ≤ 4 beliefs per source and ≤ 12 links with honest totals, ETag + `ProvenanceCache`, wire pinned by a shared fixture) and the card's **Where this comes from**, which replaces *Where this came from* and *Look it up at*: coverage ("6 of 7 beliefs here have a place to check them") and freshness (last said, sources last checked), the sources grouped by fact with the beliefs each backs (each opens its timeline), the `## Links` a conversation named with that conversation (opens in the Reader) and *Use as a source*, then the conversations by app; a belief whose fact has a source wears a *Check at <site>* chip that lands on it. The "usable purpose/freshness UI" gap is half closed: freshness and what a source backs are shown; the purpose picker mapped to real predicates is still the free-text "Change what it is for…". **2026-10-14 (night) — owner ruling "Update, dated; the newest evidence wins" (TODO ruling 26), amending the S3 rail; branch `feat/source-refresh`.** Rodrigo: "Marking a dead site and being able to recheck a website after a certain time to see any updates … If I have the LinkedIn of a person … being able to update these things through consolidation would be nice", and the same evening: "it can override something I said if it has evidence, there is no problem with that." Built (`source_refresh`): a tracked source (trusted, a `url`) carries `refresh` state and an optional `recheck_days` (30 by default); `due` lists what is due with no fetch and no model; a public page is re-read on the Sleep tail behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (≤ 20 a night, one per site) and states ONLY what the page says in schema.org JSON-LD (`jobTitle`, `worksFor`, `homeLocation`… — deterministic, no model; a page whose words changed with no such field is handed to the person's agent); a login-walled source is never fetched by the backend and is leased to the person's own agent (`cicada_refresh_claim` / `cicada_record_refresh`, the explore queue's rules, the Reading-the-web switches as consent). One fold for every writer: a reading dated after a belief was last said closes it — the person's own included — and writes a new dated belief whose text says what changed, from where and when; a later statement stands; the same day or undated asks one `divergence` question. Two failed visits a week apart (not found, gone, parked, moved) mark a source dead with its last good capture kept. `GET /sources/due`, `POST /entities/{id}/sources/refresh` (check now, or the cadence), the overview's `updates`, the SessionStart line "N tracked sources are due for a check". Tests: `test_source_refresh.py`. **Still open:** the app's rendering of `updates` and the due state; a deterministic read beyond JSON-LD (a public page without it is the agent's). **2026-10-16 — owner ruling "a web lookup settles public things" (TODO ruling 27), amending S3; branch `feat/web-settles`.** Rodrigo: "A web lookup answers 'what is X' for public things and closes the question. The answer is saved with its link and quote, and you can undo it from a 'Settled by a check' list. Never searched: people's names, your own files, your projects, your life. Runs from your Claude plan in runs you start; a scheduled metered run costs a few cents more." And, the same day: the answer carries a citation but is not a tracked source (it never joins the re-check set, never makes a site trusted, never drives a logo); promotion is one tap, never automatic. Measured by the coordinator on the owner's bank (real values redacted): 222 open items, of which about 60 of the 126 conflicts and clarifications asked about public things (films, papers, tools, companies, products) and about 13 whether a Cicada PR or G row merged. Built: `web_settle` (the public/private filter — `company | concept | tool | media` subjects only, never a person, an untyped name the description says is someone, a person's name or alias, the owner's page, a file, path or handle, a project, a "you/your" question, a multi-valued or person-locus predicate; the pinned query = the name plus words from a fixed category list; one settle writer: a `web-check` episode holding the link and the quote as a `page` span, the claim `observer: agent`, `source_trust: external`, `origin: web-check`, a conflict's other open options closed, a page-less name held on its pending line, the question moved to `inbox/settled/` with a `settled:` record; the undo takes back exactly that), `web_lookup` (the Sleep-tail step: the Claude plan's own web search as the call's one tool on a person-started run, never on a scheduled one; a metered provider's search at its price, an `llm_call` of stage `web_check` in the run's scope so Past reads prices it; behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`; every reported search must equal the pinned query or nothing settles; 8 a run, a 30-day memo outside the bank), `cicada_record_check(outcome="settles")` (the person's own agent, never a connection; repository-state questions from a commit, trusted local agents only), `GET /inbox/settled`, `POST /inbox/settled/{id}/undo`, the `web_check` ledger kind (ids and enums), and the Inbox's "Settled by a check" list (Undo; Track this source through the person's own add-source route). Tests: `test_web_settle.py` (synthetic banks, a fake search tool), `SettledChecksTests`. **Not yet seen live:** a real person-started run; the quote is what the engine reported from its search, not re-read by Cicada. **2026-10-16 (later) — the lookup reads local context (ruling 27a; branch `fix/web-lookup-context`).** Rodrigo: "Yes, use local context." Measured by the coordinator on the owner's bank (counts only): the first person-started run looked up 8, all 8 searched the pinned query, 0 settled, all unsure; a no-write replay found 69 eligible (31 type conflicts, 1 other conflict, 37 clarifications), the 8 picked all conflicts, and three replayed clarifications all unsure. Cause: the model saw only a name — often one a restaurant, a town and a product share — and a category word that was sometimes wrong (a place searched as a "company", from the page's type); a direct probe with the same argv answered sure with a citation, so the tool works and the input was the gap. Built: `web_settle.local_context` (Cicada's one-line description — left out of a type question, where it is one of the options — and up to two mention lines from the item's conversation and its option claims' evidence; ≤ 600 characters; `episode_scrub`, links, addresses, handles and paths replaced, every person's name or alias in the bank replaced by "someone"), carried in the prompt only to tell which result is the thing meant; the query is the name plus at most one category word from the description (never the type alone, none on a type question, none an option label carries, a bare name when unsure), the pinned-query check unchanged; clarifications first, then conflicts with a mention line; `api/scripts/web_check_dry_run.py` (the real engine, counts only, writes nothing — no settle, memo, ledger row or commit). Tests: `test_web_settle.py` (bounded and scrubbed context never in the query, a context-laden search rejected, a type question without Cicada's guess, category words, order, the dry run writing nothing). **Not yet seen live:** the dry run on the owner's bank. **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06), current remaining scope:** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md) traces the actual code. Refresh entries, optional source-node links, fact grouping and source edit/add UI are implemented; these are distinct from wikilinks and evidence citations. The pending-question agent check is shadow-only, not general refresh for an ordinary current-fact query. Add an easy profile/social source flow with a purpose picker mapped to real predicates (`works-at`, `works-on`, `website`, general reference), access/trust and fact-check freshness; do not conflate confirmed site identity with fresh facts. Reuse an existing canonical source node and offer explicit save/create-and-link when useful; mandatory source-node creation is a design question, with lightweight refs retained in the recommendation. Expose the ranked cheat sheet to relevant recall/detail/CLI, define on-demand checks independent of inbox items and their finding-record path, then bounded Sleep pre-checks/agent handoffs. A permitted running agent can read the relevant source before answering; adding a source alone does not grant access. Current S3 never settles/reorders/changes a belief; old automatic-settlement sketches below are superseded by that rail. See the note for the approved-vs-proposed distinction and acceptance criteria. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** A contributor (model *or* user) can attach **sources** to a fact: a URL, a file, or a plain-English instruction ("my LinkedIn profile has my current job", "the `about` page of the company site lists the team"). Stored as a `sources:` frontmatter list on the entity (`{ref, kind: url\|path\|note, predicate?, added_by, added_at}`) and optionally on individual claims — distinct from `source_episodes` (provenance of *where a belief came from*) and from the `## Links` body section (loose bookmarks): a source is a **cheat-sheet for refreshing** a specific fact. Uses: (a) Stage 3 conflict scoring consults sources before asking — if a `works_at` conflict has a LinkedIn source, the model can propose "your profile says X" as the leading option, or resolve silently when trust rules allow; (b) the app's entity page shows sources with an "add source" row (URL or free text) and a "check now" action (`POST /entities/{id}/sources/check` → fetch → LLM compare → claim proposal via the inbox, never a silent overwrite); (c) `cicada_write_claim` accepts `sources=[…]` so a model can record "user told me to check <url> for this" mid-conversation. Fetching is keyless and rate-limited; private pages simply stay `note`-kind reminders the user acts on. → extends G60 (conflict inputs), M3/A2 (contributors), G9 (origins), G22/G30 (media & links). **Shipped 2026-08-30 (minimal slice):** `sources:` frontmatter via `api/services/fact_sources.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /entities/{id}/sources`, `cicada_write_claim(sources=)`, the conflict-card `hint`, and the app's entity Sources section. Fetching/"check now" remains a follow-up. **Update 2026-09-23 — agent-first clarification (Rodrigo: "remember in our cicada system inbox, some things can be clarified by checking a link to a website or an app using browser harness or computer use or something similar, so make sure to accomodate entities to have sources that can be used for clarification before scaling it to the user itself").** Reopened: this is (a) and (b) above, unbuilt. **The design is an escalation ladder:** before an inbox question reaches the person it climbs (1) Cicada's own public fetch — `link_enrichment.default_fetch` verbatim, zero-LLM, user-triggered "Check now" (`POST /inbox/{id}/check`, not the `/entities/{id}/sources/check` sketched in (b)) ungated like `sync_now`, the Sleep-tail pre-check behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`, no fourth gate; (2) an agent the person runs (browser harness, computer use, an app, a file), in flow or in a routine the person set up (`cicada_check_nudges(checkable=true)`), reporting through a new `cicada_record_check` (remote scope `record`) — the G140 watch-record shape: one `source-check` episode, ≤ 3 × 240-char quotes, `processed: true` from birth, a new seventh evidence kind `source` with a required `source [<host or app>]:` marker (never `page`, which `kind_for` reserves for an entity document); (3) the person, with the check on the card. **Which items are checkable** is one pure read-time function over kind (claim-path single-valued conflicts; divergence, clarification and merge inform-only; decay/normalization/removal never) and a new predicate `locus: world\|artifact\|person` (G121's marking at the predicate level; `person` is never checked, `artifact` recommends only, unknown needs a person-added predicate-matched source). **Sources grow additively:** `access` (public/signed_in/local, inferred), `kind: app\|repo`, `add_source` keyed on `(ref, predicate)`, the hint derived at read and voiced by `added_by` (today it says "You said" for agent and cicada sources), `cicada_add_source`, "Where could this be checked?" and "Only I know" on the card. **Resolution:** a finding settles only when two witnesses agree — a local agent's (or, 💸 later, Cicada's own LLM) semantic `supports` plus Cicada's own rail re-read of the same site within 24 h that locates the quote and finds no other option's label anywhere on the page. It never closes a human claim, the owner's own page recommends only, a remote report never settles, and the winner is capped at 0.8 below a person's 0.9. A settle is authored `Cicada-Author: cicada`, no engine, rule in the manifest (the G116(a) question with a second caller), and moves to `inbox/settled/`, reviewable, with "Ask me instead" as a real revert that writes `_check_overruled.yaml` so the pair never auto-settles again. **Ships in shadow first:** a settle-grade finding takes DR-42's first highlight and the person's pick grades it (`check_verdict`); the owner flips "Let a source settle a question" to auto with the numbers in view — never automatically. **The ToS rail binds Cicada's bytes; an agent in the person's own session is the person reading** (the G140 precedent); Cicada never spawns or drives one, never relays a credential, never searches for a person, and (pending owner ruling) gives no check line for a host it refuses to fetch itself. **Also fixes, in S0:** Stage 5.57's `default_summarize` fetch is ungated and looser than the rail (`sleep_cycle.py:1348-1352`); the duplicate `source_episode` key in `_conflict_nudge`; organic resolution not checking `is_human`. An agent's `proposes` report writes no claim (a check-time claim would supersede the machine options through the reconciler); it adds an option the person can pick. Slices S0–S8 (S0–S2 backend-only, startable now; S2 is a read-only coverage census that gates the rest), 16 rulings R-AC1…R-AC16, and owner decisions D-AC1…D-AC5 (settle default, LinkedIn-class hosts, the seventh evidence kind, G116(a), the contract change) are in `docs/specs/2026-09-23-g61-agent-first-clarification-design.md`. → uses **G118**, **G140**, **G113**, **G135**, **G115** (its Phase 2 "Research this" judge becomes a checker writing the same record); gives **G121** its world/personal marking and its re-verification mechanism; decides with **G116(a)**. APPLY, M×9 (S8 💸). **Ruled 2026-09-23 (owner: "i'd go with your suggestions"):** D-AC1 shadow first, flipped only by the owner; D-AC2 a `Check first:` line for refused hosts, only in the person's own open session, never signing in, inform-only forever; D-AC3 the `source` evidence kind; D-AC4 `cicada` authors a check-executed write; D-AC5 contract step 2 lets an agent check before asking. **S0 shipped 2026-09-23 (`feat/g61-sources-checkable`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-g61-s0-s2.md`):** Stage 5.57's page read goes through `link_enrichment.default_fetch` behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (it had no gate and downloaded whole bodies on a 5 s timeout); `add_source` is keyed on `(ref, predicate)`; a conflict's `hint` is derived at read (`fact_sources.served_hint`) in the voice of whoever added the source, and the Sleep writers stop storing it; `_conflict_nudge` cites the newest episode (a behaviour change for new items); organic resolution requires `claim_reconciler.is_human` (a narrowing: a `user_stated` claim from a non-human origin no longer closes a question by itself); the demo bank's works-at conflict carries a person-added `example.com` source. **S1 shipped 2026-09-23:** the source record gains `access` (stated, else inferred at read), `kind: app\|repo`, `accepted` and `only_me` (also on `POST /entities/{id}/sources`, where the person's repeat applies them); the vocabulary gains `locus: world\|artifact\|person` (seed + bank map, most conservative wins); `cicada_write_claim(sources=)` takes `{ref, access}`; new `cicada_add_source` (remote `record`, never a path or repo remotely); Stage 5.56 attaches a URL found verbatim in a new claim's cited span on a world/artifact predicate (zero LLM). **S2 shipped 2026-09-23:** `InboxItem.check` `{state, reason, locus, targets, rungs, settle_eligible}` derived at read by `source_check` (pure, engine-free, no fetch, nothing stored; no ETag or app change — the app's decoder ignores it); `GET /inbox/check-census` and `scripts/check-census.sh <bank>` give ids-free counts — the coverage measurement spec §15 takes at S2 and again two weeks after S5. Found on the way: `paper_metadata` already writes a `cicada` source (a DOI link), contrary to the spec's §4.3 note; it is never settle-grade. DS-3a (2026-09-24): the entity card's Look it up at shows S1's fields in words — the fact, the stated access (a path or repo reads as a file on this Mac, an app as an app), who added it, accepted / only-me — and no check line; `GET /entities/{id}/sources` serves only the STATED access, so an unstated url shows none until it also serves `effective_access`. **2026-09-29 (reading-the-web spec):** the Reader ladder (**G164**) is this row's rung 1 and the agent reading queue (**G166**, `cicada_reading_queue` / `cicada_record_read`) its rung 2; S4's `default_fetch` reuse becomes the ladder, so G61 S4 and G164's S1 share one implementation. The optional classifier navigator (**G168**) is where 'find the page that holds this fact' from a source's home page would use a small model. **2026-09-30 — Rodrigo:** "source should be something that was supposed to be built already, as also to give agents a place to look for information to update memory before surfacing it to the user … where does rodrigo work, my linkedin profile might be a source for update … Sources are of extreme importance, and from them even the images can be derived." Two things follow. (1) **Sources onto pages is the next step, as the census already said** (2026-09-23: 22 of 35 open items need a source first, 0 checkable): a company, tool or project page needs its own website as a source, and the person's own pages their profile pages, before any checker has something to check — so the next slice puts sources on pages (Stage 1 proposing an official site for a world-locus page, verified on Cicada's rail before it is kept; an agent adding one with `cicada_add_source`; "What would settle this?" on the card), then S3's agent check through G166's queue. (2) **Pictures come from sources** (G146's brand rung): `logo_service.domain_for` should read a page's website source first and never fall back to guessing a domain from the name — on the live bank the name guess gave a small company a stranger's mark and an AI provider a fireworks show's (real names redacted). A page with no website source gets its monogram until it has one. **2026-09-30 — S3-a shipped (sources as a living set; PR1 of the plan, branch `feat/sources-set`).** Rodrigo: "sources … could be multiple, not just one. An agent can store/change/delete sources depending on whether they are relevant for information update. Sources themselves can be linked to their own memory node which might have more info about it." Built: a page holds many sources per fact (caps `MAX_SOURCES` 30, `MAX_PER_PREDICATE` 8, `MAX_TOMBSTONES` 20 — D7); `fact_sources.rank` is the one picker (the person's, then a taken or verified one, Cicada's, an agent's) and `source_trusted`; `cicada_change_source` (`update \| remove`, `record` scope, `reason` required for a removal) and `entity`/`origin` on `cicada_add_source`; `fact_sources.owns_source` (an agent changes only what it added; a connection only its own `origin: remote:<id>`; the person's, a taken one, Cicada's and a Sleep model's never); `POST /entities/{id}/sources/change` keyed `(ref, predicate)`; a removal leaves a `sources_removed:` tombstone every non-person writer respects (today `attach_cited_urls`, `write_claim(sources=)`, `cicada_add_source` and Cicada's own DOI/skill-page adds; Stage 1's proposal and the website backfill will consult it when PR2 lands; an agent's own removal does not block another agent, the person's and Cicada's do — D6; the index DELETE tombstones too); `entity:` on a source (validated, never creates a page, a stale id reads as none, exact-match backfill `source_links` on the Sleep tail and `POST /maintenance/link-sources` — D8 — a read-time graph edge like `has repo`, the card's "Open page ›"); `entity_merge` now carries `sources`, `sources_removed` and repoints `entity:` (a page merge used to drop a page's sources); the card groups by fact with a per-row menu. Contract 12 / remote 9. **Deviations:** the unidentified `agent` label owns no entry (Stage 1's proposals carry it too), so a harness with no label cannot change its own; `cicada_add_source` used to write uncommitted while Sleep runs; it is refused then too (fixed in the review). **Still to build:** PR2 (a site verified on Cicada's rail, `domain_for` reading only a trusted website source, the logo purge, Stage 1's `website` field) and PR3 (the agent check on G166's queue). **2026-09-30 — S3-b shipped (PR2 of the plan: websites verified, pictures from sources; branch `feat/sources-sites`).** Rodrigo: "the image resolver is not working the best for not as known things. My idea is maybe to get it from the icon of the actual website source … Sources are of extreme importance, and from them even the images can be derived." Built: Stage 1's optional `website` (company/tool/project; `sanitize_website` keeps an https origin of a public, non-platform host); stored UNVERIFIED by the create branch (`fact_sources.propose_site`, never over a tombstone); an engine-free backfill from a `website` claim or a `## Links` host that is the page's own name; `link_enrichment.fetch_identity` on `default_fetch`'s transport (`_stream_html`); `site_sources.judge`/`verify` (the name plus two words of the page's own summary; D1, revised in review: verified stamped; only a positive namesake (the name on the page, its summary words absent) is removed and remembered; anything unproven — a thin page, a redirect, a bot block — is "not confirmed"; a network failure is tried three nights, then again in a month, never tombstoned); the Sleep-tail step behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (≤ 25 fetches, one per site, `sleep/site-check`) and `POST /maintenance/verify-sites`. `logo_service.domain_for` now reads ONLY a trusted `website` source (no name guess, no `## Links`, saved-link or claim fallback, no platform host, never a person); `LOGO_RULE` 2 purges an older cache once (`sites/` spared); a page that loses its site drops its mark. The card shows "Official site · confirmed <day>" or "not confirmed yet · Use this site", a type-aware add field, and each row's site mark (`GET /entities/{id}/sources/icon/{site}`). **Still to build:** PR3 (the agent check on G166's queue). **Not yet seen live:** the confirmed / not-confirmed / mismatch split on the real bank (run `POST /maintenance/verify-sites` on a copy first and read the counts). **2026-09-30 — S3 shipped, shadow (PR3 of the plan; branch `feat/sources-check`).** Rodrigo: "give agents a place to look for information to update memory before surfacing it to the user … where does <person> work, my profile page might be a source for update." Built: check entries in G166's reading queue (a pending `checkable` question's own ranked sources; the one consent is the per-site permission, D5; the owner's page only what the person added or took, D2; least recently checked first); `cicada_record_check` (`record` scope) writing one `source-check` episode with `attachment [host]:` page-kind quotes (D4) and a `checks[]` row on the item; `check_agent` ledger kind; contract 13 / remote 10; `GET /inbox` `checks[]`; the card shows what the agent reported with no highlight, no tag, no reorder; Settings' site list shows how many questions a site could answer. `website` sources match page-level questions and a verified one waives the unknown-locus gate. **Nothing settles, holds or reorders** (S4–S8 wait: the fetch rung, settling, the hold window, retirement, the paid witness). **Not yet seen live:** a real agent checking a real profile page; run `scripts/check-census.sh` on a copy of the bank before and after to see how many questions became checkable (counts only). **Audit 2026-10-02 A05/A06 (`fix/audit-app-consistency`):** the card's source writes ran as independent tasks with whole-list snapshots, so a failure could roll back another write's success; they now run through a card-local `SourceWriteQueue` in order. An add cleared the field before its request and swallowed failures (`try?`); it is now the `EntitySourceAdd` mutation, keeps the draft and toasts the server's sentence. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · core sources built; general refresh + source UX priority, P2. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** **Sources on people, proposed by inference, and a better name** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22: "this update sources (maybe come up with a better name), is of extreme importance, so any agent and cicada as well can provide/have a heads up as to where to check the info … it might as well be a personal website, a google scholar account or anything"). **Measured (owner's bank, read-only):** 0 of 277 person pages carry a source and 276 show initials; 327 of 328 sources bank-wide are `website` on companies, tools and projects. No writer reaches a person: Stage 1's `website` proposal covers company/tool/project only (`site_sources.sanitize_website`), `attach_cited_urls` takes only `world`/`artifact` predicates, `cicada_add_source` takes only "a source the person named, never one the agent guessed" (`mcp_tools.py:3106`), and the LinkedIn import and Contacts seed matched nobody there. The picture ladder keys every non-owner rung on a source (`person_photo.is_own_account`, 213), so it has no input; 5 pages hold profile links in their text that it cannot see, by design. **Proposal:** (1) an engine-free step on Sleep's tail, person pages only, proposing profile links already on the record — a URL in the page's Links, claims' cited spans or Key Facts; the handle path of a saved post or repo the record ties to the page; an `X (handle)` extraction name — stored as a proposal with its evidence span, respecting `sources_removed`, never a URL guessed from a name; **one grouped inbox review** ("Confirm N links", each line Yes = taken, No = tombstone — owner 2026-10-22: never one question per page; a dry run on the owner's bank would have added 36 questions to an inbox of 218), settled only on the person's own located words (ruling 29); (2) agents may propose too: `cicada_add_source(inferred=true, evidence=…)` lands the same untrusted proposal (no picture, no refresh) until the person takes it, and the tool text says so; (3) a taken source does the rest with existing machinery — the picture ladder (GitHub at once; X and LinkedIn with ruling 36 D5's gate, now on), and ruling 26's dated re-check (walled sites only by the person's agent). Contacts are a source too (owner: "run tests for that too"). A person's own links may show as a row at the top of their page in the link-view grammar (owner left it to the acting owner; proposed yes). **Name — three options, one pick, confirmed by the owner 2026-10-22:** **"Where to check"** (pick: says what it is for, fits a personal site, a scholar page, a repo or any link, and reads as an instruction to an agent); "Places to look"; "Keep-current links". Agent-facing definition, the same words in the tool schema and the skill: *a link where the current state of this thing can be checked — a profile, a personal site, a publication list, a repository or any page; Cicada re-checks it on a schedule and agents check it before answering about the thing.* The stored key stays `sources:` (a rename of a frontmatter key is a Hyrum change; only the words change). **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed (`sources`, `people` workers); the redesign briefs cite this as G208. | PARTIAL · P1 |

Scheduled: its own job every 6 h (`tier2.scheduled_sync`), behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`, skipped before any request while Sleep writes; Sleep's tail skips `TIER == 2`. Every connector token (the saved-content ones too) is stripped from engine children (`CONNECTOR_SECRET_ENV`). **Readwise** (`readwise.py`): the export endpoint with the person's access token, GET only; `readwise:<book>:doc` (the saved item, the person's document note as `user:`) plus `readwise:<book>:<UTC day>` (each highlight as `attachment [<title>]:` quoted — `page` evidence — and the note on it as `user:`), dated when highlighted; incremental by `updatedAfter` + `includeDeleted`, a changed book re-read whole (`ids=`); linked through `media_entity_id` to a saved link with the same URL or the one non-person page with the exact title — never a page made. Readwise's terms (readwise.io/tos §8) allow "connect AI assistants and other tools to your content … through our APIs"; the token has no read-only scope, so Cicada asks only reads. **Notion** (`notion.py`): see G219. **Spotify next** — needs the person's own app at developer.spotify.com (client id, redirect URI on the loopback callback, scopes `user-read-recently-played user-library-read`, PKCE); the recently-played endpoint holds only the last 50 plays, so history before that stays the extended-history export (G32). Evidence: `test_connectors_tier2.py` (32 tests, fake servers over recorded shapes in `fixtures/connectors/`). **Ruling 47 (owner 2026-10-28):** the choosing rule (TODO ruling 47) picks each source's path: a pasted URL is read through the person's own browser by default (tier 1, this row's agent read), and a service where an official API gives what the harness cannot — history, metadata, reliability — offers **Connect** instead (tier 2: off by default, read-only scopes, what it reads in words, one-click revoke). Spotify → API (listening history and metadata; the harness sees only what is playing); Letterboxd → harness plus its public per-diary RSS (its API is granted by request; adopt it only if granted). Research whether a personal install can get Spotify API access without a business registration. Re-triaged RESEARCH → APPLY. **Builds on** G61's quick add (PR `feat/traces-sources-projects`: a pasted link becomes a page's source or a saved link). **The step after:** a pasted URL of a service (a Spotify profile, a Letterboxd diary, any site) becomes a *way in* — the person's own agent visits it in their own signed-in browser session, says whether it can read it, and when the person is not signed in asks them to sign in (the G205 agent-request card), then tries again. **Rails:** "Reading with the person's own agent" — Cicada never spawns a browser and never signs in, the backend never fetches a login-walled page, only a link the person added can be recorded, and the ToS rail holds; per-site reading stays the person's switch (G166). **Named services:** Spotify (G32's streaming-history export, G94) and Letterboxd (its diary and lists, export first) as the first two adapters. | APPLY · P1 |

Do not report this end-to-end path as proven from queue UI or a synthetic record test alone. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** First-class **`video`** entities (a link/reference to the video) and **`image`/`frame`** entities. **Source of truth = the video's transcript/captions**, stored with the entity; the *agent* watches the video (via a Claude video skill — Rodrigo flagged [`bradautomates/claude-video`](https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video), not yet set up) and saves a **summary + any discussion thoughts** relevant to the conversation as the entity body. Later a query like *"show me all the robotics videos I've saved for my research"* surfaces them and the agent knows what each entails. → extends **G2** (media taxonomy), builds on `cicada_save_url`; the summary-grounded-in-transcript pattern is the media cousin of **Phase 3 source-grounded rewrite** (`docs/specs/2026-07-03-retrieval-and-bookworm-improvement-design.md`). **The 2026-09-05 in-app video renderer (Track V) is G11's *preview* half and moves this row not at all:** a saved video now plays in the app, but transcripts/captions as the entity body — the half that answers the query above — remain the open work here. **G140 (2026-09-23, `feat/memory-quality`):** the four defects in the documented watch chain are fixed — a note for an already-saved video is kept as its own episode instead of dropped; both save replies name the episode to cite; `external:<name>` is a value the observer schema accepts; and a watch is recorded by `cicada_record_watch(url, summary, excerpts=[{t, quote}], chapters?)` as one episode (`assistant:` summary, `video [m:ss]:` quotes) plus one `describes` claim whose quote spans have the new `media` kind — excerpts only, never a transcript (R5 D3), Cicada never downloads a video. Vimeo/Loom descriptions are kept and chapters parsed from them (`media.chapters`). The row's "transcript stored with the entity" is superseded by cited excerpts. Open: the app's watch UI (Track P/O). **2026-09-29:** the app's watch UI (this row's open item) is designed as G162 — states, a queue outside the bank, a thumbnail picker and a person-triggered watch run ([`2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md)); 'transcript stored with the entity' stays superseded by cited excerpts. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · recording and Sleep intake built; useful-video acceptance open, P2. | PARTIAL · P1 |

Prove recording → Sleep → useful grounded recall; fix observed unknown-host, returning-to-run and seek seams. Optional duration/model rungs are not prerequisites. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g162). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Owner follow-up (2026-10-06):** [source/video follow-up](../research/2026-10-06-source-refresh-and-video-gaps.md). Prioritize the usable watch workflow and G22 end-to-end acceptance before peripheral capture channels. States/queue/handoff and unprocessed watch records are implemented. Verify the reviewed skill path/re-pin (V4b), allowed acquisition, current login/reading-method integration with G166, unknown-host queueing, return-to-run UX and timestamp navigation against current code. Queue credit, agent-reported basis and Sleep processing are different states. V5 optional lengths and V6 direct keyed understanding stay DECIDE and do not block the existing agent path. Include one allowed watched-video trial in G10; do not require optional direct API processing or new embeddings before it. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The gap, from the code (spec §1, read at `dev` @ `2155940`).** `cicada_record_watch` (G140) writes one episode and one `describes` claim per watch, but nothing says *how* a video was read (a transcript or the frames), the app has no surface for the record (G22's open item, Track P/O), and nothing lets the person choose which of the saved videos deserve an agent's time. Watching is the expensive step, so it must be deliberate; a title and description cost nothing. **The design ([`2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-video-understanding-design.md), 20 review critiques checked against the code, 19 held and are fixed; §12 is the record).** (1) *Two steps named apart:* Sleep consolidates what an agent recorded; a **watch run** is a separate, person-started act in the Feed's Videos view (a thumbnail picker and a run card, then *Copy for an agent*). Cicada never downloads, watches or transcribes; the person's own agent does the work under G140's rail. (2) *Modes:* metadata always (free); transcript the cheap per-video default; watch always a deliberate click. Sizes show as Light, Medium or Heavy plus minutes, never a price (the standing no-price ruling); there is no batch cap (owner 2026-09-29/30): a hand-off takes every selected video, an agent's claim leases 10 a call and its prompt loops, and the queue file's only ceiling is a 2,000-row safety limit. (3) *Five honest states*, a set-union over the records: `none`, `transcript`, `watched`, `watched_and_transcript` and `recorded` ("method not given" — every legacy or basis-less record, never labelled 'Watched'); the owner named four, the fifth is the honest answer for every record made before this change. (4) *Identity:* the queue and the state key on the url-index `videoKey`, never the media entity id, because same-titled videos share an entity id (see G172). (5) *The queue is outside the bank* (no commit noise, no dirty-tree smear), with a satisfaction table (a `transcript` record closes a transcript request, `frames` or `both` close either, a basis-less record closes only the leaseholder's entry) and a `VideoRefresh` sync component. (6) *MCP:* `cicada_video_queue` (read scope) and `cicada_video_claim` (record scope) — two tools because one tool cannot carry two scopes; `record_watch` itself is unchanged apart from a `basis`. Copy says 'picked up by an agent', not 'being watched', and derives 'Read by Sleep' from `processed_by`. (7) *Rails:* the 'Use my browser' method is removed for YouTube; a new grep test with a named allowlist guards the no-download rule; optional V5 API data stays outside the bank with a 30-day life. **Slices:** V1 states and basis; V2 queue and the two tools; V3 picker, run card, hand-off and demo (the queue lives outside the bank, so the demo shows the run card by an app fixture plus the person's own click, and adds two saved videos that move the pinned media counts); V4a neutral skill wording, V4b after a re-pin; V5 an optional YouTube key for lengths (DECIDE); V6 an in-app Gemini watch on the person's own key (DECIDE, gated). Acceptance tests A1–A9, B1–B11, C1–C11, D1–D4, E1–E5, F1–F5. **Also filed here, as sub-items not new rows:** a Cicada-launched agent for watching (needs a non-`--safe-mode` spawn; RESEARCH, not recommended) and a logged-in-browser read for non-YouTube videos (decided with **G166**). **Open questions for the owner (spec §8):** Q1 may Cicada itself ask Gemini to watch a link (recommend not yet); Q2 the queue outside the bank (recommend yes); Q3 auto-queue transcripts on save (recommend no, one click plus an off-by-default setting); Q4 the run lives in the Feed, not the Sleep page (recommend the Feed); Q5 an optional YouTube key for lengths (recommend yes, off by default); Q6 how loud to be about the local downloader (recommend keep it listed, and lead with the path where the person's own agent opens the video link itself, after a re-pin — the copy names no provider); Q7 keep the word 'watch run'; Q8 a logged-in-browser read for non-YouTube videos (recommend deciding it with G166); Q9 keep the fifth state. → extends **G22**, **G140**, Track V; reads against **G138** (the skill catalog), **G125** R10 (Sleep keeps one trigger), **G166**. Triage: V1–V4 APPLY, V5 and V6 DECIDE. **2026-09-30 — the boards were approved and the backend built (branch `feat/video-watch`, spec §13; TODO ruling 17).** Rodrigo: "I also like the watch video designs, apply them." Amended by the owner's words: **no batch cap** (a hand-off takes every selected video, a claim leases 10 a call and the prompt loops; the queue file's safety ceiling is 2,000 rows, not 200), **provider-neutral copy** (nothing names a provider or model as the one doing the job; the engine `gemini_url` became `video_link`), and **no site list for video** (any saved video can be queued; a login wall is handed back with a `needs_login` code and surfaced, and whether an agent may use the person's browser is the reading plan's single permission — the hand-off carries it as an instruction only while it is on). **Built (backend):** the states and their derivation (`video_state.py`, one shared kind rule with the Feed, `video_kind.json`), the record's `basis`/`engine`/`duration` at all five argument sites, the queue outside the bank (`video_queue.py`, `videoQueue` sync stamp that makes a lease lapse visible, `nextChangeAt`), `cicada_video_queue` and `cicada_video_claim` at every MCP site (not refused while Sleep runs; a lapse is judged only when Sleep does not hold the pages), the hand-off prompt and `/videos/*` routes, the provenance `watch` block and fidelity, the demo's three states, the `video_queue` ledger kind, the contract clause (CONTRACT_VERSION 10, remote 7). **Built (app, same branch):** the Feed's video rows and the Videos strip, the picker and the watch run card (the boards' four states, *Copy for an agent*, progress on `batch.done`), `VideoBlock` in the Feed's detail and the entity card's media block, the Reader's watch header and per-turn fidelity, the Sleep page's one Details row (`VideosWaitingRow`), `VideoStateCache` (revalidates at `nextChangeAt`), all copy provider-neutral under a test. The librarian's `/watch` line (V4a) is rewritten neutral. **Not built:** V4b/V5/V6; the room sentence's "An agent has picked up n videos." rung (left to the Sleep v5 branch, which owns `RoomSentence`; the input is `VideoSummary.claimed`); the needs-login row's *Allow your agent to use your browser* button and its sentence (both wait for the reading branch's permission API; `VideoActions.for(_:permission:)` takes `nil` until then, and `nil` shows no sentence — final review 2026-09-30); and "How your agent watches" (a seam: `video_prompt.method_clause`, filled by the reading branch's `agent_methods`). **Open:** a video on a host `video_urls` does not know is a link and cannot be queued (recommend a follow-up "Have an agent watch this" on any saved non-paper link); the boards do not show how to return to an unfinished run (ask the designer). **Model check (2026-10-22, `.claude/skills/verify-video-queue`):** TLC over the lease lifecycle (one video, two callers, for a stable session id, a shared one, none, and one that changes on restart; two videos for orphans) verifies no double lease, no lease taken away, idempotent release/record, no attempt burned while Sleep holds or a newer satisfying record exists, a holder can always hand back its lease, and leases and failed rows ending. Five findings, decided by **ruling 34 (2026-10-22)** and done on `fix/video-lease`: **F1** `release` never checked who asks, and **F5** two remote conversations that omit their handle share one id, so one could close the other's lease with a no-basis record: fixed by a per-claim 128-bit `lease_token` that `cicada_video_claim` prints on each video's line and that `release` and a no-basis `record_watch` require (a tokenless or wrong one answers `not_yours` / `left`, and the lease lapses on its own; a session-id compare was model-checked and rejected: it fails for CLI, restarted and shared ids); **F2** every writer read the saved videos before its lock, so a stale snapshot could drop a freshly queued video: fixed, every writer reads them under the lock and takes no `saved` argument; **F4** a caller using the queue tools kept every lease under its id forever: fixed, a refresh never carries a lease past `MAX_LEASE_PERIODS` (3) lease periods from its claim; **F3** the credit closes a row on a record older than the request, and `requested_at` is kept to the second: accepted (a watch that meets the request exists; revisit if a lease is seen ending on an older record, then take the microsecond compare the model keeps). The model's `code-*` checks now run the fixed code (P3 and P9 under a caller that never stops now verified; P2 in `code-churn`, `--deep`); each finding's check runs the code with that one fix taken out and still fails. In `api/tests/test_video_queue_formal.py` the F1, F2, F4 and F5 tests pass and F3's two stay xfail strict. Whether it finishes without an interactive prompt is unverified until S6. **What is on this Mac today (§8.3):** detected by bundle id, never by opening a profile folder; there is no per-browser 'Connected' state and no 'in your browser' copy. **The two ways an agent reads (§8.4):** *Route A*, the recommended one — the person's own agent (with its own browser skill or extension) reads through `cicada_reading_queue` and records with `cicada_record_read`, contract item 9 and its own primer cache key; *Route B*, a spawned browse call class (`BROWSE_FLAGS`), only if three spikes say yes (S6, then S7). **Safeguards:** pages Cicada's reader could not read reach an agent only via a per-link *Ask an agent* or a per-site permission the person turns on (one entry per site per call; nothing is on until they do), and Cicada promises nothing about what the agent does in its browser; an ask's URL is visible to any connection holding `read` (TODO ruling 14 departs from the spec here — the person's own "Ask an agent" is the consent to hand that one URL to an agent; `sources` still gates every verbatim word of the person's conversations). A read episode uses the `attachment [<host>]:` grammar so its spans are `page`, is `processed: true`, and its excerpts are labelled unverified ('as <agent> read it'). Page content is untrusted input, and what Cicada can and cannot check is said once (§8.5, §8.6). **Reworked 2026-09-30 (owner: "limiting the amount of sites makes no sense to me, because we will never know which sites this will happen"; TODO ruling 14 amended):** there is no pre-picked site list. A site is *surfaced* when Cicada's own reader could not read one of its pages (`reading_walls`: a sign-in, a consent wall, a refusal or a host the backend never requests, from stamps the fetchers write at save time, in the in-cycle pass and in the backfill, only while the page holds no words), listed on Settings → Reading the web ("Sites that need your browser") with measured counts and a per-site switch (`reading.agent_sites`, `GET /reading/sites`, `PUT /reading/settings` `sites`); the queue is the person's asks plus the wall pages of allowed sites, derived at read (`reading_queue`), so a switch writes one line and a new page joins with no write. Per-page *Ask an agent* stays and needs no site permission; an agent's `needs_login` pauses a site's derived entries until the row expires or the person turns the site on again. Favicons for the surfaced sites come from the icon service only (the walled site is never contacted). **How your agent reads** (`agent_methods`; G178 generalises it): the person chooses "Let my agent choose", the agent's own tools, or a catalog skill (`browser-harness`, `macos-harness`, pinned and hash-verified, copy-only `agent-prompt` install) — an instruction in the hand-off prompt, the stdio queue reply and one primer line, never to a remote connection — and a chosen skill gets a `type: skill` page tagged `agent-skill` in the memory graph. All reading copy is provider-neutral (owner, 2026-09-30: providers are "literally just providers"; `test_provider_neutral_copy.py`). Terms of the walled platforms forbid automated access even when signed in — the person's account is the one at risk (§5.1, research, not legal advice). **Modes by the owner's word (§8.7):** Fast reader (default) · With an agent · With my browser. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW5 ship 'With my browser' at all (recommend yes, last — S4's Route A needs no Cicada code that touches a session — behind an acknowledgement, per-link asks and a per-site permission offered only for a site Cicada's reader could not read); the video spec's Q8 (a logged-in read for non-YouTube videos) is decided here. → extends **G61** (rung 2), **G102**, **G140**, **G105**; the skills half is **G138**; the video seam is **G162**.
