Built: `cicada_reading_queue` / `cicada_record_read` (stdio and remote: `read` / `record` scope), the ask store outside the bank, `reading.json` settings with the versioned first-use acknowledgement, `page_read.py` (episode with `page` quote spans, a `describes` claim closed on re-read, stamp, thin description), contract item 9 (CONTRACT_VERSION 9, remote 6) and the hook's waiting-links sentence, the `reading` sync component, `GET /sources` `read` block, `read_agent` ledger kind, R-RW4/5/8/9 as TODO ruling 14. **Deviations from the spec, all recorded in ruling 14 or here:** outcomes other than `read` never touch the bank; an ask's URL is visible to `read` scope; public rows (`needs_js`, `robots`) wait for S1; "Ask an agent" in the Reader's Links section waits for S2 (the REST already takes any URL). **The chip label (review 2026-09-29, closed):** a quote Cicada never had is never shown as a bare "From the page". `/episodes/{id}/text` and each provenance conversation row carry the episode's `source` (`page-read`; both ETags gained `src1`), and the claim chip, its hover, the Reader's turn and "Noted from this conversation" say "From the page, as <agent> read it" (`EvidenceLabel.speaker(source:)`). **Ambient "needs login" (review):** the Feed row carries "Needs sign-in" in its second line, and a toast announces a link that just hit a wall (never on the first look after launch or a bank switch); the agent's own `note` shows in the Read section as "<agent> noted: …". The last-read day on Settings → Reading the web is a small file (`reading-last.json`), not a ledger scan. **Not built:** the Reader's Links section, Route B, S1. **Review 2026-09-29:** `cicada_record_read` refuses every outcome for a link with no live ask row (before, a saved link with no ask took any; since 2026-09-30, a wall page of a site the person allowed is recordable too), and a `read` is refused while Sleep runs on stdio, with a reply that says to keep the summary and record it when Cicada has finished (holding a `read` in the ask store to commit after Sleep is open). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · agent reading built; source/video handoffs and Reader extensions open. **Exploring saved sites (owner rulings 2026-10-14, TODO ruling 25; slice 1 on `feat/explore-sites`):** the person clicks *Explore saved sites* on Sources (or asks a trusted local agent in chat, `cicada_explore_start`, D5), one batch lands in `$CICADA_HOME/explore_queue/<bank>.json`, and their own agent leases one site at a time (`cicada_explore_claim`: one live lease per site across agents, ≥ 8 s between pages, ≤ 30 an hour, 15-min leases, three tries, a wall pauses the whole site 7 days) and runs Cicada's pinned capture script in browser-harness, recording with `cicada_record_capture`. **D2 amended by the owner:** the agent explores in the person's everyday signed-in Chrome session by default, every capture marked `signed_in`; a Settings switch turns it off, and off means a private cookie-less `Target.createBrowserContext` — verified drivable through browser-harness against a headless test Chrome (cookies do not cross; three daemons in parallel), unverified in a headed Chrome behind the inspect approval. **D3:** the acknowledgement is v3 with an exploring paragraph (the person's accounts carry the terms risk) and a per-site *Never explore*. Walls and captchas are `needs_login`/`blocked` with no screenshot and never retried with other headers or context; consent banners are never clicked or hidden. **Slice 2 (owner 2026-10-14 evening, branch `feat/explore-depth`):** Rodrigo: "By default we explore the bare minimum of a website … If I say 'I love the design' in all the pages or something like that (or not so explicitly), then the agent has a reason to dive into the website … and this, maybe a sitemap of sorts, can live in memory also." Built: `explore_plan` turns the reason — G191's ids, the person's own words about the save, and the person's turns in conversations about the page — into a per-site plan with no model (closed keyword families; design → front page, pricing, docs, product, blog with full/mobile/dark and Looks; product → front page, pricing, about, docs; technique → the section matching their words; "every page" widens; a named page is opened), at most six pages, cut to the hour's room; the capture script v2 follows it (robots.txt read once without cookies, ≥ 8 s between pages, never a sign-in page, stops at a wall) and the agent may swap in its own pages or section within the plan; the record vets every further page (host, robots, pace, lease window, cap, walled-before); `site_map` keeps `captures/_sites/<site>.md` in the bank, append-only over visits, read by `cicada_site_map` and `GET /explore/sites[/{site}]`; a design reason keeps up to three distinct pictures as Looks through `design_refs` (`via: explore`). **Open:** recon over the outline (G102), read-depth summaries and excerpts through `page_read`, *Keep as a Look* on any capture, the app's page strip and site-map view; slice 3 (D6 change probe, then/now gallery, dead-site states, export with captures). **Looks v2 (owner 2026-10-15, branch `feat/looks-v2`):** Rodrigo: "Show what you did for a website's aesthetic extraction." A hand run on a public design-heavy editorial site (one cobalt ink on off-white; real values redacted) showed five gaps, each fixed: (1) the screenshot never drew — the app's bytes loader took `/entities/…` only, so `/design/…/image` was a remembered miss and the palette gradient stayed (the route was fine: 200, a valid 1440×900 PNG); (2) the area-ranked palette read "off-white 90 %, pale blue 4 %, grey 2 %" and lost the ink — now the ground, then salience (chroma and contrast against the ground × a presence term), at 1600 px not 256, blends folded, declared colours merged, each with a role; (3) an agent's note showed under "What you liked" — now `note_by`, "Noted by <agent>", and the REST door takes `author`; (4) fonts were only what a snippet named because `next/font` hashes family names — the capture script v3 reads computed styles in the page (`_DESIGN_JS`: body, headings, captions, links, drop cap; ground, ink, accent, wash; most-used colours), validated by one schema (`design_tokens`, `design-tokens-1`) and shown as *Type* and *Colour roles*; (5) one action — *Save its look* on a saved link (Feed detail, ⌘B row) or `cicada_explore_start(look=…)` queues a design visit (`asked: look`) whose landing picture becomes a Look even when unchanged. **Pin:** site-explore's file hashes are re-pinned in the branch; the pin's `sha` still names the previous dev commit and must be moved to this branch's squash commit in a follow-up commit after the merge (`scripts/verify-skills.sh` fetches by that sha). **Open:** a live run of the v3 script through browser-harness on a real saved site. **Looks polish (owner 2026-10-15, branch `feat/looks-polish`):** Rodrigo, looking at a live Looks detail for a design-heavy site: "Having an Aa with the font and the name would be cool, or fonts if several"; "The pic was taken too early, before the site loaded properly, it looks like"; "Clicking the pic should expand the view smoothly, like an overlay." Capture contract v4: (1) every first screenshot waits for the page to settle — web fonts, first-viewport images, 500 ms of still layout, finished viewport animations, a 2.5 s grace for endless or script-driven motion (two tiny viewport frames compared), hard cap 9 s — and `page.settle` records it (`{ms, timed_out, waiting_on, motion}`, ids and enums only), verified in a headless Chrome with its own scratch profile on a synthetic page with a 1.2 s-late web font, a 1 s lazy image, a 1.5 s CSS intro and a 1.5 s script-driven slide (the shot is the settled state); (2) `design.specimens`: "Aa" drawn by the page itself in each family it renders on a detached canvas (a face not loaded is left out, never faked), kept through `design_refs` as `assets/design/<id>.type-<n>.png` (≤ 6, ≤ 400×120, alpha PNG ≤ 96 KB) and shown in *Type* beside the family's name and its roles and sizes, a labelled generic stand-in when a look has none — chosen over a crop of page text because "Aa" is what was asked, a drawing needs no element in view, and the app can never load the web font itself; (3) the screenshot enlarges over the board from its thumbnail and shrinks back (keyboard instant, Reduce Motion a fade); (4) ink and accent show no area share. **Pin:** site-explore's hashes re-pinned; its `sha` moves to the squash commit in a follow-up after the merge. **Open:** a live v4 run on a real saved site. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** two extensions: an agent that meets a wall asks the person through one Inbox kind and a notification (**G205**); the person's saved lists on X, LinkedIn and Instagram come in through their browser like YouTube's (**G206**). | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G98 | <a id="g98"></a>**Roughly half the inbox is structurally unanswerable — fix the queue before dressing it up** (found 2026-09-01 while diagnosing G97; 41-agent sweep, every claim adversarially re-checked) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** #196 implements served-count parity and read-side filtering. Remaining upstream canonical subject/object generation and retirement of dead-subject questions are not fixed by hiding them at read time. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g98). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** During this review the served-inbox count fix merged as #196 (`3efba432`): shared read-side subject/defer filtering and count parity are implemented. Do not implement those again. Remaining scope is upstream retirement of invalid-subject questions, resolver/object canonicalization and generation-time validation, plus any author-scoped resolution gap confirmed against current code. The multi-valued predicate card rule and G171 decay dedup/cap are already implemented; close this row only when upstream cleanup is covered. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The measured headline: 22 of 49 pending items (45%) have a subject that is missing from the graph or archived, and nothing in the read path checks.** `load_inbox` filters only deferred items (`inbox_service.py:112-130`), the router filters only by kind (`inbox.py:22-26`), and `refresh_open_questions` reasons only over claims (`inbox_questions.py:127-182`). So **the decay engine archived these entities and the inbox kept asking about them** — including 7 archived conflict subjects at near-zero confidence (π0 at 0.14, π0.5 at 0.09, Octo at 0.09, <person-a> at 0.014, heat_parallel.c at 0.0). *This is very likely what the user saw when a conflict about "pi" looked nonsensical.* These items are 74 days old against `inbox_stale_after_days = 90`, so the existing staleness escalation has not reached them yet either. **Fix:** have `refresh_open_questions` close any item whose subject page is absent or `status: archived` (trigger `inbox/subject_archived`), and have `load_inbox` skip them defensively. **Removes 22 items with no UI change.** **Second: predicate multiplicity accounts for most of the conflicts.** 9 of 27 treat `uses` as single-valued when it is a set — a hybrid solver legitimately uses MPI *and* OpenMP, Hono runs on Deno, a project can be Python *and* C++; Cicada's own item offers `awake-sleep-architecture / neo4j / fastapi / react-three-js / leann-vector-index` as if a tech stack were mutually exclusive. **Fix:** mark predicates functional vs set-valued in `predicates.py` and raise conflicts only for functional ones (`works-at`, `role`, `status`); `uses`, `is-a`, `located-in` accumulate. **Third: 6 more conflicts are entity-resolution artifacts** — the two "conflicting" values are the same concept under two names (`vla` vs `vision-language-action-models`, both `type: concept`, both `active`, near-identical summaries; same for `world-models` vs `world-models-for-robotics`). One merge silently kills six questions — so **run merge before conflict in the Sleep pipeline**, and normalize the OBJECT side of a claim through the entity resolver before comparing. **Fourth, and it is the most embarrassing single item:** `inbox-1446` asks where **the user himself** works (<company-a> vs <company-b> vs <company-c> (real values redacted)) but `rodrigo-sagastegui` has no entity page — the user is split across `rodrigo.md` and `rodrigo-jesus-sagastegui.md`. Resolving it would write to nothing. Cause: `entity_id` is minted as `sanitize_id(entity_name)` with **no existence check** (`clarification_manager.py:94`), even though `id_utils.resolve_entity_file` is already used a few lines later for merge targets. Refuse to write an item whose subject does not resolve. **Context:** all 49 were written in one bulk backfill on 2026-06-18 over an import of 1,197 episodes of which only 3 are `mcp` captures — 787 are bookmarks and 193 apple-notes — so the extractor was largely reasoning over third-party web content rather than the user's life. That is a one-off condition, but it is why the queue reads the way it does. **Two upstream gates remove ~26 of 49 without touching a pixel**, which is why this row outranks G97: 49 well-rendered unanswerable questions is still an inbox nobody opens. → fixes the input side of **G60** (question objects), unblocks **G97**, all deterministic (**G80**); relates **G65(a)** (slug collisions) and **G21** (dedup sweep). **2026-09-03 evidence:** a project's `uses` predicate rendered a 7-option conflict card (architecture concept vs database vs three frameworks) — multi-valued predicates must never open a conflict; see G115 for the card-side rule. **Card-side rule shipped 2026-09-03 (G115 Phase 1):** `predicates.cardinality` (bank map ∪ committed seed, `multi` winning — `install_predicate_map` never refreshes a populated map, so a bank seeded before commit `e9a7c6b` still calls `uses` single-valued; anything in neither list is `unknown` and still asked), `write_claim_nudges` skips `multi` (counted), `load_inbox` serves an existing multi-valued conflict as `informational` (list + `Got it`, no claim touched). The subject-gone gate and the entity-resolution half are unchanged and still open here. **2026-09-29:** the decay half of the pile-up is closed by **G171** (PR #141): one open decay question per entity, at most 10 new per cycle, the overflow counted and raised next cycle. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · inbox freshness; local changes need validation. | PARTIAL · P1 **Status 2026-10-07 (trial):** the first real batch raised 24 'Confirm a predicate fold' items, all pure slug formatting (~3,500 projected for the drain). Core fix shipped #211 (a label's own slug is not a fold). Per-pair dedup, remembered answers and the clean-up migration are on `fix/g98-no-slug-fold-questions` (review round 1: coverage, locking, migration admission). Also observed: extraction emits sentence-long predicates — prompt guidance to review. |
| G118 | <a id="g118"></a>**Full provenance and traceability of memory — every belief shows the raw span, the contributor's reasoning, and the prompt that caused the write (a better NotebookLM)** (Rodrigo 2026-09-02: "full provenance and traceability of memory, even for the UI where I can see a raw reference with the whole page or the highlighted part that is referenced… highlights of where the contributor got the information or what different parts of conversation (can also reference own reasoning) helped consolidate something in memory… also the user's prompts that made the model act like this, like traces. Like an even better version of how NotebookLM references something from the material you give it" — marked by the owner as central to the vision) | **2026-10-29 — a saved item's fetched text is `page`, never `user` (branch `fix/saved-link-words`).** A save has no turn marker, so R4's default read a bookmark's title, header lines and fetched description as the person's writing: promotion counted each description paragraph as an exchange in their own words, a span there was `user` (so `person_said`, project birth, ruling 42 and `page_relevance`/`web_context` read it as theirs). Now `authorship.of(fm)` — the declared `evidence_kind`, else `evidence.SAVE_KIND` for a saved item known by its record (`media_entity_id`, `url`, a saving door's origin/source; never by a line) — is the one `override` every reader passes; in a save only `evidence.PERSON_SECTIONS` (`## Why it was saved`, `## Saved because`, `## User note`) are `user`. Derived on read, nothing rewritten; Stage 1's input unchanged. Measured on a read-only copy of the owner's bank (4,000 non-media pages): 347 current claims on 89 pages carry a `user` span inside a save's fetched text (136 description, 112 title, 77 header lines, 18 tab titles); at most 36 single-conversation pages rested only on such spans; 16 spans in a reason section stay `user`. The repair (rewrite those 347 kinds; keep or archive the 36) is a dry run awaiting the owner's per-item OK. Not changed: a reasonless save still counts as one conversation for the 2+ rung (36 pages rest on 2+ saves alone). **2026-10-08 — section-level provenance slice 1a-i shipped (#236):** Sleep records, per prose item, the evidence spans it drew on (spans, not copies); every writer path keeps or invalidates records; an unreadable section keeps its records as `metadata_unavailable`. Slice 1b (app: per-section *Supporting passages*, opening the Reader at the passage) is approved and waits to merge until the clean run ends. 1a-ii (the Stage-1 prompt change that raises coverage) is 💸 and owner-gated with a G148 pilot. Parked: the 1b layout repeats section text and is verbose — revisit with the owner after first use (G192). **Section-level provenance (owner 2026-10-07).** "for me provenance is a massive edge … even through the app i'd like to see what are all the conversations and chunks that contributed to certain parts of the memory entity's page." Today only claims carry spans; a page's synthesized prose knows its contributing conversations only page-wide (`source_episodes`, `GET /entities/{id}/provenance`), and claims written before this row's spans have none (no backfill). Next slice to plan: when Sleep writes or rewrites a section, record the evidence spans it drew on per section (spans, not copies), so the app's entity card shows, per section, the conversations and chunks behind it and opens each in the Reader at the passage. The qmd research (2026-10-07) is checked for chunk-retrieval ideas here. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Six evidence kinds, exact offsets/hash and Reader citations exist. Trigger/prompt traces and citable contributor rationales are not implemented; validate video timestamp seeking and history/span navigation separately. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g118). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists today, and where it stops:** a belief carries `source_episodes` (which conversation) and a commit carries `Cicada-Author:` / `Cicada-Session:` (which model, which conversation) — file-level and conversation-level provenance. It does not say *which sentence* (G100, unbuilt), *why the contributor concluded it* (Stage-1 output has no `evidence`/`reasoning` field that survives into the claim), or *what the person asked for* that led an agent to write (an MCP `cicada_write_claim` / `cicada_save_episode` call records the claim, not the turn that triggered it). `GET /entities/{id}/history` answers "when and by whom", never "from what". **The bar:** NotebookLM shows the cited passage inline; Cicada must do that *and* keep it bi-temporal (the span as it was when the belief formed), attributed (model, session, observer), and two-directional (from a span: everything it convinced; from a belief: every span, across conversations, links and notes). **Design — four layers, each additive to the existing files:** (1) **Spans, not copies.** A claim gains `evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind: user\|assistant\|reasoning\|page}]` — byte offsets into the stored episode/page text, never a quoted copy (privacy: the bank already holds the text; the ledger stays ids-only). Stage-1 extraction returns the exact substring it relied on; the resolver validates it exists in the episode and stores offsets (an unverifiable span is recorded as `kind: reasoning` with no offsets, never faked). Absorbs **G100**. (2) **Reasoning as a citable source.** The contributor's own justification (the model's `why` for a merge, a conflict pick, a decay archive, a skill) is stored as a short `rationale` on the claim/nudge with the model id — the model citing itself is a first-class evidence kind, shown distinctly from what the person said. (3) **Trigger traces.** Every agent write records the *turn that caused it*: for MCP writes, the calling tool's `context` (the user's last message excerpt as a span in the episode the MCP seam already captures — never read from `~/.claude` transcripts, G48 rail) and the tool name; for Sleep writes, the cycle id and stage. Surfaced as `trigger: {kind: user_prompt\|agent_decision\|sleep_stage, span?, tool?, cycle?}`. Consumes **G113**'s ledger shape (ids/enums) and **G105** (deterministic capture is what makes 'the turn that caused it' always exist). (4) **The viewer.** Entity page → claim → evidence chips → a raw-source pane that opens the whole episode/page with the cited spans highlighted and the rest dimmed, scrollable, with the other beliefs that cite the same passage listed alongside; from `cicada_ask` answers, every citation is a span, not a page (**G93**'s output half); from an inbox card, the cause span (**G97**/**G115**). Keyboard: ⌘[ back to the belief. **Rails:** engine-free read path (offsets → slices, ≤ a few ms); ETag ship-together (episodes join the entity/inbox ETags where the span text is served); transcripts under `~/.claude` are never read — if the MCP seam did not capture the turn, the trace says so; spans go stale when an episode is rewritten — store the episode's content hash beside the offsets and mark `stale` on mismatch rather than mis-highlight; nothing here is auto-applied. **Ordering:** span capture at extraction (Stage-1 + resolver) first, because every later layer reads it; then the viewer; then triggers; then reasoning. → absorbs **G100**; consumes **G105**, **G113**, **G48**; feeds **G93** (cited answers), **G97**/**G115** (cause on cards), **G106** (conversation ↔ entity browser is the same pane), **G101** (the evidence layer is what makes raw conversations worth keeping), **G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` becomes real citations). APPLY, L (four slices, each shippable). **Slice 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #44, `feat/provenance-spans`):** `Claim.evidence: [{episode, start, end, kind, hash}]` (offsets into the parsed body, ```claims fence excluded for a page; `sha256[:12]` → `stale` on mismatch; kinds `user\|assistant\|page\|reasoning`, speaker by the last `<role>:` marker, no marker = user); one engine-free module `api/services/evidence.py` (exact → whitespace → case-insensitive, never fuzzy; unlocatable = `reasoning`, claim still written); Stage-1 `evidence_quote` verified in `extract` against the chunked body with the chunk as window; `cicada_write_claim`/`write_claim` take `evidence: [{episode, quote}]`; link recon `page` spans on the media entity; Telegram `saved-because` cites its section; `_reinforce` merges spans; `evidence` on `/claims`, `/timeline`, `/transclude`; `GET /episodes/{id}/span`. Legacy claims: no evidence, no backfill, `to_dict` omits the empty key. **Slice 2, server half, built 2026-09-23 (`feat/provenance-viewer`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-provenance-backend.md`):** one freshness rule, `evidence.span_status` → `current \| grown \| stale` — design amendment A7: a turn-boundary prefix of an appended episode that still hashes to the stored value is `grown`, exact, and highlights (the Stop hook and G20 both rewrite by appending; pages never grow) — used by `/span` (+`grown`), the inbox cause and every new payload, and a stale span travels without wash offsets; `GET /episodes/{id}/text` (the whole evidence text ≤ 400,000 chars, `turns[]` from the one marker parser `speaker_kind` reads, per-turn `ts` only from a stored sidecar, an asserted `start/end/hash` or derived `focus=<entity>`); `GET /entities/{id}/provenance` (contributors = claim `authored_by` + one trailer-only `git log` of the page, conversations grouped by `session_id`/`source_id`, best quote asserted → derived → stale, coverage over current claims stated honestly); `GET /episodes/{id}/citations` (raw-text prefilter over `entities/`, spans + derived + reasoning rows, capped with `partial`); `/ask` citations carry `claimId` + `evidence`; `ClaimModel`/`EntityHistoryEntry` gain `authorKind`/`authorProvider` (+ `sessionIds`, `recordedAt`). The chat importer keeps each message's time as `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}]` outside `content_hash`, capped at 500 because frontmatter is parsed on every cold scan (costs measured in the plan); `markdown_parser.parse` reads with libyaml's `CSafeLoader` when present, because the sidecar made the pure-Python scanner ~7x slower on every full episode scan (2,000 imports: `list_all_episodes` 2.88 s → 0.42 s, the Stop hook's first-capture scan back under its 3 s timeout). G100's derived-span class exists on read payloads only — never in `EVIDENCE_KINDS`, never written. None of the payloads is a Store domain (ETags for the client's in-memory cache only; no `VersionVector` mapping). **Slice 2, the app, built 2026-09-23 (`feat/provenance-ui`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-provenance-ui.md`):** the app decodes `evidence` (dropped since slice 1 — `Claim.CodingKeys` never named it) and every slice-2 payload tolerantly, and slices every offset as a Unicode scalar through one `ScalarText`. Every claim carries **evidence chips** — "You said", "<agent> replied", "From the page", "Inferred", or "Mentioned here" for a legacy claim's name match — whose hover preview shows the words in the quote face, washed when quoted ("Quoted by the contributor"), bold when derived ("Found by searching the conversation"), plain when stale; a click opens the **Reader**, a trailing inspector beside whatever is open, with the conversation as turns (speaker labels, a time only where one is stored), scrolled to the washed span, stale/grown/derived/inferred/truncated said in words, the capture-honesty line, Resume, a "cited here" navigator and "Noted from this conversation" (the inverse index, each belief jumping to its words or landing on the graph). The entity card gains **"Where this came from"** — the writers with their real marks and two nouns (beliefs, edits), the conversations that fed it with the best sentence of each, and coverage stated as "N of M beliefs here have an exact quote"; G61's "Sources" became "Look it up at"; the claim footer and the History tab draw `ContributorAvatar` with plain trust words; the inbox cause gains its harness mark and "Show in conversation"; Ask answers carry evidence chips. Nothing is written, cached on disk, or made a Store domain. **Open:** P6 (palette rows → the Reader, Track S), find-in-conversation (Track S's search field), an episode id on `ConversationSummary` (so "Open conversation" works outside an entity card), a `span` read-telemetry surface, a speaker name on `/span` for meeting chips, a remote write's app label bucketed as `harness` by `author_identity` (done, F2-back R-B9), seeking a video from a `media` chip, per-turn times for Stop-hook episodes (their `turns` key is a count today), slice 3 trigger traces, slice 4 rationale, `describes` claims on link enrichment. **G140 (2026-09-23):** a sixth kind, `media` (after G134's fifth, `speaker`) — what a video said, a `video [m:ss]:` line in a watch episode (the time is required, so a person's own "Video: …" line stays theirs); `speaker_kind`, `media_time` and `turns()` read one marker grammar (`evidence._marker`, which since the merge with G134 also reads `speaker:<label>:` lines, and an episode's `evidence_kind` override still wins first), and the position is derived at read (`evidence.media_time`, served as `t` on `/episodes/{id}/span` and each turn), never stored. An agent's note on a saved link is written under `assistant:` so it never reads as the person's. **DS-2 (2026-09-24):** the Reader is a column; asserted spans wash, found mentions are semibold, in the Inbox's quote too. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ slice 1 ✅ (PR #44); slice 2 ✅ (server PR #72, app PR #78); slices 3–4 open. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G147 | <a id="g147"></a>**Frequency-aware decay (spacing) + per-type tuning** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, on the proposed fix: "ok i like the fix you propose so please go ahead and work on that") | **2026-10-21 — the merge remainder is built (branch `feat/next-row`):** `entity_merge.merge_entities` unions both pages' `kept_on` (`decay_policy.merged_keeps`: deduped, oldest first, the newest 52), so a keep the person gave the absorbed page still counts a mention week on the merged one — every door (dedup sweep, inbox merge answers, placeholder-people merges) goes through it. Tests: `test_merge_keeps_person_marks.py` (the primitive, the cap, and every door of `test_merge_edges.DOORS`). Nothing else of this row is open: reinforcement as an increment stays G85 (4). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Mention-week spacing, per-type tuning and visible effective pace exist. Entity merge omits the loser's kept_on list, losing feedback; preserve it with tests. Reinforcement increments remain G85, not part of this fix. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g147). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The defect (verified 2026-09-24):** decay was a flat per-class weekly rate from the last reference (`conflict_resolver.py:168-196` at `ecb59c7`) — a page mentioned in 50 separate conversations faded exactly as fast as one mentioned twice, while CLAUDE.md claimed decay was "proportional to how frequently it used to be referenced" (it read nothing about frequency) and that archived pages move to `archive/` (they keep their file, `status: archived`). **The rule now:** weekly rate = base (class rate or explicit `decay_rate:`) × f(w) × the bank's per-type pace, `w` = distinct ISO weeks among `source_episodes` dates ∪ the person's *keep* days (`kept_on`; every unparseable id together counts once), f(w) = max(0.25, 1/(1+0.6·ln w)). Weeks of silence to the decay question / to archive from 0.6: active w=1 4/8, w=4 7.3/14.7, w=12 10/20, w=52 13.5/27; durable w=1 10/20 → w=52 33.7/67.4; volatile w=1 1.3/2.7 → w=52 4.5/9.0. α=1.0 was rejected (floor at ~20 weeks: five months and five years look alike), α=0.3 too (a 52-week page asked after 8.7 weeks, barely twice a one-week page). **Keep** records the day in `kept_on` (not `source_episodes`, whose entries every reader treats as episode ids; not git-derived, which would put a subprocess in every pass). **Claims** multiply their existing amount by f(w_claim), weeks from `source_episodes` ∪ cited `ep_*` evidence documents ∪ the subject's keeps — session ids carry no date, `recorded_at` moves on every restatement, so neither counts; no new claim field. **Per-type pace** (agent proposes, person disposes): `GET /memory/decay-suggestions` counts, per type, each page's latest decay answer in the last 180 days of the bank's own git history (never the telemetry ledger) and suggests 0.5 ("fade more slowly") at ≥ 5 answers and ≥ 80 % keep, 1.5 at ≥ 80 % archive; `PUT /memory/decay-tuning` stores `<bank>/_decay_tuning.yaml` (travels with the bank, committed alone as `Cicada-Author: user`, 409 while Sleep runs); both engines multiply by it. Settings → Memory shows "Let people fade more slowly?" with Apply · Not now (per viewer, back after 5 more answers) and each chosen pace with Reset. **Visibility:** `GET /entities/{id}` gains a derived `decay: {class, effectiveRatePerWeek, mentionWeeks}` from the pass's own function (`decay_policy.effective`), and the card's Details "Fades" row reads "Slowly — mentioned across 12 weeks". Unchanged: the one-week cap per cycle, the `decayed_through` watermark, the 0.2/0.4 thresholds, `RECOVERY_CONFIDENCE`, G85's `cicada`-authored decay commit; nothing stored is migrated. **Disclosed, not fixed (fixed 2026-10-21, above):** a merge (`entity_merge._LIST_FIELDS`) unions the absorbed page's `source_episodes` but not its `kept_on`. **Not here:** ranking recall by confidence (nothing ranks by it today; a question for G148's benchmark pass to measure before anything changes); reinforcement as an increment (G85 (4)); the Feed's media relevance (media is evergreen). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-09-24-r4-frequency-aware-decay.md` (rulings R-FD1 … R-FD13). → answers **G85** (3), and corrects **G120**'s premise; builds on **G66**, **G113**; respects **G78**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ built on `feat/r4-decay` (round 4). | VERIFY · P1 (merge remainder built 2026-10-21; review, then DONE) |
| G93 | <a id="g93"></a>**Ask/search is built — the open question is what "smart" adds, and whether any of it is chargeable** **2026-10-23 (live test on the owner's bank; fixed in `fix/ask-project-continue`):** Ask answered "latest release 0.3.1" from a page whose newer claim said 0.6.0 in other words — a page's claims were ranked by shared words alone, then cut by the prompt budget. A question with no past cue now alternates the word-ranked claims with the same claims newest first (`ask_service._rank_claims`; never confidence), and `text_fold.stem` folds a silent final "e" (release/released). Ask also said "no page" for a page recall found: a claim object that is a whole sentence was resolved as a file name, `Errno 63 File name too long` emptied the retrieval (`id_utils.is_page` refuses it; Sleep's Stage 5.5 hit the same). A page the question names is now retrieved by recall's lexical leg (`_named_pages`), also with no search model. Latency: of ~12 s median, ~10–19 s is the model call (telemetry `duration_ms`); retrieval ~1 s; no cut taken. Still open: stale claims beside their replacements (the conflict_resolver work). (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a smart search inside cicada or ask ai inside cicada might be useful and maybe even part of a paid plan (add idea to backlog for later discussion)") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Grounded Ask, lexical/vector/claim fusion and name-triggered hook recall exist. Cross-stream curated relevance and right-moment usefulness remain unproved; drive changes from the hands-on trial. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g93). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Already shipped:** `GET /search` (cross-graph entities + episodes) and `POST /ask` (grounded NL answer with wikilink citations and gap analysis) are both mounted (`api/main.py:154-155`), and the app has a ⌘K Ask panel (G52). So this row is not "build search" — it is **what would make it smart**, and that has a precise answer from **G79**: today Ask retrieves *within* whatever the question names; the north star is **cross-stream retrieval** — surfacing a curated artifact inside an experiential context the user never explicitly linked ("you saved this three months ago and it answers the thing you're deciding now"). That is the capability worth building and the only one that is genuinely hard. Concrete gaps under it: no temporal scoping ("what was I thinking about in March"), no saved/standing queries, no proactive surfacing (Ask is strictly pull — the north star's "smartly gives" half is unbuilt), and Ask is a single-shot answer rather than a conversation over memory. **On the paid-plan idea — the honest complication, worth deciding before designing around it:** if **G74(a)**'s BYO-compute model holds, the user's *own* Claude subscription pays for the inference behind every Ask. Charging for the query while they fund the tokens is a hard sell and invites the obvious "why not just ask Claude directly". The defensible monetization is the thing the user cannot trivially self-host: **hosted E2E sync, a mobile client, continuity across devices, and a managed engine for people with no Claude/Codex subscription at all** — i.e. G92 route (b). Park the pricing question there rather than attaching it to Ask. → extends **G52** (Ask panel), **A5** (gap analysis), **D3** ("answers not pages", already decided); depends on **G79** for what "smart" means and **G92** for whether anything is charged. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ search/ask half already shipped (`GET /search` + `POST /ask` mounted `api/main.py:154-155`; ⌘K Ask panel, G52); the cross-stream-retrieval "smart" half is open — see TODO Wave C #13. G136 (2026-09-23) made ⌘K a find palette with Ask as a mode (⌘⏎); what "smart" adds — cross-stream retrieval — stays this row's.. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G103 | <a id="g103"></a>**Surface the observer model in the UI — whose belief is this, and who was in the room** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a small detail in the UI that makes reference to the model of entity observer relations (like who was in the conversation, who's belief, etc). this goes in hand with the import of conversations") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Claim/evidence observer fields and some labels exist. Finish meaningful option-level observer labels and meeting participants; this affects current trustworthy answers as well as future sharing. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g103). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** The data model already supports this and the UI barely admits it. Every claim carries `observer` — `agent \| rodrigo \| external:<name>` (`claims.py:68`) — the graph aggregates per-node observer sets (`graph_builder.py:115-122`), and the decay engine already weights trust per observer. But the only surface is the **All / Cicada / You / External** segmented control, which **G84(d)** found filters on claim observer, dims rather than filters, and returns true unconditionally for the 56% of nodes carrying no claims — so three of its four segments look identical and it communicates nothing. **What to build:** (i) on an **entity card**, show per-claim attribution inline — "you said this", "Cicada inferred this from a conversation", "heard from <person>" — so a page stops reading as one undifferentiated set of facts; (ii) on a **conversation** (Activity ▸ Conversations, and the import flow), show **who was present** and let a belief be traced to the participant who asserted it; (iii) relabel the observer control to what it means (G84(d) already proposes `Everything · Inferred by Cicada · Stated by you · Heard from others`) and gate segments on non-empty membership. **Why it pairs with conversation import, as the user says:** an imported agent chat has exactly one human observer, so today's model is adequate and invisible; the moment **G95** lands and a *meeting* is imported, a single episode carries several humans, and "whose belief is this" stops being cosmetic and becomes the difference between recording what *you* think and what someone told you. Building the surface now — while the answer is simple — means the multi-party case has somewhere to render rather than forcing a UI scramble later. **Dependency:** identity is only meaningful once speakers resolve to real people (**G81** contacts), otherwise every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `external:` observer. → extends **G84(d)** (the observer control), the claim layer's existing `observer`/`source_trust`; pairs with **G95** (meetings), depends on **G81** (contacts) for names that mean anything; supports **G100** (a citation is stronger when it names who said it). **(iv) First surface (G115 Phase 2):** each inbox option carries `observer` / `authored_by` from its claim, rendered through one `observer_label(observer, owner)` — `you said` / `Cicada inferred (<model>)` / `heard from <name>` — and the owner literal comes from a new `observer_owner` setting (default `"user"`) replacing the hardcoded string at `inbox_service.py:813`; a conflict card then shows the two disagreeing sentences side by side with who said each. **G118 slice 2 (server) makes "who wrote this" legible:** `GET /entities/{id}/provenance` returns per-author claim and commit counts with `kind`/`provider` from one `git_service.author_identity`, and `ClaimModel` carries `authorKind`/`authorProvider`, so the card and the chip render a contributor without re-deriving it; per-claim observer labels and "who was in the room" stay this row's. **G118 slice 2 (app) renders who wrote what:** every claim's footer names its writer with the contributors strip's own face (`ContributorAvatar` — a provider's real mark, "You", or Cicada's bookworm) and a plain trust word ("You told Cicada", "Cicada noticed", "Cicada concluded", "From a source"); every evidence chip says who spoke — "You said", "<agent> replied", and "Someone else said" for a meeting speaker, never "you" (R-N2); the entity card's "Where this came from" lists the writers with belief and edit counts. The inbox option's observer label and a meeting's "who was in the room" stay this row's. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |

Test passport freshness and attribution in fresh sessions under G10; do not duplicate _state.md into a second authoritative MEMORY.md. MHS's state dictionary — "each device described and onboarded once, its variables, controls and sensor values recorded in a single dictionary in shared memory, in a documented format", which *fresh* agent instances read to coordinate — is the missing awake-state layer. | G113 | <a id="g113"></a>**Grounded reward — record every human verdict on memory so extraction quality becomes a number** (Silver & Sutton, *Welcome to the Era of Experience*: a reward must be "grounded" in the environment's reply rather than in a designer's prior judgement; Rodrigo 2026-09-01 shared the paper as Cicada's framing) | **2026-10-28 link (G179):** the person's tapback on a texted agent's message (❤️/👍 agreement, ❓/👎 doubt) is a grounded reward from the environment on that turn — now read by Stage 1 (`turn_signals.person_stance`) as the person's own signal on the claims drawn from it; recording it as a verdict row here is not built. **2026-10-28 (vision item 10):** every `resolution` row also carries `asks` (the question's shape, e.g. `still`) and `page_use` (`high|mid|low`, the bucket of its page's derived use), so `/consumption/feedback` can later say whether the use-weighted order asks what the person answers and how "still?" questions are answered; the answer to a "still?" question is also remembered in the bank (`_still_answered.yaml`, an enum and a day) so it is not asked again for months — the reward read as a quiet interval, never as a learned setting. **2026-10-17 (ruling 29):** Sleep now answers inbox questions itself — from the person's own located words in a conversation, or by assuming the small stuff — through the SAME resolvers the person's answer uses, so every settle writes the same `resolution` row, stamped `by: sleep` and its `rule` (`inbox_actor.ledger_refs`); a retired dead question writes one too. `GET /consumption/feedback` keeps them OUT of the person's agreement rates and reports them apart as `sleepSettles {settled, undone, byRule}`; the person's undo of a Sleep settle is a `resolution` row `by: person, settled_by: sleep, verdict: overruled` — the grounded reward against the rule that assumed it, and ruling 29's revisit trigger. The commit trigger is `inbox/<kind>/settled:<label>`, never `resolved:`, so G147's decay-pace reader never counts Sleep's archive as the person's. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Human verdict ledger/reporting and decay-tuning suggestions exist. Feedback-conditioned extraction/prompt changes and broader learning slices remain; nothing auto-applies learned settings. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g113). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The finding (verified 2026-09-01):** the user grades the extractor dozens of times — every decay keep/archive, conflict pick/both/neither, clarification answer, merge accept/reject — and NOTHING records the verdict as a signal. `inbox_service.resolve` commits `trigger: inbox/{kind}/resolved` whatever the action (:295-297) — keep and archive produce byte-identical manifest lines; the chosen option survives only as a diffable inbox-file deletion. No telemetry event is written on any resolution (`telemetry.py:21` KINDS has no resolution kind; `inbox_service.py` never imports it; `kind="ask"`/`"import"` are declared and never emitted). `GET /consumption/*` and `GET /contributors` count cost and volume, never agreement (`consumption_stats.py:91-252`; `git_service.py:645-745`). Extractor confidence (`entity_extractor.py:28-47`; relationship default 0.6 :514; "<0.5" gates clarification :106) is never compared with an outcome. `claim_reconciler._reinforce` (:143-162) bumps confidence with no count, so `session_ids` (`claims.py:97-106`) is the only precision proxy. Stage-3's `audit` list — the one machine record of "an agent claim was overruled by the trust table" — is returned by `claim_pipeline` (:159) and read by nobody (`grep audit sleep_cycle.py` → 0). **Four verdicts are actively lost:** (a) the `divergence` ("Keep my statement / Update to X") and `normalization` ("Correct fold / Wrong fold") items Stage 5.56 writes (`inbox_generator.py:333-338`) are NOT in `InboxKind` (`schemas.py:765-769`) — `InboxItem(kind='divergence')` raises ValidationError (verified), `load_inbox` skips them as unparseable (:169-172), resolve would 400 (:286-287) — so the two questions that most directly grade the extractor accumulate on disk unanswerable; (b) merge REJECT has no action — the app sends `dismiss`, which deletes the item (`inbox_service.py:712-713`; `InboxCardView.swift:177`), and `clarification_manager.find_open` (:73-83) re-proposes the same pair next cycle; (c) decay `keep_active` never touches the claim the nudge came from (`claim_id` written at `inbox_generator.py:365`, never read in `_resolve_decay` :331-368) and `remind_later` writes a `snooze_until` nothing reads (:354-355) while still committing "entity updated"; (d) a clarification answer writes body prose only (:667-710) — no `user_stated` claim, so it earns none of the human protection a conflict "neither" earns (:549-574). Also: `dropped` status is read in five places and set by none outside a test fixture; an out-of-band editor correction is swept into the next Sleep commit under `sleep/extraction` with the MODEL's author trailer (`sleep_cycle.py:1462-1479,:1553-1569`) — the `user/manual_edit` trigger in CLAUDE.md is emitted by no production code; `dedup_sweep` verdicts (:82-99) are returned, never persisted, and re-judged (paid) every sweep. **Why this matters beyond bookkeeping:** the paper's argument is that an agent cannot improve past its designer's priors without a reward grounded in consequences. Cicada already HAS the grounded signal — the user's verdicts are the environment's reply — and throws it away, which is why G78 calls measurement "the weakest link" and why G98's 22/49-dead-subjects number came from a manual sweep. **Design — a feedback ledger, $0.** (1) **Carry the verdict in the commit:** trigger `inbox/{kind}/resolved:{action}` (`keep_active`, `archive`, `pick:<key>`, `both`, `neither`, `answer`, `merge`, `reject`, `defer`) — inert to entity-line parsing like every trailer; `_infer_change_type` learns `archived`. (2) **Emit a telemetry event** `kind="resolution"` with refs `{item_id, kind, predicate, entity_id, action, option_key, winner_claim_id, loser_claim_ids, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, item_age_days}` from `inbox_service.resolve` (the MCP `cicada_resolve_inbox` path only POSTs, so one writer covers both); plus `kind="audit"` for Stage-3's `audit` list and `kind="dedup_verdict"` for `dedup_sweep`. (3) **Make the four lost verdicts real:** add `divergence` + `normalization` to `InboxKind` with resolve branches (keep-mine / update / both; correct / wrong-fold → unfold + record the pair); a `reject` action on merge that writes the pair to `_merge_rejected.yaml`, consulted by `find_open` and `dedup_sweep` before proposing; decay keep restores the source claim's confidence; clarification answers write a `user_stated` claim (`origin: clarification`) beside the prose. (4) **Read it back:** `GET /consumption/feedback` — per-model / per-predicate / per-kind agreement rates (keep vs archive, pick-agent vs neither, correct vs wrong fold), a calibration curve (extractor-confidence bucket → user-agreement rate), and a corrections count from `Cicada-Author: user` commits — rendered as a fourth card on Activity. (5) **Close the loop later (💸, DECIDE):** feeding rates back into prompts or thresholds is G78's territory and needs the ledger first; the ledger is the prerequisite, not the optimizer. **Rails:** the ledger lives in `~/.cicada/telemetry/` like every other event — machine-global, never in the bank, ids and enums only, no personal text; a verdict is recorded, never auto-applied to future extraction without the user seeing the rate. → the measurement half of **G78** (gbrain-evals) and a live number for **G98**; extends **G51** (ledger + `/consumption`), **G60** (claim-aware resolve), **G85** (author-attribution correctness is the precondition); consumes **G97** (context on the question side); relates **G103** (observer in the UI), **G100** (span citation as the thing a user confirms or denies), **G72**/**G112** (a compiled skill's `## Evidence` is only trustworthy once verdicts are counted). **(2b, decided in G115, 2026-09-02; slices 1–2 merged as PR #31 at `09a4b66`, `_verdict` at `inbox_service.py:302`, `remind_later → _defer(7)` still Task 5):** the wire carries the verdict before the answer — each option gets `recommended` (the one option `_verdict` scores `agreed`: Sleep's proposal, never freshness, so a stale-escalated `neither` and a no-claim entity-path conflict never carry it) and `verdict`, computed once from R3 so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `merge_suggestion` never carries `recommended` (G81); the resolve response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces show only `changed`; the shipped `resolution` event gains additive refs `surface (app\|mcp\|telegram)`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`, and a new `ask` kind (ids only) records each item an agent surfaced so asked-vs-answered is a number; `session_id` lives in the ledger, not in a `Cicada-Session:` trailer on the user commit (contract unchanged pending G116). **Shipped 2026-09-05 (PR #59, `feat/feedback-ledger`) — slices 3–7:** trigger `inbox/<kind>/resolved:<label>` + `statusChange` for decay (slices 1–2, already covered above); `divergence` + `normalization` resolvable in API and Swift; merge reject persisted in `_merge_rejected.yaml`; decay `keep_active` and clarification answers reach the claim layer; `GET /consumption/feedback` + a Feedback tile in Sources ▸ Advanced. Still open: slice 5 (rates → prompts, 💸 DECIDE under G78) and the 2b additive refs above (G115 Phase 2/3). **2026-09-23 (G61 phase 2):** two feedback kinds join the ledger — `check` (every source check: checker kind, method, outcome, grade, ids and enums only, a regex-guarded no-URL rule) and `check_verdict` (the person's reply to a check: agreed/overruled in shadow, confirmed/undone after an auto settle, silent expiry graded as neither) — and `resolution` gains additive `checked_key`/`picked_checked`; a `settled:` or `check:` trigger is never counted as the person's verdict, and `_verdict` stays byte-identical. **First consumer (G147, 2026-09-24):** decay answers in the bank's own history (`inbox/decay/resolved:<label>`) become per-type pace *suggestions* in Settings → Memory — applied only when the person says so. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G115 | <a id="g115"></a>**Inbox redesign — one question object, cause on the card, Sleep's proposal marked, a scoped "don't ask again", and an in-conversation ask gate** (decision memo 2026-09-02, four-design study judged on three lenses — UI-port spine 115 / stream-first 114 / conversation-first 109 / reward-first 109; a four-point spread is a mandate for a spine and grafts, not for one design; critic pass applied, base `dev @ 09a4b66`; owner's complaints in G97/G98/G100/G103/G113) | **2026-10-29 — round 2 (branch `fix/speed-quality-2`).** A request's ETag and read may answer from the SSE tick's walk while the same watcher reports nothing and this process wrote nothing since (`local_writes`, `sync_ticker.reusable_listings`): `GET /inbox` 304 p95 68 → 1.1 ms, 200 p95 65 → 5.2 ms (load ≈ 20). The inbox ETag carries the predicate map, held names and owner's id, and the entities component the claims ledgers (a ledger shrinks just after its page). Recall's whole-token check and inbox block, `/status`, `/origins` and the owner's card cut (load ≈ 7: owner's card p95 175 → 46 ms, `/status` 120 → 79, `/origins` 158 → 59, in-process recall p95 1,670 → 633, `cicada recall` p50 2,160 → 975). Detail: `api.md`, `features.md`. | **2026-10-29 — the inbox read, fast (branch `fix/speed-quality-1`).** `load_inbox` keeps its built list on the stamps of every input it reads (the day, the inbox, pages, episodes, `_predicates.yaml`, held names, `_state.md`), so an unchanged read builds nothing; `GET /inbox`'s ETag and read share one listing per directory and an ETag computes only the components it names; a rebuild re-parses only moved item files, reads the predicate map once and reuses each cut excerpt while its episode is unchanged. Owner's scale on a read-only copy (423 questions), load about 8: `GET /inbox` 200 p95 529 → 69 ms, after one changed item 410 → 305 ms CPU p95; the floor is the ETag's stat walk (~55 ms). Detail: `docs/architecture/features.md`. **2026-10-28 — the catch-up chat, the app half (ruling 48 (1), (2), (8), (17); branch `feat/home-inbox-catchup`).** Rodrigo 2026-10-28: "I don't want a monotone interrogation, more so a conversation where the questions themselves can change and more questions can be answered there in the moment as follow ups, same as a human conversation." Built: the Inbox off the rail (⌘6 unused, the badge on Home); Home's *Catch me up* block and one row per page with a question (picture, plain question and reason, page link; `GET /inbox` now serves `spokenQuestion`/`spokenReason`, derived at read); the full list as Home's sub-view with its kind groups always open (DR-81); a page's questions strip; the chat (`CatchupView`/`CatchupSession`) with the worm's head as face (the menu bar sheet's own clips, ruling 18). Who speaks: the person's words go to `POST /inbox/catchup/turn` — Ask's engine resolution (`user_triggered=True`; rulings 4/31/46), one JSON turn that may follow up, rephrase or move to an offered upcoming question; it writes nothing, and its reading is kept only when its quote (and any free answer) is found in what the person typed (`evidence.locate`, never fuzzy), and even then it is only proposed ("Note it as …? Yes / Not quite") and applied through the inbox's own resolve on the person's Yes, with the quote — never from typed words alone (coordinator's ruling on #509, after a live turn read "the arm is paused" as Done). The chat is kept as one episode per session (`POST /inbox/catchup/record`, `companion_app`/`catchup`), so what they say in passing reaches Sleep. Harness parity: `mode="catchup"` blocks carry the same page context (`catchup_chat.context_lines`) and the skill/prompt ask for a conversation; no new MCP tool (the harness agent is the conversational step); remote stays refused. Not built: chains of same-person questions as one row in the app (the MCP door groups them; the app asks each pair). **2026-10-28 — the catch-up chat (vision item 10, backend and agent half; branch `feat/vision-inbox-catchup`).** Rodrigo 2026-10-28: "a nice quick, hop on a chat with cicada to catch him up to speed … just have the user answer at first those things linked to more accessed entities … asks questions in more human concrete format". Built: (S1) `page_use` — a page's use derived from the record (conversations log-scaled, halved per 90 silent days, a small lift for links; the owner's page 1.0), because the telemetry ledger is machine-global and never auto-applied; `load_inbox` and `mode="ask"` sort by priority + 0.5 × use. (S2) `cicada_check_nudges(mode="catchup")` — three at a time, most-used first, a session cursor outside the bank so nothing repeats, local doors only (remote schema unchanged), `cli_map` parity through `mode`. (S3) an MCP prompt `catch_up` and the skill's "Catching up" section. (S4) `inbox_wording` — each kind said plainly with its reason, at read, option keys unchanged. (S5/S6) `still_questions` — "Are you still working at <org>?" / "still interested in <topic>?" on the owner's own page from quiet, time-boxed claims, the source cue from ruling 26's stored refresh state ("your LinkedIn seems a bit stale"), never a fetch. (S7) chained same-person questions asked once; spelling proposes a nickname/long-form pair (a question only — ruling 41). (S10) one budget of 3 new questions a drain, each proposer capped on what is open, answers to "still?" remembered in `_still_answered.yaml`, and the G113 resolution row gains `asks` and a `page_use` bucket. Dry run on a copy of the owner's bank (counts only, report `vision-pr3-inbox-catchup`): 7 interest questions eligible (1 written per drain under the predicate rule), 0 roles (every open role said within 180 days or its topic still said), 2 nickname pairs (5 before the 0.6 ratio — the 3 dropped were two different given names), 2 same-person chains already in the inbox, 418 catch-up-visible questions. Waits on the owner: the weights and what "accessed" should mean (views would need the ledger rail amended), N, remote access, third-party roles, the group question's evidence bar. Detail: `docs/architecture/mcp.md` "Catching up", `capture-and-sleep.md` "The catch-up questions". **2026-10-14 — questions come up naturally (owner: "questions that are only relevant to the current conversation … come up naturally and be responded to also naturally"; branch `feat/natural-questions`).** Item 6's relevance half shipped as `inbox_ask_gate` behind `cicada_check_nudges(mode="ask"|"list")` — page in scope AND a subject word of the question (predicate, options, its words) in what the person said; no vector-score leg; decay never comes up in conversation. The copy-only discipline below (quote the Cause line, lead with the Recommended option) is amended: the agent weaves a question in or leaves it, and records an answer the person already gave with their words (`quote` → a span episode cited as evidence, `answered_in: conversation` on the ledger row). The same gate drives recall's inbox block and the recall hook. Detail: `docs/architecture/mcp.md`. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Question cards, cause display, resolutions and defer flows exist. Verify remaining interaction slices and implement only the scoped rule/ask-gate work allowed by G116; no silent automatic belief settlement. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g115). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Problem.** The inbox is a priority-sorted list of cards that hide their cause (G97), never say what Sleep is betting on, grade the person forever one item at a time with no way to write a ruling once, reach an agent only through a body-text substring match that returns everything (`mcp/server.py::_topic_matches`, no G98 subject gate, no kind check, a hand-rolled frontmatter parser at `:738` that disagrees with the API about which files exist), and make decay a three-button special case outside the one component G60 built (`InboxCardView.swift:162-164` short-circuits `actionRow` on `.decay`; `_resolve_decay`'s else-branch unlinks the file). **Evidence (verified).** G98: 45% of items unanswerable; G105: 0 MCP invocations in 12 days, so "agent-initiated" is theoretical; `InboxItem` (`schemas.py:795-819`) carries no `source_episode`, `entity_type` or recommendation; **the ETag trap has two halves** — `api/routers/inbox.py` must widen `etag_for(mp, "inbox", "entities", "episodes")` AND `VersionVector.swift:8-9` maps `entities` → `[.graph,.contributors,.origins]` and `episodes` → `[.status,.origins,.sources,.channels]`, neither refreshing `.inbox`, so `.inbox` must be added to both mappings in the same commit or `SnapshotCache` keeps stale context until the inbox itself moves; `graph_builder._dir_mtime` (`:536-544`) globs `*.md` + dir mtime, so a YAML rule file under `inbox/` would tick nothing; a per-cycle write cap is a silent drop for Stage-2 clarifications (`entity_resolver.pending_actions` on an episode then marked processed) — so **no cap**. **Design (the ruling).** (1) One question object for every kind — `decay`/`divergence`/`normalization` synthesised at read in `_item_from_file` (decay: `Still tracking {name}?`, options `archive`/`keep`, `allow_defer=True`), no file rewrite; clarification `title` becomes the question (`Who is {name}?`). (2) **Item shape, all additive** — `InboxOption += {recommended, verdict, action; Phase 2: observer, authored_by, confidence, episode_id, excerpt, preview}`; `InboxItem += {entity_type, source_episode, source_episode_timestamp, cause: {episode_id, timestamp, conversation_id, harness, origin, conversation_title, excerpt, mention_offsets, tier: item\|claim\|entity, span_kind: derived}, silent_days, extractor_confidence, extractor_model, recommended_key, group_id, defer_count; Phase 2: neighbours, rule_offers}`; `InboxResolveRequest += {surface: app\|mcp\|telegram, harness, session_id (ledger refs only), note, rule}`; response echoes `{changed: {closed, kept, created, archived}, verdict, extractor_confidence}` and both surfaces render only `changed`. `age_days`, `silent_days`, `cause.excerpt`, `mention_offsets`, `recommended`, `verdict` are derived at read, never stored. (3) `cause` resolves by G97's three tiers (item → freshest option claim → subject `source_episodes`), `[ no source recorded ]` served when all fail, never a hidden card; a `bank_index.parse_count` budget test guards the ~100 ms figure. (4) **`recommended` == the one option the shipped `_verdict` (`inbox_service.py:302`, R3) scores `agreed` — Sleep's proposal, never freshness** — computed once so a test asserts wire == ledger for every kind × key; `verdict` is on the wire for agents and tests and never rendered as copy. Corollaries (critic C1/C0): on a stale-escalated conflict `Neither anymore` is `overruled` under R3, so it is NEVER marked — either nothing is marked or Sleep's own claim stays marked and freshness orders the rest; an entity-path conflict with no item `claim_id` grades every pick `neutral` (the Task-2 footnote) and therefore never carries a `(Recommended)` — a large share of live conflicts per G98. **Never on `merge_suggestion`, no initial highlight, never in a bundle (G81)**; `reject` persists to `_merge_rejected.yaml` (R5). (5) `<memory>/_inbox_rules.yaml` at bank root beside `_merge_rejected.yaml`, permission-rule grammar `kind(scope[, scope]): decision`, scope values enums/slugs only (parser rejects whitespace, so a rule string is ledger-safe), precedence `never > defer > action > ask`; Phase 2 ships `never` + `defer:N` + `decay(entity=X): never` → the existing evergreen `PUT`; merge rules are `never` only; a `rules` sync component (`file_mtime`) mapped to `[.inbox]`; rule EXECUTION (`archive\|keep\|accept`) waits on **G116(a)**. A `never` rule is NOT the tool for G98's entity-resolution artifacts (~15 of 27 conflicts) — that half stays G98's Stage-2 fix and must not be `never`-ruled away. (6) `cicada_check_nudges(mode="ask"\|"list", entity_ids, topic, limit=3, kinds, conversation_id, include_context)` gated server-side by a pure `inbox_ask_gate.candidates(...)`: `entity_id ∈ entity_ids` OR a top-5 `search_entities` hit with score ≥ 0.55 (`vector_index.py:295`, `1.0 − distance`) OR a ≥4-char name token — body-only lexical matches dropped; every `load_inbox` gate (G98 subject gate, not deferred, `kind ∈ InboxKind`, no `never` rule); an in-process `_ASKED_THIS_SESSION` set (one MCP process = one conversation, G48 — a skip is never written to the bank); `normalization` hidden from the agent path; hard cap 5. Both MCP readers (`handle_check_nudges`, `_relevant_inbox`) go through `inbox_service.load_inbox`. `render_question` v2 prints `inbox-NNN · <kind> · entity_id=<slug>` (the slug is what the next call's `entity_ids=` needs), the question, a `Cause:` line, `k) label (Recommended) — <age> · <observer_label>`; the existing `a) Label — 6 months ago` prefix is preserved for `test_mcp_inbox_questions.py`. The discipline (one question per turn, after the work, quote the Cause line, never blocking, `skip=true` never re-asks, resolve only with the person's own answer) is G75's primer text — server-side filter, copy-only discipline. (7) Card: kind icon (never a monogram for non-entity kinds — made on the certain facts only; G97's refuted pfp explanation is NOT re-derived here and stays open there), line 1 question, line 2 cause (`From "<title>" · <harness> · <age>`), preview pane with the mention bolded from `mention_offsets`, `(Recommended)` row first, descriptions age-first then provenance (`you said this` / `Cicada inferred` / `Cicada's guess at 0.42`), `Other…`, footer `Not now — ask again in 7 days (l)`; copy rules enforced by a NEW `inbox_questions.validate_question` (Phase 1 code, ~30 lines — it does not exist today) in tests; keyboard via `QuestionSelection` (`1–4` pick, `⏎`, `o`, `l` = defer, `Esc` = collapse with NO write — a new enum case beside today's `pick/openOther/moveUp/moveDown/activate`, `c` conversation, `e` entity, `⌘[` back to the same card). (8) Silence: explicit later = defer + `defer_count` on the file; `defer_count ≥ 3` → 90-day auto-defer by the Stage 5.56 sweep as `cicada` (G85 shape); belief kinds (decay/conflict/divergence/normalization) silence-close under `inbox/silence_closed` (verdict `neutral`, reopen through the producers' merge-on-collision) — **clocked in Sleep cycles actually run (or the decay `decayed_through` watermark), never wall-clock** (TODO ruling 1: an engine outage is not user silence), and `silent_days` copy must never read as "why this decayed"; clarification/merge silence-close is owner question 3 (recommended: close too, 180 Sleep-counted days, traced and reopenable — recommended, not yet ruled). (9) Toast/tool result say what changed (`Archived alpha-project · 1 belief closed`), never the grade. **Rails.** Zero LLM on any read/generate/refresh/render path (G74/G80; a monkeypatched-providers test raises if touched); ledger ids/enums/counts/floats only — additive refs on the shipped `resolution` event (`surface`, `harness`, `session_id`, `recommended_key`, `picked_recommended`, `cause_tier`, `silent_days`, `defer_count`, `via_conversation_match`, `rule_applied`) plus new `ask` (ids only, per surfaced item) and `rule_written` (rule string) kinds in `FEEDBACK_KINDS` (R7) — never a behaviour input (settled #6; the proposal engine stays G113 slice 5, 💸 DECIDE under G78); user commits stay `Cicada-Author: user` and session-less pending **G116(b)**; the `"rodrigo"` observer literal at `inbox_service.py:813` becomes a `settings.observer_owner` (default `"user"`) rendered through one `observer_label`; no owner name in any template; every new field optional so older app builds decode. **Phases.** **1** (1–2 days, engine-free, no dependency — parallel to G113 slices 3–7 in its own worktree; the two touch disjoint functions of `inbox_service.py`): `inbox_context.py` (new: `resolve_cause`, `excerpt` with `mention_offsets`), schema fields, `_item_from_file` cause + decay synthesis + `recommended` via the shipped `_verdict`, `write_claim_nudges` persists `source_episode`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2, `validate_question` (new), Swift `InboxCause` decode + `QuestionSelection` cases + `.decay` routed through `QuestionView`, `observer_owner`. **Phase 1 DOES touch the resolve path** — it accepts `resolve`+`archive\|keep` on decay as aliases of `archive`/`keep_active`, which must preserve the shipped R1 trigger labels that `test_inbox_resolution_provenance.py` asserts; `remind_later → _defer(7)` (R6) is G113 Task 5 and is pending on `dev` (`inbox_service.py:607` still writes `snooze_until`) — Phase 1 leans on it, does not own it. **2** (after G113 slices 3–4 and G106(i)): ask gate + MCP through `load_inbox` + `ask` event + handshake text (with G75), `never`/`defer:N` rules + `rules` component + Rules sheet, observer capsule + per-option excerpt + merge two-column preview (G103's first surface), `c`/`e`/`⌘[` navigation, response echo + `defer_count` persisted. **3** (after G116 and G75/G49): grouped cards (≤4 per entity, header chips, Now 12 / Later, `⌘⏎` accept-all-recommended excluding merges, `_inbox_counts` via `load_inbox`), Sleep-counted silence-close, the rule executor with the rule string in the manifest line, SessionStart injection replacing the copy-only per-session cap. → delivers **G97** (Phase 1, ahead of its wave position because every later phase stands on it) and the inbox half of **G113**; consumes **G60**, **G98**; first surface for **G103**; first caller of **G106**; evidence for **G108(b)**; carries **G75**'s inbox contract; gated by **G116** for rule execution and the session trailer. **Complaints answered by id:** **G114(3)** — the Telegram `/remind` reminder item is owned here as a `decay`-shaped `defer` with `surface: telegram` (G114 R4 parks it as a `capture_kind: reminder` note until then); **G110(b)** — `cause.conversation_id` on the wire plus `conversation_id?` on `cicada_check_nudges` is its approximation and `via_conversation_match` measures it, so do not reopen it; **G13** ("you had an open idea about X — still relevant?") rides the `decay` kind on a `concept`/`project` subject, no new kind; **G95 / G55 / G72** depend on the inbox staying one generic component — which is the argument for no new kinds (R4); **G100** stays where it is (asserted spans render as `span_kind: asserted` beside today's `derived`). **Owner, 2026-09-03 (evidence for Phase 1's card):** "the card has a chevron arrow at the end; I click on it but it doesn't do anything… the long list of URLs that doesn't end is not great" — two concrete defects the redesign must close first: a chevron that does nothing (either it opens the question or it is not drawn), and merge/clarification items over media that render every URL inline (collapse to a count + the top 3 with 'show all', never an unbounded list). The design study (judge panel memo, 2026-09-02) is already done — Phase 1 should start with these two. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — suggested outcome with a sentence of why (Phase 2, gated):** "some sort of agent that looks at the raw context of where the conflict or duplication is happening; if it passes a certain confidence threshold it politely gives a sentence of what it thinks the outcome should be — 'according to context I think it should be like this' — and the human confirms with a check mark or picks himself. Not every conflict gets a suggestion. Log these suggestions for later training." Design: this is the `(Recommended)` option plus a **rationale**, produced only when a judge over the item's cause spans (G118) is confident ≥ `inbox_suggest_min_confidence` (default 0.8) — below that the card shows options with no suggestion, exactly as today. The rationale is one sentence, written into the inbox item (bank, so it is provenance), attributed to the model, rendered under the recommended option as *Cicada thinks: …* with a one-key accept (⏎ on the recommended row) and the ordinary options beside it. Never auto-applied. **Training log:** the G113 `resolution` event already carries `recommended_key` and `picked_recommended`; add `suggestion_confidence` and `rationale_id` (ids/enums only — the sentence stays in the bank) so agreement-with-suggestion becomes a rate on `/consumption/feedback` and a labelled set for a future model. Runs in Stage 5 (the item is created there) or on demand for existing items, engine = the Sleep engine of the cycle, capped per cycle. **Owner, 2026-09-03 — research-resolvable conflicts (same Phase 2 agent, second tool):** "there are conflicts that can be solved by just looking up the repo — a conflict or clarification solved with research can be the outcome; maybe part of the Sleep cycle." Example seen on a project entity's `uses` predicate: `<architecture-concept>` vs `<database-x>` — the linked repo answers it in one grep (no such dependency). Design: the suggestion judge gets a bounded, read-only tool set drawn from what the entity already declares — `repos:` via `git_service` (file list, dependency manifests, grep; on demand, never cached), `sources:` URLs (G61, fetched under the G102 rules), and the cause spans — and its rationale must name what it checked (*"the repo's `pyproject.toml` has no `<database-x>` dependency; `<architecture-concept>` appears in `CLAUDE.md`"*); a rationale with no checked source is downgraded below the confidence gate. Runs where the item is created (Stage 5) or on demand from the card (*Research this*), capped per cycle, engine-free when only file reads are needed. The same item also shows a **G98** artefact: `uses` is a multi-valued predicate, so five true values were rendered as a conflict — Phase 1 must not ask for a winner on a predicate the schema marks multi-valued (cardinality from `_predicates.yaml`), only on single-valued ones. **Phase 1 shipped 2026-09-03 (PR #48, `feat/inbox-phase1`):** `inbox_context.py` (cause, three tiers, `[ no source recorded ]`), additive wire fields (`entity_type`, `source_episode`, `claim_id`, `cause`, `extractor_confidence`/`_model`, `recommended_key`, `informational`; options `recommended` + `verdict`), decay synthesised at read with `archive` recommended, `recommended_key` == the unique `_verdict`-agreed key (never neither/both/merge/clarification; absent on entity-path conflicts), options served recommended-first, `remind_later → _defer(7)` (G113 R6 landed here), `_owner_observer(settings)`, ledger `recommended_key`/`picked_recommended`, ETag both halves, `render_question` v2 + decay as a question on MCP, `validate_question`, the card (kind icon, question/cause lines, bolded excerpt, `QuestionView` for the two kinds that carry a question object — decay and conflict; clarification/merge keep their rows until Phase 2 — `1–4 ⏎ o l Esc`, real chevron, collapse past four lines, merge two-name preview, honest empty state), and the G98 informational rule. **Rulings made in the plan** (`docs/plans/2026-09-03-g115-inbox-phase1.md`): the informational dismiss grades `overruled` (the overruled belief is "these conflict"); clarification never carries a Recommended; decay's Recommended is `archive`; `cardinality` unions its two sources with `multi` winning rather than letting a stale bank map out-vote the seed; `_owner_observer`'s unset fallback stays the historical literal (not `user`) so an existing bank keeps one observer lineage — the choice moves to G117; the merge preview is two names + suggested type until G103. **Final review (3 fixes, same branch):** decay's `allow_other: false` is now honoured end to end — MCP's `Other / Later` line gates each half on its own flag (it was inviting free text an agent could not have honoured) and `_normalize_decay_request` 400s a `resolve`/`answer` carrying free text (it used to be appended to the entity body while the page stayed `decaying` and the item was deleted, inverting a “still relevant” answer); `recommended_key` synthesises decay's read-time options so `_emit_resolution` records the recommendation the card actually showed instead of `null`; and both MCP reader loops build ONE `InboxContext` instead of one per item (a per-item `episodes/`+`entities/` scan measured 425 ms for 40 items on a 2,000-episode bank, in the conversation path). Phases 2–3 unchanged. **G118 slice 2 (server):** the cause's asserted span now survives a continued conversation (`grown` — `_asserted_span` reads `evidence.span_status`), and "Show in conversation" needs no new field: the Reader opens `GET /episodes/{id}/text?start=&end=` at the cause window's absolute offset plus the mention offset. **G118 slice 2 (app):** the cause line wears its harness's real mark, the excerpt reads in the quote face, and "Show in conversation" opens the Reader on the mention — a cause found by name stays labelled found by name there. **DS-2 (2026-09-24):** the Inbox is D's progressive columns; one tap with a 5 s send-delay Undo, so an undone answer never reaches the ledger. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G141 | <a id="g141"></a>**Project timelines — dated happenings with every participant linked, a "you are here" band, and the knowledge around a project, planned or not** **2026-10-23 (live test; fixed in `fix/ask-project-continue`):** `cicada_project` read a project developed every day as "planned · 0 of 1 milestones" ("planned" meant only "has a milestone") and listed whole conversations naming it once. The head line is now a status from evidence (`project_text.status_word`: active / quiet / resting / fading / archived), activity counts every day an agent session ran in the project's repository (`_repo_days`, the hook's own observation, consolidated or not; wire `lastRepoDay`, g141-6), and a moment needs the project discussed in substance (two claims about it or a quarter of the conversation's, `_substantive`), led by those claims.
