Stuff like this would be awesome." — real names redacted) | **2026-10-28 — Projects v3, no folds (decisions 9, 10, 17; `feat/projects-v3`, wire g141-8):** the owner on #493: "too many of those dropdown things", and the head "still too dense". The page now draws only what has something in it, open and capped at five rows with "All N" (a full list in the column, Esc back): Pulse (`project_pulse`, read at today, no model — the tied conversations per week, each counted once on its newest day (`conversationDays`), what was done in 28 days, an open thread's `from` participant as Waiting on, a thread silent past Q as Gone quiet; Python and Swift run one fixture), In motion, Coming up (planned milestones + open backlog), Timeline (done milestones as filled diamonds). Pages in this project (`project_pages`): the automatic layer (claims and event participants, the commons guard) plus `entities_added`, less the person's `entities_removed` ("Not part of it", final) — frontmatter because a negative must beat the automatic layer and a `part-of` claim already makes a sub-project. `cicada_link_entity` on stdio, CLI (`projects tie-page`), remote and `/v1` (`projects_tie_page`): a trusted harness ties at once, any other proposes (`proposed: true`). The head is one standing line, the provenance meta in `.help`. **2026-10-28 — a project worked on today never reads as Past (`fix/event-object-project-past`):** #493's verification showed the synthetic persona's canoe under Past, "Resting · last heard <today>", above "Last · Today · <progress logged today>". The cause: `ProjectState` (and its Python twin) put any page Sleep's decay had marked `decaying` or `archived` in Past before it looked at activity, and decay is charged from the conversations Sleep has read — progress logged in the app, or a conversation not consolidated yet, never reaches it until the next drain. The section now reads activity first: heard within Q (the project's own rhythm, max(14, 2× median gap)) is in motion whatever the status; the status rests a page only once it is quiet past Q; nothing for 90 days rests as before (one fixture, `timeline_state.json`, four cases changed/added; Swift and Python agree). Activity also counts a conversation tied without a claim yet — by hand, by the provider's project id, by the relaying app's word (`_tied_days`, list and detail alike, an untied one never). List p95 on R-PJB9's 2,500-page synthetic, 40 runs, idle Mac: 54–64 → 55 ms with no ties, 65–74 → 56–58 ms with 300 relayed and 100 hand-tied conversations (p50 +0.4 / +1.5 ms), inside the 80 ms budget. **2026-10-27 — ruling 45, Projects v2 (branch `feat/projects-v2`):** the list is the person's — created in the app,
their shell or by a trusted agent on their ask, a folder they named, one they logged progress on, or a suggestion they
took — and everything detected waits under Suggested with its reason, never archived (`project_ownership`); the

naming it, Sleep's claims, a tie by hand, the provider's project id, the relaying app's word — never co-occurrence;
`project_conversations`, FTS-backed, `partial` while the index builds; p95 13 ms on R-PJB9's bench); influences
(`project_influences`); `cicada_create_project`, `cicada_link_conversation`, `cicada_add_influence`; Claude export
projects and ChatGPT `g-p-` groupings (`provider_projects`). Wire g141-7. The SwiftUI page (card + "project knowledge"
panel, Q7) is the second PR. **2026-10-26 — the Projects page is hidden (owner) until G222's redesign:** `PageSwitches.projectsPage` off; the rail, ⌘K and the tour drop it, every hand-off to a project opens the project's own page, nothing is deleted and no project page changes (PR `feat/traces-sources-projects`). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Happenings, milestones, participants and timeline read models exist. Finish named graph/project/date/evidence/interaction seams and verify them; do not rebuild the event writer or claim done from backend-only events. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g141). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** The owner wants four things: projects as first-class things to track, with or without a plan; a graphical timeline per project showing "me today" and progress; dated happenings narrated with every participant linked (the owner, a person, a document with its URL, a tool whose specs live on its own page, a sub-goal) and with a status (done yesterday, ongoing today); and a cluster of the people, tools, documents and sub-projects around the project. None of this exists as one thing. There is no per-project timeline, no milestone (`grep -ri milestone api/` finds only comments), no record with more than two participants, and nothing reads "an ongoing thing nobody mentioned for three weeks". The dated data that does exist is never drawn together: G17 `due` claims (`entity_extractor.py:113-117`), G140 `expected_end` (`claims.py:184`; `ClaimModel` does not even carry it, `schemas.py:710`), supersede chains, G118 spans with turn times (`evidence.py:399,431`) and conversation timestamps. **Four defects the design leans on (verified at dev 6eea9ee).** (1) Sleep drops every claim whose subject has no page, under a false comment ("re-emitted next cycle"; the episode is already marked processed), and it keys the subject by `sanitize_id(raw name)` rather than Stage 2's id, so "the user connects to X" lands on a page that does not exist (`claim_pipeline.py:139-146`, `entity_extractor.py:530-531`). (2) Reconcile folds Monday's and Thursday's same-object claim into Monday's and never moves `valid_from` (`claim_reconciler.py:436-445`). (3) Expiry closes a met `due` and a missed one the same way, so an expired date says nothing about done (`claim_expiry.py:41-133`). (4) Stop-hook episodes store `turns:` as an integer count (`transcript_capture.py:261,284`), so a Claude Code session resumed over three days dates everything to day 1. **The design** (spec `docs/specs/2026-09-23-g141-project-timelines-design.md`, rulings R-PJ1 to R-PJ23; three architect designs judged twice). Happenings and milestones are **claims**: not prose, not a new file type, and never an 11th entity type (G17's "July 8th" lesson). There are two predicates: `happened` (ongoing / done / dropped) and `milestone` (planned / done / missed / dropped; one slot per slug, so a moved target is a visible supersede chain). There are four optional fields, each omitted when empty: `status`, `target` (which expiry never reads), `participants` (a closed role set, each linking an exact substring of a plain sentence, with no wikilinks in YAML) and `date_basis` (stated / turn / episode / person / written). A milestone's slug is its `object`, never its `context`, because a non-`general` context becomes a graph facet satellite (`claim_contexts.is_facet`); the person's app writes use `origin: companion_app`, which joins `_HUMAN_ORIGINS` (`claim_reconciler.py:62`) so they are protected. A done happening is **born closed** (`valid_to == valid_from`), so every reader that treats open as "currently believed" stays correct with no change. The few history readers learn one test, `claims.is_event`, which a grep-gate test enforces. Events are not records, so they stay in FTS and in episode citations, where they render as dated happenings rather than "No longer current" (`provenance.py:443`, `search_service.py:444`). Only `progress.py` writes an event predicate. Event cardinality lives in code, because an existing bank's stale `_predicates.yaml` cannot be trusted (`predicates.py:159-189`). Dedup matches span identity first, then date-aware ids, so a G104 re-read never duplicates. Python decides every date through one resolver against the turn's time, and the resolver beats the model. Nothing relative is stored, and neither wire carries today or a viewer zone. **The order is derive, then write, then spend.** A $0 read model assembles moments from what every bank already holds, with the owner's own washed sentence as the narration. $0 writers follow: `cicada_note_progress` over MCP and the person's in-app Log field, milestones and Done / Still going / Stopped. Sleep extraction comes last, 💸 and gated on owner-graded measurements. A quiet ongoing thread or an overdue milestone raises an engine-free `followup` inbox item instead of decaying. Its answers are G113 verdicts that grade the extractor. The app is a Projects page in the owner's Direction D grammar: list, then project, then Reader, with a 96 pt band (a Today marker in `textPrimary`; milestone diamonds hollow, filled, ghosted or slashed; done dots, ongoing capsules, a density strip). An unplanned project draws only what happened and an open end. **Absorbs and cites.** It absorbs **G4** for projects: "how did it end up going?" becomes the follow-up, and "solved by X" is the answer written as a done happening. It absorbs **G13**'s per-project half: the status alphabet, the per-project list, and the inbox surfacing. G13 keeps the app-wide board and undated "we should…" capture. It extends **G17**: a project `due` reads as a milestone until the person touches it, and an expired `due` reads "passed, no word on how it went", never done. It delivers **G140**'s open P10 (open-loops cursor: `_state.md` v3 `now`) and P11 (last time in this project: `cicada_project` and `GET /projects`) for projects; G140's app surface for its bank-wide `cicada_timeline` stays with Tracks P/O. It cites **G53** (`_state.md` v3 `now`/`next`), **G75** (Current lines, R12), **G106**, **G108** (the rail cell needs its ruling), **G113**, **G115** (the cause rail), **G118** (spans; the Stop-hook sidecar amends R-PB4) and **G124** (`ConversationRow`, Resume, sparklines). **Triage: APPLY.** PJ-0 to PJ-6 cost $0. PJ-7 (Stage-1 happening extraction) is 💸, about +15–30% Stage-1 spend on BYOK, $0 on the plans, and ruling 4 is unchanged. It is built only if M1–M3 show derived moments plus agent writes fall short. PJ-8 (a consented per-project re-read) is 💸, user-triggered only. **Size: L** (nine PR-sized slices). The backend slices PJ-0 (page-less fix), PJ-1 (read model) and PJ-4 (Stop-hook turn stamps) can start now, then PJ-2 (`cicada_project` + handshake) and PJ-3 (event claims + writes). PJ-5 (the app) waits for the DS shell and the G108 ruling. PJ-6 follow-ups come after PJ-3. **Open DECIDEs:** the Projects rail cell and its number (G108; default ⌘7 after Sources so no shortcut moves); the band's colour (neutral shapes vs DESIGN_RULES §10's "dots use data hues", R-PJ21); holding an unpromoted subject's claims in the pending store (split out of PJ-0 on purpose). **Shipped 2026-09-23 (PR #88, `feat/g141-capture-side`).** PJ-0: Stage 2 returns its `name_to_id` and Stage 5.56 keys every claim endpoint through it (`entity_resolver.endpoint_id`, the edge rule as one function), maps the closed owner surfaces ("the user", "me", …) onto the `owner: true` page, and counts the claims whose subject still has no page (`claims_page_less`: counts in the log, the claim ids at DEBUG, never a subject; carried into the `sleep_run` row as M3's measure); the false "re-emitted next cycle" comment is gone, and the R-PJ17 hold is `claim_pipeline.hold_page_less`, a seam that holds nothing until PJ-0b (the hold was ruled yes on 2026-09-23). Claims now follow Stage 2's matches, its fuzzy merges included (disclosed; G98's). PJ-4: the Stop hook writes `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}]` through `episode_staging.stamps_for` (the hook's `role: text` body is the stager's line shape byte for byte, so the spec's "cannot reuse" did not hold), outside the hash, the last key, head-stable at 500; the episode `timestamp` stays the session's start, a pre-PJ-4 episode keeps its count until its session's next rewrite, and a lint keeps the `turns` key to its three modules. **Carry-forward:** the on-disk `memory/banks/demo` holds one non-synthetic capture, so every screenshot and fixture comes from a freshly generated demo bank. From this track on no capture writer can write into a demo bank (`demo_guard`, see G117), so the capture leak cannot recur; the old copy still needs regenerating. **Ruled 2026-09-23 (owner: "i'd go with your suggestions … maybe like a green bar that fills up? something that fits the aesthetic. make nodes in the timeline clickable"):** Projects is the eighth rail item (⌘8, after Sources; Home took ⌘1); the band is a meadow-green fill that fills up to Today with every node clickable; Sleep holds an unpromoted subject's claims with the pending entity (new slice PJ-0b); R-PJ16 accepted. **Built PJ-0b (2026-09-24, `feat/g141-hold-page-less`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-g141-hold-page-less.md`, R-HP1…R-HP13).** The pending store moved to `pending_store.py`, now its one writer; `vector_index` delegates. Stage 5.56 holds a page-less subject's Stage-1 claims on the pending line whose slug is that subject (`held_claims`, as `Claim.to_dict()`: spans, not copies; at most 50 per name, head-stable, the rest counted), and releases them first, through Stage 3, in the cycle whose Stage 5 gave the name a page: onto Stage 2's own exact verdict for the name, with the conversation they were heard in credited on the page. A holding line leaves the store only then. Promotion keeps it and a re-park carries it, so a cancel or a failure between Stage 2 and the claim write loses no held claim. Never held (still counted as `claims_page_less`): a subject with no pending line (Stage 1 named the endpoint differently from the entity), the owner surfaces, a claim closed in its own batch, and an event. Counts only: `claims_held`, `claims_released`, `claims_hold_capped` and `claims_waiting` on the `sleep_run` row. **Open:** nothing expires a pending name (an owner question; R7 and D2). **Built PJ-5 (2026-09-24, `feat/g141-projects-page`, plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-g141-projects-page.md`, R-PP1…R-PP27).** Projects is the eighth page (⌘8). The list, at D density, shows Active · Quiet · All, a mini green bar per project, the next milestone or "No plan yet", people from the graph and the compact age. A project opens with the band — a green bar that fills up to "You, today", every mark a button (ring, hover words, ←/→, ⏎ into the Reader) — then Log progress (dated by the server from the words, with Undo), Now, Lately (one sentence per happening, every participant a chip), Plan and Around this project; the Reader or a card is the third column. Writes are `Mutation`s with the server's own 409 sentence. `/projects` stays out of the Store (an in-memory, ETag-revalidated cache). `ProjectState` runs `timeline_state.json` beside the Python, and the app's fixture is the demo's real wire, pinned by a pytest. **Reported, not built:** `GraphNode` dates (a backend change), people on `ProjectRow`, a hash on `TimelineQuote`, Home's "In motion", the Graph card's "Open project ›", event rows elsewhere ("day · status · sentence"), Move, and the PJ-8 menu item. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🟡 PJ-1/2/3/6 built (feat/g141-read-write); PJ-0b built (feat/g141-hold-page-less); PJ-5 built (feat/g141-projects-page); GraphNode dates reported (backend). **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** **A project is born from a repository or from the person's words; standard sections** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22, D4: "Projects tho can as you say be started from just me talking to claude about something that spans outside software only … timeline, what has been done, what is expected and sections that can be standard throughout projects in general. People that somehow can be involved to projects will be linked to them"). **Problem:** the Projects page cannot say where a project came from and shows no repository (app inventory); Sleep mints projects from consolidation alone. **Ruling:** a project page is born from (a) a repository the person works in with an agent — the git remote the hook already observes (G110 D2, the bounded read-only pair; the backend never inspects the workspace) — or (b) the person saying, in any conversation, that something is a project (their own located words, the day they said it shown as "You said this is a project"). Consolidation alone never mints one. Every project carries the same standard sections: timeline, done, expected, design (looks and design notes as markdown, G196), people involved (each a linked person page). Existing projects with neither origin go to one review list in the Inbox after a dry run the owner reads. **Constraints:** promotion rail unchanged for non-project types; G150 backlogs stay their own files; `progress.py` stays the only writer of events (G141's own rail). **Status 2026-10-23 — the fallback page (owner, amends ruling 36 D4; PR #441):** something never called a project now shows in the app. Evidence: on `benchmarks/newuser` the canoe (five conversations, never "project") had no page — its facts were held under five project-typed spellings while Ask still answered from the conversations. A project mention with no birth is decided after Stage 2's loop (`entity_resolver._settle_unborn`): the judge, never an alias alone, weighs it against project pages, fallback pages, the batch's other unborn names and parked project lines (shared conversation first, then a shared word, at most 12, a no-shared-word pair told that is no lead); the spellings judged the same form one group and the ordinary promotion rule is read over the group. Over it: one `concept` page (the closed set's general type, no 11th type) marked `proposed_type: project`, the other spellings as aliases, every pending line and held claim folded in. Below it: held as before. A later birth makes the same page the project with `born` (`project_birth.retype_on_birth`, only on a marked concept with no `born`); a project page is never retyped or archived by this. Constraint for any change: the fallback is never a shortcut around promotion, and never a project page without a birth. **Status 2026-10-22:** backend built (`feat/projects-v2`, plan `docs/plans/2026-10-22-g141-projects-v2.md`). **The birth rule** (`project_birth`): Stage 1 computes a `project_birth` record per project mention, no model — *repo* when the episode is a harness session the hook observed in a git checkout (`workspace_identity` with a `family_hash`, bound to `project_dir`) whose folder name (the last component, or the one before `.worktrees`) folds to the mention's name; *words* when one clause of the person's own turn names it within five words of *project* (six languages), kept as a span — and Stage 2 promotes a project only on a record (the ordinary rungs never mint one). Stage 5 writes it once as `born:`; a named watched folder is `words, via: folder`. **Deviation, disclosed:** the ruling's text says "the git remote the hook already observes" — the hook observes no remote (G110 D2 allows only the `rev-parse` pair), so the folder name stands in; reading `remote.origin.url` would amend D2 and is the owner's call. **Sections** (`project_sections.SECTIONS`, derived on read, nothing written): overview (Summary; Sleep, `cicada_edit_page`) · timeline (items; `progress` + derived moments) · done (`progress`) · expected (planned milestones, open backlog items; `progress`, `backlog`) · design (the page's `## Design` markdown, then `## Looks`; the person, `cicada_edit_page`, `design_refs`) · people (every linked person page). **Wire** g141-5: a row's `origin`, the detail's `origin` {kind, stored, why, day, episode, quote, repoName, via}, `sections`, `people`. **Dry run** `python -m api.scripts.project_origins_dry_run --bank <path> [--json] [--quotes]`: every project page as repo / words / neither with why, read only; the acting owner runs it on the real bank and asks the owner before any review list. Nothing is archived or deleted. **Measured on a synthetic bank (verify-cicada, Claude plan, haiku):** a repo session → page born `repo`; one conversation saying "a new project called …" → page born `words`; a name Stage 1 typed project in two conversations with no origin → a pending line, no page (the old rule would have made one). **Open:** the SwiftUI page (`app.md`, *Projects v3*, needs a Mac); the review list in the Inbox after the owner reads the dry run; the demo bank's projects carry no `born` yet; an agent tool to say "this is a project" for the person (today only their own words in a captured conversation count). The redesign briefs cite this as G211. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G222 | <a id="g222"></a>**Projects, redesigned — the person's own projects, like Claude or ChatGPT projects "on steroids"** (Rodrigo 2026-10-26: "The Projects page really doesn't convince me. Hide it for now — some detected projects aren't even mine. The idea of projects is like Claude Desktop or ChatGPT projects: all the chats of one topic. A redesign will be needed." Then, fuller: a project is started manually by the person or by an agent; it has a name and a description both the person and agents can change; it carries in-progress and done items with a timeline of how it developed; it shows every conversation related to it across providers and services; and the outside sources and influences on it — a film that influenced it, for example) | **Problem.** The page showed detected projects, and some were not the person's: detection (ruling 36 D4's repository and located-words births, G141's evidence) is not the person choosing. **What a project is now:** a grouping of the person's chats on one topic, chosen or confirmed by the person — started by them, or by an agent and confirmed; a detected project that is not theirs must never show. **What it carries:** a name and description (person and agents edit; an agent's edit carries provenance like `cicada_edit_page`), in-progress and done items (G141's happenings, G150's backlog) on a timeline of how it developed, every related conversation across providers (G105 capture, G135 relays, exports), and outside influences (a film, a paper, a site — G61 sources and media pages linked to it). **Constraints:** the person disposes (UX 3) — no project appears without their yes; provenance on every agent write; portable (no owner names). **Until it lands** the page stays hidden behind `PageSwitches.projectsPage` (PR `feat/traces-sources-projects`); project pages still show in Clusters, on the graph, in ⌘K and on a person's card. Another worker builds the redesign. | DECIDE → APPLY · P1 |
| G169 | <a id="g169"></a>**Every bank starts with its owner — a "(you)" page, seeded on creation and filled by chats and consolidation** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "the owner page should be created automatically with a (you) parenthesis at the start of a new bank, with a small sentence like the main user or something. It gets populated with beliefs as consolidation and chats happen. Lets not assume users will have a bank to carry knowledge from, tho in the exports, chatgpt and claude might have a 'memory' markdown that might be useful to extract info about the user but a lot might be outdated too, that why cicada is better.") | **2026-10-07 — shipped (#228):** a speaker's self-reference (user, the user, me, I, yo, mí, el usuario…) resolves to the owner page through one qualified decision used by Stage 2, claims, holds and wikilinks; a real non-person entity with that name wins; prompts name the owner by the page's name; a link to the owner page no longer promotes a first mention (CLAUDE.md rail). Known limitations documented in capture-and-sleep.md: a later collision re-materialises earlier `[[Yo]]` links; synthesis drops in-cycle unioned fields (pre-existing). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bank owner seeding and owner-flag rendering are merged. Confirm an empty/new bank and imported consolidation correctly enrich it; stale not-merged text does not mean missing implementation. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g169). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The seeded owner-page implementation is present in merged dev; the historical PR #140 “open” label below is stale. Validate a clean bank, required owner setup, neutral seeding and no cross-bank carryover in G10. This row stays active for that live acceptance, not for a second implementation. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The gap:** a new bank had no owner page until onboarding's owner PUT, so a first drain read a hundred conversations into a graph with no 'me' to attach the person's own statements to (G117's owner-page question, left open in round 4). **Shipped in PR #140 (branch `fix/first-run-extraction-owner`, opened, not merged):** `create_bank` seeds one `owner: true` person page in its own `cicada` commit — the machine-level name from `owner.json` when one was saved, else a neutral `Owner` placeholder (`owner_placeholder: true`, id `owner`) that opens 'The main person this memory belongs to.'; never the demo bank; the lifespan also seeds the first-boot default bank (which never goes through `create_bank`), only when brand new. **Rulings inside it:** `name` stays the plain name, because Stage 2 matches a mention to a page by `name` and a stored '(you)' would stop the person's own name resolving — the app renders 'Name (you)' from the flag; `PUT /settings/owner` adopts a placeholder (renames it, keeps its id and claims) rather than writing a second owner page; **nothing is carried from another bank** — only the machine-level name and an id, so no user is assumed to have prior knowledge to import. Because the seeded page would make `graph.nodes.isEmpty` false and hide the Welcome and the empty states, the app now uses `hasNoContentBeyondOwner` (FirstRunGate, Graph, Clusters). Existing banks are not migrated; the page is evergreen so a drain's decay never touches it. **The exports' own memory files** (a Claude or ChatGPT 'memory' markdown) are useful leads but often outdated — see **G170**: they arrive as dated, lower-trust *assistant* words that Sleep checks against what the person actually said. **Not verified live:** a clean first run on a fresh bank (Welcome with the owner node present, '(you)' in the graph and card). **Follow-up:** the Sleep v5 first drain shows the page filling ('your page has N beliefs', **G163** §4.8). → resolves the owner-page question in **G117**; feeds **G163**, **G170**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · live fresh-bank acceptance in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G170 | <a id="g170"></a>**A first import reads honestly — quoted attachments are documents, memory files are background, and an import is not silence** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing the first-run path with a real ~1,200-conversation export in mind; no bank content recorded here) | **2026-10-16 — read on time (owner: "Yes I agree with read on time then"; TODO ruling 24 amendment):** a conversation read late counts as if read the day it was said — the decay pass places a late mention on its day (revived, then charged the silence since; proven silence keeps the repeat floor, live silence in full, ruling 1's cap on what one pass adds), only raising, so when a conversation is read never changes the result and late conversations land the same in any order or batch split. A live mention now raises any settled page to `max(current, 0.6)`; a late-read claim restatement is placed on its day like its page. Existing banks: `api/scripts/recompute_read_on_time.py` (the old rule's mark is `last_referenced` later than `learned_on`). Tests: `test_decay_read_on_time.py`. **2026-10-13 — "an import is not silence" is superseded (owner, merged #268; TODO ruling 24):** age counts from when a thing was said; one-offs may archive by age, things on 2+ dated conversations floor at 0.39 and only live silence archives them; a mention never lowers; a decay question only when live silence crosses 0.4. Owner bank recomputed: 2,197 one-off pages archived, 821 faded, 1 question. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Import attachment/background/date/silence fixes are merged. Check representative fresh/delta exports under G10 rather than rebuilding parsers from historical failure notes. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g170). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Import attachment/authorship and decay-stamp fixes are implemented in merged dev; historical open-PR status is stale. Verify real export shapes through the permitted import UI, document versus speaker attribution and date preservation in the G10 trial. Do not infer real-data quality from synthetic unit tests. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Two defects, both on the first-drain path, fixed in PR #140 (opened, not merged).** (1) *Extraction:* a quoted `attachment [...]` turn is a document the person shared, not their words, and a `claude_memory` episode is dated, lower-trust background; Stage 1 is now told both, and `parse_anthropic_memories` declares `evidence_kind: assistant` so those spans read as Claude's, never the person's (builds on the `attachment [<file name>]:` grammar, G118). (2) *Decay:* an import is not silence. A page or claim a cycle creates or references from months-old episodes keeps its content date (`last_referenced`, `valid_from`) but gets `decayed_through` = the cycle's date, so silence counts from when Cicada learned it. Without this a multi-cycle drain of a backdated export charged, and could archive, what it had just read. `decayed_through` is a stamp, not a change to the arithmetic, so **TODO ruling 1 is respected** (still at most one week per cycle, measured from `max(last_referenced, decayed_through)`). CLAUDE.md updated in the three places these made it wrong (decay, evidence kind, owner page). **Tests:** `test_decay_backdated_import.py`, `test_evidence_extraction.py`, `test_conversations.py`. **Not verified live:** a real multi-cycle drain of a backdated export. → relates **G147**, **G118**, **G163** (the drain runs both decay engines once at the end), **G169**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · real export/drain acceptance in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G171 | <a id="g171"></a>**Sleep at scale — decay questions asked once and capped, and both derived indexes update incrementally, off the event loop** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing what a bank with many fading pages does to a cycle) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Decay-question dedup/caps and incremental off-loop indexes are implemented. Validate a realistic drain's capped questions/index updates; remaining write-lock defects are G183, not proof this feature is absent. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g171). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Incremental indexes and capped/deduplicated decay questions are implemented in merged dev; historical PR #141 open status is stale. During G10 check progress, pause/resume and UI responsiveness on the chosen batch. Avoid a new scale benchmark or embedding upgrade before observing the actual bottleneck. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** (1) *Decay questions.* A bank with many fading pages piled up duplicate 'Still tracking…?' items every cycle, and a *keep* answer only reached the first claim of a merged question. A decay inbox item is now keyed by entity: an entity with an open decay item (pending or deferred, raised by the entity path or by any of its fading claims) is refreshed, not duplicated; a cycle opens at most `decay_inbox_cap_per_cycle` (10) new ones, lowest confidence first, through one `DecayBudget` shared by `inbox_generator.generate` and `write_claim_nudges`; nudges the cap turns away are counted in the cycle's `sleep_run` row (`decay_nudges_deferred`) and raised again next cycle — deferred, never dropped silently. Each fading claim joins `claim_ids`, so a *keep* reaches every claim the question covered. An older pile is collapsed once by `inbox_migration.dedup_decay_items` (own `.deduped_decay` marker, oldest kept, claim ids folded in), registered in `bank_migrations`. (2) *Indexes.* Sleep rebuilt both derived indexes in full each cycle, blocking the event loop while embedding the whole bank. The sqlite-vec index keeps a stable `key` and the `hash` of the embedded text per row (`SqliteVecIndexer._sync_kind`): a cycle embeds only new and changed texts, removes deleted ones and refreshes metadata in place without an embed; a missing table, a pre-`hash` schema, another model (recorded per kind) or another width rebuilds that table in full, and an embed that fails leaves the previous index untouched. FTS goes through `search_index.refresh` (the `ensure_fresh` stamp diff), so an idle night re-indexes nothing; a full build only when the file is missing, damaged or of another schema. Sleep runs both through `asyncio.to_thread`. **Shipped in PR #141 (`fix/sleep-inbox-and-indexes`, opened, not merged);** CLAUDE.md updated in the vector index, FTS freshness and decay sections; tests `test_decay_inbox_dedup.py`, `test_decay_inbox_dedup_migration.py`, `test_index_incremental.py`. **Not verified live:** the wall-clock gain on a large bank, that the event loop stays responsive during a real cycle, and the one-time dedup migration against a large real pile. → relates **G98** (the queue must be answerable), **G147**, **G136**, **G163** (a drain calls the indexes once per batch). **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · real drain responsiveness in G10. | VERIFY · P1 |
| G172 | <a id="g172"></a>**Saved links: same-titled saves never overwrite each other, and reading new pages is never starved by reuse** (found 2026-09-29 reviewing the link path; also the identity fact G162 depends on) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** URL identity/collision and fair read-budget fixes are merged. Include same-title saves and new-versus-reused links when the trial contains media; conversation-only trial can proceed without this acceptance slice. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g172). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** ID collision/enrichment-floor implementation is in merged dev; historical PR #139 open status is stale. Verify only the remaining live acceptance: same-titled saves do not overwrite and new-page reading never drops below the configured floor. This can follow the first export trial unless that trial includes saved links. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** (1) *Collision.* `media_ingestor._media_entity_id` slugged only the title and `write_media_entity` overwrites, so a second link titled e.g. 'Home' replaced the first's page and orphaned its `url_index` row. The id now takes the URL-hash suffix (already used for truncated slugs) when the plain id names a page written for a *different* URL, `media-<slug>-<sha256(normalized url)[:8]>`, deterministic so a re-save finds it again. Ids are never renamed: a URL already in the index returns `duplicate` before the id is minted, a page for the same URL (index lost) keeps the plain id and is rewritten in place, a legacy page with no `media.url_hash` is judged by its `media.url`. Pages already overwritten before the fix are not recovered. **This is why G162 keys the video queue on the url-index `videoKey`, never the entity id.** (2) *Throughput.* Reuse (a description the page already carries, zero LLM) and fetch-plus-summarize shared one per-night cap with reuse spent first, so a night with at least a cap's worth of reuse candidates read no new page. Both the tail `backfill` and the in-cycle `enrich_media_links` now reserve a floor of the cap for fetch and summary work: `fetch_floor(settings, cap)` = `CICADA_LINK_ENRICH_FETCH_MIN_PER_CYCLE` when set, else half the cap rounded up, clamped to `[0, cap]` (`0` restores reuse-first). The floor only reserves slots for fetch candidates that exist and only when the fetch tier can run; unused slots return to reuse, so the nightly total stays at the cap. In-cycle `_candidates` now honours the 30-day fetch backoff for a page that needs a fetch. Rails untouched: the ToS rail, never-scraped papers and video hosts, the per-cycle cap. **Shipped in PR #139 (`fix/saved-links-collision-and-throughput`, opened, not merged);** tests `test_media_entity_id_collision.py`, `test_link_backfill.py`. **Not verified live:** the floor's effect on a real backlog's drain rate and the collision behaviour on existing banks. → relates **G102**, **G164** (the Reader ladder replaces `default_fetch` under this floor), **G162**. **Previous execution label (historical):** CODE MERGED · saved-link acceptance remains. | VERIFY · P1 |

its Filesystem baseline is per-session `gpt-4o-mini` extraction plus query-word substring matching, not Claude Code's memory; each provider ships its own answer prompt; its LoCoMo loader grades 444 of 446 adversarial questions against the string `"undefined"` (`String(qa.answer)` on items with only `adversarial_answer`); the published run's dataset, models and commit are not in the repo; and the vendor wrote the harness it wins. **Evidence:** [`../research/2026-09-24-memory-benchmarks-and-implicit-recall.md`](../research/2026-09-24-memory-benchmarks-and-implicit-recall.md) — MemoryBench read at commit `94e2af5` (providers, six checkpointed phases, `gpt-4o` answerer and binary judge, per-type prompts, MemScore), LongMemEval/LoCoMo/ConvoMem methodology and ten known pitfalls (a full-context baseline beating a memory system on LoCoMo is the one that matters most at personal scale). **Verified blocker in the existing harness:** `benchmarks/run_table3.py:223`, `run_table1.py:90` and `run_ablation.py:96` pass `Settings(memory_path=…)`; only `CICADA_MEMORY_PATH` takes) — a Sleep run there can land in whatever bank the environment resolves, and `benchmarks/_bootstrap.py:42-43` loads `api/.env` into the environment first. **Two more:** Sleep's "today" is the wall clock (`sleep_cycle.py:1330` passes no `now` to `conflict_resolver.resolve_and_prune`, `:41,145`; Fixed in that PR: calendar events dated the day they happened; "worked with" is history and "dropped" ends a tool's use (seed endings); skills need conversations, never bookmarks or calendar entries (G112); the timeline and Sleep history count created pages; the handshake's recent conversations exclude calendar entries. Written up, not fixed (owners elsewhere): a full-name mention held beside a first-name page (G209: `Teodor Brandt` with aliases `Teo`, `tbrandt` beside page `teo`); a project's later conversation filed under a held project mention, so a canoe update was lost to Ask (G141 D4); "New project with Teo: fernpost" not read as the person's words (the colon is a clause break); a project page born without `born` when a sibling extraction types it (G141); every curated seed fold raising "Confirm a predicate fold" (G21/ruling 29); Stage 4 topic summaries as skills (G112); a relative ending ("a week from today") closing a job on the day it was said; `reports-to` left open after the job ended. **Second pass (2026-10-22, `fix/new-user-memory-2`, ruling 37):** a shipped-seed fold settles by rule and a follow-up names its thread (A); a new save is dated when saved (B); W1 (held full name → "same person?"), W2 (a project mention's alias brings its page to the judge), W3 (the colon form counts as the person's words), W4 (the D4 gate reads the written type) fixed with tests. W6 (Stage 4 topic summaries as skills, 💸) and W7 (relative endings, `reports-to` after a job ends) stay open. **2026-10-23, third pass (branch `fix/newuser-memory-3`):** (1) "dropped <tool-a>" closed only a plain `uses`: the extractor writes object-qualified labels (`uses-for-auth-and-data`, `stores-data-in-<tool-a>-postgres`) and relatives (`depends-on`, `backed-by`), and `_place_ending` matched the canonical predicate exactly — the objects were already one id, so compound objects were not the cause. The seed's `endings.uses.also_ends` plus qualified labels (`EndingFn.ended_by`) now close them too, each under its own predicate; measured: the project's every use of the dropped tool closed in 4 of 5 Sleeps. The fifth put the drop on the owner's page ("<owner> dropped <tool-a>") and wrote the project's line as "replaced by <tool-a>", so the project's page had no ending to read — closing a project's belief from an ending said about the person is a cross-subject rule and is left for the owner. (2) a held full name whose given name is one person page's whole title asks "same person?" (G209). (3) the old job did not outrank the new one in recall or `ask` (confidence ranks nothing; the old `works-at` claims were closed), but every page's `## Related` named a link by the first edge ever extracted — the person's page read "<old-employer> — works at", "<new-employer> — interviewing with" — on the page, in a recall of it and on the app's card; each link is now named by the newest open claim behind it (`conflict_resolver.related_edges`), and the claim pass refreshes it. newuser after (3 Sleeps, one ask each): 24, 23.5, 24 of 25 by hand (q22's timeline wrong in all, as before). **2026-10-23, fourth pass (branch `fix/newuser-memory-4`):** (1) the drop said only about the person: replaying the drop conversation's Stage 1 on haiku 20× put the ending on the project 18 times; the two misses wrote "<project> replaces <tool-a>" (one also "<owner> dropped <tool-a>"). Cause on our side: the prompt offered the passive "replaced by" as an example label and said nothing about switches; our normaliser never flips (`inverse_pairs` is applied nowhere). Owner (2026-10-23): an ending said about the person never closes a project's claim; fix it at extraction. The prompt now writes a project's switch on the project in the active voice, and the seed's `uses` endings read `replaced`/`replaces`/`switched from`: 20/20 after (5/5 in the first five, the brief's count). (2) W6: a skill whose summary rests on a claim that closed after its newest cited conversation leaves *How to work with me* (`skill_staleness`, engine-free). (3) W7: Stage 1's `ends` stores the stated last day (2026-09-11 in every replay, said "a week from today" on 09-04); `reports to` said with `at` names the job in `premises` and closes with it. newuser after (3 Sleeps, one ask each): 24.5/25 by hand each (auto 22.5, 24.5, 24.5 after a grader phrase fix); the project's uses closed, the stale skill out of the handshake and the job closed on its last day in all three; one manager claim with no `at` stayed open in one Sleep (nothing guessed from timing). (4) Addition from a live test (acting owner): a newer value under another wording ("released version" after "latest release") never closed the older — a one-word attribute matches only itself — and "moved from A to B" closed no A variant; the seed now declares `value_attributes` (one attribute, several spellings) and reads `moved from`/`migrated from` as endings; reproduced on synthetic claims, never the owner's bank. | UNBUILT · P1 |
| G179 | <a id="g179"></a>**Sync the message conversations the person picks — iMessage first, then WhatsApp, Telegram by export — so a texted agent's conversations consolidate without anyone thinking about it** (Rodrigo 2026-10-05: "Could i sync conversations i have in imessage, whatsapp, telegram for my memory consolidation? For example i use <agent-a> a lot which is an ai agent i text on imessage. Can i process the conversations i have with it and consolidate them in memory without me thinking about it and just setting up the sync and selection of conversations i want consolidated?" — the agent's name redacted) | **2026-10-28 (branch `feat/messages-replies-groups`): replies, reactions and the rest reach Sleep; group chats are read; WhatsApp and Telegram planned as live sync.** *Interaction signals* (owner: "replies in messages should be used by sleep, anything relevant in message interaction chats should be, such as reactions and such"): no Sleep module read `turn_extras`, so Stage 1 saw a bare "yes" with no idea it answered a question two days old, and never saw the person's 👍 on a plan — the #491 misread family. `turn_signals` turns the stored extras into one short note per chunk, before the conversation (offsets untouched, G118): replies (`r<n>`, the target quoted from this sitting or from the earlier sitting of the same `conversation` that holds it, found by `message_keys`; an unfound target is said to be unknown, never guessed), reactions (`s<n>`, a signal, never the reactor's sentence), forwards, unsends (a position only). Source-agnostic: any writer that fills `turn_extras` is covered (an export through `draft_from_export`, the WhatsApp reader to come). A tagged short quote is located in its own turn first; the person's own reaction on the turn a claim's located spans sit in is read from the extras (never the model's tag) — agreement lifts its confidence a little, doubt halves it, and the claim records the reaction by turn id; a reaction never changes the observer, so a texted agent's claim stays `external:<agent>` (#499 review: a 👍 often only means "got it"). Measured on the owner's bank (read-only, counts only): 65 of 76 sittings of one chat have replies, 828 reply turns (741 same sitting, 87 an earlier one, 0 unfound), 195 reactions (all the texted agent's like/emoji, so not noted), 4 edits, 0 unsends, 186 turns with attachments; after the reading-order skips, 308 noted signals, ~194 tokens per sitting (median 138), ~+3.5 % per Stage 1 call. Haiku on the plan, synthetic chats, before → after: a group's "me" answering "who is driving?" went from a misread ("confirmed she made the decision") to "is driving"; a two-day-old "yes" went from unread to "replied yes when asked whether to book the appointment"; the person's 👍 on a plan is recorded on its claims, which stay `external:<agent>`. *Group chats* (owner: "should support group chats and should pair nicely with our structure of saving who said what to who, believes what"): `chat.style` 43 is listed and read once ticked, the backend still refusing any other. Every member is one **participant key** per chat (the app's hash of the chat and the handle — never a handle, never linking two chats), spoken as `speaker:<label>:` (their Contacts name, else `member-<key>`; a phone- or address-shaped name is dropped) or `assistant:` for the one member whose own chat the person marked an agent; the episode records `participants` and `group: true`, no `correspondent`. A claim resting on one member's lines is that member's (`external:<member>`); in a group only the person's own lines count as their words for promotion, project birth and applications (a meeting still counts every speaker); `person_said` never counts an `external:` claim. The addressee is recorded only where the source says it — the reply's target speaker, in the note — never guessed; `@mentions` are not read (they live in `attributedBody` attributes the decoder does not keep). A claim on several members' lines (or an unnamed member's) is `external:group`, never the person's (#499 re-check). *Follow-up (not built):* `quiet_pages`' frontmatter half still wakes an imported page when a group sitting is among its `source_episodes`, so a member's mention can wake it that way; the claim half already ignores `external:` claims. Not here: one person across sources (the next PR resolves a participant key to a page through Contacts), per-member marks in the UI. *Other conversation sources worth having* (survey, none built): **WhatsApp** — live, see below; **Telegram** — live only through the client API, see G29; **Signal Desktop** — its local database is SQLCipher with the key in the OS keychain (since 2024), so public reports; reading it means a keychain prompt and a key the app holds; no official export — DECIDE; **Mail threads** — the app could read Mail's `Envelope Index` with Full Disk Access like Messages, quoted replies make a thread's own turns hard to separate — RESEARCH; **Slack / Discord** — official exports (Slack workspace export JSON with `thread_ts`; Discord's data package, the person's own messages only) map straight onto `turn_extras.reply_to` — APPLY when asked; **meeting transcripts** — Wispr Flow meetings exist (G134), Zoom/Meet transcripts as files through watched folders already land, speaker labels intact. *WhatsApp and Telegram* (owner: "WhatsApp and telegram import export don't matter, I want a live tracking like we do with messages plus a historical if I want"): **WhatsApp — live, the Messages model.** No official personal API; unofficial clients (Baileys, whatsmeow) speak WhatsApp Web's protocol, which its terms forbid ("access, use, copy … through automated or other means", "reverse engineer", "collect information of or about our users in any … unauthorized manner", whatsapp.com/legal/terms-of-service) and get numbers banned — ruled out. Plan: the APP reads WhatsApp Desktop's local store from a copy — public write-ups place it under `~/Library/Group Containers/group.net.whatsapp.WhatsApp.shared/` with `ChatStorage.sqlite` (`ZWAMESSAGE`: `ZTEXT`, `ZISFROMME`, `ZMESSAGEDATE`, `ZCHATSESSION`; `ZWACHATSESSION`, `ZWAGROUPMEMBER`; where a reply keeps its quoted message is to verify) — one write-up names `Axolotl.sqlite` instead, so the build verifies on a test install, never the owner's (and reading another app's group container raises macOS's "access data from other apps" prompt: the person's consent, asked once); write-ups report it unencrypted. Ticked chats only, FSEvents, bytes posted, the backend parses, the same `turn_extras` (replies, reactions, edits) and participant keys; *history* = what the desktop app holds, read once on the person's "Read the history". Reading a file the app already keeps is not automated access to the service. **Telegram** — ruling 47a, G29. The plumbing this PR built is what both reuse.** **Ruling 47 (owner 2026-10-28):** Telegram moves from export to tier 2 live: **47a** — its official client API with the person's own login (the full history and live updates no harness sees), ticked chats only, the session secret 0600 in `~/.cicada`, the connect step saying that Cicada keeps a signed-in session and how to end it; history on request (branch `feat/chats-live-whatsapp-telegram`). WhatsApp stays on its local database, read by the app — no sanctioned personal-account API, and an unofficial client risks a ban. **Built (2026-10-15, branch `feat/imessage-instinct`, not merged; owner: "Can we get the Messages conversation extraction and consolidation working? Try consolidating Instinct … It's important to distinguish who is who in the conversation").** *Read:* Settings → Integrations → Messaging → Messages lists the one-to-one chats (names, last activity, per-side counts; group chats out of this slice) with the contact's name and Contacts photo when Contacts is allowed; only a ticked conversation's rows are read, by the app (Full Disk Access; a refused read shows the browsers' fix), past a `message.ROWID` cursor per memory plus rows edited or unsent since (an edit keeps its ROWID). The backend refuses (409) any conversation not ticked. Re-sync: FSEvents on `~/Library/Messages`, 10 s debounce, 60 s floor. *Decode:* `attributedBody` is decoded on the backend (`typedstream`, pure Python) — the Awake rail, one decoder for every client and testable headless; `NSUnarchiver` is deprecated and instantiates whatever classes a blob names. Tested on blobs `NSArchiver` built on macOS 26.6.2 (length forms 1/2/4 bytes measured at 127/128/40,000 characters). *Shape:* one episode per sitting, **3 h gap** (measured on the real conversation: 1 h → 87 sittings of a few messages, 6 h → 44 merging morning and evening; 3 h → 55, ~15 messages each), keyed `imessage:<conversation hash>:<first message>`; each pass merges into the stored sittings it touches, rebuilt from the episode's body, `turns` sidecar and `message_keys` — an edit rewrites in place (re-queued), an unsend removes the message and an emptied sitting is tombstoned, a late message that bridges two sittings merges them into the earlier. Tapbacks are skipped, not noted: a tapback row is the reactor's row quoting the other side's words, so keeping it would put the agent's words under the person's marker. Attachments named (`[image: plan.png]`), never read; a message's line breaks fold to spaces so nothing in it opens a turn. *Who is who:* the person's lines are `user:`; an AI agent's (the person's mark, or an `identified` detection) `assistant:`; anyone else `speaker:<label>:`; the episode's `correspondent` record names the other side, and a changed mark re-renders the saved sittings in place without re-reading the Mac. Stage 1 is told whose lines are whose before the conversation, the model can never set an observer, and a claim resting only on the other side's words has observer `external:<name>` with `assistant`/`speaker` evidence — never `user_stated`, never the owner's (proved through a fake-engine Sleep, `test_messages_sleep_attribution`). The Reader and the evidence chips name the agent ("Instinct replied"). *Its own page (2026-10-16, branch `fix/agent-identity`):* the real run resolved the agent by name onto an archived same-name `concept` page (an old chat about the ordinary word), which kept the concept's type and fading state while it carried the agent's claims and an article about it (a person-started run read 55 sittings; the page stayed `decaying` at 0.380). Now the episode's decided `correspondent` (person's mark or `identified` detection; never `unknown`) marks the entity Stage 1 extracted from that very episode under that exact folded name or alias, types it (`tool` for an agent — software the person uses; `person` for a person), and Stage 2 resolves it to a right-type page, or — under the unchanged promotion rule — creates one under `<id>-agent` / `<id>-person` (never the plain same-name id), with that episode's own claims pinned there; observers unchanged; every other mention of the letters takes the general resolver, unchanged (`correspondents`, `capture-and-sleep.md`). Below the promotion bar (coordinator 2026-10-16: the rail stays as it is) the correspondent waits in the pending store under its own key — the line named by that id and marked with the correspondent — so its claims are held there, never on the same-name page; once the rule passes (e.g. a second sitting) the page is created with everything held. Repair: `api.scripts.repair_correspondent_pages` subtracts the conversation's part from an absorbed page as it is now — claims resting on it alone, its credit, and prose/list values only when every change since the pre-conversation base was the conversation's Sleep commits (else `prose_left`, facts from claims) — and moves it to the right page; nothing else moves or reverts (person-set keys, later closures, other conversations' facts; status from the page's remaining history), no model; counts only, one `cicada` commit, a re-run is a no-op. **Not covered:** a by-name mention of the agent in another conversation, with two same-name pages, still goes to whichever the general resolver indexed last (a type-aware tiebreak is general homonym handling — owner's call). *Consolidation view:* the Sleep page's Details › What's waiting and Home's waiting line show the waiting conversations as stacked profile bubbles (Contacts photo, else a monogram) with the number of messages Sleep will read (`GET /sources/messages/waiting`); hover names one. **Left:** the coordinator's real tick and run on the owner's Mac; group chats; WhatsApp and Telegram; a message deleted locally (not unsent) stays captured; a contact renamed with no body change keeps the old name in that sitting's frontmatter until it next changes. **Owner priority/detection follow-up (2026-10-06):** Selected iMessage sync is P1; the owner explicitly wants this implemented. Build the minimum G188 reader/parser/stager seam alongside it. Add opt-in modular AI-agent identification, with Instinct as the initial detector, separate from transport and inference providers. Use permitted scoped metadata, expose uncertainty and durable user overrides; detection cannot widen selected capture, raise trust or turn agent speech into the owner’s words. Verify selected conversation → continued/revised sync → actual Sleep → grounded answers with correct speaker/source/evidence. Other messenger exports follow later. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-provider-and-ingestion-modularity.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No scoped iMessage conversation reader was found. App must read selected bytes with FDA, backend parse; implement speaker/source/date/attachment/privacy semantics before any capture, then exports for other messengers. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g179). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests selected iMessage conversations as consolidation input. The app obtains permission and reads bytes; the backend receives scrubbed staged data, never opens ~/Library. Start with explicit selected threads/date range, stable source keys, speaker attribution, delta updates/deletions and visible consent/revocation. Do not capture the whole message database by default. No personal thread names/examples belong in public docs. Other messaging providers follow later. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Not G29:** G29's bot sees only what is forwarded to it, never an existing conversation. **The sources, as found 2026-10-05 (from documentation and the platforms' known layouts; nothing on the owner's Mac was opened):** (1) **iMessage** — `~/Library/Messages/chat.db`, SQLite (`message`, `handle`, `chat`, `chat_message_join`); recent macOS often leaves `message.text` empty and keeps the body in `attributedBody` (an archived attributed string), so both are decoded; `date` is nanoseconds since 2001; `message.ROWID` is the cursor; with Messages in iCloud the phone's conversations reach the Mac's file. **Reading it needs Full Disk Access for the app — there is no lighter route.** Tapbacks (`associated_message_type`) are skipped; attachments are named, not read, at first; an edit or unsend (`date_edited`, `date_retracted`) updates the staged episode. (2) **WhatsApp** — the Mac app's `ChatStorage.sqlite` in its group container (`group.net.whatsapp.WhatsApp.shared`; `ZWAMESSAGE`, `ZWACHATSESSION`): undocumented and unverified here, behind macOS's app-data permission, and only the history the desktop app synced; check its structure (never content) on the owner's Mac before building. Unofficial WhatsApp clients are out (the ToS rail). (3) **Telegram** — the Mac apps encrypt their local stores, and the API would mean Cicada signing in as the person, which G166's rule forbids; the route is Telegram's own *Export chat history* (JSON) into a watched folder, so a manual step now and then. **Shape (existing rails, no new ones):** the app reads `~/Library` and posts bytes; the backend stages through `episode_staging` keyed by service + conversation + sitting (edits in place, removals tombstoned); **one episode per sitting** — a gap of a few hours closes it (G104's one-episode-per-session reading); **nothing is read until the person ticks a conversation** (Settings lists chats with their last activity, one toggle each — the browsers' read-only-after-turned-on rule); Sleep consolidates on its own schedule. **Provenance:** a texted agent's replies are that agent's turns, never the person's own words (as G135's remote claims and G105's agent replies); group chats carry other people's words, and per-conversation opt-in is the gate; every writer scrubs. **To decide:** Full Disk Access (the owner found it "too much" for bookmarks, 2026-09-23 — here it is optional and scoped to this feature, with the exact-fix sheet); whether attachments are ever read; the sitting gap; what unticking a conversation does to episodes already saved (stop syncing; keep unless removed). **Order:** iMessage (covers the texted agent; a stable local store) → WhatsApp after the structure check → Telegram by export. Relates **G29** (the bot), **G12** (chat-history import), **G104/G105** (sessions, deterministic capture), **G134** (the shared stager), **G81** (names for handles), **G30** (opt-in local reads). *Follow-ups (2026-10-16, branch `fix/messages-followups`, not merged):* a ticked chat's shared Messages photo is its page's picture everywhere (rung after Contacts; the app posts it, `messages_photos` keeps it outside every bank by conversation and links it through the correspondent record; untick removes it), and a mark whose words do not move still reaches the waiting sitting's `correspondent` record (frontmatter only, no re-queue of a read sitting). **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · scoped iMessage prototype; permission boundary to decide. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G207 | <a id="g207"></a>**A quick read after each conversation, and recall that sees today — inference without waiting for Sleep** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22, D2: "that opt-in should be the default if provides better performance and no need to have this as something that can be changed on settings") | **Problem.** A conversation is useful to an agent only after the next Sleep. Measured on the owner's bank (read-only, 2026-10-22): the episode is in the FTS index on the next read, so `cicada_ask`'s search leg finds it by words; but recall's "Related conversation excerpts" leg is vector-only (`mcp_tools._leann_search_episodes`, `mcp_tools.py:3570`) and episode vectors are built only by Sleep's index sync (`sleep_cycle.py:2299`) and index-only history (1481) — the 3 episodes captured that day had 1 FTS doc each and **0** vector chunks. Pages, claims, aliases and inbox questions all wait for Sleep. Supermemory's comparison point: raw chunks searchable in seconds, facts in minutes. **Step 0 (no rail change):** embed a new episode's chunks right after capture, off the capture path, through the existing worker, never during Sleep; fuse FTS episode passages into recall's excerpt leg under the same `sources` scope Ask uses; label each "<day>, not yet read by Sleep". Retrieval, not belief: no page or claim is made from raw text (ruling 5). **Step 1 (ruling 36 D2 amends the capture rail):** a separate job, once a conversation ends (Stop's idle or the session's end), reads it with a small model and writes **provisional** claims (an `inferred` kind with the episode as parent) that Sleep confirms or drops; nothing provisional settles an inbox question, counts toward promotion or is the person's own words. **The bar that decides the default** (no setting either way): on the verify banks, recall and Ask answers for questions about the last day's conversations with and without Step 1 (≥3 runs each, the #370 spread), plus Sleep's confirm rate of the provisional claims; ship on only if both beat the spread. **Cost basis:** Sleep ≈ 15 calls per conversation (measured 2026-10-21); a quick read is 1 call, ≈ 6k in / 1k out with Cicada's caps, ≈ 1¢ per conversation at $1/$5 per million (assumed small-model price, check before quoting), ≈ 30¢ a day at 30 conversations; on a plan it counts against limits. **Constraints:** capture calls no model and returns as fast as today; `CICADA_CAPTURE=off` spawns are never read; the demo guard holds; the read uses the engine ruling 36 D1 resolves and, on a plan, follows ruling 4c's switch (owner, 2026-10-22); never during Sleep's write window (G177). Research: memory-quality §3c, supermemory. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed; Step 0 is the first slice. | APPLY · 💸 · P1 · proposed |
| G209 | <a id="g209"></a>**Duplicate people — one person as a name page, a handle page and a waiting line** (found 2026-10-22 on the owner's bank; real values redacted: `<person-a>` and `<handle-a>`) | **2026-10-28 (vision item 10, G115's catch-up):** Rodrigo: "in my conversations with <assistant> I use <nick-a>, <nick-b> and <nick-c> interchangeably" — asked once as "Are <nick-a>, <nick-b> and <nick-c> the same person?" when Sleep's pairwise questions about people chain (`inbox_catchup.groups`; on a copy of the owner's bank the owner's own example was one of the 2 chains found); and Sleep now proposes the pair spelling alone suggests — a single-word short form of the other's first name, ≤ 0.6 of its length (`nickname_pair`; 2 pairs on the copy, the 3 rejected by the ratio being different given names) — as an ordinary `merge_suggestion` (`detected: spelling`), at most 3 open. A question only: a "yes" is joined by Sleep from the person's own captured words (ruling 29 part 1), never by the agent. **2026-10-27 (three People cards for one person, real values redacted):** three more mechanisms, closed for every type — (1) resolve looked candidates up by the new name only: a new extraction's own aliases and a close spelling (a dictated misspelling, no shared word, under fuzz 85) met no page, so the spelling was born; now both bring the page to the judge, and a page that is both a close spelling and a shared alias is never cleared by the judge alone (held and asked); a person's fuzz>85 direct join now needs one spelling once case/accents are set aside; (2) an alias came from co-occurrence: Stage 1 gave one person the other person's name from the same conversation and Stage 5 unions new aliases for good; now an alias that is another entry's name or nickname in the same conversation is not written (unless the two list each other), while a nickname others share is kept (ruling 41); pronouns are references; (3) nothing compared person pages by the exclusive relation they hold (partner, mother, father): now one "Is <A> the same as <B>?" per pair, never a merge. Ruling 41 (owner, 2026-10-27): a nickname is kept as an alias, titles a page with no name until a name is found (the name question stays open), and never joins on its own; a name many people go by is not judged against every holder, but still meets the page titled by it and any more specific lead, and a held name's edges and claims never land on a page that shares its name. Bank repair (which aliases to remove, which pairs to ask) left to the owner on a per-item dry run. **Problem.** One person was held three times: `<person-a>.md` created with `aliases: [<handle-a>]`, `<handle-a>.md` ("known so far only by this handle", asking for its own full name), and a pending line `<handle-a> (<person-a>)`. Both pages were born in the **same** Sleep commit. **Cause (code read, `api/services/entity_resolver.py`):** the alias index is built only from pages on disk (`aliases = _alias_index(existing_by_name)`, line 127; `_alias_index` 1179); names are judged most-specific first, so the full name became an in-cycle create carrying the alias; then `_find_direct_candidate_match` compares an in-cycle create's name only, and `_created_llm_candidates` (1660) needs a shared content token and never reads the create's aliases — the handle got zero judge candidates and was promoted. Once both pages exist, an exact name match wins forever: the disk-alias path runs only when there is no exact page, and `dedup_sweep.find_candidate_pairs` (G21) gates on name-embedding cosine ≥ 0.85 and never reads aliases. A handle is never treated as identity evidence, and the parenthetical form `h (Name)` parks a third line. **Measured:** 14 of 277 person pages (≈ 11 pairs) carry a name that is another person page's alias; 2 pairs born in the same commit, 9 across commits (2026-10-07 to 10-16); some may be genuinely different people. **Fix, smallest first:** (1) index in-cycle creates' aliases like disk pages, so an alias-marked candidate reaches the judge (an alias is a lead, never a decision, 2026-10-08); (2) when a name has its own page and a different same-type page lists it as an alias, file one "Possible duplicate" question, engine-free, never a merge — plus a one-off sweep over existing pages, dry run per item first; (3) split `Name (h)` / `h (Name)` at extraction into name plus alias, and an engine-free detector for handle evidence (an `x.com/h` or `github.com/h` path in a link the person saved, shared evidence episodes) that raises "same person?" citing spans. **Constraints:** ruling 29 — never who a person is without their own words, so evidence from an agent's summary or a URL path asks, it never merges; ruling 27's web settle excludes people; G118 spans; `role_people` and the Contacts rules unchanged. Uses G207's `inferred` kind for the question's provenance. Research: memory-quality §1. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed (`people` worker). **2026-10-23 (benchmarks/newuser, branch `fix/newuser-memory-3`):** a held full name with no alias naming a page now asks "Possible duplicate of …" when its given name is the whole title of exactly one person page and no other person page's name begins with it (`entity_resolver._given_name_pages`; never the owner's page or a relation-titled one, never a name with its own page) — W1's cap of three and `merge_rejections` hold, never a merge. Found: `<given> <surname>` held beside a page titled `<given>` (real values redacted) raised nothing, because W1 reads only a recorded alias. **2026-10-27 (review of #483, branch `fix/held-release`):** a held name's claims were released only when Stage 2 joined the name again, so a person's "same as B" answer on a name never mentioned again left them held for good; now that answer lands them on B in the resolver's own commit (object-side claims re-pointed, undone with the answer), "someone else" / dismiss / skip release nothing and the name waits for the promotion rule. | APPLY · P1 · proposed |

Age arrives only through prose ("As of November 2024…"), History dates when the budget keeps them, and "Changed recently". Research: memory-quality §3b. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed. | APPLY · P1 · proposed |

## P2 — lean CLI/MCP, distribution, headless and portable procedures

| G180 | <a id="g180"></a>**A `cicada` CLI beside the MCP — agents with a shell run commands instead of loading 30 tool schemas; the skill teaches it** (Rodrigo 2026-10-05: "do we still need an mcp? i've seen developers say that mcp is only useful if u need to give an agent some type of auth." … "add the cicada cli as very important in the backlog along with the skill if needed") | **2026-10-13 — parity slice merged (#266; ruling 21a):** 34 commands cover the app's actions; commands on backend-owned state go through the backend's routes after `/healthz` confirms the root; person-only commands refuse from an agent's shell; one `cicada import` (chat export → intake, platform export → the saved-exports reader). The web-readiness map: hard blockers are only the Mac readers and the app's own lifecycle; soft blockers to move behind the API, in order — a client-state store for UserDefaults-only state, fade dismissals into the decay tuning file, a feed-kind field on `/sources`, claim-digest fields, search-model and engine-card action fields, a `/home` figures route, Sleep mood, source liveness, the tab-group parse. Owner 2026-10-13: keep the door open for a browser front end; new features live behind the API. Unexposed on purpose: inbox resolve, source, claim, backlog, progress, link save, ask, hub, perspective, video (MCP wording, untyped errors, `ask` can spend). **2026-10-08 — slice 1 shipped:** #231 (entry point, single root/settings resolution + `/healthz` root cross-check, JSON envelope, `recall`/`status`/`commands`), #235 (`get`/`project`/`continue`/`save`/`handshake`), #238 (release `~/.cicada/bin/cicada` launcher, Settings *Install the command-line tool* linking `~/.local/bin/cicada`, generated usage skill). Ruling 21 holds. MCP tools keep the `cicada_` prefix; grouping them is the lean-MCP slice, decided on measured schema numbers with old names kept as aliases. Verify setup, post-Sleep reads and attributed contributions through G76’s integration matrix; a package install alone is not connected memory. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-release-and-integration-acceptance.md). **Portability follow-up (owner 2026-10-06):** Expose G110's working-context read/selection service through the CLI and portability skill, using the same permissions/provenance as MCP. The primary acceptance is automatic new-session continuation; a user-authored handoff file or mandatory manual CLI export/import does not satisfy it. G110 does not wait for the CLI to prove same-harness rollover. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Backend services/MCP exist, but no packaged cicada command entry point was found. Build governed commands, stable structured output, discovery/skill and error contracts; measure MCP schema savings without breaking no-shell clients. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g180). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner confirms a leaner MCP and CLI as priorities. Consolidate G157 here. Build a headless cicada CLI over the same governed services with machine-readable output, structured errors, bounded paging/discovery and import/consolidate/status/recall/export operations. Measure the existing 30-tool schema and total task context, then shorten descriptions or evaluate scoped profiles/grouping. Keep hosted/no-shell MCP clients, capability scopes, provenance and R12 parity; a generic execute door must not bypass them. AMR export is G186, packaging/platform work G181/G187. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why.** The MCP server lists 30 tools whose definitions are ~34.6k characters (~8.7k tokens, measured 2026-10-05), paid up front by every client that loads tool schemas eagerly; an agent with a shell (Claude Code, Codex, an agent on a server — G181) can run a command and read `--help` only when it needs to. Capture and recall already left the tool-call path for hooks (G105, G149) because depending on a model choosing to call a tool was unreliable; the CLI is the same move for an agent's deliberate actions. **MCP stays** as the door for clients without a shell — Claude Desktop and claude.ai, ChatGPT, the phone through G135 (the auth case) — and for the `initialize` briefing and the harness's identity. **Shape — one implementation, two thin doors.** The CLI and the MCP handlers call the same `api.services` functions, so no rule gets a second copy (scrub, staging, `episode_lock`, the per-bank git lock, provenance trailers, the revision-checked mark of #176). It lives in this repo (`api/cli.py` and an entry point; a Homebrew tap is a separate one-file repo holding only the formula — G181). Commands mirror the tools: `cicada recall \| ask \| timeline \| project \| save \| claim \| retract \| pending \| mark \| inbox \| sources \| backlog \| handshake \| sleep status \| bank`, each with `--json`; writes carry `Cicada-Author` and the session from the harness's environment (G48). **Launcher:** never a venv console script — G88 found the shebang hardcodes the interpreter and breaks when the repo moves — but a small launcher that resolves the repo's venv, or `uv tool` / brew. **The skill:** `SKILL.md` and `skills/cicada-librarian` teach the CLI first to shell agents (a few lines plus `cicada --help`) and the MCP to the rest; `install.md` (G76) installs both; the handshake names whichever door the client has (R12). **To decide:** whether CLI writes go through the running backend's HTTP API (one writer process) or in-process like the MCP server (works with the backend down, same locks); which MCP tools to merge once shell agents use the CLI. Relates **G76**, **G88**, **G105**, **G135**, **G149**, **G181**. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §1: owner asks for a leaner MCP alongside CLI-first shell agents. Fresh static `TOOLS` baseline: 30 tools, default `json.dumps` 35,109 chars (8,777 tokens by chars/4), compact JSON 33,772 chars — proxy, not client billing. Audit shared description policy into the existing handshake/skill; evaluate related-operation grouping and optional profiles against representative workflows and compatibility. Preserve R12, sources/read/record/answer scope separation, provenance and ownership; no generic dispatcher bypass. Measure total schema/primer/skill/discovery overhead and task/tool-selection outcomes before/after; do not pick a reduced tool count without evidence. G186 is the export consumer.** **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · first implementation after trial blockers, P1. | UNBUILT · P2 |

Instinct's signals are the conversation's own name (folded) and an agent's cadence (the other side ≥ 20 messages and ≥ 2× the person's); **a name alone is only `uncertain`** — surfaced as "Is Instinct an AI agent?", never applied — and a similarly named human ("Jane Instinct", a human's cadence) is `unknown`. The person's mark (agent | person | cleared) always wins, is kept with its history in the bank's `sources/messages.json`, survives every sync and re-renders the saved sittings; detection never ticks, widens or raises trust (`suggest_agents` turns it off, and an emptied registry leaves capture and marks working — both tested). Preserve stable IDs, bank/demo guards, consent/revocation, cursors/revisions and attribution/history when an adapter is removed. A separate opt-in detector registry starts with Instinct, permitted scoped signals, unknown/uncertain outcomes and durable user overrides; recognition cannot widen capture or treat agent statements as owner statements.
