| G191 | <a id="g191"></a>**Ask why at the moment of saving — a native one-tap "what interested you?" prompt when the person bookmarks or pins something** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07) | **2026-10-28 — the reason is a signal for reading (vision item 1; branch `feat/vision-sources-reason`).** `saved_why.reason_of` is the one reader every consumer uses (G191's newest answer, else a `## Saved because` section): the reading queue's row carries it (`— saved because: …`, remote only with `sources`), link enrichment's summary leads with what the page says about it and re-reads a page described before its reason once (`read_for` stamp), and a saved link's where-to-check proposal cites it (`reason_span`). Stage 1 is unchanged by measurement: on the owner's 12 reasoned saves haiku already records the reason faithfully (12/12) without a note; a note cost 89–130 tokens and gained nothing. **A reason is no promotion weight of its own** (the brief's default, kept): a marker-less save already reads as the person's writing in `promotion.exchanges` — its page's own description included — which is older than this row and left for the owner. **2026-10-23 — v7, the notch's second version (branch `feat/save-notch-v2`; `app.md`, "Ask why, v7"). Rodrigo 2026-10-23, paraphrased:** he loves the notch appearing on a Chrome save; (2) the field should grow smoothly for the first few newlines, then scroll; (3) more options that appear smoothly based on what is being saved — "maybe an extremely light classifier model" choosing from a bank (found it funny, good asset for video, moodboard potential, interesting design, product, inspiration, aesthetic …) — "let's have a lot of options"; (4) document every idea; (1), saves in other apps, is **[G221](#g221)**. **Built (APPLY):** (2) the field grows one line at a time to **five**, then scrolls inside — the words cap at 500 characters at ≈ 60 a line, so five show ≈ 300 (most answers whole) while the card stays a short drop from the notch; it eases 0.12 s (`CicadaMotion.fieldGrow`) and changes in one frame under Reduce Motion — DR-60 lists keyboard commands, a field growing with typed words is the content's own size (dated DESIGN_RULES ruling 2026-10-23); the window is reserved tall up front, so it never resizes under the person. (3) The four stay the fixed first row — keys, labels and order unchanged (Hyrum's law: the options' `reasons`, the stored keys and `explore_plan`'s depth table all read them); a bank of 38 short reasons (`saved_why.MORE`, served as `more`, one fixture both sides) feeds three to six suggested chips that fade in once the link is known, ranked on the Mac with **no generative model** — the site (`SaveWhySuggest.sitePriors`, weight 3), the link's title and path words (2; never the query), the person's own past picks from the answers the bank already holds (1.5 a pick on the same site up to 3, 0.4 a pick anywhere up to 5; nothing new stored) and EmbeddingGemma's cosine similarity **only when it is already installed** and answers within 0.35 s (`POST /sources/saved-why/suggest`, read-only: never a download, never the bundled small model, never a metered service). Ranked once per link (a late answer never reorders chips on screen); a tap as they appear is ignored like the panel's own first half second; a chosen one is stored exactly as a reason (key in `saved_why`, label as the person's line). Also fixed: the field never showed an answer's words filled in on a saved page (its chips filled, its words did not). **Open:** bank reasons do not steer site exploration yet (`explore_plan`/`explore_candidates` read only the fixed keys, so a bank answer explores at capture depth) — a small mapping (moodboard, aesthetic, typography, UI pattern, colours → the look; a tool to try, to buy, a product idea → the product) is next and needs a ruling on whether a tapped suggestion may deepen an agent's read (DECIDE). Line breaks typed in the field are folded to spaces when stored, as every reader expects; keeping them is a format change (DECIDE, only if the owner wants them). **v2 option — a classifier (DECIDE: needs an owner ruling against "No LLM at capture time"; ruling 36 D2's amendment covers a quick read after a conversation, not the save path):** (a) Apple's on-device Foundation Models framework (macOS 26 or later with Apple Intelligence on) given the title, the host and the bank as a closed list, its guided generation constrained to bank keys — local, nothing downloaded, but a generative model at the moment of saving and an answer in about a second, after the chips should already be there; (b) the search model's image encoder (its own second click, the search-model rail) over a screenshot of the saved page — a classifier, not generative, but a screenshot needs Screen Recording, which activity traces (G218, ruling 38 §4) is bringing; taken only on the save, kept outside every bank, dropped after scoring. Either only re-ranks the bank (never invents a reason; the person still picks) and ships only if it beats v1 on the owner's own past answers. **2026-10-19 (round 6, `fix/save-why-panel`; `app.md`, "Ask why, v6"):** the four reasons on one line; an answer is never dropped for a dismissal (before: an answer given before the backend confirmed the bookmark was dropped at `confirmBound`, and a sync refused while Sleep wrote never confirmed it; a 404 dropped held answers) — held in the outbox awaiting its browser's sync instead; a touched panel (tap, word, or a click into the field — dictation) closes only on the person's act; the star on a saved page opens its answer filled in for editing (**owner ruling 2026-10-19:** Chrome's front-tab URL only when Automation is already allowed, never prompting — without it the star on a saved page still asks), a no-change edit writes nothing; a click into the field makes the panel the key window without activating Cicada (typing and dictation land there), and Save/Return/Esc hand the keyboard back to the browser. | **Owner, verbatim (2026-10-07):** "when i bookmark something like a website in chrome or safari or any other browser, i sometimes have an intention, e.g. i save a technical blog that explains something in interest that some things there are useful to replicate in my own projects. Or i save a website because i love the design of their application and web and also their launch video. So i thought maybe cicada as the macos app can have a slick prompt that appears that looks like part of MacOS and asks for a what interested you in saving this? and you write a small note on it (even better if cicada gives me options to just click, with the option to further describe too. if i click anything else tho the prompt dissappears. Detecting also when i pin somehting in pinterest, twitter or anything else would also be amazing." **Why it matters:** a bookmark records *what* was saved; the person's intent is the *why*, and today it is lost — Sleep must guess relevance later from the page alone. A stated intent is the person's own words at the moment of the action (highest trust, `observer: person`), so it grounds recall relevance (G93: "the blog I saved for its sync technique"), feeds the projects the save was meant for (G141/G150 — "useful to replicate in my own projects"), and is exactly the grounded reward signal the Era-of-Experience framing asks for (G113). It also turns a passive bookmark pile (G86, G3) into intentions an agent can act on, and later into skills (G112). **What exists:** the app already watches browser bookmarks and knows the moment one is added (G129 Chrome/Safari bookmark watch, seen-set; G119 other browsers; G160 tab groups) — the trigger is there; nothing asks. **Design constraints:** (1) native look — a small non-activating floating panel (NSPanel, chrome-layer Liquid Glass only, DR rules; follows DESIGN_RULES direction D), positioned near the menu bar / the saving app, never steals focus or keyboard input from the browser; (2) one tap — a few deterministic choices (e.g. design I like, a technique to replicate, read later, for <project>, inspiration) plus an optional "say more" field; (3) any click elsewhere, a timeout, or Esc dismisses it silently and the bookmark is still captured as today (UX principle 4: non-intrusive, never required); (4) **no LLM at capture time** — the quick-pick options must come from deterministic sources (a fixed set plus the person's active project and backlog names, G141/G150), never a model call; (5) the answer is stored with the bookmark's episode as the person's own words through the one staging path (episode_staging, scrub), so Sleep reads it with the page; (6) rate limit and "don't ask for this site/folder" so a bulk import or a bookmark-bar reorganisation never floods prompts; (7) a Settings switch, on by default only after the owner tries it. **Pins and likes elsewhere (Pinterest, X/Twitter, …):** RESEARCH — the ToS rail forbids reading behind a login and the backend never fetches login-walled pages; candidate routes are an opt-in browser extension the person installs (sees the pin/like click in their own browser, posts bytes like the app does), official exports/APIs the person connects, or the person's own agent reading on request (G166). Detection is not capture until the person allows the site (per-site switch). **Designed in [G221](#g221) (2026-10-23).** Relates G129/G119/G160/G86/G3/G93/G113/G141/G150/G166/G112. Triage: **APPLY** for browser bookmarks (P2), **RESEARCH** for third-party pins/likes. **Built for browser bookmarks (2026-10-14, branch `feat/ask-why`; owner 2026-10-14: "a prompt that comes down from the mac notch … marking the time and date is important too"):** the sync reports `fresh` bookmarks with the browser's own `added_at` instant and `first_sync`; the app asks only on a live watch event with exactly one fresh bookmark made in the last 5 minutes, at most once per 3 minutes, never for a muted site (`SaveWhyPolicy`); a non-activating notch panel with multi-select chips (one tap + a 2.5 s linger, so design *and* product is one more tap, never a confirm); `POST /sources/saved-why` writes a `## Why it was saved` section and `saved_why` frontmatter on the bookmark's episode through `episode_staging.amend_section` (scrubbed, re-queued, committed as `user`); `GET /sources` → `savedWhy` is the wire. Reason ids `design`, `product`, `technique`, `read_later` (+ `project`, `text`). Settings → Integrations → Browsers switch, off by default per (7). Open: pins/likes elsewhere (RESEARCH, unchanged); Esc reaches the panel only once it is clicked (a global key monitor would need Accessibility). **Read by exploring (G166, 2026-10-14):** `explore_candidates` reads the episode's `saved_why` frontmatter (`{reasons, project, text, at}`; reasons `design`, `product`, `technique`, `read_later`) as an optional field and steers the depth from it (design → the look, product or a project → the product, technique → read, else the default capture, D4); `added_at` becomes the capture's `saved_at` and `saved_why.at` its `why_at`. The claim's reply hands the agent the person's reason and words. **v2 (2026-10-14, branch `feat/notch-v2`) — the prompt actually appears, and looks like the notch.** Measured on the owner's Mac after "I bookmarked in Chrome and nothing appeared": the switch was never set *and* the Chrome and Safari bookmark watches were both explicitly off, so no live sync could start. Now one switch works: turning "Ask why" on turns on the watch of every installed browser Cicada can sync (named before the flip; explicit offs included, since that is the measured case; turning it off leaves the watches on), and the row names a refused read with its permission and one click (Safari's `Bookmarks.plist` sits in `~/Library/Safari`, which needs Full Disk Access to read and to watch — Safari offers no bookmarks API, so there is no lighter route; Chrome needs nothing), and says the next bookmark is the first one asked (the turn-on sync is a seed, never live). The panel is the notch growing (owner: "the same black as the notch … seamless … it has the mascot"): top edge flush with the screen's, notch black in both appearances, an outline that starts as the notch and springs out with continuous corners, the curious bookworm in the header, the same shape from the top centre without a notch (DESIGN_RULES §9 ruling 2026-10-14). The menu-bar badge says "99+" past 99 and names what it counts ("133 questions waiting"). A developer-only menu item (`cicada.debug.saveWhyPreview`, never in a release app) shows the panel on demand for a placeholder bookmark and writes nothing. Open: the owner's look check on a real notch in Chrome and Safari. **Live (2026-10-14, branch `fix/notch-live`) — measured on the owner's Mac:** Chrome save → panel 18 s (the sync's page fetches and two commits, 6-7 s, then the store's four-domain reconcile and an options fetch, all before the question); a Safari save never synced (a kqueue on `~/Library/Safari` sees only entries renamed in that folder, and was armed once at launch, before Full Disk Access); "Something else…" found open with nothing chosen. Now: a live sync first asks the read-only `POST /sources/sync-bookmarks/fresh` (the sync's own parse and index check, no write) and the question is decided on that with cached options — an answer that beats the sync is held on its 404 and sent when the sync lands; the watch is an FSEvents stream filtered to the bookmark file's name (300 ms settle), re-armed once a refused file becomes readable; a save during a sync is read after it; a click on the panel within 0.5 s of it showing chooses nothing and the panel takes keys only after a click inside; the panel opens on the screen of the browser's front window; the three-minute limit is gone (owner, bookmarking several sites in a row: "shouldn't it be immediate?") — each single fresh bookmark asks and a newer one replaces the open panel, keeping any tap, with a 3 s guard against one save's double event and 60 s for the same link. Tested: save → panel ≈ 0.5 s against a 3 s sync (budget 2 s); Safari replaced in place, from beside and from elsewhere twice, each seen live. **Retest after #288 (2026-10-14, branch `fix/notch-sign`):** first Chrome bookmark ≤ 2 s; the second, 12 s later, +12 s — it waited for the first sync; now a save that cannot sync yet is looked at at once and a later look counts only links new since the earlier one (tested: two saves 10 s apart under a 14 s sync, each ≈ 0.5 s). Safari never asked because the ad-hoc dev build's Full Disk Access is keyed to its cdhash and dropped by every update: `make signing-identity` makes the self-signed "Cicada Local" identity the install already prefers, so grants survive rebuilds (docs/RELEASING.md; a release keeps them only with a Developer ID). Open: the owner's re-run of both browsers on the real Mac after the one-time signing setup. **v3 (2026-10-15, branch `feat/bookmarks-v3`) — owner, verbatim:** "When I bookmark something in Chrome, it takes quite a bit for the prompt to appear. How can we make it immediate?" · "If I remove the bookmark and bookmark it again, the prompt doesn't appear. Is that on purpose?" · "We should have the option to select various reasons of interest as well as input something in the text bar." · a Maccy-like bookmark history in the menu bar ("If we click, the app opens quickly to the page of that site, where we can modify and edit stuff, or remove the bookmark (which removes it from Chrome too)") · "No extension is best … Can't Cicada non-intrusively monitor Chrome's window?" **Cause of the wait (measured):** Chrome holds a bookmark change 2,500 ms (`BookmarkStorage::kSaveDelay`) before writing the file; with the watch's 0.35 s and the early look's 0.34–0.40 s round trip on a synthetic 20k-bookmark file, click → panel was ≈ 3.25 s. **Built:** (1) the panel opens on Chrome's own "Bookmark added" bubble, seen in the window list from bounds alone (no title, no permission), only while Chrome is frontmost and right after the person's key or click, pending until a sync confirms the bookmark (else it withdraws after 6 s and keeps nothing); the detector learns the bubble's size and refuses sizes that came with no bookmark (`BookmarkBubbleDetector`); click → panel ≈ 0.1 s; (2) the app diffs the bookmark file it just read (`BookmarkFileDiff`, parse 78 ms + diff 82 ms on 20k) so the link fills in before the backend's look; (3) a re-save is fresh (`resave: true`: absent from the channel's last seen-set, or a browser instant newer than the newest it saw; a move keeps both) and its answer is a new dated reason (`saved_why` newest, `saved_why_earlier` the rest); (4) toggles plus an always-present field and Save, no linger, leaving keeps what was chosen; (5) **Bookmarks…** in the menu bar (search, ⌘1…⌘9, capture thumbnail → stored preview → monogram) opening the Feed's detail, where each answer is editable (`PUT /sources/saved-why`) and **Remove** archives the page and tombstones the episode (`POST /sources/bookmarks/remove`) and deletes Chrome's nodes through Apple Events after asking; Safari is named as not editable by another app; (6) `make signing-identity` step 2 finds the key by its certificate's public-key hash (`security import` labels every key "Imported Private Key"). Safari's ⌘D popover appears before anything is saved, so Safari asks on the diff, not a bubble. Open: the owner's live check of the bubble sizes on his Chrome (the detector learns them on the first bookmark); the optional Apple Events front-tab link (not built — the diff fills it without a permission). **v4 (2026-10-15, branch `feat/bookmarks-v4`) — owner, verbatim:** "some of the options … have nothing to do with it. Can you keep options to a minimal? Like the design, read later, might be useful for the future, or something else" · "I'd like to know that Cicada has the status, like it's not linked, or is no longer a bookmark saved in Chrome" · "the size check of the popup seems like depending on that is not great. What about just checking when the star is clicked and bookmarked? And confirm that it actually was bookmarked if added to the file. Also make sure this polling is efficient and not some crazy running thread." **Built:** (1) four reasons — `design`, `product`, `read_later`, `someday` ("Useful someday", new; `explore_plan` reads it as the minimal capture, like `read_later`) — in two fixed rows of two, the field as "Something else…", no project chips (options' `projects` always empty); `technique` and stored projects still read and stay editable; the app carries the four (`SavedWhyOptions.basic`) so no panel waits on the options fetch. (2) A bookmark removed in the browser is kept and marked from the sync that saw it gone (`bookmark_seen` `left`, `leftBrowser` on `/sources`, `left` on a bookmark channel's items, `leftMarked` on the sync): "No longer in Chrome since Oct 15" on its page, a dimmed ⌘B row with a quiet mark, a dimmed Sources row; re-adding clears it and asks again. Guards: never on a first read, a folder-scope change, an empty read (the last set is kept) or a wholesale swap (no link in common with ≥ 5 before); a move never marks. The "Still keep …?" removal question is retired for bookmarks (pending ones are folded into the status on the channel's next sync); it existed because the sync had no other way to say a link left and no Remove of its own. `bookmark_seen.json` now rides the `sources` ETag component by its mtime (the directory fingerprint reads only `*.md`, so before this a seen-set change was invisible to the ETag). (3) Measured on the owner's Mac by the coordinator: Chrome's "Bookmark added" and "Edit bookmark" bubbles are both 448 × 224 (v3's width range refused it every time); file write → panel ≈ 0.3–1.3 s. The bubble watcher is now click-triggered: nothing while Chrome is not frontmost (activation notifications), one global mouse-down monitor while it is (no timer), a window-list read per click and a ≤ 400 ms burst only after a toolbar click; any new small window anchored under the toolbar on the right opens the pending panel; nothing is learned or refused (v3's size keys removed). ⌘D has no permission-free event, so it is caught by the file. Idle with Chrome in front over 60 s (synthetic): 0 reads, 0.11 % CPU for the whole test process. The file path looks at once on the first event (`instantLook`, before the settle), never behind a busy sync, its floor, another browser, the network or the options: write → panel 17 ms on 354 bookmarks with every other path hung. Open: the owner's live check that a star click opens the panel at once on his Chrome; ⌘D stays at Chrome's 2.5 s plus ≈ 20 ms. | PARTIAL · P2 |
| G221 | <a id="g221"></a>**Saves in other apps — notice the person's own save, bookmark or pin on YouTube, Instagram, LinkedIn, X, Pinterest … and ask why** (Rodrigo 2026-10-23, paraphrased: detect when I save something in more apps — YouTube, Instagram, LinkedIn, X, Pinterest — it's so useful to draw a quick "why I saved this"; extends [G191](#g191)'s "pins and likes elsewhere") | **Problem.** The notch asks only after a browser bookmark (Chrome's bubble, a bookmark file). Most of what the person keeps is now saved inside a site — YouTube's Watch later and playlists, Instagram's and X's bookmarks, LinkedIn's saved posts, Pinterest pins — and none of it touches a bookmark file, so nothing asks and the intent is lost at the one moment it is known (G191's argument). **What the platforms' terms touch.** The terms reading of 2026-10-23 (browser-sync report, kept outside the repo with the redesign research) is about *automated access* — a bot or an agent reading pages. The person clicking their own Save button is not automated access, and noticing that click on their own Mac is not access to the platform at all; only what comes after — reading the saved item's page to enrich it — can be, and that stays on today's rails (G206's lists through the person's agent, ruling 30's index-only, the ToS rail). One caveat: LinkedIn's "Prohibited software and extensions" page also names extensions that scrape its site, and an extension reading the clicked post's link sits near that line (below). **Options.** *(A) A Cicada browser extension* (Chrome MV3; Safari later as a Safari Web Extension inside the app): a content script, injected only on sites the person turned on (`optional_host_permissions`, asked per site by Chrome's own prompt at runtime), listens in the capture phase for the person's own click (`isTrusted`) on that site's save, bookmark or pin control — a small per-site table of stable attributes (aria labels, test ids) — checks a beat later that the control's state flipped (pressed, label changed), and sends `{site, the item's own canonical link, its title, the time}` — never the feed, never other posts — to the app by **native messaging** (a host manifest the app installs under Chrome's `NativeMessagingHosts`, a tiny relay in the bundle, a 0600 Unix socket to the running app; no new localhost HTTP door). The app opens the same notch, pending then confirmed, saves the link through `POST /sources/save` and the answer through `saved-why`. Costs: a Chrome Web Store listing and review (or developer-mode sideloading for testers only — Chrome refuses off-store installs on macOS outside enterprise policy); a selector table that breaks when a site changes its markup (each site then fails closed to no question, never a wrong one, because the state check fails); one more component to sign and update; Safari separately. Privacy: per-site grants, no `<all_urls>`, no history, nothing read but the clicked item. *(B) Accessibility right after the person's click:* the app already sees clicks through the bubble watcher's permission-free mouse monitor; with the Accessibility grant it could ask `AXUIElementCopyElementAtPosition` what was clicked (role, label: "Save", "Pin"). Costs: the broadest grant macOS has; Chromium builds its web-content tree only when an assistive client asks (`AXManualAccessibility`), which costs Chrome memory and CPU all session; labels are localised; the button carries no link, so the item needs the tab's URL (Automation) or a walk up the tree. Not the web's primary route — the heaviest permission for the least exact answer — but the route for native apps later. *(C) Activity traces ([G218](#g218)):* focus spans with titles and URLs (and screenshots, ruling 38 §4) show the person was on a post, never that they saved it — context, not a trigger; once G218's Accessibility grant exists, B's permission cost is already paid. *(D) Per-site native signals:* YouTube's Watch later and history through the person's agent (G71), X/Instagram/LinkedIn saved lists (G206), Pinterest's official API (v5 `pins:read`, the person's own OAuth app; `saved-content-integrations.md`) — all batch, hours after the moment: they confirm saves and catch the phone's, and could carry one grouped "why did you save these?" review (the D5 "Confirm N links" shape), never the instant notch. **Recommendation: A for the moment, D as the confirmation and the phone's catch-up.** The extension is the only route that sees the person's own click with no OS-wide permission, scoped per site by Chrome's own prompt, knowing exactly which item was saved — the same shape G191 has for Chrome's star (the click triggers, a second signal confirms). YouTube and Pinterest first (the cleanest markup, the mildest terms on extensions), then X and Instagram, LinkedIn last and off unless ticked with its clause named in words; D's lists later reconcile what the extension saw or missed. B waits for G218's grant and native apps. **Rails:** no model at capture; the app is the one Mac reader (extension → app → backend, bytes only); no page fetched behind a login (the extension sends only what the person's own page shows of the clicked item; enrichment stays G206/ruling 30); a block never retried; capture never into a demo bank; the notch's own rails (an answer never dropped, only the person closes a touched panel, Don't ask for this site); provider-neutral copy; the remote connector untouched. **Owner decisions:** (1) build a browser extension at all — a new component to review, sign and keep current (recommended: yes, Chrome first); (2) distribution — a Chrome Web Store listing (public review, a privacy-policy page) or developer-mode sideloading for testers (recommended: an unlisted Store listing once the first two sites work); (3) which sites and in what order, and LinkedIn given its extensions clause (recommended: YouTube and Pinterest, then X and Instagram, LinkedIn off by default); (4) whether a save on another site becomes a saved link in Cicada even when the person answers nothing (recommended: yes — the save is the person's act, like a bookmark — indexed per ruling 30 when the post is walled); (5) Safari — a Safari Web Extension in the app needs an Xcode target and Developer ID (G182): later or never. **Slices:** native-messaging relay + app socket S–M · extension skeleton with per-site grants M · YouTube + Pinterest tables and the state check S · the notch from an extension save S · X + Instagram S · reconcile with the G206/G71 lists M · Safari L. Relates G191, G206, G71, G166, G218, G219 (Pinterest's API), G182 (signing). | RESEARCH → DECIDE · P2 |
| G192 | <a id="g192"></a>**A minimal design revamp — fewer elements, better layouts, every page** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07) | **Owner, verbatim (2026-10-07):** "add to the backlog another design revamp run to make everything more minimal with better layouts". **Context:** direction D (docs/design/DESIGN_RULES.md) is binding and shipped on every page; the 2026-10-06/07 owner reviews showed layout issues the rules did not catch (a card clipped under Home's field, #208; the rail/titlebar corner reading as a spike, #220; Home's painted band turned off, G189(a)). G189(b) (Home revamp, mockups first) is the Home-only slice of this. **Method (DECIDE first):** (1) audit every page and sheet against direction D for density — what can be removed, merged or deferred behind disclosure — with screenshots light/dark at 1×/1.4× and narrow/wide windows; (2) bring the owner 2–3 directions as rendered mockups (offscreen snapshots, never the owner's running app) — e.g. "quieter D" (same tokens, fewer surfaces), "content-first" (chrome recedes, one column), "dense pro" (more on screen, tighter rhythm); (3) the owner picks; record a dated DESIGN_RULES amendment; (4) page by page, each PR citing DR ids, checked live light and dark. **Constraints:** the rails stay (SF only, accent in six uses, depth is a ring not a shadow, Liquid Glass only in chrome, keyboard actions never animate, CicadaMotion durations, never blank); no data behind painted art; provider-neutral copy. Merge with G189(b) rather than duplicating it. Relates G189/G130/G137/G125/G176/G184. Triage: **DECIDE** (mockups before any build). | UNBUILT · P2 |
| G193 | <a id="g193"></a>**Port the app to GPUI (Rust) — performance, and maybe Windows** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07) | **Owner, verbatim (2026-10-07):** "see if maybe we can port the app to gpui rust for better performance and maybe that way it runs on windows too?" **What GPUI offers (checked 2026-10-07 via current docs):** a GPU-accelerated hybrid immediate/retained-mode Rust UI framework (Zed's); runs on macOS (Apple silicon + Intel), Linux (Wayland/X11) and Windows x86_64; a component kit exists (longbridge gpui-component). **Web content is the weak spot:** the WebView (Wry-based) is experimental, macOS + Windows only, and renders on top of all GPUI content — the graph is d3-force in a WKWebView today and **G109 rules that d3-force stays**, so a port must either keep a web view for the graph (in its own layer/window) or re-implement the graph natively (G109 would need revisiting). **What a port would cost or lose:** (1) the SwiftUI app and its ~3,000 Swift tests; (2) native macOS integration the app relies on — EventKit (Calendar), Contacts, Apple Notes via osascript, Safari/Chrome reading under the app's own permissions (the backend never reads ~/Library), the menu-bar worm, Liquid Glass chrome, the updater, login items — each needs Rust bindings or stays Swift; (3) direction D's SF type/motion details. CLAUDE.md's key decision "SwiftUI + FastAPI" would change (a dated ruling). **Windows is mostly not a UI problem:** the backend is Python and portable, but macOS-only readers (FDA, EventKit, Contacts, Notes, Wispr) have no Windows equivalent, and G187 (Windows evaluated separately) / G181 (app-free server, one writer) already frame Windows. A Windows *client* of the same API could be GPUI (or another toolkit) without porting the macOS app. **Measure first:** is the app slow, and where? G184 profiles energy; the Sleep page went 12% → ~1% CPU with a CALayer sprite fix (#170), and type-as-you-go FTS answers in ~12 ms — collect a perf baseline (launch time, page switch, graph FPS at 2k nodes, memory) before attributing anything to SwiftUI. **Research plan:** a time-boxed spike porting ONE page (Sleep or Clusters) plus the shell to GPUI against the existing API, measured against the SwiftUI page on the same machine; a separate spike of a Windows GPUI client reading/answering through the API only (no ~/Library readers); a report on options (a) full port, (b) SwiftUI stays on macOS + a GPUI Windows/Linux client over the API, (c) status quo. Relates G184/G187/G181/G109/G180/G50. Triage: **RESEARCH** (no build before the spike report and an owner decision). | UNBUILT · P4 |
| G194 | <a id="g194"></a>**Sleep writes old material as dated, never as now** (Rodrigo 2026-10-07: "i see that the .md says im interested in an internship there but this was from a conversation months ago… Time of the ingested material during sleep should be accounted for") | **2026-10-28 — the act is on the event, never its venue (`fix/event-object-project-past`, ruling 42's class):** #493's verification showed the persona's Reader noting "<person> signed up for <makerspace>", labelled "You said", from "I signed up for an <event> workshop at the <makerspace>". Stage 1 pinned the act on the venue as well as on the event. Fix in two layers: the Stage 1 prompt's APPLICATION rule names the venue/organiser case, and a deterministic guard after the spans are located (`act_object.hold`) reads the act's object from the person's located sentence — the target after a venue or organiser word, with another object between it and the act, becomes "<event> at / run by / with <venue>" when the act on that event is in the same extraction (dropped if that link exists), else "<act> something at <venue>"; a label that already names where folds only beside the act on the event; `application_page` gives such a name no application. **Measured** (plan, haiku): replaying Stage 1 on the persona's fiberglass conversation 5× — before (dev prompt, no guard) the act sat on the venue in 5/5 runs (2 "signed up for <venue>", 3 "signed up for workshop at <venue>"); after, 0/5, each run writing "<person> registered for <workshop>" and "<workshop> held at <venue>". Full persona drain (39 episodes): before 2 act claims, 1 on the venue; after 1, 0 on the venue, the event→venue link present. The owner's bank (read-only copy, ids only): 91 act claims, 82 with a located span; 1 reads its object as a venue or organiser by its sentence, and 3 of 45 labels that already name where sit beside the act on the named object (would fold); the rest are untouched. **2026-10-27 — facts keep their context (`fix/facts-keep-context`; TODO ruling 42):** an owner-page fact had lost the event it was about because a displaced orientation was split at every sentence (`entity_body.retain_orientation` and its four sibling writers); a texted agent's explanation became the person's completed act (Key Facts carry no speaker; the prompt told the model to write what the user did after a suggestion); pages repeated a fact with and without its tail; an agent's act carried no agent or session. Now: a sentence that leans on the one before it stays joined to it (`fact_policy.standalone`); a claim resting only on a harness agent's or a chat export's assistant's words is `external:<agent>`, a harness transcript is told whose its assistant lines are, the prompt says who did it and that a plan stays a plan; every Stage-1 claim carries its episode's session; a fact another says word for word and then more folds into it, sources moved, never a word lost (`fact_policy.extends`, ruling 23's trigger: 109 facts on 79 pages of the owner bank, 25/25 sampled loss-free; paraphrase stays synthesis's). Ruling 42: an event or program the person or a harness agent acted on gets its page from one conversation, holding the application (`application_page`); the owner keeps the link. Follow-ups: the owner-approved repair of existing owner-page lines, a "did you send it?" nudge through the inbox rails, and the ~2.7k paraphrase / 2.7k re-introduction facts the loss-free rule cannot fold. **2026-10-22 — the conversation's day is its local day (TODO ruling 34 follow-up, `fix/sleep-local-day`):** `source_dates.episode_day` read a conversation's day from the first ten characters of its UTC `timestamp`, so the A1 date note, a claim's `valid_from` and a page's `created`/`last_referenced` dated words said just after local midnight the day before. They now read it through `episode_ids.local_day` (the person's zone; a naive legacy stamp is this machine's time, a bare day is that day). No decay arithmetic changed. **2026-10-13 — pages are articles (owner, merged #263/#264; TODO ruling 24):** a lead plus `## Article` prose sections over a record that is never shortened, for pages on 2+ conversations (2026-10-17: and a one-conversation or new page with enough material — the thin pages, median 45 words against 454 with an article, were the ones the writer never ran on; Stage 1's "usually 0-5 facts" lifted; `rebuild_page_articles` newest first, batched commits, resumable), provenance per paragraph, an edited article becomes the person's; behind `CICADA_PAGE_ARTICLES_ENABLED` (on for the owner). Owner bank rebuilt: 1,050 pages, 0 record lines lost, median +259 words. Follow-ups: retire summary synthesis once articles are default; History lines carry no G118 rows; the owner page (over the input budget) has no article yet; an MCP tool for agents to write the article (agent prose stays machine prose). **Shipped 2026-10-07 (#230), A1+A2:** every Stage-1/synthesis/contradiction prompt carries the source day(s) and today (absolute only; evidence offsets untouched); the entity header says *last mentioned Feb 2025* instead of the confidence word when the newest source is older than 90 days (owner D1), confidence moved to `.help`. Verified on the clean run: old material is written "In April 2023, …". **Deferred with open critique blockers (see the plan's *Deferred* section):** A3 deterministic prose floor; B person-started dated-pages pass (owner: prose only, 1 call/page, dry run first, after the drain); C intentions as dated claims aged through the existing decay (owner: "remember our decay feature" — claim-level class via the G66 resolver, G147 pacing, ruling 1). Owner decisions: 90 days; stamp all Key Facts from old material. **Page quality (Rodrigo 2026-10-09: "seems like the markdowns of each page are too short and some saved facts are useless"; branch `fix/page-quality`).** Measured on the owner's clean run (296 conversations, real values redacted): 3,588 pages, 70% from one conversation (median 85 words, 3 facts); 2,139 of 16,578 facts phrased as what the assistant said, 386 about the conversation; median 10% (p75 22%) near-duplicate facts on 4+-conversation pages; 55 glued Summaries; 31 "not established" fallbacks beside rich facts; 667 "Undated background" bullets. **Causes (code):** the promotion rungs were proxies nearly every extraction cleared (confidence ≥ 0.75 + 200-char description, 2 history entries or 2 relationships; any model-drawn link to a 0.6 page) and ">3 exchanges" was never counted; a pending line kept only description/tags/history, so a name's first-conversation facts were gone when it promoted; same-batch fold and every merge deduped facts by exact text only and turned each losing summary into a fact; a distinct incoming orientation went to History as "Undated background"; an over-budget Summary fell back to "…not established"; with synthesis off the legacy whole-body call replaced the page body — its prompt described the old v1 layout and length rules (4-8 sentences), glued orientations, and the extraction's key facts never reached the page on that path (12 of 50 lost in the offline eval). **Shipped on the branch:** `promotion` counts exchanges and the person's own words on the transcript, an explicit link needs a person-located span, a resumed re-read is not a second conversation, pending lines carry the text and every conversation (folded and credited on promotion); `fact_policy` folds only an equal fact (never an existing item, never containment) and drops pure narration at Stage 1; displaced orientation becomes Key Facts, Summaries keep whole leading sentences and one introduction, the fallback names only what the thing is; the legacy rewrite contributes only Summary and new History; Stage 3 makes no call for a re-read; opt-in synthesis returns `restated`/`covered` lists (guarded) to fold restatements in other words at no extra call; Stage 1 prompt rewritten around the person's relation; `repair_page_quality` dry run (counts) and a lossless `--apply`. Offline eval (`benchmarks/system/page_quality.py`, synthetic): pages 7 → 4, one-conversation 4 → 1, facts lost 12 (off) / 1 (on) → 0, background bullets 5 / 11 → 0, near-duplicates (eval measure) 2 / 6 → 10 / 3 (owner ruling 2026-10-09 after two review rounds: the rule-based fold drops only an EQUAL fact after trivial normalization — case, accents, whitespace, wikilink brackets, trailing punctuation — never a contained one; paraphrases and more specific restatements fold only through opt-in synthesis; dev's lower off count is the 12 lost facts). Synthesis calls per 25-update batch (2,000 pages, fake engine): off 0 when Stage 1 gives no `description`, 50 when it does; on 50 (25 for facts-only). **Open / owner:** synthesis on or off; whether to archive one-conversation pages below the bar (counted, not built); near-duplicate facts already on pages (counted, never changed); the prompt's effect needs a paired real-model pilot. | PARTIAL · P1 |
| G195 | <a id="g195"></a>**Search every file by meaning — documents, pictures and video beside the memory, one index** (Rodrigo 2026-10-14: demos of a Spotlight-like file explorer "where all the files are in embeddings… results from videos, images, documents"; prior art github.com/ARahim3/DigUp, MIT; "borrowing DigUp's idea … so that it stays in the same index … between images, videos, documents, text, data … or maybe code, not") | **2026-10-28 (vision item 3, de-risking the meaning index):** an image vector is stamped with the model AND a measured fingerprint of its preprocessing (`vision_embedder.fingerprint`: a built-in reference picture through the real decoder — macOS's `sips`, whose resampling can move with an OS update — cut into the vision feed and hashed with the recipe; 29 ms once per process). The design index re-embeds a picture only when model or fingerprint changed; pre-stamp rows stay while the pipeline measures the pinned legacy fingerprint, so the stamp itself re-embedded nothing. `test_vision_fingerprint.py` pins the recipe and checks the live decoder. **Owner ruling 2026-10-14 (answers to the five questions):** (1) **searchable, promoted on use** — every indexed file is searchable at once; a file becomes memory only when the person or an agent opens it in a conversation, links it or saves it, and Sleep never reads the index; (2) **agents see hits only** — name, path, a snippet or page/timestamp and a short description; a whole file is opened only on the person's ask; the remote connector (G135) never sees file search; (3) **folders the person picks** (Documents, Desktop, Downloads, Pictures suggested), no Full Disk Access; (4) **documents and text, images and video** — code, audio and data files are out of the first scope; (5) defaults the owner may overrule: index in the background while idle and on power, re-reading only changed files; the macOS floor follows the runtime chosen (DigUp runs the image encoder on macOS 14 through llama.cpp; the Core ML export's floor must be measured, not assumed). **DigUp read 2026-10-14 (source, not run):** Swift app, llama.cpp + Metal, `embeddinggemma-2-GGUF` Q8_0 pinned by revision and sha256 (text 310 MB, image/audio encoder 555 MB; F16 avoided for overflow); one SQLite file with `files`, `segments` (768-d float16, L2-normalised, brute-force over an mmap copy) and FTS5. Per type: images thumbnailed to 1024 px (screenshots 1536 px) at 140/280 vision tokens, OCR feeds **words only** (an OCR vector made screenshots outrank better hits and was removed); PDF pages with ≥ 200 characters of text embed as text, others render at 1400 px and embed as an image; documents chunk at ~1,800 characters with 200 overlap; video samples a frame every 3 s (≤ 1,200), keeps one when an 8×8 average hash moves > 4 bits (~one per shot); long files index in steps (30 pages first). Every segment carries `loc`/`loc_end` (page, seconds, lines) so a hit opens at the place. Changes by FSEvents + size/mtime, moves by (device, inode) without re-embedding; a fingerprint of model + extraction settings plus a reference-vector test re-embeds only when vectors really change. Ranking: cosine, minus each modality's mean, divided by one shared deviation; a file qualifies at z ≥ 2.0 or ≥ half the query's words; score = z + 2·coverage^1.5 (+1 when the query names a kind); ID-like queries weigh words double — measured R@1 0.88 vs 0.73 for RRF. Mostly-digit text gets words, no vector; dataset-like folders (≥ 300 files, ≥ 70% machine-named) are skipped. **Borrow:** that ranking and its honest "nothing found"; OCR-as-words; the fingerprint + reference-vector test; frame dedup and `loc` on every hit; stepwise long files and the dataset detector. **Do differently (rails):** the **app** walks the picked folders, watches them and posts derivatives (thumbnails, page text/renders, OCR text, kept frames) — the backend never opens a path a request names; the index is disposable and sits beside the vectors/FTS outside every bank, never in git, the query never logged or cached; the encoders download only on the person's click (already the rule); track content by hash as well as mtime. Files and memories share one space so one query returns a PDF and the conversation about it; vectors from DigUp's GGUF Q8 build are not interchangeable with Cicada's Core ML fp16 vectors, so one runtime serves both or the file index is its own table with its own fingerprint. The vision lock and image embedder exist (#271, Looks). **Next:** a design note (runtime choice measured on this Mac: Core ML vs llama.cpp, speed, memory, macOS floor), then build behind the find palette's Files section and an agent tool that returns hits only. **UI frame shipped, indexing pending (2026-10-14, `feat/search-files`):** Settings → Search & files (*What Cicada can look through*) holds one folder list where each row says its depth in words — today only *Kept in memory* (G133) — plus *Understand what you mean* and *Understand images* as two consent clicks in plain words, the version picker behind Advanced; G195 adds the *Find things in it* depth, kinds per folder, "Reading N of M files" and the idle/on-power pause into that frame, and none renders until something computes it (`docs/architecture/app.md`, Search & files). **Pictures in the one index (vision item 3, 2026-10-28, `feat/vision-meaning-index`):** the pictures Cicada already holds — saved looks, the newest in-bank capture thumbnail per saved site, saved links' stored previews, saved and watched YouTube stills — are a fifth kind of `vector_index.db` (`image_index`), embedded by a background refresher outside Sleep in a picture child (`embed_worker.images`: nice 10, 4 math threads, a 2 GB self-checked ceiling), never a byte fetched for it, never a frame (G162). Text queries reach them through `/search?kinds=image` and recall's "Pictures that match" on local doors only. **DigUp's z-score measured and not borrowed for the floor:** on the real encoder a query about nothing pictured has a fifth of a relevant query's spread, so its best unrelated picture reaches a right picture's z (2.6 vs 2.2–3.3); the undivided lift over the kind's mean separates them (≤ 0.03 vs 0.10–0.15; 32/32 right, 0/30 shown for nothing on 400 synthetic pictures), and RRF is rejected for a merged list (a picture in the top 5 of every documents-only query, `benchmarks.perf.image_fusion`). Files from folders (the app walk), documents, video frames and audio are still to do; audio is a spike with a recommendation in the PR, not a build. **Audio and video decided (owner said go with the recommendation, 2026-10-28, through the coordinator):** speech is transcribed on the Mac by Apple's own on-device recogniser and its words embedded in the same space — no audio model; a video's frames are sampled from the local file the person picks (3 s, an 8 × 8 average hash per shot, a time per frame) into the picture kind. **Built (meaning index 2, `feat/meaning-index-2`):** the app reads a dropped recording or video (`MediaFileIndexer`), posts only its words and kept frames (`/files`), stored outside every bank (`media_files`); the words are the recording's, never the person's — never an episode, never read by Sleep, never counted for promotion; the `files` text kind and `source: frame` pictures in the one index, opt-in `kinds=file`, recall on local doors; Speech Recognition asked only by the person's drop (whether `SpeechAnalyzer` itself is gated was not tested — that raises the prompt); the design board's store is the `look_vectors` table of the one index, its old file folded in and deleted. Still to do: folders the person picks (the app walk), documents, a palette group (a design-direction D decision). | APPLY · P2 | |

G105's rule holds for every agent: capture comes from the agent's own documented hook, never from a model choosing to call a tool, and keeps only the person's turns and the agent's final reply. **The matrix** (docs checked 2026-10-14; full table in `capture-and-sleep.md`, *Capture from every agent*): Claude Code and Codex — `Stop` + transcript, captured (G105). **Cursor — done (feat/capture-everywhere):** `beforeSubmitPrompt.prompt` and `afterAgentResponse.text` carry the words, so `api/hooks/cursor.py` posts them to `POST /capture/turn` and `turn_capture` writes one episode per conversation through the Stop hook's own writer (same caps, scrub, sidecar, G104, demo rule); no transcript is read. **Gemini CLI — done:** `AfterAgent` carries `prompt` + `prompt_response`; `capture.py --harness gemini-cli` posts the exchange; registered by its setup prompt. Both are built from the documented payloads with synthetic fixtures — **unverified in the installed apps**; the `capture.log` line per firing is the check. **Windsurf** — `pre_user_prompt` / `post_cascade_response` with `trajectory_id` / `execution_id` are the same shape as Cursor: next, a registry for `~/.codeium/windsurf/hooks.json` and an adapter on `/capture/turn`. **GitHub Copilot CLI** — `userPromptSubmitted` carries the prompt but the reply only sits in `agentStop`'s `transcriptPath`, whose format is undocumented: needs a transcript reader under a proved root. **OpenCode** — JS/TS plugins only, payloads undocumented: needs a shipped plugin, not a command hook. **Hermes, OpenClaw** — no hooks documented. **The Claude app (desktop)** has no hooks at all: its chats reach memory only through what is saved during them or a later claude.ai data export (the importer handles it). Options there: the export on a schedule; a local cache the app keeps (only if readable and documented, and the app — never the backend — reads it); a standing instruction to save a summary is ruled out by G105 as the capture path. | G198 | <a id="g198"></a>**Trusted agents edit a page directly, with blame** (owner ruling 2026-10-14) | **The problem.** An agent could write and withdraw facts, add sources and save episodes, but only Sleep wrote a page's prose and aliases. Measured on the owner's bank (real values redacted): the owner's page carried 2 accented misspellings of the owner's full name among its 11 aliases, and 2 project pages carried the misspelling in their prose; an agent could record the right spelling as a fact but could not fix the text it was looking at. **The ruling (Rodrigo 2026-10-14):** "I like the safeguard ... but it would be nice to have a list of trusted models or agents that we give permission to write into the page directly"; "we also record what part they contributed, the same as ... a doc in Git: who changed what and at what time"; "the agent that's changing the page gives a reason through a commit message"; "the agent edits apply immediately. They don't have to wait for Sleep"; "these things can also be used to modify things in the inbox, because maybe a change can answer something that needed an answer in the inbox." **Built (branch `feat/agent-page-edits`):** `cicada_edit_page` (stdio and a proposed CLI row; never remote in this slice) — exact `{old, new}` words, one section, or aliases, with a required one-line reason that is the commit's `Reason:` line and cited quotes as `Evidence:` spans; the claims fence and its ledger are never touched. Trust is a list of local harness labels in `$CICADA_HOME/editors.json`, edited only from Settings (`PUT /settings/editors`); **amended (Rodrigo 2026-10-14): "Make it so that the default is that they apply immediately, at least for Claude Code and Codex"** — a list the person never changed (no `trusted` key; reading never writes the file) trusts `claude-code` and `codex`, and once they change it, removing both included, their list is the list; an unreadable file trusts nobody; Settings shows the defaults on, with a note naming them (branch `feat/editors-default`); `agent` and remote apps are never trustable, because a harness label is the only name Cicada can check (an MCP client discloses no model). An untrusted edit is a `page_edit` inbox proposal (one tap: apply or leave), chosen over a refusal because it keeps the agent's work and gives the person the decision (UX 3) and a graded verdict (G113). Sleep does not undo it: a word fix is pinned (`corrections`) and re-applied by every machine prose writer and told to the article writer; a rewritten lead or Article section marks the page the editor's (`human_edited`); a removed alias is remembered (`aliases_removed`) like `sources_removed`. Blame is `git blame` plus the commits' trailers, served per section by `GET /entities/{id}/blame`, never stored. An edit answers inbox items it names only with the person's own quoted words. **Open:** whether a remote app may ever edit; pins are exact strings, so a misspelling the writer inflects differently (a possessive, a case change) is not caught; the trust list is a policy on a self-reported label, not authentication. | APPLY · P1 · built, awaiting review |

No hostname, address or home path is hardcoded (env-var config only). **2026-10-15 — phase 1 built (`feat/headless-server`), [research](../research/2026-10-15-headless-server.md), [area doc](../architecture/headless.md):** options compared (git push/pull and Syncthing break one-git-writer and the id rule; the Mac as primary keeps Sleep stopping with the lid); the primary-writer server is the choice, in four phases. Phase 1: `install.sh` on Linux installs a systemd user unit (`scripts/install-backend-service.sh`, loopback only, no credential created, lingering checked never changed), `doctor.sh` checks it, the `sw_vers` crash under `pipefail` is fixed, and a bank on a machine with no git identity commits as a repo-local `Cicada <cicada@localhost>` (measured: a fresh server could not commit at all). Next, phase 2 (owner decisions first): per-device tokens, `CICADA_API_BASE` for hooks/MCP/CLI, a turn-posting capture route for a harness on another machine (amends the transcript rail), a client spool, the CLI's root check by bank id. Phase 3 is the app's server setting (Swift). **2026-10-16 — phase 2 built (`feat/headless-clients`), owner rulings in TODO ruling 28:** per-device tokens (named, hashed, revocable, issued only by the person on the server; `/devices` master-only); `CICADA_API_BASE` read by `runtime_layout.backend_url()`, the hooks, the stdio MCP server and the CLI (https, or http to a loopback tunnel; unset = unchanged); the Stop hook on a client reads its session with Cicada's own extractor and posts capped turns to `/capture/session` (re-checked and written by the same writer, ids minted on the server, `superseded` by the client's byte counts), spooling under `$CICADA_HOME` when the server is away; the stdio MCP server forwards every tool to `POST /tools/call`, which runs the same bodies as a local stdio call (a device counts as local for G198; no caller path is ever opened; repo git probed on the client); the CLI pins the server's bank by name and `id` (`banks.yaml` ids, `/healthz` `bankIds`, `X-Cicada-Bank-Id`), ruling 21a now compares ids. Measured end to end with two real backends on two loopback ports. Open after phase 2: skills tools act on the server's agent folders; a client command that reads the memory folder in-process refuses rather than reading over HTTP. **2026-10-16 — phase 3 built (`feat/headless-app`):** Settings → General → *Where your memory lives* (This Mac or a server: address + device token, checked against `/healthz` and `/devices` before anything is written, then `$CICADA_HOME/api_base` + `device_token`, used from the next launch — the hooks, stdio MCP and CLI read the same file); on a server the app runs no backend, its Store cache, picture stores and every reader cursor are per connection, the readers' posts wait in an outbox (`$CICADA_HOME/spool/app`) flushed on reconnect, and the demo stays local. Owner ask (2026-10-16): "a page in settings … where I can see the synced devices … whether the memory comes from one server or another" → Settings → Devices (connection reachable as of when, devices added/last seen, add with the token shown once and revoke on This Mac; `GET /devices` now readable by a device, marked `current`), and every new episode a device posts records `device` (`device_context`), shown as one word on Saved rows, the Reader and the entity card's conversations; the bank picker names the server. Measured: a Swift client against two real loopback listeners talks only to the server it was given and flushes its waiting post there. Open: switching needs a restart; a claim an agent wrote with no conversation shows its contributor, not a device (no device trailer on commits)2026-10-16 — the packages (`feat/install-anywhere`; owner: "install it with a `curl` command on a Linux machine … can I use Brew for that? If not make it so"):** (1) `scripts/install-server.sh`, one `curl | bash` line on Linux: prerequisites (git, curl, uv, a reachable systemd user manager) named together with their fixes; the newest `vX.Y.Z` tag (CI tags only on publish) or `CICADA_REF` (branch/tag/commit, validated); cloned to `~/.local/share/cicada`; then the checkout's own `install.sh --cli`, so there is still one installer. Same ref = a no-op (the backend keeps its PID); a new ref re-syncs and restarts the unit; `--uninstall` removes unit, hooks, command and code, keeps the memory and `~/.cicada`; never root, never lingering, never a credential (it says where the backend's token lives). (2) `Formula/cicada.rb`, this repo as its own tap: backend + CLI + MCP in a venv on `python@3.12`; `cicada-setup` runs `install.sh` on Homebrew's opt path so hooks, MCP entry, plist and unit survive `brew upgrade`, and restarts only a service that runs that install; **no `service do` block** — `install-backend-agent.sh`/`install-backend-service.sh` stay the one source of the service; settings in `etc/cicada/api.env`, logs in `var/log/cicada`; the url's tag is a version stamp (`check_version.py agree`, `release.sh bump`). No cask: the zip is not notarized and a cask download is quarantined, so Gatekeeper would block it — a cask follows notarization. (3) Both build the venv with `scripts/sync-server-env.sh`: the lock minus sentence-transformers' subtree, the release app's set — measured 338 MB on Linux arm64; the full lock pulls torch plus ~15 CUDA wheels on any Linux (`sys_platform == 'linux'` markers, no arch filter). `fetch-embedding-model.sh` uses `sha256sum` and `~/.cache` on Linux (it needed `shasum` and wrote `~/Library` there). Proven in Docker on real systemd: ubuntu 24.04 and fedora 41 (install, loopback-only listener read from `/proc/net/tcp`, token never printed, re-run no-op, update restarts, uninstall), and Linuxbrew (`brew style`, `brew audit --new --strict`, install, `brew test`, `cicada-setup`, upgrade, uninstall). A macOS `brew install` was not run (this Mac's prefix is off limits). Known limit: the backend resolves its repo path, so the app's Agents panel against a Homebrew backend would name the Cellar path; `cicada-setup` is the way to connect agents there. **Website/access follow-up (2026-10-06):** On supported package/service installation, add verified server install/upgrade commands and harness access instructions to the website through G182/G158. Prove remote/local clients can read consolidated memory and contribute with scoped authentication; do not equate a running backend with a configured harness. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-release-and-integration-acceptance.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Python backend can run from source, not a supported app-free server package/service/install lifecycle. Ship one-primary-writer macOS/Linux service with install/upgrade/smoke checks; choose brew/curl/container packaging after CLI. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g181). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests a package that runs without SwiftUI on a server and easy brew/curl or comparable installation. Separate portable core/CLI from macOS readers and app lifecycle; define bank/config/secrets locations, authentication, engine options, bundled/downloaded embeddings, service start/upgrade/backup/uninstall. First target a reproducible Linux/macOS headless install and one server bank with many authenticated clients. Choose one reliable primary install route before supporting brew, curl and containers in parallel. Current pyproject and POSIX locks are not proof of an installable server package. G187 owns Windows feasibility; G132 owns replicated/offline sync, G16 multi-person permissions. No multi-active git writers. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Today** one Mac runs everything (the launchd backend, the app, the stdio MCP); G135 lets cloud apps reach this Mac's memory through a tunnel the person runs, but only while the Mac is awake, and Sleep stops when the lid closes. **Install:** `brew install` from a tap (`<owner>/homebrew-cicada`; Homebrew also runs on Linux) puts the backend, the CLI (G180) and the MCP server on the machine, and `brew services start cicada` keeps it running (launchd on macOS, systemd on Linux); a Docker image for VPS hosts. Headless: no app, no `~/Library` reads. **Sync — one writer, not two banks.** The bank is a git repo, but two full stacks pushing and pulling one bank would break "one git writer per bank" and give Sleep two minds. Shape: **the server is the bank's only writer; the laptop app — and its capture hooks and its browser/Notes/folder readers — becomes a client of the server's API** over an authenticated connection (bearer auth exists, G50; G135's capability tokens), with an offline queue on the laptop that flushes on reconnect; the app's Store and SSE sync already run over HTTP. A bank on this Mac stays the default. **Remote agents** on the server or anywhere contribute through the CLI or the MCP, each with its own harness identity in the provenance. **Sleep on the server** runs on its schedule without interruption — and **ruling 4 still binds**: a scheduled run never uses a Claude or ChatGPT plan, so the server needs a metered key or a local model (a run the person starts may use a plan). **Rails kept:** the app-reads-`~/Library` rail stays on the laptop (readers post bytes to the server); the server keeps its own `secrets.env`; the network gates and the ToS rail apply on the server; the remote listener serves only the API/MCP, behind auth. **Hosted Cicada (G92's question):** server mode is its self-hosted form; a hosted service would run one isolated server per person — their own bank directory and git repo, so the filesystem rule survives — and G92 says holding people's memory is a different product to decide deliberately. **To decide:** API client vs git sync for the laptop; the transport (an encrypted overlay like G132's rail, or public TLS); how the app picks "this Mac" or "a server" (Settings → General); backups; several laptops on one server. Relates **G92**, **G135**, **G132**, **G50**, **G76**, **G88**, **G180**, **G182**. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: push-only backup is separately bounded from server mode/two-way bank sync. Primary writer pushes committed snapshots under the git lock to the person's configured destination; no pull, automatic merge or force-push. Report divergence/freshness without blocking capture. Privacy/encryption and deliberate restore remain G92/G132 decisions. No remote creation or push is authorized by the research note.** **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → APPLY in slices · app-free package, P1. | UNBUILT · P2 |
