| G135 | <a id="g135"></a>**Remote connector — any AI app, and the phone, can use this Mac's memory** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "we should have a good connector working for all ai apps like claude, gemini, etc, ideally for phone too") | **Portability follow-up (owner 2026-10-06):** G110 requires project-context continuation in Grok/other supported remote clients as well as local coding harnesses. Deliver the latest selected project/task context through permitted initialize/read surfaces without a manually composed handoff; validate current account/client support before promising automatic behavior. Restricted sources/workspace access, ambiguous project selection and unavailable delivery must be explicit. A remote connection alone is not full context, automatic capture or a transferred native thread. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Separate off-by-default remote MCP listener with hashed scoped expiring tokens exists. Establish desktop/phone compatibility and write races/scopes in actual supported clients; connection is not automatic capture. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g135). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The remote MCP listener and scoped capability tokens are implemented. Verify current support and setup for each named desktop/iPhone client during G10; do not promise all phone apps accept connectors. Test read, claim write, source restrictions, revocation/expiry and cross-bank isolation. A query-capable connector does not imply automatic session capture. S3/S4 and disclosed remote/local write races remain separate work with G177/G183. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The problem.** A cloud AI app (claude.ai on the web and the phone, ChatGPT developer mode, Perplexity) can only reach a *public HTTPS URL*; it cannot launch a local process. Cicada's only agent door was the stdio MCP server, which talks to the backend over loopback, and `ConnectView` told the person a hosted connector was possible future work. So every conversation outside a local harness on this Mac was invisible to memory, and memory to it. **The exposure class is new** (round-3 spec, Decision 4). G132's rail was "loopback unless behind an encrypted overlay". This row adds a public door under stricter terms: a **separate listener** that serves only MCP and none of the FastAPI routers; **per-connector capability tokens** — hashed at rest, shown once, scoped, expiring, revocable; tools outside a connector's scopes absent from `tools/list`; **no delete tools**; **off by default**; any `Origin` header refused; and **Cicada never opening a tunnel itself** — the person runs Tailscale Funnel or ngrok, Cicada only detects it (R-R1, R-R3, R-R17, R-R18, R-R32). **Owner review of this class is the one decision taken without the owner.** **What shipped (S0–S2, `feat/remote-connector`; the rulings live in the Track R plan, `docs/plans/2026-09-23-remote-connector.md`).** (1) One SSRF rule, `net_guard`, on every server-side fetch of a URL someone else chose — `save_url`, the link backfill, logos — judged by `is_global`, which also closes a tailnet (`100.64/10`) gap in G59's own guard (R-R10). (2) Agent writes name their author and commit alone: a stdio claim used to be stamped with a placeholder author and never committed, so the next `git add -A` writer swept it under its own trailer (the G85 smear, locally); it now commits by itself as `Cicada-Author: <harness>`. The `_commit_media` single-save bug found while planning — every one-link save raised a `TypeError` and was never committed — is fixed in the same slice (R-R11, R-R12, R-R13). (3) One tool implementation, `mcp_tools` behind a `ToolContext`, shared by both servers and proven byte-identical on stdio by a golden fixture (R-R2, R-R14). (4) An explicit remote primer, chosen by the caller and never by a client name (R-R15). (5) The runtime: scopes, server-minted conversation handles, provenance, fenced read replies, writes that wait for Sleep, limits and an ids-only ledger row per call (R-R22…R-R29, R-R36). (6) The door: tokens, the secret link and the bearer header on one verifier, the embedded listener without an access log, reach detection and the loopback management API (R-R16…R-R21, R-R30…R-R33). (7) The app page: Settings → Agents → *On this Mac \| From anywhere* (R-R7, R-R34, R-R35). **Corrections to the research the build rests on** (each changed a task): **eight** test files collided with the `mcp` SDK's package name, not two; an MCP claim was stamped with the literal `"mcp-agentic-write"` placeholder, not the Sleep model — the real defect was that it was never committed; and uvicorn's stock access log writes a secret link's path — which IS the token — to the backend log, so the listener runs a quiet protocol per connection rather than any process-global logging switch. **What stays open.** **S3:** OAuth "approve on your Mac" (client-ID metadata documents, a dynamic-registration fallback, step-up for `record`), which takes the secret out of the URL. **S4:** the read mirror on G132's always-on box. The **live check from claude.ai and from the Claude phone app** — owner-present, and it needs a tunnel the owner runs. ChatGPT and Perplexity on the phone (unverified in vendor docs; the page says so). A dedicated `harness` contributor kind (R-R26) — done in F2-back (R-B9). Per-connector activity (R-R35, via G118 slice 2's viewer). Bank pinning per connector (R-R30). DNS-rebinding pinning for the fetchers (R-R10). The `answer` scope's verdicts commit as `user` without naming the connector (R-R35's note; G116(b) decides whether a session trailer rides). **Round 4 C8 (2026-09-24):** Grok is a link app (R-AG7 — it runs on xAI's servers and registers a server from a chat message); Settings → Agents opens the New connector sheet preselected for Claude, ChatGPT or Grok, the link still shown once in that sheet (R-AG18); an agent's live ✓ reads a connector's `last_used_at`, touched by every authenticated request, so it lights on the app's first `initialize` and still answers with telemetry off (R-AG5). → answers **G132** fork (b) for this path (per-connector tokens) and fork (e) (live HTTP recall); serves **G76** (the "other harnesses" story) and **G91** (a phone read path without an iOS build); depends on **G75** R12 (a primer never names an argument the schema rejects — now per scope set), **G48** (conversation identity; a remote handle is never resumable), **G118** (evidence spans on remote claims). **APPLY** S3 after the owner reviews the exposure class — L, $0 (no LLM on this path; `cicada_ask` spends the person's plan and is opt-in). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · remote implementation exists; client compatibility unverified. **Ruling 32 (owner 2026-10-19):** sign-in — the listener is its own OAuth 2.1 authorization server (DCR + CIMD, S256, consent only in the app, rotating refresh tokens mapped to connector rows; `api/remote/oauth.py`) — so claude.ai (incl. its phone apps) and ChatGPT add Cicada as a custom connector; the person funnels this listener only, never the API; and `cicada_capture_turns` captures hookless apps every turn, stored as relayed (`evidence_kind: assistant`) and upgraded in place by the app's export. Unverified against the live apps until the owner connects them. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** **Phones through the AI apps** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22: "see how other locally run services can connect to phone apps, so that my phone can also use cicada through claude app or chatgpt or even gemini if possible"). Research (agents-api-phone §3, secondary for Gemini): none of the three adds a connector on the phone — Claude adds it on the web and it appears on iOS and Android; ChatGPT's developer mode is a web feature; Gemini reportedly accepts a custom remote MCP URL on the web since June 2026 and shows it on mobile. All call from the vendor's cloud, so the URL must be public (Tailscale Funnel, ngrok) — a Serve or tailnet address cannot work. **Proposal:** the Agents page's Claude, ChatGPT and Gemini rows copy the public connector URL and open that app's web connectors page; the person types the code (ruling 32); the app detects Funnel vs Serve and says "Serve is private to your devices; the apps need Funnel". Gemini joins the remote set once verified first-hand. The phone's own capture app is G91. The listener also serves G213's HTTP API behind the same tokens (owner 2026-10-22, ruling 36 D3). **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed; the redesign briefs cite the phone half as G214. | PARTIAL · P2 |
| G92 | <a id="g92"></a>**Onboarding at scale — decide what Cicada IS as a product before optimizing the funnel** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "think how the deployment to onboard users at scale would work") | **Onboarding v2 (2026-10-22, PR #433, ruling 36):** five steps of one verb — Welcome, Intelligence (a signed-in plan said in words, a key one click away), Agents (found on this machine, Connect or Copy setup prompt, green on first call), Sources (a tick grid that does what the Sources page does), Done (what happens next, never a promise the schedule does not keep); words on screen per step (Vision OCR of the light screenshots, first screen): Welcome 26, Intelligence 38, Agents 51, Sources 41, Done 35, against the seven steps' 51, 135, 137, 79, 183, 184 and 50 (Agents and Sources go past 40 by their row and tile names). Still open: Home's Getting started repeats the agent and chat-export rows a person just saw, and a fresh install's schedule is manual, so nothing consolidates until Read now (owner's call). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Local setup/exports and remote scoped tokens exist. Hosted ownership, backup versus sharing, restoration and selective evidence disclosure remain product/security decisions before broad deployment. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g92). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Keep export, full-bank backup, device sync and selective sharing distinct. Public releases contain no bank or credentials. Before any private-remote backup or multi-user hosting, settle destination disclosure, transport/at-rest encryption and restoration semantics. G16 adds scoped contributor permissions; G132 owns replicated-client freshness. No upload is part of this documentation change. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **The architecture already makes one strategic choice, and it is unusually good: compute is BYO.** G74(a) runs consolidation through the *user's own* Claude subscription, and embeddings are on-device (EmbeddingGemma). So marginal inference cost per user is **≈ zero** — no GPU bill, no per-token exposure, no rate-limit pooling. Almost no memory product can say that, and it should be treated as the core of any distribution plan rather than an implementation detail. **The tension:** every other design decision is deliberately single-user and local — filesystem as the single source of truth, one git repo per bank, no database, secrets in `~/.cicada`, an MCP server registered per machine, a launchd backend. **A hosted multi-tenant Cicada is a different product**, not a deployment of this one: it would break the storage decision, and it means holding other people's episodic memory on your infrastructure — the highest-sensitivity data class there is, with the correspondingly worst breach story. **Three honest routes, pick deliberately:** (a) **Local-first distribution** — signed + notarized `.dmg` (Apple Developer Program, notarization, an update channel), G76's paste-prompt install for the developer audience, everything stays on the user's machine. Cheapest, keeps every architectural claim intact, ceiling is "power users who can install things". (b) **Local-first + optional hosted sync** — E2E-encrypted bank sync/backup and a mobile client that reads it; the server never holds plaintext, so the storage decision survives. This is the only route where a subscription is honest, because you would be charging for *convenience and continuity*, not for inference the user already pays for. (c) **Hosted** — abandons the thesis claim; note it and move on unless the goal changes. **The real funnel is permissions, not marketing** — each of these is a drop-off point and all are already known: Gatekeeper on an unsigned app (**G88**), TCC prompts for Contacts (**G81**) and Notes, per-client MCP registration (**G76**), and a Claude/Codex login the user must already have. Instrument nothing until the route is chosen; the install is the funnel. **Prerequisites the 2026-09-01 portability sweep surfaced, neither named elsewhere:** the human observer id is the literal `rodrigo` (`agentic_write.py:325,:331-333`; no `CICADA_USER`/owner setting exists in `config.py`), so a second user's manual claims are silently NOT `user_stated`; and a bank has no manifest, schema version, or export/import-as-archive (`bank_registry.py`/`routers/banks.py` → 0 hits for `manifest\|schema_version`; `POST /banks/{name}/import` imports a chat export, not a bank), while `repos:` stores tilde paths + `socket.gethostname()` device ids with no remap table (`local_refs.py:64-87`) — a bank moved to another machine keeps its pages but loses live repo resolution. The shareable artifact that needs none of this is a compiled skill bundle (**G112**). → depends on **G74(a)** (BYO compute is the premise), **G88**, **G76**; constrains **G81**, **G91**; the D1 bank-export line of `context-passport-roadmap.md` is where the manifest belongs. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: G132/G181's push-only backup does not settle disclosure. A private hosted git remote is not E2E encryption and may hold complete history. Decide owner-controlled storage vs explicitly accepted plaintext hosting vs encrypted archive before implementation/upload; retain this row's product/privacy decisions.** **Previous execution label (historical):** DECIDE / APPLY · privacy boundaries for release, hosting and sharing. | DECISION · P2 |
| G143 | <a id="g143"></a>**Open at login and a background service the app can install** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, round-4 brief, paraphrased: everything should keep syncing while the app is open or in the background, so Cicada must be allowed to run in the background) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Login-item and launchd backend install/status/quiet launch exist. A signed downloaded build's persistence and retiring the old developer login path still require release acceptance. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g143). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Launch-at-login and launchd setup are implemented. Transfer clean downloaded-build/start/restart/uninstall acceptance to G182, and server service management to G181. Do not repeat the old macOS startup implementation; investigate only gaps confirmed on current builds. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What was there (verified at `ecb59c7`).** The only login-item path was `make login-item`'s System Events script (`app/CicadaApp/login_item.sh`); the app imported no `ServiceManagement`. The backend's LaunchAgent plist lived only inside `install.sh` step 6, so an app-only install had no way to get one. `BackendProcess` spawned the venv's `uvicorn` console script through `/usr/bin/env` — the shebang that breaks when the repo moves, which `install.sh`'s own note forbids for the plist. **What shipped (round-4 D3).** `LoginItemService` over `SMAppService.mainApp` behind a seam; it remembers what the person asked for, so an ad-hoc-signed build whose status never reaches `.enabled` says macOS did not keep it rather than showing "off". `scripts/install-backend-agent.sh` is the one source of the plist (idempotent: rewrite, boot out, bootstrap with three tries); `install.sh` calls it behind its healthy-skip guard; the app runs it from Settings → General after the click, argv pinned to its own checkout, `CICADA_CAPTURE=off`, then stops only the child uvicorn it spawned so launchd's KeepAlive binds the port. Status is a read-only `launchctl print`. `BackendProcess` spawns `python -m uvicorn`. **Answered (round 4 phase B, R-OB18):** Cicada starts quietly at login — a launch macOS makes as a login item opens no window; the menu-bar bookworm, the watchers and the sync engine run, the Dock icon stays, and a Dock click, the menu bar's *Open Cicada*, a reminder tap or a file open brings the window through `AppRouter.showMainWindow()`. `LaunchKind.from(event:)` reads the 'oapp' event in `applicationDidFinishLaunching` (the only moment it is current); an unknown launch is never quiet, and a window the person asked for is never closed. F-06 and Settings → General say it ("Starts quietly: no window, just the bookworm in the menu bar", or "…just Cicada in the Dock" with the menu bar off). The live check needs no logout: a `-CicadaLaunchKind loginItem` launch argument (read from the process arguments, never defaults) can only make a launch quieter, so the orchestrator can exercise the quiet path on the installed build and a plain launch is unchanged. **Open:** a signed build to confirm the login item persists; retiring `make login-item` once it does. → relates **G88**, **G76**, **G117**. **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · app startup exists; release lifecycle verification remains. | PARTIAL · P2 |
| G138 | <a id="g138"></a>**Recommended skills — a reviewed catalog of agent skills, installed by the agent's own installer after consent** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "having recommended skills to install, for example … claude-video that supposedly allows claude to see videos"; "could match well for storing and having info about videos") | **2026-10-15 — the Skills page (`feat/skills-page`; owner: "check for skills like macos-harness and browser-harness, see them linked to a GitHub repo, and detect if the skill is outdated").** Amends R-O24/R-O26: the backend now installs, through one writer (`skill_store`), only on the person's route or person-only command, pinned (a previewed commit, or a catalog tool via `uv tool install git+<repo>@<sha>`), never an MCP tool; the app writes no skill folder (`SkillInstaller` and the consent sheet removed). `GET /skills` reads every installed agent's folder (one list, `agent_wiring.AGENTS`: Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, OpenCode, Hermes, OpenClaw, and Gemini CLI shown only when installed; plus the shared `~/.agents/skills`; folders from each agent's docs), names each source (Cicada's marker, the `npx skills` lock, the catalog, or local), checks upstream on a click (commits that touched the skill's folder, or newer releases, each listed), and catches a tool skill whose text is older than its tool (a `uv tool upgrade` does not rewrite SKILL.md: the marker's tool version, or the text the installed tool prints). Cicada imitates `npx skills` (same folders and spellings, pinned, previewed, copies not links, no Node or telemetry) and reads its lock. Onboarding suggests browser-harness and macos-harness ticked, and the video skill (`bradautomates/claude-video`, `watch`; it downloads the video and since v0.3 can send it to Google with a key, so it stays at the reviewed v0.2.0 and off by default). The macos-harness pin moved to v0.2.1 (verified by `scripts/verify-skills.sh`). The page follows the owner's third take (a skills × agents table, one status word, an Update chip with the changes, a side column, tabs Installed · Discover · Drafts, onboarding as three switch tiles). Settled by the owner the same day: the tenth page has no ⌘ digit (DESIGN_RULES ruling 2026-10-15 amends DR-22); permissions are labelled "Checked for Cicada" with a line that another app may differ; Remove never deletes a skill's only text (it goes to `removed/`); nothing is written for an agent that isn't installed; a saved onboarding answer is where a later run starts. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Reviewed catalog and consent/installer handoff exist, including watch/browser/macOS recommendations. Verify clean installation/role selection; bundling third-party code and complete lifecycle inventory are different scopes. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g138). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Reviewed recommendations and installer routes already exist in api/data/recommended_skills.json and skill_catalog.py, including browser-harness and macos-harness. Validate install/detection and reading/watching handoff on clean supported harnesses. Ship recommendations or license-permitted artifacts with clear prerequisites and opt-in installation, not arbitrary executable bundles. Video capability is G162/G166. A server does not acquire a person’s desktop/browser permissions; the local agent performs those actions. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why a catalog:** Claude Code loads every installed skill's description into context (~1% of the window, ≤ 1,536 characters each), so the right number to recommend is few, reviewed, and ones that feed Cicada. **What ships (Track O, server half — Task 7):** `api/data/recommended_skills.json` — nine entries (watch, paper-lookup, the Wispr Flow and Granola MCP servers, skill-creator, the arXiv MCP server, Anthropic's pdf, literature-review, OpenAI's transcribe), each with its source, licence, the reviewed commit and SKILL.md hash, what it needs, which agents, a terms note (YouTube's for `watch`, with "Cicada itself never downloads video"), and the Cicada tool it bridges; `scripts/verify-skills.sh` re-checks them (every page, commit and SKILL.md hash OK on 2026-09-23). `GET /skills/recommended` returns at most five not-installed entries by rank plus the installed ones; state is derived per request from `SKILL.md` files and Claude Code plugin ids — no agent config is opened, MCP entries read `unknown`. Installers follow upstream per agent (Claude Code plugins, `npx skills add` pinned to the reviewed commit, `claude/codex mcp add`); hosted MCP servers are copy-only (their OAuth is the agent's). The backend never installs anything. The handshake gains at most one line per installed, active bridge (`papers → cicada_save_url(url)` today; `CONTRACT_VERSION` 4 — the branch's 3 collided with G140's 3 at the merge of `dev`, so it moved one past both; remote stays at 2, since a remote connection carries no bridge — and the stdio cache key carries the bridge set's hash beside G140's timezone; R12 holds every bridge line against the stdio schema). **Role skills (2026-09-30, G166):** entries may carry `roles` (`reading` \| `watching`), an `invoke` name, a `pageName`, a catalog-authored `pageSummary` and a `reach` line, and the install method **`agent-prompt`** (copy-only: a reviewed text for the person's own agent to run upstream's installer, so a skill whose CLI matters is never half-installed); `browser-harness` and `macos-harness` join at ranks 10 and 11 (pinned, SKILL.md hashes verified by `scripts/verify-skills.sh`, whose `--print-urls` and root-path join are fixed) and are chosen in Settings → Reading the web, How your agent reads (`agent_methods`), not on this page. Rulings R-O23…R-O29 in `docs/plans/2026-09-23-settings-v3.md`. **App half (Task 8):** Settings → Skills — Cicada's own `cicada` and `cicada-librarian` written by `SkillInstaller` into `~/.claude/skills/` or `~/.agents/skills/` with a `.cicada-managed.json` marker (never over a copy that no longer matches it; removes only what it wrote); at most five recommended cards (a client-side cap too) opening a detail sub-page; a consent sheet showing the exact resolved command, the Install button waiting on a checkbox; the installer runs as an argv (never a shell), allowlisted to `claude`/`codex`/`npx`, `CICADA_CAPTURE=off` forced, stdin closed, 300 s cap, terminated on cancel. A lint holds the skill-folder paths to that one file. **Bridges (F2-back R-B14):** `watch → cicada_record_watch` and meetings → `cicada_save_episode` (one `speaker:<name>:` line per utterance, never `user:`) are active; documents stays off — no evidence kind names a document's author, and an unmarked episode reads as the owner's. **Not verified:** that re-adding an already-added Claude Code marketplace exits non-zero (the step tolerates either); the `installed_plugins.json` shape beyond its top-level keys (two accepted, anything else reads as not installed). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · recommended catalog implemented; fresh-install checks remain. **2026-10-23:** **G220** — the owner's "show me" skill (heyclicky- and Hoff-style on-screen walkthroughs) — is a catalog candidate once public; its review checks that the overlay never captures and that consent comes first. | PARTIAL · P2 |

But for now i want to use the macos-harness, the browser-harness and claude-video to watch videos, as selections in settings, amongst the other default options models can use through their harnesses.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Catalog role/method selection exists; arbitrary Add/import of user skills and validation are not implemented by that selector. Build BYO without bypassing consent, role contracts or provenance. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g178). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 wants useful skills to come with Cicada. G138 owns reviewed recommendations; this row owns adding/importing an existing user skill, choosing reading/watching roles, validation, permissions and removal. Keep imported installed skills separate from automatically learned G112/G55 procedures. Do not auto-execute an imported skill or make desktop-only tools server dependencies. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Now:** `agent_methods.JOBS` is a closed table (`reading` and `watching`, both shipped 2026-09-30; the person picks `watch`, `browser-harness` or `macos-harness` for watching from the catalog, one choice per job) and the skills a job offers are catalog entries with a reviewed pin (G138). The choice is a standing instruction to the person's own agent, never authority, so it changes what Cicada *says*, not what Cicada *does*. **The ask:** a person can add a skill of their own (a folder with a `SKILL.md`, a git URL, or a name their agent already has), tell Cicada which jobs it serves, and pick it in the same "How your agent reads / watches" selections, with a page in the graph tagged `agent-skill` like the shipped ones. **Constraints any design must keep:** (1) the backend never installs or runs anything and reads no agent config beyond the `SKILL.md` presence check (R-O24, R-O26, R-O27); (2) a person-added skill has no reviewed commit or hash, so it is shown as "added by you", never as reviewed, and its terms are the person's to read; (3) the clause names the skill only as data the person typed (the neutral-copy lint scans templates, not data); (4) a "job" the person invents needs a tool contract (what the agent should call to record the result), so v1 is *which skill*, not *which new job*; (5) the page it gets is created by the person's own action (`Cicada-Author: user`), not imported silently from a folder scan; (6) no personal data in the repo: the example is a placeholder skill name, real values redacted. **Open questions (DECIDE):** where the list lives (`$CICADA_HOME`, machine-wide, vs a bank file, since a skill folder is machine-specific); whether a person-added skill may also serve as a *bridge* (record tools) or only as a method; import from Claude Code's or Codex's own skill folders (a listing of names only) vs a typed name; per-agent choice (a skill installed for one harness only). The first half (the three named skills as selections for watching) shipped 2026-09-30 on `feat/video-watch-method`. Relates **G138** (catalog, consent sheet), **G166** (reading), **G162** (video queue), **G112** (compiling one's own skills; the reverse direction). **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY / DESIGN · explicit user skill import, P2. | UNBUILT · P2 |

Drawing needs no Screen Recording. **Rails a review checks:** installed only on consent, pinned to what the person saw, one writer, never hooks or settings (the skills rail, G138/G178); consent first, naming the app it reads and saying it will not click; **the overlay never captures**, and sets `sharingType = .none` so it appears in no capture, G218's traces included; the screen is read only inside a session the person started, the overlay its indicator, Esc ends it; it shows and never clicks unless the person switches to "do it for me"; nothing it sees enters Cicada unless the person asks to save it; Reduce Motion places instead of gliding; provider-neutral copy; it runs in any harness on any plan (WikiSkill's bar). **Where it meets Cicada:** a walkthrough asked for twice is a G112 skill candidate through the existing hold; live show-me could replace G64's zoomed-cursor import videos. **Owner decisions (recommended answer):** its own public repository, listed in G138's catalog once reviewed; Cicada remembers a walkthrough only when asked. **Slices (Cicada's part):** a catalog entry once public S · a G64 note S. | G112 | <a id="g112"></a>**Portable skills — compile procedural memory into SKILL.md bundles anyone can load on their own plan** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, on WikiSkill: "relevant to the capture of skill learning and make it as portable as possible so I can have other people use it on their own with their ai plans and stuff"; the Era-of-Experience framing now in CLAUDE.md) | **2026-10-15 — agents draft skills, held (`feat/skills-page`).** `cicada_create_skill` (and `cicada skills create`) writes an agent's SKILL.md to `$CICADA_HOME/skills/drafts/`, never an agent's folder; the Skills page lists it under "Made by your agents" with its author, and the person publishes it to chosen agents with one click (`skill_store.publish_draft`, origin `draft` in the marker). The page also lists what Sleep noticed (this bank's `skill_hold` lines and skill pages, read-only). Still open: the deterministic compiler from a skill page to a portable bundle (design (2)–(4) below). **2026-10-09 — three owner rulings (Rodrigo 2026-10-09), applied on `fix/skill-rules`.** (a) *A skill needs two or more conversations before it gets a page* — the entity-promotion bar; before, one conversation was enough when the skill's named entities came up in it. Built: a skill grounded on one conversation is held in its own store (`pending_skills.jsonl`, `skill_hold`, keyed by the page id it would get, with that conversation, its description, confidence and evidence pages; written by `skill_grounding.settle` in Stage 5), and a later batch that grounds it on another conversation creates the page citing both, joins the held evidence pages, and removes the line. Lines never expire, so batch 3 and batch 9 meet; an existing skill page is still updated on one conversation, and a held conversation joins a skill page that appeared meanwhile. **Not Stage 2's pending store** — the first attempt shared it and review blocked it: that store is keyed by name and Stage 2 promotes any line a Stage-1 entity repeats under the entity's type, so a held skill became a concept page and lost its first conversation, and a skill create taking a same-named Stage-1 line dropped that line's history, description and tags. Separate key spaces cannot consume each other's lines; the cost is that a Stage-1 mention of the same name does not count toward a skill's bar (a name heard is not the pattern seen) and claims about a held skill's name are not held with it. (b) *Skill pages with no traceable conversation are archived* (`status: archived`, never deleted). After the #251 repair the owner's bank still had 44 skill pages with `source_episodes: []` (counted `skill_no_evidence`). Built as `repair_skills_aliases --archive-unsourced`, off by default, same rails as the rest of the tool (dry run by default, refused while Sleep runs, dirty pages skipped, one `cicada` commit). A page is archived only when git proves the old Stage-4 writer made it and only Sleep's decay touched it since: added by a Sleep cycle commit with that writer's exact frontmatter, every later commit a Sleep commit with Sleep's own `sleep/decay` line for it, and today's page differs from the added one only in `confidence`/`status`/`decayed_through` with the body unchanged. Anything else is counted `skill_archive_unproven` and kept; no inbox item, and decay skips archived pages. (c) *No contradiction check on skill updates* — no extra model call; nothing to build. **2026-10-08 — design (1) grounding shipped without a model (`fix/skill-provenance`).** Measured on a fresh-bank run (296 conversations): 129 of 146 `skill` pages had `source_episodes: []`, the cycle's date as `created`, no section provenance, no `related`; every other type had 0 unsourced pages. Cause: the Stage-4 writer in `inbox_generator.generate`. Fix: `skill_grounding.ground` matches `evidence_entities` to the batch's conversations (co-occurrence of two named entities) and hands `apply_changes` a normal create/update change — dates, `reasoning` section provenance, `draws on` edges, commit line with sessions; a skill with no evidence in the batch is not written; a re-detected skill updates its page. Chosen over routing through `entity_resolver` because that adds judge calls per skill for a name-only dedup the name index already does; contradiction on a skill update was left open (paid) — ruled out 2026-10-09, (c) above. Existing pages: `api.scripts.repair_skills_aliases` (dry run by default) grounds them from the creating Sleep commit. (2)–(5) unchanged. **Tool-call capture — kept for future RESEARCH (owner 2026-10-07).** Offered capturing agents' tool calls as procedural evidence for this row, the owner kept the G105 rule (the person's turns + the agent's final replies only) and asked that the idea stay here for later research: "maybe the tool stuff keep it in the backlog to further research in the future". The sketch to evaluate then: per session, an *action trace* of calls, not outputs — tool name, scrubbed and capped arguments, success/exit code, repo-relative files touched, time — as its own evidence kind, never the person's words; raw output stays in the harness transcript, referenced by session id and position, never copied into a bank; Stage 4 and this row would read only the trace to find repeated successful procedures. Open risks to settle first: secrets in arguments, size on long sessions, Sleep cost, and the G105 seam (transcripts are read only by `transcript_capture`). Not scheduled; revisit on the owner's word. **Start-prompts linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** G73 owns raw objective briefs, candidates, variants and user-facing reuse. This row may compile a validated start prompt into a cited portable skill with declared prerequisites and use evidence; prompt save/copy never waits for the compiler. Preserve original/variant provenance and model/project limitations. [Start-prompts acceptance](../specs/2026-10-06-start-prompts.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Pattern detection writes new prose skill pages only; existing pages are skipped and source_episodes/related are empty. Ground and update skills, then compile cited SKILL.md/artifact bundles that work in another harness. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g112). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Keep learned procedural memory distinct from the installed-skill catalog (G138/G178). Compile grounded experience into standalone SKILL.md bundles plus versioned artifacts, with usable provenance, declared prerequisites and no dependency on Cicada’s runtime to read them. Include incremental grounded updates rather than create-only behavior. Acceptance: a different harness/model family reuses a synthetic SQL/script artifact safely; stale or failed artifacts take the R2 correction path (G55). **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What exists today (verified 2026-09-01, five-agent grounding sweep, critic spot-checked):** `skill` is producible by Stage 1 (`schemas.py:45-56`; `entity_extractor.py:56,:72,:96`) and by Stage 4 (`skill_extractor.detect_patterns` :57-101 — one LLM call over this cycle's changes + pairs co-occurring in ≥3 episodes, output `{skills:[{name, description, evidence_entities, confidence}]}`), and its only metric is a count (`skills_detected`, `schemas.py:946` → the Sleep-view chip). **The Stage-4 writer is create-only and ungrounded:** `inbox_generator.py:248-270` writes a page only `if not skill_path.exists()` (:254), dedups on `sanitize_id(name)` alone (:252), stamps `source_episodes: []` / `tags: []` / `related: []` (:265-267), body = the raw description (:270), and DISCARDS `evidence_entities`. Stage-4 output bypasses `entity_resolver` and conflict detection entirely (`sleep_cycle.py:781-786,:819-824`), so a re-detected pattern is a silent no-op that never bumps `last_referenced`, a near-duplicate name makes a second page, a contradicting preference is never a conflict, and the page only ever moves via durable decay (`conflict_resolver.py:146-232`) into the generic "No recent mentions" nudge. Contrast the Stage-1 branch (`conflict_resolver.py:257-317`), which stamps episodes, composes a v2 body and merges on update; `source_rewrite` cannot re-ground a skill because its `source_episodes` is empty. **Nothing exports skills:** the only installable skills are two hand-written files (root `SKILL.md` = recall policy; `skills/cicada-librarian/SKILL.md` = consolidation loop) — neither is generated from memory, `install.sh --skill` (:339-347) and `ConnectView.swift:75-76` copy only the root one, nothing installs `cicada-librarian`. `_procedures/` and `_preferences.md` are scaffolded in every bank (`bank_registry.py:45-55,:228-234`) and read by nothing; MCP `initialize` carries no `instructions` (`mcp/server.py:413-431`); `cicada_write_claim` cannot create a `skill` page (`agentic_write._infer_entity_type` :48-79 has no skill signal). `context-passport-roadmap.md:22` rates Skills "✅ Strong" — an overstatement; the honest rating is *captured, not grounded, not exported* (corrected in that file). **Why this is not G72:** G72 is the inventory + lifecycle of skills the user already HAS on disk (WikiSkill's raw→wiki→skills loop, R2's gate, the negative-transfer finding). G112 is the third layer WikiSkill names and Cicada lacks: compiling what memory has learned INTO skills, and shipping them so someone else can load them on their own plan. **Design.** (1) **Ground the skill page first ($0):** route Stage-4 output through `entity_resolver` like Stage 1; on update merge `source_episodes` from the evidence episodes and bump `last_referenced`; write `evidence_entities` into `related` (and edges); give the page the v2 layout so `source_rewrite` and hubs work on it; let a Stage-4 preference that contradicts an existing skill page raise a normal conflict item. (2) **Compile, don't author:** a deterministic `skill_compiler` turns a `skill` entity into a SKILL.md bundle — frontmatter `name`/`description` written as a TRIGGER block (G76's finding: the description is what a harness matches on), body = the preference/procedure text plus a `## Evidence` list of the claim ids it rests on with their `observer`/`source_trust`, and a `Cicada-Origin:` line naming the bank commit it was compiled from — provenance only, never a runtime dependency. WikiSkill's executor-wiki-access result (−2.8) is the rule: the compiled skill must stand alone on a harness that has never heard of Cicada. (3) **Redact by construction:** a compiled skill carries the RULE, never the episodes — no `source_episodes`, no conversation text, no third-party names unless the user pins them; `## Evidence` cites claim ids, not quotes. That is what makes "other people use it" safe: a skill bundle is shareable where a bank is not. (4) **Surfaces:** `GET /skills/{id}/export` (one bundle) and `GET /skills/export` (zip of every `active` skill with confidence ≥ 0.6); `install.sh --skill` and the Connect page install the compiled bundles beside the two hand-written ones; per-harness targets are the three the ecosystem converged on — `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, `~/.codex/skills/`, and an `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` block for harnesses without a skills dir. (5) The always-on primer half (top-N preferences <200 tokens at conversation start, `docs/goals/d2-research/procedural-preference-memory.md:9-34`) is NOT this row — it is G75's `initialize.instructions` payload, and it should read the same compiled bundles rather than a second format. **Rails:** a compiled skill is proposed, never pushed (R2's gate; G72's negative-transfer finding); the compiler is deterministic and free; Stage-4 grounding is the one paid change and it reuses the existing call. **Sequencing:** (1) is a bug fix and ships first; (2)+(3)+(4) are one PR; (5) waits for G75. → completes the WikiSkill mapping in **G72** (that row = inventory of existing skills; this = compiling new ones) and the SKILL.md-export line of **G55** (executable procedures ride the same compiler); serves **G76** (install both skills + the compiled ones), **G75** (primer payload), **G92** (a shareable skill bundle is the first zero-bank onboarding artifact), **A4** (what "enrich skill capture" should have meant); consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G66** (durable class), `entity_body` v2, `source_rewrite`; bounded by **R2** (human gate) and **G48**'s no-transcript-content rail. **2026-09-23 (Track O):** Settings → Skills is being built (G138); a compiled bundle's install will use the same marker-owned writer as `cicada`/`cicada-librarian` (`SkillInstaller`, Task 8). Constraint for the compiler: spec-only frontmatter (`name`, `description`, `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`) with provenance under `metadata: { cicada-origin: <commit> }`, never a top-level key (claude.ai rejects unknown keys); `name` equals the folder name, lowercase-hyphen, ≤ 64 characters. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §3: preserve linked procedural artifacts in standalone bundles. Ground/update skills first: the current writer still creates only absent pages with empty `source_episodes` and `related` (`inbox_generator.py:363-385`). Compile portable links, provenance and deliberate redaction; selected bundles may ride G186. G55 carries verification and R2 execution governance; stored memory is not automatic execution authority.** **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY · learned portable skills, P2. | UNBUILT · P2 |

Use the CLI once available; do not rename the live bank’s internal files. Import external AMR content through scrub/staging with source attribution and no implicit executable trust. Related items are G55/G112, G16 (now requested design), G132/G181/G92 and later G148. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why:** the public AMR release is a five-file spec/skill, not a capture/consolidation runtime; its minimal format is useful to agents without Cicada. Evidence and acceptance: [`2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §2, AMR `1db04a5` / Cicada `934711c9`. **APPLY export:** candidate `cicada export --amr <dir>` via G180; generated bounded root `MEMORY.md`, root-relative links and current-claim bullets with usable source/date/attribution metadata, outside the live bank. Deterministic committed snapshot with source-commit provenance; no bank/cursor/index/history mutation, no symlink escape, explicit disclosure, safe destination ownership, no remote setup. Lossy interoperability is separate from full-bank backup. Acceptance: an AMR-skill filesystem agent navigates synthetic exported memory without Cicada/MCP; bank hashes/history stay unchanged. **RESEARCH import:** external AMR bytes through shared scrub/staging with attribution preserved; define updates/removals/dates/links/artifacts, never execute files or promote external bullets to owner statements by default. No runtime bank was inspected. Fold related work into G180 (lean MCP), G76 (demo), G55/G112 (procedures/bundles), G16 (parked composition), G132/G181/G92 (backup/privacy), G148 (fair benchmark). **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY export after G180 · RESEARCH import. | UNBUILT · P2 |
| G199 | <a id="g199"></a>**The MCP error and argument contract: a refusal says so, a bad argument is refused** (the 2026-10-22 Hyrum audit A2, A3; filed by the acting owner under the owner's standing go) | **The problem.** The stdio server never checks a tool's advertised schema: every argument is read with `arguments.get(key, default)` (`mcp/server.py`), so invalid data is written today — `cicada_write_claim` with an `observer` outside its pattern is recorded, and `cicada_save_episode` with no `content` (required) saved an empty episode that then counted in `cicada_timeline`; `top_k: "lots"` is dropped and `since: "last tuesday"` silently falls back to 7 days. Refusals answer `isError: false` in at least eight wordings, while an uncaught exception answers `isError: true` with the raw Python message on stdio; the structured `Reply.code` (`not_found`, `out_of_range`, `demo_bank`, …) exists and is dropped at `mcp/server.py` before it reaches the client. **The design constraint.** Validate once at dispatch against `TOOLS[name].inputSchema` and answer `isError: true` with `Invalid arguments: <field> …`; fix the two integrity cases first (observer pattern, empty content). Keep every refusal sentence, set `isError: true` for refusal codes and add `structuredContent: {code, data}`; replace stdio exception text with the remote shape. One release accepts a digit string for an integer with a warning, then rejects. Both doors refuse the same inputs. Decide this before the lean-MCP rename (G180) so renamed tools ship with it; it re-records `api/tests/fixtures/mcp_stdio_golden.json` once, grouped with A1/A4/A6/A9 per the fixture header. [Audit](../research/2026-10-22-hyrum-audit.md). **Shipped 2026-10-22** (#403: the two integrity refusals; branch `mcp-error-contract`: the rest) — `tool_contract.check` at both doors' dispatch against their own advertised schema, `isError` + `structuredContent: {code, data, message}` for every `cli_types.REFUSAL_CODES` code, exception text replaced by the class name on stdio and the tool door, a digit, decimal or true/false string read with a note in 0.7.0 only, the first release after v0.6.0 — `since`/`tz` refused when unreadable (in the bodies too, so the CLI refuses alike). Review round: read replies fenced whatever their code with Cicada's directions outside the markers, enums case-insensitive, tolerated values without an enum, release batches checked by shape only, failures logged as class + file:line with no message. A drift probe over 52 test files (1,375 tests) checked every tool call the suite makes against its schema: no call a body accepts is refused, beyond the deliberately bad ones; it found the excerpt `t` typed string on both doors while agents send seconds, now `string | number`. **The leniency ended in 0.7.1** (#445): such a string is now refused on both doors like any other wrong type, `isError` with `invalid_arguments`, the sentence naming the type and saying to send the value without quotes; the dating test is gone. | DONE · P2 |
| G200 | <a id="g200"></a>**MCP surface hygiene the audit found: no owner name in the schema, a truthful handshake, one untrusted-content rule, JSON-RPC basics** (the 2026-10-22 Hyrum audit A7–A11; filed by the acting owner) | **A11** — `cicada_write_claim`'s advertised `observer` pattern lists the legacy observer value, which is the owner's first name, so a hardcoded owner name ships to every client (CLAUDE.md: a hardcoded owner name is a bug against the mission); keep accepting it server-side, stop advertising it. **A7** — the primer names two HTTP routes no MCP agent can call, and picks its variant by substring on `clientInfo.name`, so a desktop client named with "claude" is told to `claude --resume`; drop the routes, match exact names, add a desktop variant. **A8** — `SKILL.md` (and the plugin copy) shows a bank layout that no longer exists and forbids the page edits G198 allows; hub pages tell an agent to read `memory/entities/<id>.md` directly. **A9** — the stdio door marks third-party text as untrusted only on video and skills replies; recall, recall_detail, sources, ask and open_hub carry fetched titles unmarked; one rule for both doors. **A10** — answer `ping` with `{}`, malformed JSON with `-32700`, a missing tool name with `-32602`; annotate stdio reads from the remote `READ_TOOLS`. All additive except A9's golden re-record. Entity and claim ids are documented as opaque (A5); the episode-id day is ruling 34. [Audit](../research/2026-10-22-hyrum-audit.md). **Shipped 2026-10-22:** A8, A10, A11 in #403; A7 and A9 on branch `mcp-error-contract` — the primer names no HTTP route (resume, span and `/state` lines gone), its variant is an exact `clientInfo.name` match with a `desktop` variant for `claude-ai` (contract 19), and every read reply is fenced as reference data on both doors while a write's provider lines are marked the same way on both (`tool_contract.reference`, `_untrusted_block`). **Still open:** the command line's text is not fenced; agent pills in the app (`agent_live`) still match client names by substring, which only lights a pill. | DONE · P2 |
| G201 | <a id="g201"></a>**The skill lifecycle — candidate, promoted, retired; proven on a model, retired when the model catches up** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22: Cicada's suggested and drafted skills should be candidates, separate from the skills that make it into the harness, because "as Claude Code and the models improve, a skill that once patched a gap starts pulling agents back to outdated behavior"; the model absorbs what the harness used to patch, and the patch should then be deleted) | **Decided as TODO ruling 35** (acting owner, 2026-10-22, standing go). Slices, in order: **(a)** the skills inventory sees Claude Code plugins (their skills and versions, read-only from the plugins' own manifests) and tags each skill `about-you` or `generic`; **(b)** the Stop hook keeps the names of skills a turn invoked (names only, beside ruling 11's model and effort — amends the capture rail and ruling 11; the implementing PR adds the rail line to CLAUDE.md) and the ledger counts fires; an 8-week silence on a promoted skill raises one retire question, a keep counts like `kept_on`; **(c)** `evals/proven.json` beside a promoted skill's evals records the model id and Claude Code version it won on; a change reruns with and without the skill (≥3 runs each, the #370 spread), and a baseline within the spread asks to retire it; **(d)** the promotion gate on the Skills page: evals beat the no-skill baseline by more than the spread; generic skills expire 90 days after their last proof; the page shows the installed set's weight and asks past 12 per harness. | G204 | <a id="g204"></a>**`cicada_write_claim` restating a fact Sleep already holds names a claim id that is not on the page** (found driving the verify recipes, 2026-10-22) | **Reproduction.** After the standard verify seed, Sleep holds `alpha-project uses rustfft`. `cicada_write_claim` of the same fact replies `Recorded … claim clm_alpha-project_uses_<hash>, action=written`, but `GET /entities/alpha-project/claims` has no such id: `agentic_write._determine_action` counts a fold into the existing claim as `written` and returns the id the agent's write minted. The surviving claim took the agent's session id and a second, one-character-shorter copy of the same evidence span. `cicada_retract_claim` with the reply's id answers `No claim …`; with the surviving id, `not written by this agent`. So an agent cannot withdraw what it was told it wrote. **Constraint.** The reply text is golden-pinned (`mcp_stdio_golden.json`'s `write_claim`), so a fix re-records it with G199's error contract (the Hyrum audit's grouping); a fold should say it reinforced the existing claim and name that id. **Id note (2026-10-22):** the redesign's worker briefs cited "G204" for the connection model; that row is **G217**. This row is the `cicada_write_claim` finding only. | APPLY · P2 · open |
| G205 | <a id="g205"></a>**Agent requests — an agent asks the person for something only they can do, as one Inbox kind and a notification** (redesign 2026-10-22) | **Problem.** When an agent reading with the person's own browser meets a login wall, it records `needs_login` and the app shows "Needs you to sign in" with Open in browser (G166) — on that link's row only. Nothing tells the person now, nothing says which agent asked or why, and other asks an agent can't do itself (open a site, approve a page edit, G198) have no common shape. **Proposal:** one Inbox kind, `agent_request`: the agent (its icon, by `Cicada-Author`), the ask in one line, the reason ("to read the post you talked about"), one action that opens the site or the item and one that dismisses; also delivered as a Mac notification. Requests sit above questions in the Inbox. **Constraints:** agents never sign in themselves and Cicada never spawns a browser (G166); a request never settles, reorders or changes a belief; questions come up naturally (`inbox_ask_gate`) — a request is not a question and is never asked in a conversation unbidden; the notification payload is ids and the agent's words, never episode text; one request per (agent, site, reason) until answered. Relates G115 (one question object), G166, G198. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed. | APPLY · P2 · proposed |
| G206 | <a id="g206"></a>**The browser as a way in for saved lists — X bookmarks, LinkedIn saved, Instagram saved, the way YouTube's lists work** (redesign 2026-10-22; extends G166 and G71) | **Ruling 47 (owner 2026-10-28):** saved lists are tier 1 of ruling 47 (the person's own browser); LinkedIn and Instagram stay here (no official API gives a person's saved items); X keeps both paths — this read by default, and the existing pay-per-use bookmarks connector (`connectors/x.py`, tier 2) for the person who connects it. **Problem.** The person's saved posts on X, LinkedIn and Instagram pile up where Cicada can't see them. These sites are walled: the backend never fetches a login-walled page (ToS rail). The YouTube Watch later and history pages already come in through the person's own agent in their signed-in browser, as a standing ask once the person turns that list on, stdio only (G71, 2026-10-16). **Proposal:** the same shape per site — the Sources page offers "Read in your browser" for each list; when on, the person's local agent reads the list page in their session and posts the items; saved items are **indexed, never read by a model** unless the person asks about one (ruling 30). A walled read that meets a login records `needs_login` and raises a G205 request. **Constraints:** only a site the person turned on; only the list page, never the person's feed or messages; no scraping behind auth by the backend, ever; a block is never retried with different headers; one site's list at a time and the same rate bounds as the LinkedIn profile read (≤ 10 a day) until measured; Instagram is new to the source catalogue (owner 2026-10-22). Relates G191 (bookmarks), G166. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed (`sources` worker). **Built 2026-10-22 (branch `feat/browser-lists`, backend):** `saved_lists.py` (the offer, a per-site week's pause, 20-hour cadence, one site at a time, ≤ 10 recorded reads a UTC day per bank), `reading.json` `saved_lists` behind `PUT /reading/settings` `savedLists`, `cicada_record_saved_list` (stdio only, `NEVER_REMOTE`), the queue's lines; each item is saved under the export's own origin and folder so it is index-only (ruling 30), deduped by URL with the post's other spellings as aliases. `needs_login` files a G205 agent request once `agent_requests` is on `dev` (a no-op before; its "I signed in" should call `saved_lists.resume_site`). **Bug fixed in the same branch:** a saved X post was titled by its numeric status id; a walled post is now titled from its URL ("Post by @<user>"), and the old id title still counts as a placeholder an agent's read replaces. Verified on a scratch backend through the stdio server (synthetic posts). **Open:** the Sources page's switch (Mac work, spec in `app.md`); a post unsaved on the site is not removed here; the 10-a-day bound is unmeasured. | APPLY · P2 · PR #426 |
| G212 | <a id="g212"></a>**Devices — every machine by its own name, a dot for connected, and pairing as easy as a link** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22, D6: "adding status stuff like to see if my server or other box is connected … it must be setup easily enough like claude does it in their apps … just going to a link or detecting cicada over tailscale would be cool") | **Problem.** Settings → Devices & access has 4 controls and 65 words (app inventory, demo bank): *Add a device* shows a token once, and remote access is explained in three sections and lives in one. Nothing says whether the server is reachable now, how (Tailscale, SSH, local), or what waits for it; the app calls its own machine "this Mac". G202 measured that client captures wait in a spool and land exactly once, and counts the spool per server in `cicada status` — the data exists, the page does not show it. `POST /devices` mints a per-device token (ruling 28); nothing mints it from a one-time code. **Proposal:** Settings → Devices lists each machine by the machine's own name (its host name, never "my Mac"), with a connected dot, the way it connects and its waiting count; the Sleep page shows the same machines as a strip with each one's queue. Pairing: the new machine (or the phone, G91) shows a short code or a link, and the person types or opens it in the app on the primary — the ruling 28/32 trust shape (possession shown on the primary's own screen); the primary is found on the LAN by Bonjour (`_cicada._tcp`, Home Assistant's TXT shape: instance id, internal and external URL), then the saved tailnet name. **Constraints:** a server binds 127.0.0.1 and is reached only through an overlay the person runs (G181); Cicada never starts a tunnel and no installer copies a credential; a device token is revocable from this page; status is ids, names and times only. Relates G181, G202, G132. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed (`connect` worker). | APPLY · P2 · proposed |
| G213 | <a id="g213"></a>**An HTTP API for agents that cannot use MCP, and a setup command that feels like a modern CLI** **2026-10-23:** `cicada setup` runs whatever an agent's `connect` still lists, so an already-wired Codex gets the new `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` block (see G110). (Rodrigo 2026-10-22, D3: "so maybe a call to work on the api. Also the cli setup should be cool, kind of those like the vercel ones") | **Problem.** Some personal agents cannot speak MCP: a texted agent told the owner it "can't plug into MCP directly" but could use "an API or a web app"; Meta's Muse builds its own client from an OpenAPI document or docs URL plus a key (reported). Cicada's only HTTP door for tools is `POST /tools/call` (`api/routers/tools.py`), behind the master or a device token, never public. **Proposal:** a REST surface that mirrors existing bodies, no new ones — `POST /v1/<op>` for recall, ask, page, timeline, save_note, save_url, write_claim (+ retract), inbox (+ resolve), optionally project and backlog_add; the same `{text, data, error}` envelope and G199 contract; `GET /openapi.json` and a ten-line `GET /llms.txt`; a one-paragraph prompt the person pastes. Auth reuses the G135 connector token (hashed, scoped, expiring, revocable), the same scope filter (`catalog.tool_names_for`) and provenance (`origin: remote:<id>`; a remote claim is never the person's own words). **Where it is served (owner, 2026-10-22, ruling 36 D3):** on the G135 listener, behind the same tokens — cloud agents reach only a public URL — which amends the rail's "serves only MCP"; this row's PR changes that CLAUDE.md line and adds `/v1/*`, `/openapi.json` and `/llms.txt` to the listener's allowlist with the same token verifier, `Origin` refusal and ledger rows. **CLI:** `cicada setup` — detect, then confirm ("Found Claude Code, Cursor, Tailscale. Connect these?"), a spinner per step and a final checklist, a non-TTY path printing the same steps; pairing by a printed code typed into the app (ruling 28/32). It keeps the Python CLI (no `npx` wrapper); `rich` + `questionary` are the new dependencies, approved by the owner 2026-10-22. **Constraints:** ruling 21/21a (the CLI is a door onto the same bodies; person-only commands refuse from an agent's shell); no secret printed; setup prompts run commands verbatim and fetch nothing. Research: agents-api-phone §2, §5. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed (`agentapi` worker). **Follow-up 2026-10-23 (owner: "whether to support Cicada tools over API"; proposal `followups-2026-10-23.md`, kept outside the repo with the redesign research, §2).** **Shipped:** `/v1` (#424) on the backend port and the G135 listener — 11 hand-picked operations (`agent_api.OPS`), OpenAPI and `llms.txt` generated from the remote schemas, agent tokens (`cic_api_`). **The question, made precise:** the person's own scripts are the CLI's (ruling 21) and the phone is the capture routes' (#432); what is open is how much of the tool surface `/v1` carries. An HTTP-only agent holds 11 of the 28 remote tools while an MCP client with the same scopes holds all 28 (no operation for the backlog, sources, progress, `record_check`, perspective, hub, the video and reading queues, `capture_turns`), and the operation names (`page`, `save_note`, `inbox`) are not `cli_map`'s (`get`, `save`, `inbox list`), with no test pinning either set. **Options weighed:** `/v1` as it is (two doors, one token, different powers — a Hyrum trap); `/v1` generated from the remote tool table with names in `cli_map` (recommended); a third MCP-over-HTTP door (the listener's `/mcp` already is one, and OpenAI's Responses API and Agents SDK take a remote MCP URL directly); a REST mirror of all ~47 stdio tools (no — the stdio-only ones are local by trust: `edit_page`'s trust list, the saved-list and YouTube-list reads, explore, design, skills, `continue`). **Recommendation:** every `REMOTE_TOOLS` entry gets one operation unless an exclusion set lists it with its reason (proposed: `handshake`, covered by `GET /v1/me` and `llms.txt`); the name comes from a new `api_op` column in `cli_map` — the 11 shipped names frozen, new ones the CLI command with underscores (`backlog_add`, `source_add`, `progress_add`, `capture_turns` …); a test pins `ops == REMOTE_TOOLS − EXCLUDED` and each operation's scope to `catalog.TOOL_SCOPE`; the G199 check stays the remote schema's. No OpenAI-format tool list (frameworks convert `openapi.json`; it would be one more stable shape for nothing). **Owner decisions — decided 2026-10-23 (TODO ruling 38 §1), all as recommended:** carry every remote-eligible tool; the 11 shipped names frozen, new ones `cli_map`'s command; `capture_turns` on `/v1` under `record`, stored as relayed, never the person's words; one G199 contract and the same scope enforcement on every door; no third door. **Built 2026-10-23 (branch `feat/v1-from-tool-table`):** `agent_api.OPS` is generated from `REMOTE_TOOLS` minus `EXCLUDED` (only `cicada_handshake`), named by `cli_map.Row.operation` (an `api_op` column for the seven frozen names that are not their command; `REMOTE_ONLY_OPS` names `capture_turns`) — 27 operations, the 11 first and unchanged; `capture_turns` keeps one episode per conversation — `"conversation": "new"` mints a handle the reply returns, later turns pass it back, another token's handle is refused, and no handle falls back to the token's day bucket as `/mcp` does (review blocker, ruling 32/G104); `llms.txt` gains the relay step. Tests pin the set to the remote table, the 11 to their tools, each new name to its row, each scope to `catalog.TOOL_SCOPE` (every scope combination), `openapi.json`'s validity, and the same refusal sentence and code on `/mcp` and `/v1`. **Slices:** `api_op` column + pinning tests S ✓ · generated OPS, +16 operations, docs M ✓ · recipes (a Shortcut, a script, OpenAI Responses with `/mcp`) S · owner-present check with one HTTP-only agent S. | PARTIAL · P2 · APPLY |
| G217 | <a id="g217"></a>**The connection model — Sources feed it, Intelligence consolidates it, Agents read and write it; Settings in six; one logo tile** (redesign 2026-10-22, TODO ruling 36; the briefs called this G204, which was already taken) | **2026-10-29 (pages v2 part B; ruling 48 parts 5, 6, 14; DR-77):** the card open read the page's whole git log on every non-304 open — 130–300 ms p95 on an owner-scale bank copy for a tab most opens never show — so `GET /entities/{id}` reads no git (`?history=true` keeps the old rows); the History tab reads `?summary=true&limit=30` when it opens (`page_history`: one `git log -p --unified=0`, a bucket rule — You · Agents · Sleep · Upkeep, decay and Cicada's housekeeping as upkeep — and a per-commit summary: beliefs added / ended / removed, page fields, up to four changed prose lines). Measured p95 229 ms, of which the bare path-limited walk is 174 ms; since `perf/commit-graph` (2026-10-29) Sleep's tail keeps git's commit-graph with changed-path filters current under the bank's git lock (a derived git file, never a commit): History tab p95 224 → 128 ms, `entity_history` 203 → 105 ms, the provenance strip 174 → 75 ms on the copy. **Places:** `LocationHero` geocoded the page's name through Apple on every first open; now coordinates are stored once (`place_coordinates`: `lat`/`lon`, `coordinates_by`, `coordinates_at`), resolved by the app's `MKLocalSearch` on the person's first open or a five-place pass after it, posted to `PUT /entities/{id}/coordinates`; the backend never geocodes. Apple's geocoder is a network request to Apple, so a place is resolved on its own only when ruling 44's local test calls it public and its name is neither address-shaped nor a home word; anything else waits for *Place on the map*; *Not this place* takes a pin back for good (`coordinates_by: none`). The card draws a cached `MKMapSnapshotter` still (Caches, outside every bank) with nearby places within 5 km read from `GET /entities/{id}/place` after first paint, and a live SwiftUI `Map` on click. **In short:** `in_short` writes 2–3 sentences in frontmatter for pages with ≥ 400 words of article and ≥ 5 conversations in 30 days (bank facts), ≤ 10 a run, only when the article's hash changed, on the engine's small tier; `page_blocks.summary` serves it to the card and `cicada_recall_detail(include=["summary"])`. On the copy: 32 pages qualify, ~4.2k tokens in and 80–600 out per page on haiku. **Not built:** Undo on a history row, a Sleep row's link into its Past read. The person's own rows wear the owner page's picture (`HistoryWriterMark`, G146's ladder) since `perf/commit-graph`. **2026-10-29 (Pages v2, part A; ruling 48 (16)):** the article carries the beliefs by numbering what the page's writer already decided — each article paragraph and Key Fact's G118 provenance, stored with the page under its exact text guard — into citation markers and References (`page_citations`); measured on an owner-scale copy (8,029 pages): 81% of article paragraphs (7,906 / 9,724) and 64% of Key Facts (23,127 / 36,296) carry write-time provenance, so most lines are cited with no new store; 601 pages hold claims but cite no line and draw their beliefs as sentences until the writer folds them in. Not built: weaving unsaid beliefs into prose at Sleep's tail (part B or later). **2026-10-28 (vision item 4, logo freshness):** `scripts/logo-freshness.py` + a weekly read-only workflow report whether each set mark is still what its vendor serves (pixels compared, so a re-encoded PNG is not a rebrand); it never commits or opens a PR. First run: 23 same, 20 pinned, 2 visible redraws waiting on the owner (Fireworks now a dark mark on a light tile; OpenClaw dark eyes), none unreachable. No re-cut at 512 px: every PNG is 256 px (≥ 4.9× the tile on Retina) but Instinct 64 and Warp 196, which their vendors serve no larger. The OpenCode complaint was the 2026-10-22 set's fix; its source is still byte-identical upstream. **Problem (app inventory, demo bank after #417, 178 screenshots):** Settings has 11 sections, 107 controls and about 2,030 words with folds closed. The same choice lives in many places: engine choice ×5, named three ways ("Claude plan · haiku", "Claude Code (your plan)", "Claude plan · Signed in"); key entry ×3; agent connect ×4 plus a Claude Code row in Integrations; chat-export import ×5; browsers ×4; two catalogues of the same sources (Integrations and Add a source) grouped differently. Third-party marks are drawn three ways (installed app icon, 30 bundled PNGs with 9 lacking a recorded licence, SF Symbol fallbacks) at 9 sizes, tiled in Settings and bare elsewhere; Grok and "Another agent" are grey glyphs beside full-colour marks; Codex, ChatGPT and Grok have no official mark in the bundle; the OpenCode mark looks far from the original (owner). Agents are listed in three hard-coded places (`agent_live.py:34`, `agent_wiring.AGENTS`, `HARNESSES`) and stdio sightings live only in the telemetry ledger, so they vanish with telemetry off. **Model:** three nouns, each with its ways in and only these status words — **Sources** (Account, Browser, This machine, Export, Feed; Connected · Needs sign-in · Off; sidebar page), **Intelligence** (plans, API keys, local; In use · Ready · Needs a key; Settings → Intelligence, the Sleep page's engine label jumps there), **Agents** (this machine, another machine, phone and web apps; Connected · Not set up; sidebar page, ⌘9). A chat export belongs on its agent's row. Settings: General · You · Intelligence · Sleep · Devices · Privacy & data, under 400 words. **Agents:** one table (id, label, tier, logo, config probe, transports, setup prompt, hook support, `clientInfo` needles) replacing the three lists; tiers per ruling 36 D3; a "last seen" registry outside every bank (names and times only) so detection survives telemetry off. **Logos:** one tile component (a 26 pt mark in a 36 pt tile with a ring, never bare), one size per context; marks from each vendor's own brand page or repository, unmodified, with the licence recorded; one umbrella per provider (Anthropic: Claude Code, Claude; OpenAI: Codex, ChatGPT) with its ways to connect beneath; Instagram added. **Constraints:** design direction D (a UI PR cites its DR ids; depth is a ring); copy is provider-neutral except where it names the person's own choice; prices only on the Sleep page (ruling 12); the Obsidian vault row leaves for now; G139's search index keeps every row; G175's pixel marks stay the study room's. Relates G50 (Intelligence), G126 (the integrations catalogue), G124, G139, G175, G212. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed; backend slice (`connect`, `providers` workers), logos (`logos` worker), app (`app` worker, needs a Mac). | APPLY · P2 · proposed |

## P3 — design, mascot and remaining app/Reader workflows

| ID | Goal | Reasoning and remaining implementation | Current status |
