| G70 | <a id="g70"></a>**Design memory — screenshots of design saves + a distilled design-identity layer** | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Saved URLs/media exist; screenshot curation, a cross-project design board and a distilled identity layer do not. G151 owns structured profiles/read surfaces; this row owns curation and board workflows. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g70). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Saved design inspiration (Pinterest boards, IG collections, bookmarked sites) should be *visual* memory: capture a screenshot per design-tagged media URL and distill a queryable design identity. **Capture (no screenshot feature exists in api/ or app/ today — verified by grep):** a Sleep tail step (pattern: `warm_logos`) shells headless system Chrome (`--headless --screenshot --window-size=1440,2400 <url>`), semaphore-bounded, offline-safe (failure = retry next cycle); login-walled pages = explicit opt-in capture via the user's own browser (Claude-in-Chrome/CDP), on-demand only — `screencapture -v` is the wrong tool (screen-not-page, TCC). **Storage — never in a bank**, same argument as logos (a shot of someone else's site is a derived artifact of the outside world): `~/.cicada/screens/<bank>/<url_hash>.png` + `meta.json`, ~30 d TTL, `GET /entities/{id}/screenshot` (ETag), `has_screenshot` on graph nodes read from the index only; user annotations DO enter the bank as body text/claims. **Design identity, zero schema change:** (i) Sleep-time VLM pass (G49 ladder; keyless degrade = skip) writes `palette`/`typography`/`layout-style` claims on each media entity — unseen predicates default multi-valued (verified, `predicates.py:171-186`), no seed edit; (ii) `exemplifies-style` claims with **node objects** → style `concept` entities ("brutalist web") project into real graph edges at Stage 5.7 (verified: `regenerate_edges_from_claims` projects only `object_kind == 'node'`), clustering the inspiration corpus in d3; (iii) the identity itself = a `skill` entity ("Design taste: …") distilled by **Stage 4 pattern detection** across design-tagged saves — its literal job, new input. **App:** the screenshot becomes the `HeroPreview`/`MediaPreview` hero for `url`-kind design media (preferred over the OG card; the app's `image` kind currently has **no server-side writer**), palette swatches under the G24 SummaryBox. **Not verified:** headless-Chrome flags against current stable Chrome; VLM model choice + palette-extraction quality (a pure-pixel k-means fallback needs no LLM at all); whether cookie-consent walls poison shots (they will — reuse/extend G65(b) junk detection at capture). **Prereq: G65(a)** slug collisions. → extends **G22** (media previews), **G23/G25** (hero), **G59** (derived-artifact cache pattern), **G47/G69** (the saves being screenshotted), **Stage 4** (skill distillation), **G37** later (capture at share time). **(Absorbs G14, merged 2026-09-01)** — the original aesthetics/mood-board ask: a first-class **`aesthetic`/mood-board** entity for designs/references that recur across projects, rich interlinking where an aesthetic *influences* projects (Rodrigo's example: **Blade Runner → futuristic cyberpunk → a robot-design project**), and a **dedicated gallery/board view** (not the force graph) for self-made designs cross-linked to Pinterest boards / external refs. A new `aesthetic` entity type was G14's original proposal; the "resist adding types" doctrine that closed **G2** argues against minting one — model cross-project influence via `exemplifies-style` claims on existing `concept`/`skill` entities instead, consistent with this row's own design (ii)/(iii). Depends on **G11** for in-app image rendering. **2026-10-06:** the design profile any entity carries — own projects, films, books — and the agent read surface (MCP tool, CLI formats, skill compile) are developed in **G151**; this row keeps the capture of design saves (screenshots) and the board view. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · P3 |
| G151 | <a id="g151"></a>**Design identity of things — the palettes, fonts, notable assets and look of what the person finds interesting, shown on their pages** 💸 (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "extracting and showing the design identity of things, such as interesting mentioned assets in a website, aesthetics, color palettes, fonts, etc. This is specific to specific things the user finds interesting of things like books, images he has seen, edition in certain videos, etc. If we can show off in a way this design things in the entity pages themselves would be amazing but i think we can have thin in the backlog for now.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No structured design-profile schema/read tool/page section/export was found. Implement deterministic own-repo/image tokens first, then vision/video extraction, board view and portable style skills; media previews are not design identity. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g151). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 keeps design identity a product priority. Start with curated references, attributable palettes/fonts/assets and an editable portable identity document; pair G70’s personal design taste with identities of specific projects/things. Add model-driven visual extraction only after the capture/retrieval contract is useful. Do not make this feature a prerequisite for testing general memory. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why a row beside G70.** **G70** designs *design memory* for design-tagged saves — a screenshot per saved URL, `palette`/`typography`/`layout-style` claims, `exemplifies-style` edges, a distilled taste `skill` — and is still 🔲. The owner's ask is wider in two ways: (a) **any kind of thing he finds interesting**, not only a design save — a book (its cover and typography), an image he has seen, a video (its edit: cuts, grade, titling) — and (b) **the entity page is where it shows**, not only the media hero: swatches, the typefaces named, the notable assets called out, so a page reads as the look of the thing. **Evidence of the gap:** no module extracts a colour or a font anywhere in `api/` (G70's own grep, still true); the only image a page carries is a G59 logo (`logo_service.py`) or a media preview; `video_chapters.py`/`cicada_record_watch` keep what a video *said* (G140 `media` spans), never how it *looked*. **Design constraints:** everything it extracts is a **claim with provenance** (`palette`, `typeface`, `asset`, `edit-style` — multi-valued, so never a conflict) whose evidence is the artifact, never free prose; a pure-pixel palette (k-means over an image already on this Mac) needs no model and is the first rung — a vision model is the 💸 rung, on the G49 ladder and skipped keyless; a screenshot or frame of someone else's work is a derived artifact of the outside world and lives beside the logo cache, **never in a bank**, under the existing outbound gates and the ToS rail (no fetch behind auth; Cicada never downloads a video — G140); fonts are named, never shipped; painted art rules do not apply (this is data, drawn as data). **Scope now:** backlog only — the owner's word. → extends **G70** (design memory), **G59** (derived-artifact cache), **G140** (video watch record), **G22/G23** (media previews). **Developed 2026-10-06 (Rodrigo: "I want to have in our interface a way to extract the design identity of different things (whether it's a movie, a book, or whatever, or a project of mine) and have that exposed in the app. I want a way that an agent can easily see and use it when replicating something else, like generating a video or coding a website.")** — the row now carries three parts. **(1) A design profile on any entity** — a film or series, a book, an image, a website, a brand, and **the person's own projects** — composed at read time from its design claims, never a second copy: palette with roles (`palette`, hex + role + share), typography (`typeface`, role and scale, named never shipped), layout and spacing (`layout-style`), motion and edit (`motion-style`, `edit-style`: cuts, pacing, grade, titling), notable assets (`asset`), mood words (`mood`), and influences as real edges (G70's `exemplifies-style` → style `concept` pages, so Blade Runner → cyberpunk → a robot project is walkable). **Extraction, cheapest rung first:** the person's own project is read deterministically from its repo's design sources (theme files, CSS variables, Tailwind config, a design-rules doc — Cicada's own `DESIGN_RULES.md` and `CicadaTheme` are the first dogfood) with no model; an image already on this Mac (a cover, a still, a screenshot the person dropped in, G70's capture) gets a pure-pixel palette (k-means) with no model; a vision model is the 💸 rung for type identification, mood and composition (G49 ladder, skipped keyless); a film or video never downloads (G140/G162) — its look comes from stills the person provides, from text that describes it (claims with spans, G118), or from the person's own agent watching it in their browser and recording design observations through `cicada_record_watch` (timestamped evidence, the agent's observation, never the person's words). **(2) In the app:** an "Extract design identity" action on any entity page (sources: a link, images dropped in, a project's repo, or "ask my agent to watch it"); a **Design** section on the page (swatches with hex, type specimens set in system type with the face named, asset thumbnails from the derived cache, motion/edit notes, references) — data drawn as data, DR rules apply; and later G70's board view across profiles. **(3) For agents — the point of the row:** the profile is a first-class read surface, not a picture. An MCP tool (`cicada_design_profile(entity)`) and the G180 CLI (`cicada design <name> --format md\|json\|css\|tailwind\|tokens`) return a structured, cited design brief: the tokens (palette with roles, type families and scale, spacing and radius) in formats an agent drops straight into work — W3C design-tokens JSON, CSS variables or a Tailwind theme for coding a site; a prompt-ready brief (palette, grade, pacing, framing, titling, mood, do/don't, references) for generating a video or an image; recall and the handshake say when a profile exists, and implicit recall (G149) injects it when the person asks to make something "look like X"; G112 compiles a profile into a portable skill ("design in the style of X") any harness loads. **Rails kept:** every field is a claim with provenance and evidence; screenshots and frames stay in the derived cache outside every bank; the ToS rail and outbound gates hold; a profile describes a style and never copies or ships someone else's assets or fonts. **Slices:** S1 own projects from their repos + image palette + the read tool + the page section (no model); S2 the vision rung; S3 film/video via the person's agent (G162); S4 G70's board and G112 skill compile. → also **G180** (the CLI), **G149**, **G112**, **G118**. **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → APPLY · P3; start with curation/schema. | UNBUILT · P3 |

[Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g127). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests a Cicada pixel mascot and an app-visible copy of the creation prompt so anyone can provide a reference image. Phase 1: complete/select Cicada art and expose a neutral “Copy creation prompt” in Mascot Settings, grounded in the existing handoff/MAKING_A_MASCOT guide (G73). Phase 2: define and implement a user import flow for a generated sprite pack: reference image, manifest/sheet validation, all moods and day/night/menu variants, preview, install/remove and selector persistence. Copying a prompt alone does not load art. Current registry contains Bookworm and requires code registration; runtime packs are unfinished. Honor sprite timing/Reduce Motion/Low Power caps and never execute imported pack contents. No paid image generation is necessary to write the prompt or pack contract. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Why it might be right:** Cicada is an agent that reads, files and asks — a librarian is the literal job, and a small robot reads as *the agent* rather than *a pet*, which fits the experience-port framing (an assistant that tends your record) better than a worm does; a robot also carries state legibly (eyes, screen-face, antenna, a book or a card in its hands) and scales from the 18 pt menu bar to the 120 pt Sleep page without losing character. **Why to be careful:** the bookworm is the app's name-adjacent identity (Bookworm MCP tool, `BookwormState`, the sprite set that shipped 2026-09-02 as G107) and the README/screenshots now show it; a WALL·E resemblance must stop well short of the actual character (Pixar/Disney trade dress) — *inspired by* (boxy body, expressive binocular eyes, tank treads, a friendly tilt) is fine, a copy is not; and the mascot is a status surface first (G107 ruling), so any new design must carry the same eight states with ≥ 2 frames each before it is allowed to replace the worm. **Cheap way to decide:** because sprites are Aseprite sheets (G107/G176), a second character supplies the same tags behind the same `BookwormState`/renderer; draw the robot's `awake` + `curious` + `sleeping` frames only, install a build with a `mascot` setting (worm \| robot), live with it for a week, then rule. Naming follows the ruling (the MCP tool stays `cicada_*`; only the display name changes). **Not started — DECIDE.** → extends **G107** (sprite system), **G125** (the `reading` state is where a librarian shines), **G117** (the mascot guides onboarding); relates **G90** (README screenshots would need retaking from the demo bank). DECIDE, S to prototype. **Update — a character selector (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "write in the backlog to work on a character/mascot selector and use strawberry browser as inspiration").** The question is no longer *worm or robot* but *let the person choose*. Inspiration: Strawberry, the agentic browser, ships named companions — a default (Lora) plus role characters (Sales Sally, Recruiter Ryan, Extractor Ella, Assistant Astrid, Competition Camilla), each with its own face, name and job, picked from a gallery and switchable, and users can make their own. **What transfers to Cicada:** a picker (Settings → General, and a long-press/right-click on the worm in the Sleep room) showing each character large with its name and a one-line personality; the choice is a *skin over the same status surface* — every character carries the same eight room states and eight menu tags × ≥ 2 frames on the room's one lattice, the same text twins and the same honesty rules (art encodes state, never quantity), so switching never changes what the page says, only who says it. The groundwork exists: part a of the Sleep v4 track made the sprite API character-agnostic (`BookwormPose`, reaction frames) precisely so a second character is one more sprite file. Candidates: the bookworm (default), the librarian robot above, and one or two more drawn to the same grid; a character's *voice* may flavour the answer-ladder copy only where the facts stay identical. **What does not transfer:** Strawberry's companions are different *agents* with different jobs; Cicada has one agent, so a character is identity, not capability — no character may imply it knows or does something the others don't. → the selector ships with the characters it has; G127's week-long worm-vs-robot trial becomes the selector's first two entries. **APPLY** (was DECIDE), M 2026-10-01: a character is now a set of Aseprite sheets with the tags `BookwormArt.requiredTags` names — a skin is a new set of sheets, never new code. **2026-10-02:** a room skin also supplies `<prefix><state>-night-dark` and `-night-lit` for all eight states, matching day tags/frame counts/timings. The menu stays one dark-outline sheet. **Owner 2026-10-02: the selector is built beside the scenery in Settings → Sleep, naming the current character Bookworm.** `MascotRegistry` holds one pure entry with id/name/room prefix/menu sheet/art provenance folder. `cicada.mascot` is per viewer, defaults/falls back to `bookworm`; all sprite readers, transitions and cover timing resolve it, and the menu cache includes mascot identity. The tile shows room/menu key frames with a checkmark, keyboard focus and selected VoiceOver text. New characters add their own prefix/sheets, manifest entries and one registry entry. Alternative base designs and the room context-menu shortcut remain open; the scenery picker chooses environment, not identity. **Previous execution label (historical):** APPLY / DESIGN · character art, copy prompt, then pack import. | PARTIAL · P3 |

| G175 | <a id="g175"></a>**Pixel-art marks — every source, agent and app in the study room drawn as pixel art, never a default icon** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "we should do pixel art for logos like gpt, codex, claude code mascot, claude app, telegram, etc. No default icons for things that have a symbol representation.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Some drawn marks and art foundations exist. The complete per-source/agent/app pixel-mark set is not delivered; track an explicit inventory and integrate each only after review. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g175). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The ask.** Inside the pixel world — the Sleep page's study room, the bookworm's props, the pile's book plates, the menu-bar worm's badge, and any sprite the room grows (the CRT, the phone, the TV, the letter tray, the calendar; `docs/specs/2026-09-29-study-room-sprites-brief.md`) — every thing that has its own symbol is drawn as a pixel-art rendition of that symbol at the room's cell size: ChatGPT, Codex, Claude Code's own pixel mascot, the Claude app, Telegram, Chrome, Safari, YouTube, X, Reddit, LinkedIn, Pinterest, Apple Notes, Calendar, Wispr Flow, Gemini, OpenRouter, Ollama, Cursor and the rest of `OriginIconography`'s map. A generic SF Symbol or a grey placeholder is used only for a thing that has no symbol of its own ("other agents", an unknown origin — which the concept already draws as a brown-paper parcel with a tag). **What exists.** The concept (`app/CicadaApp/Art/sprites/concept-2026-09-29/`) puts the app's real raster marks on the pile's plates (12 px, `boards/Queue*`), not pixel renditions. **The rule this amends.** Track L in CLAUDE.md (*Brand marks*): "Nominative use only — a vendor mark is never restyled or recoloured … Drawn brand glyphs are gone and do not come back." This row is the owner's decision to allow pixel-art renditions **inside the pixel-art world only** — the study room, its props and the worm's sprites; every other surface (rows, cards, Settings, the Feed, onboarding) keeps the real marks under Track L unchanged. **Constraints any build must respect.** (1) Recognisable and faithful at the cell grid — the mark's own shape and colours, no parody, no merging two marks, no mark on the worm itself (the worm's 11-key palette stays its own). (2) Nominative only: a mark names the source or app a book, phone or screen stands for; it never implies an endorsement or a partnership. (3) Provenance like `logos.manifest.json`: each pixel mark is a file with its source mark, the vendor's brand-guideline URL where one exists, and a note that it is a pixel rendition; a vendor whose guidelines forbid altered logos is listed there and gets the owner's call (keep, or fall back to a neutral kind glyph for that vendor). (4) Authored with the sprites pipeline (headless Aseprite + Lua, per-mark tags if animated, e.g. a phone notification pulse), exported with the room's sheets. (5) A dated TODO ruling records the Track L amendment's scope when the first pixel marks ship. → relates **Track L** (brand marks), **G107/G125** (the study room), the sprites brief, **G146/G159** (pictures and logos). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 DECIDE (scope ruled by the owner 2026-09-29; the per-vendor guideline check is the open part). **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** third-party marks in the app's chrome come from each vendor's own mark in one tile (**G217**); the study room's pixel marks are unchanged. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G184 | <a id="g184"></a>**App energy left after the Sleep page fix — the stage strip's pulse, the painted scenes' frame clocks, and the menu-bar worm** (measured 2026-10-05 while fixing G176's Sleep page CPU) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Sleep-page CPU fix landed. Stage pulse uses TimelineView and other scene/menu clocks remain; profile visible/hidden/current app before changing them. Reported costs are dated measurements, not new live profiling. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g184). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The mechanism (ruling 18's player amendment):** on macOS 26 a SwiftUI `TimelineView` whose entries fall under ~0.3 s apart lays the whole window out at the display rate (240 host layouts a second for 4 ticks in an isolated test). #170 moved the room's sprites and the wall clock onto layers (`SpriteLayerPlayer`): the Sleep page went from 12% to ~1% of a core. **Still on that pattern:** **(a) — done 2026-10-16 (`fix/sleep-run-strip`): the stepper's live dot breathes on its own Core Animation layer (`LiveDot`), no `TimelineView` left in `SleepStageStrip`.** Was: `SleepStageStrip`'s pulse, `.periodic(by: SleepStages.pulsePeriod / pulseSteps)` = 0.1 s, so while a run is active the Sleep page lays out at 120 fps again — the page most often left open during a run. Fix: the pulse as a Core Animation opacity animation on a layer (runs in the render server, no main-thread frames), still stepped and still capped at 1.2 s, static under Reduce Motion. **(b)** Home's painted scene (`PaintedScene`), the drifting clouds (`MeadowBackdrop.DriftingCloud`) and the export walkthrough use `TimelineView(.animation(minimumInterval:))` — ≤ 30 fps of content by design (R-HO7), but the host still lays out every display frame while they are visible. Measure first; if it shows, move the drift to a layer animation. **(c) The menu-bar worm** costs ~0.7% of a core whether or not the window is open: each frame AppKit redraws the status item and re-snapshots its copy on every other display, ~10 ms a frame (baking the frames into bitmaps did not help — tried and reverted 2026-10-05). The owner (2026-10-05) left it as is: "is it negligible?" — yes. Options if it ever matters: hold still after a mood change's beat, or slower calm loops — a design call (DECIDE). **How to measure:** `top -l 13 -s 5 -pid`, the SwiftUI Instruments template's `RootDisplayList` count, and a screenshot diff to prove the surface is actually animating (a covered window pauses the room by design and reads as 'cheap'). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 APPLY (a, b) · 📝 DECIDE (c). | CONDITIONAL · P3 |
| G137 | <a id="g137"></a>**Meadow — a visual system where nature is the ground and glass is the chrome, friendlier to a general public** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "improve the design of the cicada app … friendlier towards the general public, and in a sense have more that nature and technology futuristic in harmony collaboration look … liquid glass and the nature like features … Add animations to the icons as i hover over them") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Meadow/Direction D components and many later style passes exist. Reconcile remaining M2 labels with current screens through a visual review; old stage labels cannot justify a whole redesign. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g137). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What was there:** one theme file with a violet near-black and a cool near-white, SF only, no display face, no motion tokens — 47 literal animation durations across 13 files, none honouring Reduce Motion outside the Sleep page — zero symbol effects, a "glass" card that is a material over an opaque page, a sidebar painting an opaque fill over what macOS 26 renders as a glass sidebar, and an Info.plist promising macOS 13 to a package built for 14. **The rules** (round-3 spec Decision 6, R-M1…R-M7; plan `2026-09-23-meadow-foundation.md` R-M8…R-M25): (1) **glass only in the chrome layer** — sidebar, toolbar, floating controls, one prominent action per page — through one `liquidGlass(_:in:)` modifier gated on macOS 26 with a material fallback that goes opaque under Reduce Transparency; content cards stay a standard material; `LiquidGlassLintTests` keeps every glass API in one file; (2) **art only on non-data surfaces** — never the graph, a list, a grid, a form or a number, and text never directly on paint; every painting ships a `-dark` sibling and a manifest entry (generator, prompt, date, licence, sha256) held to its bytes by `ArtAssetTests`; `MeadowPlacementLintTests` keeps the allowlist of files that may draw it; clouds drift (≤ 8 pt, 60–120 s, paused under Reduce Motion and in a non-key window), grass never moves; (3) **font roles** — **SF Pro Display** for display at ≥ 22 pt (semibold, 2 % tight; the owner found the serif too ornate, 2026-09-23 — F1 R-FX12), New York italic for the person's quoted words, SF for everything else and every number; (4) **motion is a vocabulary** — `CicadaMotion`, nil under Reduce Motion, the only place outside `SleepMotion` a duration is spelled (`MotionLiteralLintTests`); `hoverLift()` for things that open, `iconHover()` for glyphs; (5) **neutrals moved, data did not** — a warm day meadow and a blue-green night meadow; entity, state and context hues are byte-identical, and graph.js's painted twins are held to the theme by `GraphPaletteTwinTests`. **M1 foundation built on `feat/meadow-foundation`:** tokens + nature tokens + sky gradients, the bundled display face (removed in F1: SF Pro Display since), the motion vocabulary + the 47-site migration, the glass modifier + primary action, the glass sidebar with hover glyphs, the art set + components, `EmptyStateView` as the first surface, and `LSMinimumSystemVersion` 14.0. **Open:** the M2 pass (every remaining page, logos wherever a service is named, hover everywhere, glass on the graph's floating controls only after the G109 frame-time check); the light entity-hue contrast debt the corrected theme comment now states (skill 2.93:1) is a separate data-colour decision graph.js must match. → relates **G130** (every new token scales with `uiScale`), **G125** (art encodes state, never quantity; every art bit has a text twin), **G109** (no glass or art over the graph canvas without the frame-time check). **Round 4 (T-Home):** the art set was repainted as pastel meadows in Monet's spirit, a touch more realistic, one composition in three lights (36 files, `docs/design/ART_DIRECTION.md`, 20 MiB budget); `-dark` retired; inside `PaintedScene` the grass sways and clouds cross one way (R-HO3 overrides R-M6 there, never in the empty states). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ M1 foundation built; M2 open. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G109 | <a id="g109"></a>**🔴 URGENT — graph physics: deceleration is invisible and disconnected nodes explode into a ring** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, testing the shipped G84 fix: "the smooth deceleration after stopped holding a node doesnt happen and sometimes when i go back to the graph view all the nodes kind of explode… Isn't there opensource graphs we can base ourselves in for the physics and reactiveness? maybe obsidian?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Several physics/lifecycle fixes landed; phases and visual acceptance remain. Reproduce disconnected-node/drag behavior against current graph.js; historical remount defects must not be reopened from old notes. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g109). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Two distinct problems; the first is a tuning failure, the second is a regression I introduced.** **(1) Deceleration is implemented but tuned to be invisible.** `graph.js:809` sets `velocityDecay(0.45)` — each tick keeps only 55% of velocity, so a throw damps to 5% in **five ticks (~80 ms)** and reads as an instant stop. `alphaMin(0.05)` (`:810`) compounds it: d3's default is `0.001`, so the simulation *halts* far earlier than normal, and the release code's `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2))` buys only ~27 ticks before the sim stops entirely. The seeding logic added in G84(b) is correct — `seededDragVelocity` (`:1384`) and the smoothed per-move sampling (`:1447-1454`) do compute a real world-space velocity — but the damping constants swallow it. **A visible glide needs `velocityDecay` nearer 0.15-0.20 and `alphaMin` nearer d3's default**, which was deliberately avoided because of a documented instability at ~1,500 nodes — so this is a real tension, not an oversight, and it needs to be solved rather than re-tuned blindly. **(2) The "explosion" is disconnected nodes, and G84(a) caused it to be visible.** Fixing the cold-paint bug (`minDegree` 1 → 0) made every **zero-degree node render for the first time**. `forceManyBody` repels them, `forceCenter` is only `strength(0.04)` (`:825`), and with no links to pull them in they fly to the periphery and settle in the characteristic ring the user photographed — hundreds of pink (family-context) dots orbiting the connected core. So the nodes were always mis-laid-out; they were previously hidden. **d3-force has no good answer for disconnected nodes**: the honest options are a containment force (`forceX/forceY` toward the centre with real strength for degree-0 nodes — the `xType`/`yType` anchors at `:831-832` already exist and just need to bind these), a separate cluster/grid region for isolated nodes, or excluding them from the simulation and laying them out deterministically. **(3) The user's question deserves a real answer — evaluate alternatives rather than keep hand-tuning.** Candidates worth measuring at ~1,900 nodes / 6,800 links: **Obsidian's own approach** (Pixi.js WebGL renderer + custom forces — the closest analogue, and this project's own `CLAUDE.md` already cites it as the scale precedent), **cosmograph** (GPU/WebGL force layout, built for far larger graphs), **sigma.js + graphology** (ForceAtlas2, mature, worker-based), **ngraph.forcelayout**, and **d3-force-3d**. The decision axes: does it fix disconnected-node layout, does it give real momentum/settling, does it stay smooth at this scale, and what does it cost to port the existing behaviour (deltas keyed on `content_hash`, focus mode, facet rings, logo bitmaps, the observer dim, hub sizing). **Do not port blindly** — the current renderer carries a lot of earned behaviour. → fixes the remaining half of **G84**; interacts with **G84(c)(d)** (legend/colour work touches the same file); relates **G107** (the same "we hand-rolled art/physics that a library does better" question). **Ruling (2026-09-02, research run: inventory → five candidates → three judges → decision memo + critic):** keep d3-force and fix `graph.js` (judges 8.33/10; runner-up sigma.js + graphology + ForceAtlas2 at 5.64 — measured to contain isolates natively and 60 fps in a real WKWebView, but no velocity model, an esbuild step, and a 12–20-day port of the earned behaviours; Pixi is a renderer-only stage deferred behind the flip trigger; cosmos rebuilds velocity in the shader so a throw is impossible; ngraph's isolates are unbounded and `stable` never fires; d3-force-3d is byte-identical in 2D). The symptoms were three local bugs: (a) `hubGravityForce` ignored `alpha` — a permanent 5 %/tick spring against the never-alpha-scaled `forceCollide`, the real origin of the "1,500 nodes bounce forever" folklore that justified `velocityDecay` 0.45 / `alphaMin` 0.05 (bench: KE/node at tick 400 = 13–23 with alpha at 1e-9; 4e-6 once scaled); (b) the release path's `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2))` reheated the whole graph (~1,000 wu mean displacement of every other node) and let the link springs cancel a throw in one tick; (c) `forceCenter` is a uniform translation and cannot pull a node, so nothing opposed charge on degree-0 nodes. **Flip trigger:** p95 tick+draw > 16.7 ms on the live bank after phases 1–2 with isolates excluded (phase 3), or > ~10k nodes. **Phase 1 shipped (PR #32)** — `graph.js` only, no Swift, no dependency: hub gravity alpha-scaled (nominal 0.05 kept, id map hoisted into `initialize`); release `alphaTarget(0).restart()` with no bump; hold `alphaTarget(0.1)`; `alphaMin` 0.001; `velocityDecay` 0.2; `alphaDecay` 0.05 and `distanceMax` 700 unchanged; each isolate (`!hub && !memberToHub && !neighborsById` — visible degree 0 from `visibleLinks`, orphan facets included) owns a stable per-type free-list slot on a phyllotaxis disc (c = 20) centred on its type anchor, pulled by the existing `forceX/forceY` at 0.3 and exerting −30 charge; a last-registered `clampSpeed` at 60 wu/tick. Measured through `graph.js` itself on synthetic 300 / 1,500 / 1,500-dense graphs (`Tests/graph/graph-physics.bench.js`, real d3): KE/node@400 13 / 20 / 23 → 4e-16 / 4e-6 / 4e-5; a 28 wu/tick flick from rest 0 → 12–13 ticks, 0 → 63 / 99 / 100 wu; other-node displacement over the second after a release 979 / 1,194 / 1,275 → 1.8 / 8.8 / 28 wu; isolate max radius 2.0× (after the hub-gravity fix alone; on dev the metric is masked by the core itself sitting at r≈2,000) → 1.3× core p90 on the 1,500-node graphs and never beyond 1.1× the farthest connected node; core median within 4 %; ms/tick unchanged (~6.5–6.9). Superseded memo values: isolate pull 0.10 (measured to leave the ring at 1.6×; the anchor scale 1.0–1.8 does not move the outcome because per-node charge sets what a body *exerts*, not what it receives), and the "≤ 1.2× core p90" / "no isolate beyond 700 wu" criteria (geometrically incompatible with a disc beside its type cluster; replaced by the four asserted above). Disclosed: reheats run longer at `alphaMin` 0.001 (cold 59 → 135 ticks, a 0.3 reheat 35 → 112) and the cold layout still freezes at the alpha cut-off while relaxing; a new isolate from a delta seeds at its type anchor ±30 and transits ~350 wu to the disc's resting radius (the disc rests ~300 wu outward of the anchor it is centred on, so the newcomer crosses its type cluster over ~112 ticks — phase 3 seeds at the slot); and **a no-op delta — the app's most frequent reheat, every SSE version tick after a Sleep — still shuffles a packed core** (bench `deltaNoop*`, final review M1): core mean / max 8.8 / 49, 31 / 173, 80 / 573 wu on small / medium / dense (was 1,289 / 1,480 / 1,723 mean at `b690b66`; the test bounds it at < 200 mean), facets the top movers (one facet accumulates 1,082 wu over three deltas), isolate discs not the source (max 155). The delta reheat 0.3 → 0.1 is a real lever on medium density (core mean / max 31 / 173 → 19 / 60) but not on dense (80 / 573 → 73 / 340; ~65 wu mean even at 0.05): the residual is the never-alpha-scaled `forceCollide` re-resolving a core the alpha-scaled forces re-compress on every reheat, and the live bank (3.6 links/node) is denser than the dense synthetic (3.0). Left at 0.3 by R8/R10 — a tuning outcome, not a phase-1 fix. **Open — phase 2** (own the rAF loop: physical settle criterion, one tick per frame, `__cicadaPerf.report()` with tick/draw p50/p95 and the isolate/core radii, then the live-bank tuning pass over `ISOLATE_ANCHOR_STRENGTH` 0.2–0.5 / spacing 12–20 / hold 0.05–0.1 / vd 0.15–0.2 / **delta reheat 0.3 → 0.1 plus a collide lever (`iterations`/strength, or collide off below an alpha floor) for the no-op-delta shuffle** / `clearFocus` 0.2 → 0.1, the largest remaining whole-graph reheat on one gesture); **phase 3** (exclude isolates from the simulation: tick 6.7 → 4.5 ms measured, containment by construction, `pickNode` fallback scan); and the **Swift track** — `ContentView.swift:137-139` rebuilds the `WKWebView` on every tab switch, which is where "explosion on return" comes from (keep one web view alive; reset `isGraphReady` on teardown). DS-3a (2026-09-24): three new bridges — `backgroundClicked`, `escape`, `setSelectedNode` — move only the zoom transform and are tested alpha-free (`graph-canvas-bridge.test.js`); the floating controls stay opaque until the frame-time check (R-DG2). **Audit 2026-10-02 follow-up (A07/A08/A09, `fix/audit-graph-energy`):** the "rebuild on each tab switch" this row describes is gone since 2026-09-03 (`GraphPage` stays mounted at opacity 0), which made the opposite the problem: a pending node kept the whole graph redrawing every frame on any tab, a strong coordinator ↔ web view cycle kept closed graphs alive, and a lost mouseup left `alphaTarget(0.1)` hot forever. Now `setGraphActive` (tab + window occlusion) stops frames and the d3 timer while hidden and resumes without a reheat; a settled graph pulses only for an on-screen pending node, at ~30 fps on elapsed-time phase; the coordinator holds the view weakly and `dismantleNSView` tears it down; one `cancelInteraction` ends an interrupted drag. d3-force stays. **Settle and organisation pass (2026-10-14, `fix/graph-settle`).** Rodrigo 2026-10-14: "The graph nodes don't stop moving ever until it freezes … They never reach a state of equilibrium. They should, and it shouldn't take long. Also some nodes, like two small ones, are pulling super far … I want to see some organisation in the equilibrium." Measured on a synthetic with the live bank's shape (`Tests/graph/graph-bank-shape.js`, no names: 3,698 nodes, 41,570 links, 150 components — one of 3,529, then 6/4/3/3/3, six pairs, 138 isolates — a 2,092-degree page, leaf stars of 226/44/23/9/8/8/8/7). **Not a reheat loop:** no-op deltas, hover, logo pushes, zoom and hide/show start nothing (tested). **Three causes:** (1) the fixed link strength 0.28 on each of a dense page's ~100 links is a spring constant past what the integrator holds, so the core ran at the speed clamp until the alpha cut-off froze it (KE/node 120–130, fastest node 48 wu/tick at tick 135 — it never looked still); (2) `forceCollide` was never alpha-scaled, so what was left boiled (KE/node at tick 125: 11 with it, 6e-3 without); (3) nothing held a pair or a triple, and leaves were pushed out by the body's summed charge (the 226 leaves at a median 933 wu from their page; the 44-leaf star's at 800, max 1,400 — the star "far outside on long links"). **Fix (graph.js only, d3-force kept):** one simulation link per connected pair (28,697 for 41,570 drawn) at d3's default strength 1/min(degree); charge −40·√(neighbours+1) (ForceAtlas2's degree-weighted repulsion) at θ 1.2; a link's rest length is both discs + 24 wu (8 for a leaf); collide's strength scaled by alpha below 0.03; every component of ≤ 30 nodes other than the largest is a satellite packed in a band just outside the body's p99 radius, one sector per type, out of the body's charge (Obsidian rings its orphans; cytoscape fcose/cola pack components instead of simulating them against the main one) — this replaces the per-type isolate discs inside the body; a cold paint seeds a phyllotaxis disc per anchor and a new satellite in its slot; a cooled layout (alpha < 0.05) ends once no node moves > 1 wu/tick and ≤ 2 % move > 0.3 (phase 2's physical settle criterion); a hold on a settled graph pins all but the dragged page and its neighbours (item 6's holds); links draw as one path per colour and alpha. **Before → after** (node, three seeds; WebKit via the opt-in `GraphWebKitBenchTests` bank-shape bench): at the stop KE/node 120–130 → 0.009–0.017 and the fastest node 48 → 0.6–0.95 wu/tick; 95 % of nodes under 1 wu/tick never → by step 97–101; the timer stops at 135 → 124–133 steps; ms/tick 25–27 → 19 in node, 17 → 12 (p50) in WebKit, so a cold layout's tick CPU 2.4 → 1.7 s there; the farthest node 1.36–1.37 → 1.28–1.32 giant p95 radii, every non-giant node in a band at 1.06–1.32; the owner page's leaves median 914–933 → 154–176 wu, the 44-leaf star's 381–881 → 99–139 (max 1,020–1,400 → 164–199); planted-community separation 2.4–2.5 → 4.6–4.9; overlapping discs 48 % → 37–43 %; canvas strokes per frame 41,586 → 18 (WebKit's JS draw time is 5 ms either way: a stroke's cost lands in rasterization, which the bench cannot see); a hold on a leaf moved the rest of the graph 234 wu mean → 0; frames requested after the stop 0. `graph-settle.test.js` holds these; `graph-physics.test.js`'s isolate checks now describe the band. **The ring (2026-10-14, `fix/graph-circle`).** Rodrigo, after #285 went live: "the graph still looks a bit weird, would be nice if it resembled a circle in the end." The live graph then carried 982 isolates, 844 of one type (bookmarks just imported), and #285's one-30°-sector-per-type band stacked them into a slab above the body. The band is now an annulus around the whole body: its thickness is what the satellites' total footprint needs at that radius, units stack outward into columns, and the columns share 360° by width, so each type is an arc sized by its count. On the bank shape with 1,000 isolates in the live mix (`LIVE_ISOLATES_2026_10_14`, two seeds): satellite coverage 302° → 357°, convex hull over the circle through the farthest node 0.40 → 0.96–0.98, farthest node per 15° bin max/min 2.07 → 1.02–1.05, farthest node 2.05 → 1.24 giant radii; the timer still stops at 123–130 steps at rest. A radial wall keeping body pages inside the ring was tried and dropped (no measurable change: ~6 body pages reach < 20 wu past the ring's inner edge). The medium synthetic's outward flick now coasts 9 ticks (was 13): it ends against the ring ~86 wu out. **Still open:** the flip trigger's p95 tick+draw on the live bank in the app, and the pending-node pulse, which redraws the whole canvas at ~30 fps while a pending node is on screen (A07, by design). **The Obsidian look (2026-10-15, `fix/graph-obsidian`).** Rodrigo: "Right now it looks like a weird circle. Can you look at pictures of an Obsidian vault and make it look different?" Measured on a synthetic with the bank's shape that day (`bankShaped1015`: 4,544 nodes, 41,570 links over only 9,046 pairs, a 3,529 giant, 984 isolates of which 846 saved links; the owner's page 16,679 raw links). **Causes:** (1) size read raw links, so the owner's page drew a 206 wu disc and a median page 14 wu — discs packed edge to edge (51-52 % overlapping) covering a quarter of the fitted view, hiding the links; (2) each of 37 hubs carried a ring, and pending pulses 6-16 px out read as empty brown circles; (3) the fit view drew only hubs' links and parallel claims drew one line each; (4) the band stacked 846 isolates into ruled concentric rows; (5) three things lined dots into ruled arcs through the body, each found by elimination on renders: hubs pinned to an evenly spaced ring 400 wu out, the owner's page dragged to the person anchor, and above all a charge that grew with neighbours, so every heavy page cleared a hole and the pages between holes formed chains (a foam; capped at 50 neighbours still visible, at 6 faint, uniform gone). **Fix (graph.js, d3-force kept, every custom force still alpha-scaled):** radius 2.2 + 0.8·confidence + 0.45·√(distinct neighbours), no hub multiplier or ring; one hairline per pair and colour at every zoom (α 0.55 neutral, 0.22 coloured); one charge for every body page (−40·√2); links dissuaded by √(50/n) past 50 neighbours, never a leaf's (ForceAtlas2's "dissuade hubs"); a page with > 50 neighbours seeded and pulled (0.3) to the middle; hubs free of their ring anchor; the band became a halo — each unit's angle and depth hashed from its own id (stable across pushes), round the body's edge read per 10° and smoothed, a soft triangular depth profile and a wobbling outer reach; the pulse ring 1.5-5.5 px out; names fade in with zoom (screen radius 3.8 → 6 px, biggest first, budget 200, no overlap); the Legend's *Show unlinked* switch (`minDegree` 1 off). Borrowed: Obsidian's small dots, faint links, uniform repel and orphan halo; ForceAtlas2's hub dissuasion and degree gravity; d3's phyllotaxis seeding. **Before → after** (node, seeds 7/3, real d3 and graph.js; PNGs rendered by graph.js's own `draw()` on a headless canvas): radius p10/median/p90/p99/max 9.0/14.4/18.1/22.5/205.7 → 2.7/3.5/3.9/4.4/23.3 wu (max/median 14.3 → 6.7); discs over the fitted view 25-26 % → 1.3 %; overlapping discs 51-52 % → 0; halo nearest-neighbour CV 0.09-0.15 → 0.49-0.50, Clark–Evans 1.67-1.75 (lattice-like) → 1.03-1.04 (random); stop 131 → 111-113 steps (2.7 → 1.9 s of node tick CPU), ms/tick p50 20 → 16.3-16.5; farthest node 1.28-1.29 → 1.49-1.50 giant radii (the halo is looser by design; the settle tests' bound is now 1.7); frames after the stop 0. `graph-look.test.js` holds these; the settle and physics tests' halo bounds were widened with the measured values beside them. **Proportion (2026-10-15, `fix/graph-proportion`).** Rodrigo, after #291 on the real graph: "the lines connecting nodes are too big compared to the size of the nodes, they should be super thin. And nodes can be a bit bigger, relative to the amount of nodes there are in the graph, so that they also have a limit of how big and how small they can be, in a way that it always looks good and proportional." **Cause:** radii were fixed world units, so a graph's on-screen dot size was whatever its fit zoom gave — on the bank shape (1200×800 view, fit k 0.27) a leaf was a 0.67 pt radius speck, the median 0.95 pt, under a 1 pt line (0.75× the smallest dot's diameter); a 50-node graph's median was 6.4 pt. **Fix (graph.js):** one `sizeScale` multiplies every radius (order kept; collide, link rest lengths and halo footprints use it); the target is the median dot's on-screen radius at the fit zoom = √(6 % of the view ÷ (π × visible nodes)), clamped to 2.2–8 pt — a coverage share, so it falls as 1/√N until the floor; because the fit depends on the layout, the scale follows the fit each tick of a full layout while alpha ≥ 0.15 (damped, ×(want/s)^0.3; the fit zoom is within ~5 % of its final value by then) and is fixed after (a warm full layout — a filter change — adapts until alpha 0.02: 251 pages filtered out of the bank shape contract its whole run, and a cut at 0.15 left a 10.0 pt median), and a push of newcomers keeps it so held dots never grow into each other. Review fix: a full push none of whose nodes carries a position (a bank switch sends a blank graph, then the new bank) is a cold layout with a fit — before, 50 → blank → 4.5k kept the demo's scale (median 1.19 pt) — and one whose carried layout wants a scale > 1.25× off (a switch back to a cached bank, a bank sharing ids) relays every node out from where it is and fits; 50 → blank → 4.5k → 50 → 4.5k now gives 2.22 / 7.97 / 2.19 pt, 4.5k → 50 7.96 pt, the people filter 8.1 pt. Two things the bigger dots needed: a satellite keeps the halo room it had at scale 1 (never under 1.8 footprints) — without it the halo grew with the dots, the farthest node reached 1.9–2.1 giant radii and the fit zoom fell to the 0.2 clamp — and `MIN_ZOOM` 0.2 → 0.1. Links: 0.12 of the smallest dot's on-screen diameter, ≤ 0.75 pt, drawn one device pixel wide at proportionally lower alpha when thinner; hover/focus-lit links 1 pt. Ambient names read the scale-1 radius, so they follow the zoom as before. **Measured** (node, real d3; 1200×800): median dot at fit 50 / 500 / 4.5k nodes 6.4 / 2.2 / 0.95 → 7.7 / 6.0 / 2.2 pt (scale 1.24 / 2.8 / 2.34), smallest page dot 4.5 / 1.5 / 0.67 → 5.3 / 4.2 / 1.54 pt; in an 800×560 view 500 nodes 4.0 pt, 4.5k 2.15 pt; disc coverage on the bank shape 1.3 % → 7.1–7.3 %; overlap 0 at every size; a resting link 1 pt → 0.37 pt at the bank's fit (one device pixel at α×0.74), ≤ 0.12 of the smallest dot's diameter at every zoom tier; stop 111–113 → 108–109 steps; farthest node 1.49–1.50 (unchanged); halo nearest-neighbour CV 0.49–0.50 → 0.32–0.33 (bigger discs exclude the closest pairs; Clark–Evans unchanged). `graph-look.test.js` holds the floor and ceiling across 50 / 500 / 4.5k (`bankShapedAt`), the view dependence and the link width per zoom tier. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ **phase 1 shipped (PR #32); phases 2–3 + Swift track open**. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G106 | <a id="g106"></a>**Two-way navigation between conversations and the entities they produced** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a page in the UI for the user to only see and search raw conversations… and the entities that were benefitted from the information there, so that you can track both ways") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Evidence Reader and conversation/entity routing exist; navigation stack code also exists. Verify the requested standalone conversation browsing, inverse links and back/forward behavior rather than assuming all navigation is missing. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g106). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** The pieces exist and are not connected into a loop. Today: `source_episodes` links an entity to its episodes, `entity_sources.py` resolves episodes back to whole conversations, G48 gives conversations identity and resumability, and `/conversations/recent` lists them — but there is **no surface that goes both ways**, and no way to search conversations by their *content*. **What to build, as one loop.** (i) A **Conversations page**: browse and full-text search raw conversations by what was said, not just by title or date — the vector index already holds episode embeddings, so semantic search over conversations is largely a query away, with a literal/regex mode alongside it for exact recall. (ii) From a conversation, **list the entities it produced or updated** — derivable today by inverting `source_episodes` — each clickable through to its entity page. (iii) From an **entity**, show the conversation snippets that justify it, each clickable through to that conversation *at the right place*. Step (iii) is where **G100**'s span citation pays off: without it the link lands on a wall of text; with it, the exact sentence is highlighted, and prev/next walks every contributing conversation — the NotebookLM behaviour the user asked for. **Why this is more than a browser:** it makes the memory system *falsifiable by the user*. Today a belief is a summary you either trust or do not; with the loop closed, any belief can be traced to the sentence that produced it, and any conversation shows what it taught Cicada — which is the honest version of "transparency over magic" (UX principle 2), and the strongest possible answer to "why does it think that". **Sequencing:** the inverse index (ii) is cheap and works today; content search is next; (iii) is gated on G100. Ship them in that order rather than waiting for all three. → depends on **G100** (span citation) for the deep link, **G101** (what raw content is retained) for what there is to search; extends **G48** (conversation identity + resume), **G52** (Ask, which should cite into this same viewer); serves **G79** (cross-stream retrieval needs the user to be able to *see* the cross-stream link). **The inbox is the first caller (G115):** its `c` / "Open conversation" needs (i)'s page to land on a conversation with the cause episode scrolled into view, and its `e` / "Open entity" already works through `graphVM.selectEntity(id:)`; ship the inverse index (ii) and the conversation-with-anchor route before G115 Phase 2, or "Open conversation" opens a list instead of a place. **G118 slice 2 (server) ships (ii) and the anchor route:** `GET /episodes/{id}/citations` is the inverse index at claim precision (spans, derived name matches for legacy claims, and the pages that list the episode), and `GET /episodes/{id}/text` is the conversation-with-anchor route (whole text, turns, an asserted or derived focus); (i)'s content search and the page itself remain (Track S, the Swift viewer). **G118 slice 2 (app) closes (ii) and (iii) in the UI:** the Reader opens a conversation at the cited sentence from a claim's evidence chip, a "Where this came from" row, an inbox cause or an Ask citation, steps through the spans the entity cites, and lists "Noted from this conversation" with each belief jumping to its words or landing on the graph; (i)'s browse-and-search page remains (Track S). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G130 | <a id="g130"></a>**App-wide zoom with ⌘+ / ⌘− — make the whole UI bigger or smaller, not just the graph canvas** (Rodrigo 2026-09-04: "using command + - i can modify the zoom of the UI") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** App zoom exists. Verify remaining fixed-width/sidebar clipping and pixel-art alignment at supported scales; graph page zoom is conditional on a demonstrated mismatch. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g130). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists today is the graph's zoom, not the app's.** `ZoomControls` in `ContentView.swift` drives `GraphViewModel.zoomAction` into the d3 transform inside the WKWebView — it scales *nodes and edges on the canvas* and nothing else. Every other surface (sidebar, Feed rows, Inbox cards, the Sleep queue, entity cards, Settings) renders at fixed sizes: `CicadaTheme` hardcodes `titleFont` 20 / `headingFont` 16 / `bodyFont` 13 / `captionFont` 11 and a fixed spacing scale, so there is no lever a keystroke could turn. **And the shortcut is not bound at all** — the app defines no `.commands` / `CommandGroup` anywhere, so it has no View menu and ⌘+/⌘− fall through to nothing; the eight existing `keyboardShortcut` calls are all view-local (⌘K, ⌘F, ⌘1–6, ⌘N, ⌘[). **(a) The lever should be one scale factor, not a font override.** A single persisted `uiScale` (`@AppStorage`, clamped, e.g. 0.8 – 1.6 in ~10% steps, 1.0 = today) that `CicadaTheme`'s font and spacing constants are *derived from* — which means those constants stop being `static let` and become reads through the same `@Observable` store the theme mode uses, so a change invalidates the views that read them. That is the PR #49 lesson repeated: a plain `static var` notifies nothing, and the setter must be idempotent or a write during render loops. **The `.id()` sledgehammer is forbidden here for the same reason it was removed in PR #49** — rebuilding the whole tree on a zoom step would tear down and reload the graph WKWebView on every keypress. **(b) The graph web view is the hard half, and it needs its own decision.** Scaling the SwiftUI chrome does not touch WKWebView content; the lever there is `pageZoom`, and it must not fight the d3 zoom transform the canvas already owns (a user who zoomed the graph to a neighbourhood must not have it silently re-fit). Cheapest honest answer for slice 1: **leave the graph canvas alone** — it has its own zoom controls and ⌘+/⌘− means *chrome*, not *canvas*, exactly as it does in a browser's own UI vs. a page's map widget. Revisit only if the graph's own labels read as mismatched beside scaled chrome. **(c) Bind it properly, not view-locally.** ⌘+/⌘−/⌘0 belong in a `CommandGroup(after: .sidebar)` (or `.toolbar`) View menu — `Zoom In` / `Zoom Out` / `Actual Size` — so the binding is discoverable in the menu bar, works from any tab, and does not collide with the graph's own controls. Note the keyboard reality: ⌘+ is really ⌘= on a US layout, so bind both `=` and `+`, as every browser does. **(d) Accessibility is the real reason this matters, and it argues for scope.** macOS Dynamic Type does not reach an AppKit/SwiftUI desktop app the way it does on iOS, so a fixed 11 pt `captionFont` is simply small on a 5K display with no system-level remedy — the app is the only place that can fix it. Which also means the setting belongs in Settings as a slider beside the appearance control, not only behind a keystroke. **Rails:** no backend involvement (this is `@AppStorage`, per-machine, never a bank file); the persisted value survives relaunch; `Actual Size` (⌘0) always returns to 1.0; scaled layouts must not clip — the existing `fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` habit in the status/hint views is the pattern; and the sidebar's ⌘1–6 must keep working at every scale. → extends **G107** (the mascot renders at fixed pt sizes and would need to pick a sprite scale), **G124** (the Sources grid is the densest surface and the first place a scale bug shows), the PR #49 theme-store work (same observation mechanism, same idempotent-setter trap); relates **G109** (never reheat or re-fit the graph as a side effect of a chrome change). APPLY, S–M — **slice 1** = the scale store + derived theme constants + the View menu commands + the Settings slider; **slice 2** (only if measured as needed) = the graph web view's `pageZoom`. **Slice 1a shipped (2026-09-05, PR #54)**: `ThemeStore.uiScale` (clamped `0.8...1.4`, stepped by `0.1`, `cicada.uiScale`), the five font + six spacing tokens now computed off it, `CicadaTheme.font(size:weight:design:)`, `zoomIn`/`zoomOut`/`resetZoom`, `BookwormRenderer.snappedPointSize` (mascot stays on 24 px cells), a View menu (`CommandGroup(after: .sidebar)`) for ⌘=/⌘−/⌘0 plus a local key monitor for ⌘⇧=, and a new Settings *General* tab (Appearance + a Text size slider). **Slice 1b shipped (2026-09-05, PR #58)** — the mechanical `.system(size:)` → `CicadaTheme.font(size:)` migration across the ~48 files that still had one, plus `FontLiteralLintTests` keeping new literals out. **Slice 2** (graph `pageZoom`) still open on a measured need. **Follow-up noted, not fixed:** the 96 literal-width `.frame(width:)` sites are untouched per R7 — any that visibly clip at the 1.4 cap on the demo bank is a follow-up line here, not a reason to touch them now. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ slice 1a + 1b shipped; slice 2 open. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G84 | <a id="g84"></a>**Graph: cold first paint hides nodes, drags stop dead, and the legend describes a different axis than the colors** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, four graph symptoms) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Cold paint and drag fixes landed. Legend/axis/observer controls and visual behavior need current acceptance; keep d3-force and alpha-scaled forces. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g84). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Four separate causes, all verified. **(a) Missing nodes on first entry** — `Coordinator.pushGraphData()` (`GraphView.swift:164-171`) pushes the full snapshot but never follows with `applyFilters`, unlike the other push site (`:74-79`, whose comment literally says "defaults hide … from first paint"). So the cold paint runs on graph.js's own defaults: `filters.minDegree = 1` (`graph.js:154`) drops every zero-degree node at `:702`, while Swift's default is `minDegree = 0` (`GraphFilter.swift:11`). The first control the user touches makes `prevMinDegree 1 ≠ 0`, `rebuildVisible()` runs, and the missing nodes appear — and stay. **Fix:** push the filter in `pushGraphData`'s completion handler exactly as `:76-78` does, and make the two defaults agree. **(b) Drag stops instantly** — not a stuck pin: `onMouseUp` (`graph.js:1386-1407`) correctly nulls `fx/fy` and zeroes `alphaTarget`. It's structural — while `fx/fy` are set d3 zeroes the node's velocity every tick, so at release `vx/vy` are exactly 0 and a throw is impossible by construction; pointer velocity is never sampled in `onMouseMove`. Compounded by `velocityDecay(0.55)` (`:770`, d3's default is 0.4 → motion damps in ~4 ticks/~65 ms). **Fix:** record the per-move world-space delta during drag, seed `vx/vy` from it on release before unpinning, then `alpha(max(alpha, 0.2)).restart()`. Related latent bug in the same seam: mouseup binds only on the canvas (`:1299-1302`), so releasing outside it never unpins. **(c) Legend vs colors** — two different fields conflated: node fill comes from `n.type` for all 1,769 non-facet nodes (`graph.js:1003-1005`), while the legend keys off the **claim-context** axis (`contextRoster`, `GraphViewModel.swift:180-183`, derived from valid claims at `graph_builder.py:141-153`). Only 89 of 1,858 nodes are facets. Worse, the palettes deliberately collide — `family → #F65BA6` is byte-identical to `typeColors.media`, `engineering → #2DD4BF` = tool, `career → #FF8A3D` = company — which is exactly why selecting "family" appears to highlight bookmarks. **Fix:** give contexts a palette disjoint from `typeColors` (duplicated in `graph.js:63-70` AND `CicadaTheme.swift:211-218` — change both or they desync), or stop encoding context as fill entirely and carry it on edges + facet rings, which is what actually holds context data; and label the legend for what it describes. **(d) All/Cicada/You/External means nothing** — it filters on **claim observer**, dim-only (alpha 0.1, `graph.js:1013`), and returns true unconditionally for the 56% of nodes with no claims (`:269-274`), so three segments look identical. Honest meanings, with live counts: All = no lens; Cicada = pages where the agent asserted a currently-valid claim (740); You = pages where you personally asserted one (107); External = pages where someone else is on record (3). **Fix:** relabel to `Everything · Inferred by Cicada · Stated by you · Heard from others`, and gate segments on non-empty membership the way `hasObserverDiversity` already gates the whole bar. **(a)(b) shipped 2026-09-01, PR #23** — `GraphView.swift:164-171` `pushGraphData` now re-applies filters on cold paint (a); `graph.js` throw-velocity tracking (`onMouseMove`/`onMouseUp`, `velocityDecay(0.45)`) fixes the drag-stop (b), both marked `G84a`/`G84b` in code comments. **(c)(d) remain open** — see TODO Wave B. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ (a)(b) shipped; (c)(d) open. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G86 | <a id="g86"></a>**Feed is an undifferentiated list, ~22% of it duplicate rows** (Rodrigo: "relevance and recent here seem to have the same view, and the view all in all i think it can be improved, its just a huge list of bookmarks, maybe something easier to navigate different types would be better") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Feed grouping/dedup foundations exist. Retain synthetic title-collision/junk cases and real relevance acceptance; new ranking should follow G10/G93 failures, not duplicate current recall machinery. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g86). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Verified and worse than it looks: 789 rows render 603 distinct pages.** `media_ingestor` slugifies the page TITLE into `media_entity_id`, so **148 separate Google consent-interstitial bookmarks collapse onto one entity** (`media-before-you-continue-to-google-search`, plus 27 on the Portuguese variant), while `GET /sources` iterates the url_index one row per URL (`sources.py:361`) — so all 175 render as visually identical rows with the same title, site and score. That padding is a large part of why the list feels unnavigable. **The Relevance-vs-Recent symptom reproduces but its first-pass cause was REFUTED on re-check — treat as unresolved and re-derive before fixing.** **Work:** (a) collapse rows sharing a `mediaEntityId` in any grouping UI, and stop minting media pages for consent/interstitial titles at ingest (prereq **G65(a)** slug collisions); (b) re-diagnose the sort toggle honestly; (c) grouping axes that exist on the wire today are thin — `MediaSourceItem` (`schemas.py:1082-1096`) has no `folder` and no `origin`, so navigation by platform/collection needs the payload widened first, which is the real prerequisite. **(Absorbs G65, merged 2026-09-01 — the fix-level detail from that row):** for (a), `media_ingestor._media_entity_id` slugifies the page *title* (not the URL), which is the root cause of the 148-way collision on "Before you continue to Google Search" — append the URL-hash suffix whenever the slug already exists for a *different* normalized URL (not only when >120 bytes), plus a one-shot migration for existing collisions. For (b), those 148 saves are Google consent interstitials, not content — detect by title/URL pattern at ingest, fetch through or mark `status: dropped`, and sweep existing ones. G65 also measured **why "Relevance" and "Recent" look identical**: the Feed badge shows §3.4 decayed confidence, constant ≈57% after a bulk sync because every item shares identical defaults and `personal_relevance_weight` is never written — either wire the search query into a vector-index score server-side or vary the inputs (per-item `last_referenced` bumps on open, weight from Sleep). *(G65's first-pass cause for the sort-toggle symptom was itself refuted on re-check — treat the toggle as genuinely unresolved, not just unmeasured, and re-derive before fixing.)* UI-side row-identity fixes (unique `entityId\|url` key, badge hidden while rendered percentages are uniform) already shipped 2026-08-31. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G87 | <a id="g87"></a>**Bank selection: the invariant is already true, the product just never says so — and one import path can silently write to a non-active bank** (Rodrigo: "the view to select different projects or create new ones can be improved. We have to be able to select only one active which is the one we contribute to by default") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Active-bank identity and migration-on-switch exist. Scheduler bank-switch behavior and remaining copy/selection seams need verification; actual optimistic switch race is G183. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g87). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Verified: exactly-one-active is ALREADY the model.** `banks.yaml` has a single scalar `active:`; `bank_registry.activate_bank:279` is a plain assignment; `resolve_active_bank_path:64-92` returns one directory; no multi-active representation exists anywhere. And **every automatic writer resolves it fresh** — `Settings.memory_path` is a computed property (`config.py:39-41`), the MCP server re-resolves per tool call (`mcp/server.py:480-500`), and Telegram, links, bookmarks, RSS, connector syncs, Sleep and inbox resolution all route through it. The user is asking for a guarantee the system already makes and never states. **The one real hole:** `POST /banks/{name}/import` (`banks.py:162-207`) writes into an explicitly named bank that need not be active and never activates it, and the app exposes it as a free picker (`UploadOverlay.swift:203-224`). Episodes staged into a non-active bank are invisible to Sleep — **a whole chat-history export can silently go nowhere**, and it is the highest-volume write in the product. **Fix:** on a non-active import, replace the success toast with the consequence plus a one-click remedy ("Imported into X — not your active project, so it won't be consolidated. [Switch to X]"), or drop the picker and always import into the active bank. **Two more gaps:** (i) the switcher's only discriminator is a raw `glob("*.md")` count (`bank_registry.py:213-244`), so **the biggest number belongs to the most abandoned bank** — add the bank's git HEAD commit date and render "last written today" / "never consolidated", plus `DELETE /banks/{name}` guarded against the active one; (ii) `BankSwitcher` is instantiated exactly once, inside the Graph tab's overlay (`ContentView.swift:197`), so **the active bank is invisible on the 5 other pages that write to it** — hoist it to the sidebar footer beside the Settings gear, which also fixes the bank-vs-filter confusion by physically separating it from the graph's lens chips (today both render with the identical `.glassCard` at the same font, unlabelled). **Latent, not yet biting:** the sleep schedule is loaded once from the BOOT bank (`main.py:119`) and never re-registered on activate, so after a switch the schedule comes from the old bank while the cycle consolidates the new one. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ invariant half shipped (`315e077`, PR #24 — `BankImportResponse.active` + a one-click "Switch to X" toast when an import lands in a non-active bank). Remaining: the BankSwitcher discriminator (git HEAD date vs. raw glob count), hoisting the switcher out of the Graph tab into the sidebar footer, and the boot-bank sleep-schedule staleness bug are all still open. **Track I T5 (2026-09-23):** every import path now reaches the non-active-bank consequence — the intake preview's *Into* (this memory, another, or a new one) replaces the overlay's project mode, and the done card says the memory isn't active, with Switch. The discriminator and the sidebar hoist stay open.. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G139 | <a id="g139"></a>**Settings v3 — Claude-style settings with a search that lands on the row** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "feel free to work on a good settings page with a search bar like the one in claude") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Settings search, indexed rows, deep links and page groups exist. Named keyboard/popover follow-ups need current checks; maintenance dedup's uncommitted merge is G21/G183, not a missing Settings framework. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g139). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What was there:** five sections in one flat list, each page a stack of glass cards with hand-retyped headers; engine choice split across Sleep's picker and a "Use for Sleep" toggle on Plans & keys; a cost-estimate Max-tier picker on Plans & keys despite the no-price ruling; "Next run" formatted from the local picker (wrong in interval and after-import modes); nothing searchable; a deep link could open a section but not a row. **Shipped on `feat/settings-v3` (Tasks 1–4):** one kit (`SettingsRow`, `SettingsGroupCard`, `SettingsDetailHeader`, `SettingsPage`, `PillPicker`, `SettingsFocus`); a sidebar grouped Cicada · Customize · Engines & keys; General gains a System appearance, followed by one app-scope observer so a flip while the main window is closed is not missed; a new **Engines** page owns engine choice (`EngineChooser`, reusable by onboarding), Sleep shows the two ruling-4 previews read-only, Plans & keys is credentials only (the Max-tier picker is gone). **Final-review ruling (A3 amended):** `engine_select.resolve_llm_mode` reads `use_for_sleep` only in `byok`; under Auto the plan is the first rung either way. So the moved switch shows only while the API key card is chosen, reads "Use my Claude plan when I start a cycle" (not A3's "Auto may use my Claude plan"), and a flip reloads the chooser so "What runs" never names the old engine. From anywhere is its own row (Track R's view under a Settings header), and Agents lost its dead onboarding mode. **Search (Task 4, O2):** the sidebar starts with a search field that filters sections, badges their matches and lists individual settings; `SettingsIndex` holds every static row in page order, a page entry per section and the names (never an account line or a command) of the channels, connections and agents the window's Store snapshots already hold, ranked by `QuickMatch` (design §1.2 — exact, prefix, word start, initials, substring; every token must match). The branch's own ranker gave way to the palette's `Search/QuickMatch.swift` at the merge of `dev` (G136 R-SU1's server fold, one-character tokens only at a word start, bold runs in Unicode scalars), so Settings and ⌘K rank one name the same way. Landing — a result, Return on the top hit, `SettingsSectionLink(section:row:)`, an in-window pointer — selects, scrolls, washes the row in dandelion for 1.2 s and announces it. The deep link's row seed is `<row>@<unix ms>`, consumed only when new and under 30 s old, and written by the same one file as the section seed (P5). `SettingsRowLintTests` fails the build when an indexed row is rendered zero or two times. **Named follow-ups:** ⌘F in the Settings window (R-O16); remote connectors in the index (R-O12). **O4 (same track, Tasks 5–6):** You over the G117 endpoint (a PUT only for a real change, never a blank name, and "Show on graph" through the router); Privacy & data (memory folder, banks with Export and Delete-to-`.trash` — the active bank and the in-place memory folder are never offered, and the server 409s on both — the three gates, the usage ledger, keys, remote access, the transcript rail stated as a fact); Memory (search index status and rebuild, link previews now); Advanced (backend, MCP command, token location, env switches by name with plain words, never values). Every row is indexed. **Named follow-up:** "Look for duplicates" (R-O17 — the endpoint blocks the event loop, its dry run never reaches the Inbox, its live run leaves uncommitted merges). **Skills** (Customize, Task 8) is built — see G138. Rulings R-O1…R-O31 in `docs/plans/2026-09-23-settings-v3.md`. **DS-3b:** every Settings row is in ⌘K and ⏎ lands on it through `AppRouter.openSettings`. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ Tasks 1–8 built. **Settings defaults (owner 2026-10-22, "work towards the good defaults"):** #405 removed every variable, path, model id and maintenance button; pass 2 (`feat/settings-defaults-2`) opens Engines on Auto with the other engines folded and *Back to Auto*, says Plans & keys' states in words, links `docs/settings-outside-the-app.md` wherever the setup fixes a value, and fixes the fresh-bank "set up for the larger version on another Mac" read (`recorded` on `GET /embeddings`). Open: B1 (merge Engines and Plans & keys) and B5 (fold a connected agent's walkthrough) need the owner's word. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** Settings goes from eleven sections to six — General · You · Intelligence · Sleep · Devices · Privacy & data, under 400 words — with Sources and Agents as sidebar pages; the model and the evidence are **G217**. The search index keeps every row that survives. **Status 2026-10-22:** proposed. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G122 | <a id="g122"></a>**Sleep page: choose the engine and the model that will run the cycle — plan, Codex, Ollama, or a key — with Ollama a built-in option** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03: "in the sleep page we should be able to select what model, what harness, whatever we're using to run the sleep cycle… we should think about having ollama built-in also as an option") | **Provider modularity follow-up (2026-10-06):** Coordinate the backend engine-capability contract and frontend chooser with G50 so a provider can be registered/removed locally without duplicating provider lists or unrelated view branches. Keep genuinely custom controls in a small provider-specific adapter, and missing saved selections explicit. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-provider-and-ingestion-modularity.md). **Cursor follow-up (owner 2026-10-06):** Expose Cursor as a plan/engine choice once G50's supported adapter is implemented and verified. Installed, signed-in, connected-to-memory and selected-for-Sleep are distinct states. A Cursor agent catalog tile does not establish an engine. Keep provider-neutral task copy, billing basis and scheduling rails. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Plan/key/local model chooser is implemented. OpenRouter catalog and remaining provider marks are named follow-ups; declined parallel reading is not an automatic task hidden in this row. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g122). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists:** the engine ladder is real but invisible from the Sleep page. `CICADA_LLM_MODE` (`agent\|auto\|byok\|local`) is an env setting; `engine_select` resolves it once per cycle; `connections/` has adapters for the Claude plan (`claude` CLI), ChatGPT/Codex, BYOK keys and **Ollama** (`ollama-local`, `CICADA_OLLAMA_MODEL`, `CICADA_OLLAMA_BASE_URL`) — but only Settings → Agents shows any of it, and no UI sets the mode or the model. The Sleep page tells you *that* a cycle ran and on which engine (`lastEngine`), never lets you pick. **Build (app + one endpoint):** (1) `GET/PUT /sleep/engine` → `{mode, engine, model, disambiguationModel, candidates: [{id, label, available, connected, models: [...], detail}]}` backed by the connections registry; `PUT` writes the choice to the bank-independent user prefs (`~/.cicada/connections.json`, where `claude-plan` tier already lives — never `api/.env`, which the installer owns) and `engine_select` reads prefs before env; (2) a compact **Engine** card on the Sleep page above the queue: a segmented picker (Auto · Claude plan · Codex · Ollama · Key) with each option's live state (connected / not installed / running / no key) and a model field (aliases like `sonnet`/`haiku` for the plan, `ollama list` output for Ollama, the litellm id for a key), plus the honest cost line the Activity page already computes; (3) **Ollama built-in**: the Ollama option shows *install → start → pull a model* as three states with the exact command for each (no bundling of the Ollama binary — it is a separate app with its own licence and updater; "built-in" means Cicada detects, guides and uses it, and the installer's doctor checks it), and `auto` keeps its order (plan → Ollama → key); (4) the next cycle's engine is previewed on the Sleep page ("next Sleep will run on Claude plan · sonnet") so the choice is visible before it costs anything. **Rails:** prefs never hold secrets (keys stay in `secrets.env`); changing the engine mid-cycle applies to the next cycle (G80); the mode names in the UI are the same four the env accepts, so a fresh install's `api/.env` and the UI never disagree. → extends **G50** (connections), **G74** (engine ladder), **G49**; feeds **G117** (the onboarding sheet's step 1 is this card). **What stays open:** Codex as an engine (`codex exec`) is G49's engine-ladder half; a new adapter is its own row. APPLY, M **2026-09-23 (Track E):** the picker is a row of cards with real marks (Auto, Claude plan, ChatGPT plan, Ollama, API key); the ChatGPT card is live — installed → shown, signed in → selectable, its models the plan's own `model/list`; a *Keep going on extra usage* switch (off) writes `sleep-engine.allow_overage`; Ask follows this choice. **What stays open:** the onboarding sheet embeds the same `EngineCard` and inherits all of it; Meadow's hover modifiers arrive with M2. **DS-3b:** the Sleep page's quick engine menu writes the same `PUT /sleep/engine` through the chooser's model, with both previews. **2026-09-24 (round 4, `feat/r4-agents`):** OpenRouter joins as its own card, first among providers (owner decision 6) — Sign in with OpenRouter by PKCE with the nonce in the callback path, or a pasted key (R-AG10); `byok` under the hood, so `VALID_MODES`/`SUBSCRIPTION_MODES` and ruling 4 do not move and the wire's `selected` names the card (R-AG12). The API key card gains a provider picker — Anthropic, OpenAI, Gemini, xAI, Groq, Mistral — each with a small-tier default pinned by a LiteLLM `supports_response_schema` test (R-AG11); a key's model pins its judge, because the env default judge is an OpenAI model (R-AG13). Ollama wears *Local* and loses "Slower."; a "leaves your Mac" note (`LeavesMacNote`, pure) names where reads go and is absent for Ollama (R-AG14). **Still open:** OpenRouter's model catalog (the model is a text field with a tested default, no new fetch); marks for Grok/xAI, Groq and Mistral. **2026-09-29:** Sleep v5 (**G163**) adds an opt-in parallel worker count (1 and 3 offered, 6 and 10 only after EX-2 measures token-refresh and reserve overshoot on both plans) and an optional small model for the Read and Sort stages on a plan engine; the ChatGPT roster shows the vendor's descriptions and preselects nothing.
