(5) **Remove the deprecated top-right buttons** on the Sleep page (the global Sleep/Upload pair duplicates *Consolidate now* and Feed's `+`; keep only `?`), and audit the same toolbar on the other pages. **Rails:** no new endpoint reads the bank at capture time; the study list is a projection of the episode queue (same ETag domain); the mascot state machine stays the single source (`BookwormState`), extended not forked. → extends **G107** (sprites), **G124** (where the breakdowns land), **G114** (queue metadata: `origin`, `harness`, `processed_by`); relates **G117** (first Sleep is onboarding step 3). **Shipped 2026-09-05 (`feat/study-desk`, PR #55):** all five redesign points landed — the `reading` mascot state and clock-free bubble (R2/R8), the book pile as the one volume encoding (R1/R9), the study list replacing `SleepQueueCard`/`SleepDebtBreakdown` with its pure logic renamed and re-tested (R11), server-parsed consolidation history with a per-cycle detail and telemetry-joined duration (R4/R5), all four schedule modes (R6/R7), and the deprecated top-right Sleep/Upload pair removed from this page (R10). **Open remainder, not this row's scope:** the same toolbar audit on Graph/Clusters, and the engine/model picker card (**G122**). **v3 (2026-09-05 evening) — the study room** (`feat/study-room`, round-2 design Track A: spec rulings **R-A1…R-A16** binding as written, plan rulings **P1…P20** where the spec left a choice; nine commits, both suites green throughout). **What shipped.** The page is two columns above 1000 pt of content width and the same single 760 pt column below (`sleepLayout(width:)`, pure and naming its own boundary) — left: the hero card, the stage strip and *In the queue*; right: *Memory sources* and *Recent consolidations*. **The room** (R-A2) is five 24×24 props — night window, cushion, mug, plant, desk lamp — plus the worm and the *real* `BookPileView`, composed by one pure `deskSceneLayout(pointSize:uiScale:)` on a single cell lattice at the worm's own snapped size, every prop placed by an integer cell count so `uiScale` enters the picture exactly once (**P12**: two pixel scales in one drawing read as a bug at any zoom, and one snapping call is G130 R6). A new `PixelRenderer` holds the generalized body (`image(grid:gridSize:pointSize:palette:)`, `snappedPointSize`, `nsColors`) with `BookwormRenderer` kept as a thin facade — all four existing `Bookworm*Tests` files pass with **no edits**, which is the proof it forwards rather than re-implements — over its own `NSLock`-guarded `sceneCache` (**P13**: the mascot cache wipes wholesale past 512 entries, so routing scene layers and stage icons through it would make the always-animating worm collateral damage of every page render) and a separate `DeskPalette` whose keys are **disjoint** from the mascot's nine, so a grid can never be drawn with the wrong palette and come out looking *almost* right. The lamp is the room's ONE data-driven bit — lit iff `ScheduleConfig.mode != "manual"`, the same field the queue card's schedule sentence reads, so the art can never disagree with the words: art encodes **state, never quantity**, and every art bit has a text twin (R-A3/**P11**). The nightcap is **baked into** the `sleeping` and `reading` frames only (**P14** — the mascot ruling forbids an accessory layer a consumer has to merge, and a cap that is a function of the state needs no new cache dimension). Geometry deviation, pinned by assertion: the lattice is 90 × 28 cells, not the sketch's 40 × 24 — 24 rows cannot hold a 24-cell worm raised onto a cushion and 40 columns cannot hold both the room and a pile column wide enough for a full-width `BookPileView` spine. **The hero** promotes the count out of the bracket line: `heroCount` / `heroQualifier` / `bracketTail` are three pure functions over one switch and `sleepDebtBracketText` is **re-composed** from them, so every asserted bracket string passes byte-for-byte and the line survives as the hero group's VoiceOver label (**P8**); `first run` is a real qualifier ranked ahead of `behind`/`overdue` (**P9** — nothing has ever been consolidated in that bank, so calling the queue a backlog would be wrong); the 24-block meter never renders without its noun — `Rested n%` idle, hidden entirely when there is no baseline (a fabricated 100% is a lie), `Read a of b` running, with the label's two numbers and the bar's fraction from ONE reading of `resolveOriginCounts` rather than a second field (R-A5/**P7**); three tiles are present-tense or measured, every unknown a `—` with a hover reason (R-A14/**P18**), and a test bans "cluster", "insight", "est" and "~" from every tile because **G107**'s estimate deferral is binding. Recorded consequence of **P8** + **P17**: while a cycle runs the mood is `.sleeping`, `heroCount` is `nil` by the asserted string, and the hero deliberately draws no numeral — the running number is the meter's `Read a of b`, and repeating the queue total beside it would draw one number twice. **The strip** (R-A8/**P15**) substitutes into the meter's slot — not a sixth card — replacing `moodDetailLine`'s "Stage N of 5" text and its bare `ProgressView`: five 16×16 icons at 32 pt with the short labels **Read · Sort · Decide · Notice · File**, read from one `SleepStages.all` that the `?` popover also renders (**P16** — the popover copy is a *hoist*, not a rewrite, its titles and details pinned as literals so a later reword fails loudly instead of forking the description). Only **Read** carries a fill, because stages 2–5 have no per-episode unit at all (`sleep_cycle.progress_pct` returns `None` past stage 0) and must not be given a fake one; `status.stage` counts COMPLETED stages and that translation happens in exactly one place; a cancel or a failure **freezes** the strip at the stage it reached — resetting to all-pending would erase the stages that really ran, which is precisely what a person wants to see after cancelling. The pulse period is a named constant (≤ 1.2 s) so R-A13 is assertable rather than a literal inside a view, and Reduce Motion holds the terminal frame. **The queue card** becomes the page's schedule statement: a 3 pt micro-fill behind the count instead of a 60 pt `ProgressView` (no spinner where a real count exists) and a dimmed ✓ at `read == total` — `queueRowState`'s **precedence** is the reason it exists as a pure function, since `total == 0` (a source the episode cap left out) is also `read == total`, so checking `.done` first would draw a finished tick over a source nothing has touched — then the schedule sentence for all four modes, and a footer naming the scheduled engine **only when it differs** from the manual one, so the standing quota ruling is shown rather than silently applied. **The right column** is a read-only projection of `store.sourcesOverview` (R-A10 — no new fetch, no new endpoint): `memorySourceRows` drops sources with no captures, orders by the last 14 UTC days then lifetime captures then id, caps at six, and carries a 30-day sparkline plus four week dots over the new sparse `activity` histogram, dense and oldest-first so a silent day is a zero rather than a gap the view has to guess at; history rows gain the commit's time of day and the **engine·author** pill, where an absent `Cicada-Engine:` trailer drops the pill's left half rather than naming an engine that never ran. **Amendment to R1, stated out loud: one volume encoding *per surface*, and no number drawn twice.** R1 was written as "the book pile is the page's one volume encoding"; v3 adds a second surface with its own quantity, so the rule is restated: the book pile encodes **queued characters per source** on the left, a sparkline encodes **captures per day** on the right, each list keeps its own noun on the row and never only in a tooltip — the queue says `waiting` (and `read of total` while running), Memory sources says `captured` — and no figure appears in two places (**P17**). R1's original refusals stand unchanged (no bars-per-source on the queue, no tiles restating the same number, no age histogram), and **P10** extends them into the art: **no painted books anywhere on the page**. The page's one chart is a pile of books, so nothing that is not the chart may look like one; the scene reserves a column and the real `BookPileView` stands in it, with a test proving no prop's ink reaches into that column at any scale. **Two spec rulings amended, each for a measured reason.** (i) **R-A7** asks for a *tree-wide* "exactly one file defines a Consolidate control"; grepped, `sleepVM.triggerManually()` lives in **five** files — the Sleep folder plus the shared top-bar controls, the Sources queue strip, the onboarding Sleep step and the app entry point — four surfaces that each legitimately own their own trigger, three of them behind another track's ownership fence. So the lint walks `Views/Sleep/` and asserts exactly one file there defines it: a deliberate scoping, not a shortcut, and still a strict upgrade on the old form, which grepped `SleepView.swift` alone and would have stayed green while the control moved to a sibling file. (ii) **R-A9** routes the "Change…" link through `AppRouter`, which has **no section field and cannot open the Settings scene** — the private AppKit selector is accepted and silently ignored on macOS 26, and a build-failing test already bans it. The proven mechanism is `SettingsLink` plus a seeded `cicada.settingsSection`, extracted once as `SettingsSectionLink` so that key has exactly ONE **writer**; the lint asserts one writer and never "one mention", because that form is false today and always will be — the literal legitimately appears in four files (the `@AppStorage` reader plus doc comments). A third amendment came out of review: **R-A12** names the snapshot's `loadedAt` as the staleness chip's date, but that field is stamped on a **disk-cache** hydrate, so a cold launch with the backend stopped printed the minute the app opened over data of any age — the fabricated timestamp the feature exists to refuse. `Snapshot.refreshedAt` was split out to mean "the backend confirmed this" (stamped on a 200 **and** a 304, cleared at every hydrate so a snapshot — possibly a previous bank's — never carries a confirmation it did not get); `loadedAt` keeps its change-token job. **Final review added the threshold R-A12 never had, and one cache bump.** `store.isConnected` means "the SSE stream is open", not "the backend is alive": `SyncEngine.start` holds it false for the whole reconnect backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s) while the loop inside that window keeps polling `GET /sync/version` every 3 s, so the chip fired on every backend restart and printed *Not connected — showing the last reading · as of HH:MM* over a timestamp seconds old: a warning contradicted by its own date, on the one feature here built to be honest about freshness. `SleepLiveness.staleAfter` (60 s, asserted greater than `SyncEngine.maxBackoff`, which is now a `nonisolated static` so the relation is checked rather than copied) is the fourth refusal, and it is what the long-declared `now` parameter was reserved for. Separately, `SourceOverview` gained `activity` without bumping `SnapshotCache.schema`: a hydrate restores the pre-`activity` body **and its etag**, the next refresh takes a 304, and the row rendered a flat sparkline and "0 of the last 4 weeks had captures" beside its own non-zero capture count until an unrelated bank write moved the tag — so the envelope goes to 2. **Ship the ETag, its client mapping and the cache version together**: the disk cache is the third half of that rail, and the docstring on `schema` now says so. **Two traps that cost measurement, recorded as evidence.** (1) `date.fromisoformat` **rejects** a datetime string on CPython 3.12 (measured in the project venv), and two call sites bound the telemetry-ledger read by the very value `--date=iso-strict` was about to change — unsliced, both history endpoints would have 500'd the moment the format moved, so the `[:10]` slice ships in the **same commit** as the format change. (2) `raw[:10]` on an episode timestamp calls a **naive-local** stamp a UTC day: silently off by one for exactly the rows nobody checks, so `_activity_day` parses all three shapes a bank holds (aware `+00:00`, legacy naive-local, `Z`-suffixed imports) instead of slicing. `activity` keys are absolute dates, never a rolling array, so a client holding a 304 across UTC midnight renders a day **short**, never a day **shifted** — the accepted asymmetry, stated in the docstring; the sparkline indexes them on a UTC calendar for the same reason (**P2**). The ETag recipe did not move — `activity` is computed from episodes, a component `/sources/overview` already ETags and `VersionVector` already routes — and that is pinned behaviourally by recomputing the tag, not by grepping the router. **Deliberately not in v3:** a time-of-day sky (art only, and state must outrank the clock), mascot identity (**G127** — the nightcap is the only character-specific art, sequenced last so a flip costs one call), any per-cycle estimate, and any price or token readout (the 2026-09-03 ruling). **v4 (2026-09-23) — the mascot page (Track Z, part a).** Rodrigo 2026-09-23: *"Change the mascot page, make it better and have it be more interactive, keeping the minimal vibes."* Round-3 decision 5 amends v3's inert room; the design panel's rulings **R-Z1…R-Z14** (`docs/specs/2026-09-23-round3-design-mascot-page.md` §3) bind as written, and the plan (`docs/plans/2026-09-23-mascot-page.md`) took **Z-P1…Z-P27** where the design left a choice or contradicted the code; nine task commits (Z0–Z8), both suites green throughout. **What shipped**, by id rather than restated: the one-column page with one sentence, one control, one whisper line and a closed, remembered Details (R-Z5, R-Z6, R-Z7); response art on the one lattice (R-Z1, R-Z4) behind an inert art layer and a tested hotspot layer (R-Z8); spines, the lamp and the whisper line as controls that never change what the machine does behind a label (R-Z2, R-Z9); the completion cheer and "See what changed ›" as the only input-free beats besides the weather crossfade (R-Z12); the window as weather (R-Z11); the sentence and the answer ladder as pure, length-capped lines (R-Z13). **The plan rulings later work must keep:** **Z-P1** — the running stage is `activeStage(completed:)` at every site (page mood, menu bar, onboarding, strip) because the wire counts *completed* stages; **Z-P3** — "the last cycle" is `kind == "sleep"`, never `!= "decay"`, since `/sleep/history` also returns inbox-resolution commits and one of those must never be called a cycle or opened by "See what changed ›"; **Z-P10** — the renderer key gains one `look` segment, measured at 216 keys per size against the design's ≤ 256 bound (keying pose × reaction would have blown it); **Z-P24** — the frame memo shipped only because its benchmark halved the time per call (measured 0.514 s → 0.003 s). **Three defects fixed first (Z0):** the stage off-by-one at three sites (`SleepMood`, the menu bar's `BookwormState`, and the onboarding step the design missed) — the worm, the bracket line and VoiceOver said stage 1 while Sort ran; the cancel that digested (any running→idle edge stamped `justFinishedAt`, so a cancel chewed for 6 s); and two invented stage lines that asserted a contradiction and a habit on every cycle, which no cycle measured. **Not in part a, landed in part b (below):** feeding (Z9, once Track I's `IntakeRouter` existed — the poses and reactions it needed were already built and tested) and the Meadow pass with the optional sky band (Z10). **Still open:** live verification with screenshots from the demo bank (Z11, the orchestrator's). **v4 part b (2026-09-23) — feeding, the Meadow pass, two live-check fixes (Track Z Z9/Z10; `docs/plans/2026-09-23-mascot-page-b.md`, rulings Z-B1…Z-B19).** Five task commits, both suites green throughout. **Two live-check defects, fixed first.** *The pile overflowed the room:* spine heights were authored in unscaled points (8…40) with nothing bounding their sum, so a large queue grew the pile upward until the card's clip cut its top spine — four full-height spines need 4 × (40 + 2) = 168 pt of a 130 pt column at 1.0× — while at 0.8× a 150 pt spine was wider than its 120 pt column and at 1.4× the spines stayed put as the room grew 40 % (two scales in one picture). *The title was still SF 20 semibold:* the Sleep header had copied `PageHeader`'s font instead of sharing it, so G137's display serif never reached it. The rule now: **`fitPile` compresses the pile to its column and never cuts it** — natural heights when they fit, otherwise one factor over only what sits above a label floor, so the order (the pile's one comparison) and every count survive (Z-B1); **at most eight spines** (seven + a remainder), because 8 × (floor + gap) fits all seven zoom steps (102.4 of 104 pt at 0.8×, 153.6 of 156 pt at 1.2×, the two tightest) while nine need 144 pt of 130 at 1.0× — `PileFitTests` recomputes every step; heights scale with the lattice's cell and the floor with the font (Z-B2); a full-width spine is exactly the column's width (Z-B3); the title is `PageTitle`, the one view `PageHeader` also draws (Z-B4). **Feeding** (R-Z10, Z-B5…Z-B11, Z-B17…Z-B19), by id: `IntakeRouter.feedGuard` at every `IntakeRouter` door — harness session roots and Cicada's own home refused before anything is walked, a refused root met inside a dropped folder never descended into, both sides compared resolved and component by component (Z-B5), with only a symlink resolved during the walk (final review: resolving every entry on the main actor made a 20,600-entry walk 0.08 s → 0.42 s; links-only measures 0.14 s); the pickers outside the router (the Add-source walkthrough's drop and *Choose file…*, its saved-content picker, Settings' local-folder picker) check the choice itself with `refusedRoot(of:)`, and a watched folder that *contains* a refused root is still walked — open; `accept` answers, so the room says a refusal in the worm's words while every other door shows it in the panel (Z-B6, Z-B18 — neither voice promises a capture that may not be wired, and the design's "feeds and notes" became "feeds and saved links" because the intake does not read a note); the verdict at the drop, never during the hover (Z-B7); the window's veil yielding by an explicit `nearerDrop` claim (Z-B8); the router's own phase as the line, with no digit in any feed line (Z-B9, R-Z3); gulp for yes and shake for no, both through the §6.4 matrix — a sleeping worm takes the drop and stays asleep, a stale page sends nothing (Z-B10); what ends a feed line (Z-B11) — including an import that finishes or fails with its panel closed, which the final review found left a live line over the status for good; the slot ranks a drag, then an answer rung, then the feed line, then the status; one `IntakePicker` for every door (Z-B17); T13 as the happy worm's invitation once the queue has loaded (Z-B19). **Meadow** (Z-B12…Z-B15): the sentence's lead is Instrument Serif 30, shrinking only to the 22 pt display floor, over a New York italic tail (the brief over design §5 — a two-line tail is text); Media pages get rung 5 (a saved link's own `og:image`, a PDF's first page rendered in the backend), fetched once and served from a cache outside the bank. Link-card site icons come only from the saved page's own tags — no icon service (G61 stands, owner 2026-10-13). **Open DECIDE:** a matched Contacts card's first email, hashed, as a Gravatar identifier for other people (touches G154 R-SR8). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Trusted-site logos, Contacts photos, media thumbnails and user picks exist. Improve remaining picture coverage only from trusted sources; never guess a person's domain/name or silently fetch their profile image. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g159). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists.** **G59** (✅) resolves a logo only for `company`/`tool` pages (`logo_service.py:73,278`), by `logo:` → `sources:` → `## Links` → `media.url` → a `<slug>.com` guess, fetching apple-touch-icon → `<link rel=icon>` → DuckDuckGo's icon service, cached under `$CICADA_HOME/logos/<bank>/` and never in a bank; a `person`, a place, a concept or a book gets a monogram. **The research question — how a bank does it:** a card network passes a raw merchant descriptor; the bank (or an enrichment vendor) cleans it, matches it to a merchant record with a known domain and a curated logo, and falls back to a category icon — a large curated database plus a matching model, not a per-transaction fetch. Cicada has no such database and must not phone one home with the person's entities. **The local-first precedence for Cicada:** (1) a **manual upload** (G146's profile picture — the person's own act, always wins); (2) the **Contacts photo** for a matched person (G154, on this Mac); (3) a **domain logo** (G59's ladder, extended past `company`/`tool` when the page names a domain); (4) a **Wikidata image** (P18/P154 logo) for a page whose identity resolves to a Wikidata item by an exact label + type match — a keyless public API; (5) the **Open Graph image** of a page's own cited URL; (6) the **monogram**. **Constraints:** every rung that reaches the network runs under the existing gates (`CICADA_ALLOW_LOGO_FETCH`, the connector gate for anything Sleep starts unattended) and the ToS rail (4 s / ≤ 512 KB / no cookies / never behind auth), through `net_guard`/`_is_safe_url`; **the lookup sends a name or a domain, never a claim or a sentence**, and never a `person`'s name to a public service (a surname is not a domain — G59's rule, extended: a person gets only rungs 1–2); a guess is shown as a guess until confirmed, and a wrong picture is one click to replace; raster only (SVG refused, as today); cached outside the bank, a manual upload aside (G146 decides where that lives). → extends **G59**; relates **G146**, **G154**, **G70**. **Slice 1 (round 4, T-People):** rungs 1 (the person's upload or initials), 3 (G59's logo, for `company`/`tool` or a page whose `logo:` names a domain — cached, or not yet known to miss) and the media thumbnail ship through `entity_picture.resolve`; rung 2 (Contacts) reads the optional `contacts_photo: {sha, ext}` and `$CICADA_HOME/pictures/<bank>/contacts/<id>.<jpg\|png>`, both T-Sources' to write (G154, its R-SR9 layout). Wikidata P18/P154, Open Library covers and multi-word domain guessing remain this row's RESEARCH. **2026-09-29:** the guessed logo for a page with no URL is recorded as a parked finding in **G173** (its fix belongs to this row's multi-word-domain RESEARCH). The classifier navigator idea (**G168**) never sends a person's name, per this row's rule. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 RESEARCH. | PARTIAL · P3 |

| G11 | <a id="g11"></a>**In-app media preview (images · videos · websites) + artifacts as memories** | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Inline players/previews and saved artifacts exist. Check remaining general preview/provider behavior and the disclosed duplicate direct-file player; playback does not establish video understanding. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g11). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Preview rich media **inside the app**: **images** (inline + lightbox), **videos** (inline player / thumbnail-to-play), and **website link previews** (Open-Graph card or a small embedded web view of saved bookmarks/URLs — reuse the M4 media-ingestion OG enrichment for the card; a `WKWebView` for full preview). Let media-as-artifacts be **saved as memories** — an image/video/site embedded in an entity or claim, rendered inline like the transclusion layer renders embedded pages. Extends the inline-transclusion model (`![[…]]`) to media embeds (D2 transclusion currently excludes images/media — revisit) and the M4 `media` entities (which already store `url`/`thumbnail`/`media_type`) gain real in-app previews instead of just opening the URL. Sources: chat-export images (Gemini takeout), saved bookmarks/YouTube/articles, pasted/diagram artifacts. → **underpins G14** (mood-boards are media-preview-heavy); relates to G2 (media types), G3/M4 (feed), the transclusion layer, M5c surfaces. **Preview half shipped 2026-07-03** (G23/G25 — `HeroPreview.swift`/`MediaPreview.swift`/`ImageLightbox.swift`: image inline+lightbox, video inline player, website WKWebView/OG hero card). **Artifacts-as-memories half remains open** — media is not yet embeddable inline inside another entity or claim via the transclusion layer. **Preview half — video (2026-09-05, Track V).** The 2026-07-03 wave played exactly one thing: a YouTube link whose id parsed, inside a `WKWebView`. Now YouTube (including `/live/<id>` and `playlist?list=`), Vimeo, TikTok and Loom play in the provider's own embed player, and a direct `.mp4/.m4v/.mov/.webm/.m3u8` or `file://` clip plays in a real AVKit player with transport controls and space-to-play — an unreadable local path shows the path plus Reveal in Finder, never a black rectangle. **The rule: the provider is DERIVED at read time from the stored URL, never written into a bank** — one classification table written twice (`api/services/video_urls.py` and `Views/Common/VideoRef.swift`) and pinned by one fixture (`api/tests/fixtures/video_urls.json`), pure on both sides, zero network. Two reasons, and the second is the load-bearing one: every already-saved item starts playing the moment the app updates, with no whole-bank rewrite commit; and `GET /sources` reads `media_type` from `url_index.json` while `site`/`channel`/`provider` come from the page's `media:` block, so a write-time reclassification would have to migrate two stores at once — the split-brain class this repo already knows. **Surfaces, all four through the same two components:** the Feed row (a play badge over the thumbnail for a *playable* ref only, plus a duration pill only when a provider reported one; the row is reused by the Sources page), the Feed preview sheet (720 × 560 for the two video kinds, 480 × 520 for everything else), the entity Content tab, and the entity hero (`EmbedVideoHero` / `FileVideoHero`, **never autoplaying** — a hero renders on every visit, not behind a tap). **The ToS rail as it applies here:** only a provider's own player URL is ever loaded; an oEmbed response is read for its *fields* only, never its `html` blob (the player URL is derived from the id we parsed); and **a stream is never derived** — no `yt-dlp`, no CDN or `.m3u8` lifted out of a page — so a direct file the app plays is one the *user* saved as a direct URL. Twitch stays external (its player validates `parent` against the real embedding origin and a top-level `WKWebView` document has none; synthesising one is circumvention), an X `/status/<id>` link stays a website card (it is *any* post, so calling every status a video would be a lie), Instagram stays external (login-walled). Backend is metadata only: a direct file classifies as `media_type: video`, which `link_enrichment._excluded_media` already honours — so the nightly enrichment stops fetching binaries with no edit to that module; one shared `_enrich_oembed` for Vimeo/TikTok/Loom at 4 s / ≤ 512 KB; a content-type guard on the OG fetch; and two additive optional keys (`media.provider`, `media.duration_s`, written only when they have a value, so a plain bookmark's frontmatter is byte-identical to before). **Two named follow-ups, neither attempted here:** (1) teaching `normalize_url`/`_youtube_video_id` about `/live/<id>` changes `url_hash`, so an already-ingested URL would re-import as a *new* entity and orphan its `url_index.json` entry — that is a **dedup-index migration**, not a one-line fix, and the player was fixed at read time instead; (2) **Dailymotion and Reddit embeds are mechanical once the table exists** — but neither endpoint was probed, and adding an unverified endpoint to a ToS-bound path is the wrong trade. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ preview half shipped; artifacts-as-memories half open. | PARTIAL · P3 |

| G49 | <a id="g49"></a>**Subscription-only Cicada — the "Session-Native Engine Ladder"** | **Cursor, OpenCode and Grok as plan engines (Rodrigo 2026-10-28: "can you work on all four engines: Cursor, Open Code, Antigravity, and Grok please?", after T3 Code's "Bring your own sub"; PR #494).** Shipped as `cli_engines`: each tool's own headless CLI on the person's own sign-in (Cursor `agent -p --mode ask`, OpenCode `opencode run --format json`, Grok `grok --prompt-file --permission-mode dontAsk`), no tools (an empty scratch dir per call plus each tool's deny switches, tested with stub CLIs), capture and recall off in every child, plans under ruling 4's switch, never Auto's rungs at #494. **Ruling 46 (owner, 2026-10-28, answering #494's two questions yes):** Auto is Claude plan → ChatGPT plan → Cursor → OpenCode (subscription model only), scheduled runs reach the two tools through ruling 4b's switch, each read from #494's cached status so a cycle waits at most 9 s and the Sleep page never, an unknown or hung tool skipped in words; Grok stays out of Auto and schedules until a live sign-in shows how a subscription call reports cost (the acting owner's guard). Rail 2 per tool: OpenCode counts only GitHub Copilot and OpenCode Go and refuses a key-backed model before a call; Cursor's usage-based pricing past the included amount is the person's own account setting, said on the card; a Grok reply with a charged cost stops the run. **Antigravity not shipped:** Google's FAQ says wrapping `agy` in another application is not supported on consumer plans (account suspension risk), and Gemini CLI stopped serving personal Google sign-ins on 2026-06-18; revisit only if Google allows it or for an Enterprise sign-in. **Review of #494 (2026-10-28):** a spawn could load the person's MCP servers (Cicada's handshake into the extraction prompt): Grok's compatibility cells off, OpenCode's servers disabled through Cicada's own inline config, and Cicada's server silent under `CICADA_ENGINE_SPAWN=1` (Cursor has no run-wide switch); Grok only on xAI's built-in `grok-…` ids with an explicit `-m`, and user-started only until a live sign-in shows what a subscription call reports. **Open, DECIDE (owner):** whether any of the three joins Auto's ladder (ruling 31 / 36 D1). **Untested until a live sign-in:** each tool's exact signed-out, usage-limit and `models` output (built from the docs, cli_engines' marker lists); Cursor's prompt rides argv (≤ 120 kB on Linux). Claude Code has no non-interactive model list, so its aliases stay a documented list. **Cursor / continuity follow-up (owner 2026-10-06):** G50 owns adding a supported Cursor provider/engine adapter; this row owns engine/session attribution and ladder behavior. Carry actual source/destination harness and reported model through G110 continuation; never infer a model or reuse another session's identity. Verify supported vendor interfaces before adding a new plan rung; preserve scheduled-plan, cancellation and quota behavior. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Plan engines and local embeddings exist. Remaining session-native attribution/engine ladder details require actual supported-client checks; the historical no-credits blocker is obsolete. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g49). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Adopt the recommended architecture in [`subscription-first-portability.md`](subscription-first-portability.md): a **Claude Code plugin** (SessionStart primer, Stop/SessionEnd auto-capture, PreCompact flush, both skills, MCP registration, new `cicada_commit` tool) with a **Codex CLI mirror** for ChatGPT-plan users; underneath, an **engine ladder** (implement the reserved `llm_mode="agent"`: subscription CLI `claude -p`/`codex exec` → local Ollama → BYOK → skip-with-queue) behind a mandatory `providers.resolve_llm_fn` seam; on top, **keyless onboarding** (plan-picker wizard replacing "paste your key" with vendor OAuth, ungated embedding default removing the HF_TOKEN gate, launchd nightly runner with env-key sanitization + stop-on-throttle). Two non-negotiables all three candidate designs converged on: a deterministic **"structural Sleep"** (decay/hubs/edges/index/inbox/git-commit — zero LLM, runs nightly no matter what, sweep-commits agentic writes) and **bearer-token auth on localhost:8000 as a launch blocker**. Phased P0–P5 (~5–7 solo weeks; Claude-plan user fully served after ~2). Key verified facts: `claude -p` runs on Pro/Max subscription OAuth (billing-split paused Jun 2026); published token-limit figures are **refuted** → budget in invocations + live throttle detection, never tokens. Re-confirm the doc's "NOT verified" checklist (hooks payloads, plugin marketplace, all Codex-side claims) before building each phase. → subsumes the engine half of **G10** (agentic extraction), extends **G9** (origin) + M3 (contributors, per-rung `Cicada-Author`), relates to G13, launch-blocker list, and `install.sh`/onboarding. **Shipped 2026-09-23 (Track E, `feat/engines`) — the engine-ladder half, on both plans.** The `claude-cli` rung hardened the Hermes way, one engine id and no Agent SDK (spec Decision 1): stream-json read for the answer and the CLI's `rate_limit_event`/`system/api_retry`; one pure `plan_limits.claude_stop` stops a cycle before extra usage unless opted in, on any rejected window, and at ≥ 90 % of the 5-hour window, each as one plain sentence with the CLI's own reset time; `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES=2`; `--setting-sources ""` as a third capture lock; reasoning-off → `--effort low` (`claude --help` 2.1.280); a Stage-1 `--json-schema` built from the rails, **off** until measured. A `codex` rung (`llm_mode=codex`, `Cicada-Engine: codex-cli`, card `chatgpt-plan`): one `codex exec` per call with the isolation flags verified on codex-cli 0.154.0, per-call instruction files that replace Codex's coding prompt, strict per-stage schemas, and a read-only `codex app-server` pre-flight (signed in, plan vs API key, limit already reached, the plan's default model — no quota). Both rungs share the semaphore, the scoped breaker and the models-used ledger. Auto: Claude plan → ChatGPT plan → Ollama → key; ruling 4 covers both plans through one tuple, `engine_select.SUBSCRIPTION_MODES`. **Measured:** a signed-out `codex exec` emits top-level `error` lines as *retry notices* ("Reconnecting… n/5") before `turn.failed` — only a turn that never completes is a failure (R-E16); one live `claude -p` on 2.1.280 (haiku, `--effort low`, temp home) streamed `system/init` → `assistant` → `rate_limit_event` → `result`, so **a `rate_limit_event` does arrive on a fresh process** (R1 V-S1: yes); `apiKeySource` read `"none"` under the plan sign-in; the event carries **no top-level `utilization`** — each window's fraction sits under `rate_limit_info.unifiedWindows.{five_hour,seven_day}.utilization`, which the reader handles so the 90 % rule can fire at all (sanitised fixture `claude_stream_live.jsonl`); and `--effort low` lowers reasoning without turning it off (the trivial call still emitted thinking); **the isolated home is not free of hidden context** (final review, 2026-09-23: a signed-out 0.154.0 `codex exec` in a throwaway home, pointed at a local capture server with `openai_base_url`, synthetic prompt): Codex unpacks its own bundled system skills into `<home>/skills/.system` and listed them in every request (~10.8k chars) — now off via `-c skills.include_instructions=false`, with `include_permissions_instructions` and `include_collaboration_mode_instructions` for the notes that surface in its place; request body 31.8k → 21.0k bytes. Still riding every call with no working switch: the code-mode/collaboration tool namespace (~15k chars — `exec`, `spawn_agent`… despite `--disable multi_agent`), a `multi_agent_role` note (~3.4k) and `environment_context` (~1k, kept for the date) — so the per-call overhead is roughly R2's ≈ 7.7k tokens *before* the switches, not gone. The signed-in `input_tokens` of one Stage-1 call is **still unmeasured** (needs a signed-in Cicada home) and is owed to the live check. **Fixed on the way:** a model picked in Settings → Sleep never reached a real cycle (`resolve_settings` with no registry dropped it), plan-cycle claims were stamped with `litellm_model` as `authored_by`, and `EngineExhausted` never tripped the breaker. **Ruled out, with the evidence (R2 §3.4):** direct OAuth against `chatgpt.com/backend-api` with Codex's client id. **What stays open:** the plugin / SessionStart / PreCompact half and structural Sleep; flipping `CICADA_AGENT_EXTRACTION_SCHEMA` after a demo-bank comparison; a ChatGPT weekly-ceiling stop (owner DECIDE); MCP `cicada_ask` still resolves from env (Track R moves its body); whether Codex's own `logs_2.sqlite` (or its other sqlite stores) in Cicada's Codex home keeps prompt text — partly checked: after six signed-out `--ephemeral` runs carrying a synthetic sentinel, `logs` held 0 rows, `threads` 0, and the sentinel was in none of the home's sqlite files; a signed-in run (whose reply streams back) is **still unchecked** and owed to the live check with a throwaway signed-in home — if the sentinel appears, point `sqlite_home` at a per-call temp dir or prune after each cycle, since episode text there sits outside the bank and git; the unscoped breaker bucket is never reset after a throttle until the backend restarts, and this track **did** widen it (final review H1, reproduced: R-E23 sent Ask onto the plan, a Codex usage limit raised `EngineThrottled`, and a near-limit stop on a failed call turned any failure into one — one throttle, then every Ask failed fast without spawning) — fixed here by giving each Ask call (`ask:<uuid>`) and each link backfill, tail or on-demand (`links:<uuid>`), its own self-purging scope, and by raising a stop on a failed call only when the plan enforced it (overage, rejected), never for `near_limit`; what still shares `_unscoped` reaches a plan only through an explicit `CICADA_LLM_MODE` (the dedup sweep), fixable with an expiry at the vendor's reset time; a Claude call with no system message and no JSON ask still runs under the CLI's own coding-assistant prompt (live: it refused a plain request as off-role) — the Codex rung has `DEFAULT_INSTRUCTIONS` for that case, the Claude rung no twin yet. **Round 4 (2026-09-24) — the model reservation is lifted for harness writes (owner; TODO ruling 11).** (1) The Stop-hook capture keeps each agent turn's `model`/`effort` in the episode's `turns` sidecar — only `message.model` + the top-level `effort` for Claude Code, `turn_context.payload.model`/`.effort` for Codex, and the hook's `effort.level` for the last reply. (2) Every MCP write, stdio and remote, carries `recorded_ts`. (3) `turn_authorship.py` joins them at read: `authorModel`/`authorEffort` on the claim wire, a model on each assistant span, `models` on a harness contributor, and `agent` plus per-turn models on `/episodes/{id}/text`. (4) `Cicada-Author:` is unchanged. **Known limits:** Codex gives an MCP server no session id, so a Codex MCP write joins to nothing even though its captured turns carry models; the same holds for an app with no hook (the Claude app, ChatGPT, Cursor, a remote connector). No backfill: an episode captured before round 4 gains models only when its session grows again. **2026-10-19 — the plugin half shipped (owner: "a Claude Code plugin … so the agent can use the Cicada MCP and conversations get stored and captured"; branch `feat/claude-code-plugin`).** `plugins/cicada` carries the MCP server, the `cicada` skill and the five capture/recall hooks, calling the same scripts install.sh registers through the app's launchers or a named checkout; with it enabled install.sh, doctor and `/agents/wiring` leave Claude Code to it (no double fire; `mcp.md`, "The Claude Code plugin"). No `cicada_commit` tool and no `episode_writer` refactor: the hooks reuse G105's backend capture as it stands. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. **2026-10-28 — "Grok and Cursor are now one" (owner), researched (branch `feat/engine-picker-v2`; sources in the report).** Since 2026-10-12, *The X Subscription* sells Grok, Cursor and X Premium as one plan (five tiers; new customers start on it, existing Cursor and Grok customers may switch). Cursor's own docs: each paid plan has **one weekly pool for "Cursor models"** — Grok 4.7, 4.6, 4.5 (jointly trained by Cursor and SpaceXAI) and Composer — **shared by Cursor's editor Agent, its CLI, Cloud Agents, Grok Bot and the Grok apps**; **third-party models in Cursor (Claude, GPT, Gemini) are billed separately at API list prices through top-ups**, and the Grok API is billed apart from the pool. Grok Build (`grok`) is still its own binary with its own browser sign-in (or `XAI_API_KEY`); its docs say nothing of the pool or of per-run cost in headless output. **What it means here:** one subscription, still two tools and two sign-ins — so two cards, each its own mark, no parent mark. **Risk to rail 2:** on an X Subscription account, a Cursor run on a third-party model draws paid top-up credit, not the plan. Proposed (owner's call, ruling 46 untouched): Cursor on Auto and on a scheduled run only on Cursor's own models (Grok, Composer, its `auto`), a third-party model in Cursor said as "billed separately at list price" before it is chosen; Grok's exclusion stands until a live sign-in shows what a subscription call reports. **Ruled 2026-10-28 (TODO ruling 46a, owner: "Yes, own models only"; built in #507):** Auto and schedules run Cursor only on Cursor's own models; a third-party model there is labelled "billed separately at list price" and never auto-picked; Grok stays off Auto; the two cards stay separate. | PARTIAL · P3 |

App: **Usage** page (Provenance) — **minimal**: tiles (cost / memory writes / tokens / streak) + 53-week heatmap with tooltips + day drill-down; **advanced**: per-connection cost cards, Swift Charts (tokens/day, cost/day vs API-equivalent, hour-of-day), by-model/stage/bank tables, `/stats`-style facts (lifetime tokens, favorite model, peak day, longest sleep run), optional harness panel from `~/.claude/stats-cache.json` + newest Codex `rate_limits` snapshot (labelled as the harness's own data). **Honesty rules:** subscription = flat `$/mo` + "≈ $X at API list price — estimate, not billed"; usage = real per-model $; local = free; no Claude rate-limit % (no compliant source) — throttle events observed by Cicada instead; no invented token budgets (G49). | G74 | <a id="g74"></a>**Sleep has no engine — agent-driven consolidation** (Rodrigo 2026-08-31: "sleep is lowkey bs… it has never run because I have no credits; can the same agent contribute to the memory?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Plan-backed consolidation and deterministic tail steps exist. Tier-0/model-free semantic writing and the larger structural engine ladder are not complete; no API-credit prerequisite for the first trial. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g74). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** The title’s historical “no engine” diagnosis is superseded: provider connections and plan engines exist. Do not make API credits or a new structural engine a prerequisite for G10. Remaining Tier-0 model-free writing and consolidation behavior must still preserve the five-stage claim/provenance/conflict pipeline; a shell skill must not bypass it. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **Verified:** the live bank's last real `Sleep cycle` commits are 2026-06-18 — every episode since (imports + today's captures) sits `processed: false`; the LLM stages silently no-op without an engine while the structural steps (migrations, warm_logos, refresh_open_questions) still run. Two concrete engines, both credit-free: **(a) the connected plan (fastest)** — implement G49's reserved `llm_mode="agent"` rung as `claude -p` subprocess calls through the user's already-connected Claude Max (G50 card shows Connected; compliance verified in [`subscription-first-portability.md`](subscription-first-portability.md): official scripted-use mode, token never touched, throttle-detect + stop), so the nightly Sleep just works; **(b) the in-session agent (the user's framing)** — the agent already talking to Cicada over MCP does consolidation itself: a `cicada_consolidate` tool (or librarian skill) hands the agent batches of unprocessed episodes + the extraction contract, the agent returns structured entities/claims through the existing trust-gated `write_claim`/staging paths, `Cicada-Author` records the actual model, G48 stamps the session — the 2026-07-14 "988 episodes → 442 claims" librarian run already proved this path with zero keys. Both rungs record to the G51 ledger (fixing the all-zeros dashboard, which was itself evidence Sleep never ran). Guard rails: engine choice surfaced honestly in the Sleep page ("no engine — connect a plan or run an agent consolidation"), never a silent no-op again — the current silence is the bug. **(Absorbs G80, merged 2026-09-01 — "the deterministic rung — keep the graph growing when no model is available"):** the argument, stated precisely so it isn't misread as "regex beats LLMs" — regex cannot resolve "Mongo"/"MongoDB"/"the database we switched to" (Stage 2's irreducibly semantic job); what a deterministic rung wins on is three **operational** properties: always **available**, **deterministic**, and **cannot hallucinate into versioned memory**. **The key mapping onto the north star:** the *curation* stream (a save, a bookmark, a paper, a `saved-because` reason) is largely pre-structured — the user already did the selecting — while the *experience* stream (chat prose, meetings) is not; so a deterministic rung keeps curation alive with no engine and cannot keep experience-extraction alive, which is exactly the shape of the 2.5-month outage: nothing was lost (`processed: false` is durable), but the product was dead. **Evidence this is cheap, not a rewrite (verified 2026-09-01 by grep): Sleep has 11 stages and only 5 call a model** — Stage 1 extraction, Stage 2 disambiguation, Stage 3 conflict/synthesis, Stage 4 skills, Stage 5.57 link enrichment. The other six already run today with no engine and zero LLM refs: Stage 5 nudge/inbox generation (`inbox_questions.py`), Stage 5.5 wikilink→`mentions` edges, Stage 5.55 media edges, Stage 5.56 the claim layer (`claim_reconciler.py`), Stage 5.6 hub tier + `_index.md`, Stage 5.7 `graph_builder.regenerate_edges_from_claims`; `promote_targets.py` and `vector_index.py` are likewise model-free, and the default embedding backend is on-device (EmbeddingGemma) — semantic clustering is already available with zero engine and zero cost. **What to build (exploration, then a slice):** a **Tier-0 structural write path** running whether or not an engine exists — harvest structure the user already supplied (URLs, domains, page titles, repo/file paths, hashtags, explicit `[[wikilinks]]`, Telegram `saved-because` reasons, platform collection/board names from G71, calendar attendees), recurrence counting against the promotion thresholds, embedding-based near-duplicate candidate generation, and structural decay/versioning (already works). **Tier-1** (still model-free): normalization + fuzzy match proposing `merge_suggestion` inbox items rather than performing them. **Tier-2** stays the LLM's: typed extraction, disambiguation, contradiction detection, pattern distillation. **The honest tension:** consolidation *is* the thesis claim — decay, versioned beliefs and per-belief provenance need a written structured graph, so Cicada cannot adopt zero-LLM writes wholesale without becoming RAG with extra steps; what it should adopt is the discipline that the graph's growth must not depend entirely on a component that can be unavailable. **Open questions:** does a Tier-0 entity earn a distinct `source_trust`/confidence band; does a later LLM pass upgrade a Tier-0 entity in place or supersede it as a claim; should Tier-0 run at capture time rather than only inside Sleep. **2026-09-29:** the plan-engine cycle becomes journal-backed and optionally parallel in **G163** (SL-1 the journal, SL-3 parallel reading); scheduled cycles stay one batch, no small model, no auto-continue (ruling 4 unchanged unless spec Q1 is approved). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · engine ladder exists; Tier-0 / structural work remains. | PARTIAL · P3 |
