Interview seeding remains G54; seeded owner-page live verification is G169/G10. Historical first-run absence and open-PR labels below are not current state. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** Today a fresh install lands on an empty Graph tab with no bank, no engine, no capture channel and no explanation — the person has to already know that `install.sh` made a launchd backend, that consolidation needs `CICADA_LLM_MODE`, that memory arrives through MCP/Telegram/imports, and that nothing appears until a Sleep cycle runs. Every one of those is discoverable only from CLAUDE.md or the README. **Evidence:** `ContentView.swift` has no first-run branch (grep `firstRun\|Onboarding` → only `ConnectView` and `CommandBox`); Settings holds Agents and Plans & keys but nothing routes a new user there; the demo bank built for the README screenshots (2026-09-02) had to be hand-assembled because nothing in the app can create a populated bank. **Scope (build, $0):** (1) a first-run sheet shown when no bank has entities — three steps, each with live state read from the API: *connect a plan or engine* (`GET /connections`, reuse `ConnectionsView` rows; `auto` picks agent → local → byok). **Rodrigo 2026-09-04 — this step is a real choice, not a checkbox, and the range is the point:** onboarding must present the ways a person can actually pay for consolidation and let them pick one — an **OpenRouter key** (one key, any model, the cheapest way in), a **provider API key** (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini — the byok rung that exists today), a **subscription they already hold** (Claude Pro/Max through the `claude` CLI, ChatGPT/Codex through its own login — the agent rung, and the one worth defaulting to, since it is money already spent), **Claude with extra usage** (a Max plan that bills overage rather than throttling — the tier override in `PUT /connections/{id}/prefs` already models this and the picker must say what it costs), and **local Ollama** ($0, offline, weakest). Each option states its cost model in one line before it is chosen, not after — the G51 honesty rule ("subscription = flat $/mo, usage = real per-model $, local = free") applied at the moment of choosing rather than in a dashboard nobody opens. The picker is the same surface **G122** puts on the Sleep page; build it once, show it in both places, and let the onboarding step write `CICADA_LLM_MODE` so nobody edits `api/.env` to get started, *pick a capture channel* (`GET /sources/channels`; MCP registration status from `install.sh`, Telegram, an import drop, a bookmarks sync), *run your first Sleep* (`POST /sleep/trigger` with the live `sleep` SSE state, ending on the Graph tab); (2) an empty-state per tab that says what will fill it and offers the one action that does (Graph → import or capture; Inbox → "questions appear after a Sleep"; Feed → add a source; Activity → "nothing recorded yet"); (3) a **demo bank** the sheet can create in one click (`POST /banks` + a seeded synthetic set, reusing the 2026-09-02 demo-bank generator once it is checked in as a script — fictional people/projects only) so the graph is never blank on first launch and the person can try Sleep/inbox/decay before wiring their own life in; (4) the persisted `hasOnboarded` flag lives per bank in `UserDefaults`, and the sheet is re-openable from Settings. **Constraints:** no new dependency; no network beyond the endpoints named; the sheet reads the same `Store` snapshots as every other view (never blank, never a second source of truth); copy follows the Claude Code question rules (one action per step, recommended first, never a modal that blocks an existing user — it only appears when the active bank is empty); portability rails — no owner name, no author-machine path, the demo bank ships with placeholder names exactly as the test fixtures do. **Not in scope:** the paste-prompt install itself (G76), the strategic what-is-Cicada decision (G92 — this row assumes route (a), local-first), the onboarding *interview* that seeds memory from a conversation (G54; a natural step 4 later). → blocks a downloadable 1.0 alongside **G76** and **G90**; consumes **G50** (connections UI), **G62** (channel state); relates **G108** (landing page), **G107** (status mascot as the sheet's guide). APPLY, M **Owner, 2026-09-03 — identity capture:** the first-run sheet also asks the person's name (and optionally handle/email, never sent anywhere) and ties it to the **owner entity** — a `person` page that renders as *Name (you)*, the way your own contact reads in WhatsApp — which replaces the hardcoded `observer: rodrigo` literal (`settings.observer_owner`, see G115/G121) and becomes the `observer` every user-stated claim carries. The demo bank ships with a placeholder owner so the sheet has something to show. **Shipped 2026-09-05 (`feat/onboarding`):** `owner_identity.resolve_observer` (R1's four-rung precedence, `~/.cicada/owner.json`, migration-safe against a pre-G117 `entities/rodrigo.md`) replaces the hardcoded `"rodrigo"` literal at all five sites (`inbox_service`, `telegram_capture`, `agentic_write`, `mcp/server.py`'s tool schema and description); `GET/PUT /settings/owner` writes the owner entity page (`owner: true`, `decay_class: evergreen`) and the app renders it, and its graph node, as "Name (you)" (R2's not-agent-not-external rule keeps every self-stated claim from misrendering as External the moment the resolved observer stops being the bare string `"rodrigo"`); the four-step first-run sheet (identity → engine → one channel → first Sleep) replaces the old single-step Connect guide, re-openable from Settings; every tab that can be empty (Graph, Inbox, Feed, Sources) now says so with one honest action instead of a blank canvas; a checked-in, deterministic `demo_bank.py` (R7 — hand-authored, no LLM, no randomness) backs a one-click `POST /banks/demo` so the sheet is never staring at nothing; three install/copy gaps shipped alongside (`install.sh`'s `-s user`, the README `/sleep/trigger` bearer header, and `sleep_cycle`'s byok failure copy no longer diagnosing a key nobody entered when no engine was ever chosen). **Open remainder, not this row's scope:** the onboarding *interview* that seeds memory from a conversation (**G54**, the natural step 4 later); entity-merge-across-identity-change (R3's disclosed gap — a person who re-onboards under a different display name gets a second page, never reconciled with the old one). **2026-09-29:** the owner-page question left open above is closed by **G169** — every bank now starts with a seeded `owner: true` page ('Name (you)' rendered from the flag, a neutral placeholder sentence, nothing carried from another bank), built in PR #140 (not merged). **Previous execution label (historical):** PARTIAL · close only outstanding first-run seams. | VERIFY · P3 |
| G133 | <a id="g133"></a>**A watched folder, and papers with why they matter to you** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "in this project i have a bunch of md files explaining ideas, referencing papers, etc. I'd like for this to just live in cicada as well. with papers with summaries and why they are relevant to me") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Watched folders, document parsing and paper personal-reason handling exist. Reproduce garbled synthetic titles and verify citation/source lifecycle; folder context policies are a later named extension. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g133). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** A folder of the owner's own markdown — ideas, plans, reading lists — had no way in except pasting it into a chat, and a paper reached memory only as a bare bookmark with a web preview: no authors, no abstract, nothing saying why it was kept. **What shipped (round 3 Track L; plan `docs/plans/2026-09-23-local-sources.md`, rulings R-LS1 … R-LS30).** (1) *One stager for every source-keyed writer:* the G20 stager moved to `api/services/episode_staging.py` — an `EpisodeDraft` keyed by `source_id`, the hash over the scrubbed body, an edit rewritten in place with `processed: false`, a rename kept by content hash, a deletion tombstoned (`source_deleted_at`), never unlinked; a multi-turn source records G118's `turns: [{offset, ts, speaker}, …]` sidecar (first written here as `turn_index` rows, because `turns:` is the transcript's integer count; the merge with G118 slice 2 kept one key — `evidence.turn_stamps` reads the hook's int as no stamps). (2) *The app reads the folder, the backend parses bytes:* a security-scoped bookmark, FSEvents and an mtime+size+sha manifest post only what changed, with relative paths; the backend never opens the folder. One episode per file, split on H2 only past 48,000 characters. Files under an agent glob (`archive/**`) land `processed: true, processed_by: parser`, `evidence_kind: assistant` — an agent's research sweep is never the owner's words (R-F2) — and flipping a glob re-queues them. The folder anchors a `project` page by name, which learns `paths: [{path, device}]`. (3) *Papers with no LLM:* arXiv ids and DOIs are parsed deterministically into `media` pages with `media.kind: paper` — no new type, the G2 closure held — keyed `media-arxiv-<id>` / `media-doi-<hash>`, an existing bookmark upgraded in place. The owner's own line about a paper becomes a `saved-because` claim with a span into his file, and its section an `about` link through the zero-LLM matcher: that span *is* the why-it-matters. Details come from the arXiv API (≤ 50 ids a request, ≥ 3 s apart) and Crossref (public pool, no email sent), never from arxiv.org pages, under the ToS rail; the abstract is a world-tier `describes` claim (`external`, authored by `cicada`) shown *under* the personal tier (G121). A paper no file cites any more gets a G129-shaped `removal` item, never a silent delete. Built and tested on synthetic fixtures only; the owner's folder is ingested after merge and its counts are not recorded here (privacy rule). **Open:** per-folder filtering of the source detail page (every folder row shares the `folder` origin); no backfill of `turn_index` on older episodes (readers accept its absence). **F1:** paper claims stored `folder:<id>:<section>` as their context, which the M5b facet rule turned into two empty graph satellites per annotated paper and a raw legend row. They now write `general` with unchanged ids (the id names the slot). A folder sync projects their `cited-in`/`about` edges, so a paper sits by its project before any Sleep cycle, and a one-shot `cicada`-authored repair migrated existing banks (R-FX4 … R-FX7). *Revisit:* a per-folder context the owner picks. **F2-back:** a rules change re-derived only the files the app re-posted; saving rules now re-derives every existing episode in place (R-B6, R-B7). A belief Sleep formed from a file later declared an agent's keeps its minted span kind — the owner's call (R-B8). **DS-3b:** 'Written by an agent' is a subfolder checklist, not a typed glob. **2026-09-29:** `papers.never_scraped` and the paper and video hand-offs are restated as the reading-the-web spec's rail (**G164**); a paper or video link found inside an imported chat (**G165**) is routed to those hand-offs, never fetched by the Reader. **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅ round 3. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G134 | <a id="g134"></a>**Note-takers as a source, Wispr Flow first — meetings keep their speakers, dictation only when asked** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23: "make sure to support note taker apps like wisprflow or others.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Wispr speaker capture, dictation opt-in and deferred todo claim replay exist. Todos-to-Inbox, other note-taker adapters and recording consent remain; old wording must not ask to rebuild todo claim replay. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g134). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** G95 named meetings the one intake class the pipeline was not built for: every episode was single-observer, so a pasted transcript would have credited every line to the owner. **What shipped (round 3 Track L, R-LS21 … R-LS24).** The app opens Wispr Flow's local SQLite read-only (one transaction, a busy timeout) through a column whitelist held on **both** sides — audio, screenshots, accessibility text, pasted text and raw ASR are never selected, and the tests plant canaries in every one — and posts a projection; the backend parses. Meetings and notes by default; dictation history is opt-in, grouped per UTC day and merged server-side through the day's marker lines and `turns` sidecar, with password-manager apps denied. An utterance is a `speaker:<label>:` line and a new evidence kind, `speaker`; it is `user` evidence **only** when its label is one of the owner's listed names. Wispr's own summary, notes and to-dos are `assistant:` lines (a model wrote them), and a to-do becomes a `committed-to` claim on the owner page at `observer: agent`, 0.5. Secrets and one-time codes are scrubbed on **every** episode writer by one module, `episode_scrub.py`, pinned by a test that fails for any writer minting an id without it (R-N3). Settings → Integrations gains **Voice & meetings** and the Sources grid VOICE & MEETINGS. **Open, on purpose:** to-dos into the Inbox (needs a `todo` inbox kind — the G60/G113 surfaces); Granola, Fireflies, Otter and Notion adapters (they reuse the `speaker:` seam; Wispr Flow's own Granola import covers a migrated person today); a consent surface for recording other people (G95). **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅ round 3. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G142 | <a id="g142"></a>**Apple Calendar through EventKit — every calendar on the Mac, not only the ones with an ICS link** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24, round 4 brief: "turn on whatever already works including calendars") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** EventKit app reader and byte-posted calendar parsing exist. Real occurrence/privacy/source attribution acceptance remains; Google account OAuth/event citations are G155, not implied by EventKit. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g142). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** Calendars reached Cicada only as ICS subscriptions (`calendar_registry.py`). A calendar with no published ICS link never arrived, and even a published one needed its URL found and pasted per calendar. The Mac's Calendar app already holds every account. **The design (round 4 D2, contract C6).** The app reads EventKit after one standard macOS prompt: every account, a rolling window, re-read on `EKEventStoreChanged`. The backend never opens `~/Library` (the Awake rail). The app posts the WHOLE window to `POST /sources/calendar-local/sync`. `calendar_local.py` stages each event through `episode_staging` keyed `calendar-local:<id>` (the external identifier, plus the occurrence start for a recurring event), so an edit rewrites its episode in place and re-queues it. An event gone from the window is tombstoned (`source_deleted_at`), never deleted, but only for a calendar the request named. Notes are scrubbed first and then cut at 2,000 characters, so a passcode straddling the cut is redacted whole. A link keeps scheme, host and path only (a meeting passcode lives in the query). Attendees stop at 50. A demo bank gets 409. Each sync makes one `user` commit (`capture/calendar`), scoped to the episodes it wrote. `GET /sources/channels` always lists `calendar-local` ("Apple Calendar"), and the Sources catalog has its row. ICS subscriptions are unchanged. **Open:** a recurring event is one episode per occurrence in the window, so watch the episode count after the owner's first sync; attendees stay names in text, and Sleep's promotion rules decide whether one becomes a person page. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ backend built (feat/r4-foundations-back); the EventKit reader ships on feat/r4-foundations-app. **2026-10-23:** Apple Reminders rides this path — the same `EKEventStore`, its own prompt, and no read-only grant (full access is read and write, so the sheet must say so) — as **G219**, with this row's sync rules (the person's chosen lists, staged by external identifier, tombstoned for named lists only). | VERIFY · P3 |
| G150 | <a id="g150"></a>**Backlogs live in memory — a project's backlog is first-class memory: filed by agents over MCP and by the person in the app, shown on the Projects page, found by ⌘K, every note signed** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "this back and forth where i tell an agent to put it in the backlog, that backlog itself i believe should go in cicada project memory, dont you think? with the brief task and an appended notes/description and maybe some other things that might be relevant to how we show and store them.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Per-project backlog files, governed writers, proposal triage and app views exist. Remaining description/link/order editing and repository-public mirroring need separate slices/decisions, not a new entity type. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g150). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The problem.** "Put it in the backlog" meant an agent editing a markdown table in some repository — this file is the example — invisible to Cicada, to every other agent and to the person's own app, with nothing saying who added a row or a finding. **The design** (round 4; plan `docs/plans/2026-09-24-r4-backlog-in-memory.md`, rulings R-B1…R-B27). One markdown file per item at `<bank>/backlog/<project-id>/<item-id>.md`: `id`, `title` (the brief task), `project`, `status` (open · doing · done · dropped), `triage` (apply · research · decide), `paid` (💸), `created`/`updated`, `added_by`, `session`, `links`, `order`, and a `notes: [{at, by, session}]` sidecar; `## Description` holds the reasoning and `## Notes` append-only `### <day> · <who>` entries — a status move is itself a signed note, and a note is edited only through git history. Never an entity page: never extracted, never decays, and the writer never touches `entities/`. Ids are `<PREFIX><n>` like G-ids — the page's `backlog_prefix:`, else the prefix the project's items share (an imported G-row list continues its sequence), else the name's initials (`Orchard` → `ORC`) — max+1 (G114), claimed by an exclusive create. **One writer**, `api/services/backlog.py`, behind REST (`GET/POST /projects/{id}/backlog`, `GET/PATCH /backlog/{project}/{item}`, `POST …/notes`, `POST /projects/{id}/backlog/import`; 409 while Sleep runs; `Cicada-Author: user`), MCP (`cicada_backlog` read; `cicada_add_backlog_item` and `cicada_add_backlog_note` record — the harness as author, the conversation as `Cicada-Session:`, refused while Sleep runs and in a demo bank; a remote connection without `sources` never reads the person's own words), the importer and the demo. Everything is scrubbed; a second open item for one idea is refused and pointed at the first. The contract (CONTRACT_VERSION 7) tells an agent to file the brief task and the reasoning when the person says "put it in the backlog", and later findings as notes on the same item; `_state.md` v4 counts each project's open items for the primer's Current line; `cicada_project` lists the top five. The Projects page's Backlog section (Open · Doing · Done · All) opens an item as the third column; ⌘K has a Backlog group. `backlog_import.py` and `scripts/import-backlog.sh` file G-row tables and `### G<n>` sections keeping ids and statuses — an id already filed is skipped, so a second run changes nothing. **Open.** (1) v2: Sleep's Stage 1 proposes an inbox item "Add to the backlog?" when a conversation says so and no agent filed it, graded by G113 like every resolution. (2) A note's model and effort: the wire carries `authorModel`/`authorEffort` (null in v1); round 4's per-turn join fills them. (3) Whether a backlog write should count as a mention of its project for decay — v1 says no. (4) **DECIDE (owner):** once this very backlog lives in the owner's bank, does `docs/goals/` in this public repository stay its public mirror — and which way would it sync — or become a pointer? (5) Editing a description, triage, links and order in the app (the PATCH already takes triage, `paid` and links). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ v1 built on `feat/r4-backlog-in-memory`. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G154 | <a id="g154"></a>**Contacts sync — macOS Contacts through one prompt, enriching the people already in memory** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "we should have the ability to sync contacts too") | **2026-10-20 — owner rulings (TODO ruling 33; branch `feat/people-seed`): Contacts as a seed, and imported-only pages stay quiet.** (1) *Cards wait, and a card is matched on mention* — never a page per card: an unmatched card waits outside every bank in the people directory LinkedIn already used (`people_directory`: `$CICADA_HOME/people/<bank>/contacts.json` + hashed-name thumbnails, 0600), rewritten whole by every sync; a person's first conversation mention naming exactly one waiting card in full (either order, folded, or by alias) promotes on that alone and the page is born under the card's name with its sources and photo; never a one-part name, a shared name, a relation-only person, a save's text, or the "me" card (the app marks it, `isMe`, and it maps to the owner's page, G169); Sleep's tail rematches the rest. Measured before: 0 of 273 person pages matched on the owner's bank (Contacts not connected). (2) *Quiet pages*: a page only an import made (saved link, video, playlist item) is dimmed and never asked about until the person mentions it — derived on read (`quiet_pages`: import evidence on the page, no conversation evidence; demo bank 74 pages → 7 imported → 6 quiet). (3) The notes field: not now. Design in `capture-and-sleep.md` (*Entity promotion*, Contacts) and `features.md`. **2026-10-16 — LinkedIn connections, on this row's pattern** (Rodrigo 2026-10-16: "extracting people profiles from my LinkedIn connections (maybe could be a skill) would be nice"; branch `feat/linkedin-people`). The export's `Connections.csv` comes in through its own door (LinkedIn tile → Connections; the G71 save-list importer still refuses it by name) and, like a Contacts card, **enriches exactly one existing `person` page and never mints one** — matched by a profile link the page already lists, else the folded full name or an alias; never the owner's page, a page naming another profile, or a duplicated name. A match gains the profile link as a `cicada` source and `works-at`/`role`/`connected-on` claims observed `external:linkedin`, dated as of the export, newest wins; the email column is never read; a link the person removed is never re-added and its connection writes nothing more there. The rest waits in a directory outside the bank (`$CICADA_HOME/people/<bank>/`) and Sleep's tail matches it against later pages. **The skill half:** `skills/linkedin-people` (Cicada's own bundle) + stdio-only `cicada_record_profile` — one person, on the person's ask, through the link their page lists, in the person's browser, ≤ 10 a day; quotes become `page` spans, facts the newest LinkedIn line. Design and rails in `capture-and-sleep.md` / `mcp.md`. **Open:** whether the photo rung should use an import-added link without the person taking it first (today it needs the person's take: a name match is not proof of identity, `person_photo.is_own_account`); a CLI door (`cicada import` does not route Connections.csv). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Contacts opt-in reader and existing-person enrichment exist. Derived identity claims/merge confirmation and deeper matching remain; keep raw contact details outside public docs. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g154). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **This is G81 moving into scope, not a second idea.** **G81** holds the reasoning (identity anchors for `person` pages, dedup on a shared email, `sources:` for people, speaker resolution for meetings) and the privacy rail (a contact is a third party's PII landing in a permanently-historied store); round 4 decision 12 puts it on the Import page (G145, *Calendar & Contacts*). **Shipped (round 4, first slice; plan `2026-09-24-r4-sources-2.md`, R-SR8 … R-SR10, R-SR16):** the **app** reads the Mac's address book through the Contacts framework after one standard prompt (`NSContactsUsageDescription`; `ContactsReader` over a `ContactStore` seam) and posts the whole book to `POST /sources/contacts-local/sync` — per card its names, **which** facts it holds (employer, job title, email, phone, birthday — booleans) and a thumbnail ≤ 64 KB. **No email address, phone number, employer name or note ever crosses the wire or lands in a bank**, which settles G81's open question: there is no value to hash or to leak. The backend (`contacts_local.py`) matches a card to **exactly one** existing `person` page by its given + family name in either order, folded like search, against the page's name and aliases; a one-part name never matches, and two pages sharing a name or two cards on one page are skipped and counted. It **never creates a page**. A match adds G61 sources `{ref: addressbook://<card id>, kind: app, predicate, added_by: cicada}` for `works-at`, `role`, `email`, `phone` and `birthday`; the sync owns exactly those entries (adds, keeps, removes) and never touches one the person or an agent added. The thumbnail is cached at `$CICADA_HOME/pictures/<bank>/contacts/<id>.<jpg\|png>` — `contacts_local.photo_path(bank, id, ext)`, the one path **G146**'s picture ladder reads — never in a bank, and the page carries `contacts_photo: {sha, ext}` so the graph's ETag moves. 409 while Sleep runs (Stage 5 rewrites entity pages); demo-gated; one `user` commit per sync (`capture/contacts`). **Cadence (decided):** on Connect; on launch only when `currentHistoryToken` moved or this bank's last post is a day old; on `CNContactStoreDidChange`, debounced 30 s; on a bank switch and on Sync now. An empty read is never posted. **A deleted card:** its entries leave the page on the next sync and git keeps the history; nothing else was stored. **Next slices:** claims with a `contacts` origin for employer, role and birthday values (the values would then cross the wire — decide that rail first); a card shared by two person pages as a `merge_suggestion`; nicknames; the notes field (Apple's `com.apple.developer.contacts.notes` entitlement); postal addresses, relations and social profiles; speaker labels for meetings (**G134**). Telemetry ids only. **On onboarding's Import page (G145 seam 4, 2026-09-25):** a *Contacts* row under *Calendar & contacts* — its tick is `ContactsReader.connect()` (the one prompt, then the first read), its untick `disconnect()`, and its idle line says macOS asks once and no page is made per contact. → builds **G81**; feeds **G146**, **G159**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ first slice (round 4) · 🔲 claims, merges. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G160 | <a id="g160"></a>**Browser tab groups — what the person is working on right now** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "some browsers like chrome support making tab groups. if we are able to detect this, awesome too, gives information too") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Tab-group reader and stable snapshot episode staging exist. Validate observational attribution and useful current-work semantics; an open tab is not a personal assertion or evidence of a completed task. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g160). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Shipped (round 4, first slice):** Chrome's OPEN groups, read by the app from the default profile's `Sessions/` SNSS file (commands 25 and 27 plus each member tab's current navigation; the metadata pickle grew in M88 and M113, so the reader stops cleanly at a short pickle), behind their own switch (`cicada.browserWatch.enabled.chrome-tab-groups`, off until turned on), FSEvents debounced 10 s and floored at 60 s, a snapshot digest per bank; `POST /sources/tab-groups/sync` stages one snapshot episode per group (identity: saved guid, else folded title + colour, else the session token), rewritten in place, tombstoned per browser and profile. Only clear version-3 files that carry the initial-state marker are read; incognito is never on disk; no page contents. **Follow-ups, in order:** (1) **Chrome's session encryption** — `kEncryptSessionStorage` is on by default at stage `write_both_read_only_clear` (clear files still written); a later stage stops writing clear files, and then this reader has nothing to read: the trigger is the row saying so on a real install, and the answer is not decryption (that is Chrome's Keychain key) but saved groups (2). (2) **Saved and synced groups** — the profile's `Sync Data/LevelDB` (`saved_tab_group-dt-<uuid>` protobuf rows; copy the directory, open the copy read-only; the saved colour enum is offset by one from the live one). (3) **Brave, Comet and Dia** — likely Chrome's session code unchanged; verify on a real install, then add each to `tab_groups.BROWSERS` and `BrowserReadable.tabGroups`. Vivaldi's tab stacks are its own format. (4) **Safari Tab Groups** — `SafariTabs.db` in Safari's container (Full Disk Access; the macOS schema needs checking against a real file). (5) **Arc** folders (`StorableSidebar.json`, no colour per folder), **Firefox** (`sessionstore.jsonlz4`, mozLZ4 + JSON), **Edge** Workspaces (mid-migration — skip until settled). (6) The product question the first slice leaves open: whether a group whose name matches a project feeds that project's `now` (G141) instead of, or beside, its own episode. (7) **Whose words a tab title is** — the snapshot has no turn markers, so `evidence.speaker_kind` reads every line as `user`; the group's name is the person's, a tab's page title is not (the same gap calendar and bookmark episodes carry). Decide whether a snapshot source declares a kind before a chip says "You said" over a page title. **On onboarding's Import page (G145 seam 4, 2026-09-25):** *Open tab groups* is a sub-row under Chrome with its own tick (`TabGroupWatcher.enable()` / `disable()`, never Chrome's bookmark tick); no Sessions file yet reads "Chrome hasn't saved any open windows on this Mac yet" and keeps the switch on. **Rails:** app reads, backend parses; the switch is its own consent (live work data, distinct from bookmarks); only titles and links; profile selection follows G119. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ first slice (round 4) · 🔲 follow-ups. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G161 | <a id="g161"></a>**What came in, by name — a list under each source of exactly which items it captured** (Rodrigo 2026-09-28: "in the page where i'm syncing everything, i'd like to see a list of the things that where captures, so out of the 229 notes for example, see which ones it detected. Maybe it can be a small list that opens below each thing.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Named channel-item lists and episode rows exist. Saved-link rows intentionally point to media pages; exposing the saving conversation is a separate provenance/navigation seam, not a missing item-list API. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g161). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The gap.** Every source row (`SourceRow` — onboarding's Import page, Home's Getting started, Integrations, the Sources detail column) says *how many* came in (`SourceRowText.countLine`, the channel's `parts`), never *which*: "229 notes" cannot be checked against the person's own library, and a wrong import (a folder that should have been excluded, a calendar that should not be read) is invisible until Sleep has read it. **The shape:** a small disclosure under each row listing the items by their own title and day, newest first, a bounded page with "Show all" into the Sources detail column, each row opening its episode in the Reader. **Constraints:** read-only and engine-free; one read endpoint per channel, derived at read from the episodes a channel stages (its `origin` / `source_id` prefix / index), never a Store domain (ETag + in-memory cache, like provenance); titles only on the wire — no bodies, no Contacts facts beyond the matched page's name, ids in `.help`; the same list everywhere a row is drawn. → relates **G124** (Sources page), **G145** (onboarding Import), **G126** (Integrations), **G118** (the Reader). **Shipped (2026-09-28, PR #134):** `GET /sources/channels/{id}/items?offset&limit` (≤ 200, `api/services/channel_items.py`) — derived at read from the set each row's count means: the notes index (the latest episode per note), calendar and tab-group episodes by `source_id` (never a tombstoned one; an event's own day), the bookmark seen-set joined to the url index (the browser's media pages when no seen-set exists yet), a channel's media pages by its catalog origins (hidden ones left out, as the Feed does), a chat export's or Wispr Flow's episodes, a folder one row per file; Contacts lists the matched pages' names and nothing from a card. Titles scrubbed and cut to one line of ≤ 120 characters, never a body; ETag over `sources`+`episodes`+`entities` with the shape, channel and page; 404 for a channel the bank does not know. The app's `CapturedItemsList` ("What came in", collapsed and remembered per viewer per channel) sits under onboarding's Import rows (a preview — rows open nothing under the onboarding layer), every Integrations source row, and is the Sources detail column's content for a source without saved links; twenty rows, then "Show more"; a row opens its episode in the Reader, a saved item in the Feed, a person in Clusters; `ChannelItemsCache` is in memory only and emptied on a bank switch. **Disclosed drifts:** the list's `total` is its own — a notes index keeps a note deleted in Notes, a calendar keeps an event that left the window, one page can hold two bookmarked URLs, and `files`' row counts the whole url index while its list is its card's. **Not done:** Home's Getting started rows (the card is transient) and the "Show all" hand-off from a row to the Sources detail column. **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G163 | <a id="g163"></a>**Sleep page v5 — a progress bar you can trust, reading everything that is waiting, and reading faster on purpose** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "if we are using the chatgpt plan, maybe even the codex harness or the plan directly we can have an option for parallel subagents with small models so consolidation can happen faster, and a reliable progress bar that shows what has been read and consolidated would be good too." And: "an improved design for the consolidation (sleep) page") | **2026-10-08 (the page during a run):** `GET /sleep/status` took ~11 s mid-batch on a ~3,600-page bank and sometimes outran a 20 s client. Cause: Stage 2's page load and Stage 5's bank-wide steps (page writes, wikilink/media edges, claim layer, hubs, claim edges, processed flags) ran synchronously on the event loop; a status request needs several loop turns, so it waited out one or more stalls. Measured with a synthetic bank and a fake engine holding calls 8 s: worst status 4.5 s → 0.22 s, worst loop stall 4.6 s → 0.12 s; the plan semaphore and the default executor were not the cause (≤ 14 of 18 workers). Fix: those steps run in `asyncio.to_thread`, `_name_tokens` memoised. Also: an engine change through `PUT /sleep/engine` from outside the app now moves a new `engine` sync component the page reloads on, and a run that keeps the engine it started with says so in one provider-neutral line. **2026-10-07 (#224):** an engine timeout, connection loss or unnamed engine-wide failure pauses the drain (transient: *Continue to try again*; needs-fix: *Continue when it is fixed*) instead of ending the run or parking the queue; Home's first-read card routes to Details. Parked: the Details *Last cycle* row still prints the raw engine exception — give it a plain sentence with the raw text secondary. Owner question (2026-10-07): does the app time consolidation without pauses? Yes (Details: *Read for X · Paused for Y*); missing: per-stage/per-conversation timing and time lost to restarts. **Incremental-processing linkage (owner 2026-10-06):** Coordinate accepted-reading journal keys and crash/plan-pause recovery with G104 source/turn/chunk revisions. Reusing accepted output after a failed batch differs from skipping already-committed prefixes of a grown thread; cache invalidation and atomic checkpoint advancement must cover both. This does not unpark declined parallel reading. [G104 research](../research/2026-10-06-incremental-consolidation.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Drain, pause/continue, committed batches and run history exist. In-progress reading journal is not implemented; Home still needs run-level last-read semantics. Parallel reading was declined and stays conditional, not an automatic priority. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g163). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Why the bar cannot be reliable today (spec §0, by reading the code, not by running it).** Stages 1–4 write nothing to disk; Stage 5 files everything and marks episodes processed (`sleep_cycle.py:1370-1379`). A cancel, a plan stop, a crash or a restart throws away every paid Stage 1 answer, and a plan stop after some episodes were read also makes Stage 2's first judge call raise. So the fix is the cause, not the paint. **The design ([`2026-09-29-sleep-page-v5-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-sleep-page-v5-design.md), revision 2; every critique adopted, none shown wrong).** (1) *A journal* keeps every paid LLM answer a cycle bought, so 'read' and 'filed' are separate durable states that survive a pause, a plan stop, a restart and a crash; it stores an answer only after the calling stage accepts it, only for Sleep calls, with an episode index of chunk keys (what 'read' and 'resumable' count) and the model that wrote each claim carried onto the claim (the 'records the model' copy waits on that). (2) *A drain* — 'Read everything' — is a person-started run over a frozen list of what was waiting; a drain hold plus one `is_active()` helper replaces the `status == "running"` checks (scheduler and trigger included); the drain is pinned to its bank with a fixed-bank `Settings`, `activate` answers 409 while a drain is reading, failures map by exception class (an empty answer is a content failure, not 'signed out'), a 3-attempt cap and a no-progress pause keep it finite, and both decay engines and the in-cycle link reads are skipped per batch and run once at the end (a backdated import must not decay mid-drain, see **G170**). (3) *Triggers:* a no-body `POST /sleep/trigger` is one batch from every door; a drain starts only from the sheet's body and continues only through `POST /sleep/run/continue`. (4) *Defaults:* one batch of 25, no body sent, `workers=3`, no small model, no auto-continue; parallel reading, the small model and auto-continue are opt-in and never run on a schedule. (5) *Reserve:* covers Claude's weekly window when the stream reports it; copy says the run 'stops starting new reads; running calls finish'; overshoot is unmeasured on both plans, so 6 and 10 workers are not offered until measured (EX-2). (6) *Usage folded in:* drain cost sums `llm_call` rows by `drain_id`; the call count is a fact; plan percentages stay on the engine menu and Details only (ruling 12 not widened); `PriceLintTests` is new work. (7) *First run:* the owner's page (see **G169**) fills visibly — after the first batch the run stops to ask ('your page has N beliefs, read the rest?'), a count of claims on the `owner: true` page, engine-free. **Slices:** SL-1 the journal, accept protocol, episode index, per-episode outcomes, authorship stamp and honest bar; SL-2 the drain controller, hold, sheet and pauses; SL-3 parallel reading (gated on EX-2, EX-3, EX-5, the authorship stamp and the owner's answers); SL-4 the drain-tagged cost summary; SL-5 later (read-ahead, per-stage model map, re-reads). Experiments EX-1…EX-5 run before any default is chosen. **Risks added by the review:** R-14 `activate` is refused during a drain; R-15 a weekly reserve is unenforceable when the stream does not report it; R-16 a stage that forgets `accept` means no replay (fails safe). **Open questions for the owner (spec §8):** Q1 may a run continue itself after a plan reset — a ruling 4 amendment, recommend yes as a per-run off-by-default checkbox, two continuations, 36 hours, never scheduled; Q1b is a first-run drain on a consumer plan acceptable at all (order of 12,000–21,000 judge calls for a ~1,200-conversation export, arithmetic on an old figure; vendor terms not re-read); Q2 when may 6 and 10 workers be chosen (only after EX-2 on both plans and sign-off); Q3 which stages use the small model (Read and Sort only); Q4 reserve defaults (Claude 10% on both windows, 25% for a weekly-only ChatGPT window); Q5 keep the Mac awake during a run; Q6 the first drain of a bank starts with one batch and then asks; Q7 may the Sleep page gain run controls beside Consolidate — a G125 R10 amendment; Q8 keep plan figures on Details and the engine menu only. → extends **G107**, **G125** (R10's one trigger), **G51** (ruling 12), **G122**, **G74**, **G104**; the video tail is **G162**. Triage: SL-1, SL-2, SL-4 APPLY; SL-3 DECIDE + measure 💸. **Owner decision 2026-09-29 (TODO ruling 13): "i dont want to cap the max episodes per sleep, why would we cap them? its just progress that cicada has to go through."** This supersedes the design's drain-only-from-a-sheet rule (V5-15/Q7) and its hold / `is_active()` / `run.json` / `continue` machinery: **Consolidate is the drain.** *Built (branch `feat/consolidate-reads-everything`, backend half):* a person-started `POST /sleep/trigger` freezes the waiting ids and reads them in batches of `sleep_max_episodes_per_cycle` (default 25, now "how often progress is saved"), each batch filed and committed by Stage 5 (`Sleep cycle <date> (batch k of n)`), so a cancel or a plan stop loses at most the batch in progress; a plan limit is a pause with the vendor's sentence and reset time; scheduled cycles stay one batch (ruling 4); decay and Stage 5.57's page reads run once per drain, the tail once per run; `GET /sleep/status` gains a measured `drain` block (batch k of n, filed of frozen, stop reason); **the app half is built (same branch):** the Sleep page's sentence tail ("Batch k of n · f of frozen filed."), the plan-stop and cancel rungs, Cancel copy that says the batch still reading is dropped, a Swift decode over the pinned `sleep-status-drain.json`, and the bank-switch 409 shown as the server's sentence. **Not built, still design:** the journal (SL-1: a cancel before Stage 5 still discards the batch in progress), the reserve, auto-continue after a plan reset (a ruling 4 amendment), parallel reading (SL-3), the drain-tagged cost summary (SL-4), per-batch grouping in Past nights, and narrowing the 409 guards (**G177**). ~~Known and disclosed: a first batch that always fails is retried first on every run~~ — fixed below (retry once, then park). **Owner decision 2026-09-30 ("I like the sleep agent v5 designs would you be able to apply them? Door: the intake card's Read now reads everything"): the approved boards are applied on top of the drain (backend half built on branch `feat/sleep-v5`, spec §11; the app half follows).** *Built (backend):* (1) **Reading options** — `GET/PUT /sleep/run-options`: batch size 10/25/50 (50 is the ceiling: a commit records at most 50 sessions), the opt-in *continue after a plan reset* switch (off; **TODO ruling 15**), the *leave room in my plan* reserve (off), each snapshotted when a run starts. (2) **Scheduled cycles read everything waiting too** (**ruling 16**; ruling 4 unchanged — still no plan, and an unattended run on a key spends with no limit Cicada sets, said in words); the scheduler reads nothing while a paused run waits (its engine-free tail still runs, review fix 2026-09-30) and counts *readable* conversations. (3) **Per-conversation outcomes** — Stage 1's swallowed failures are classified by exception class (`sleep_drain.classify_episode`): the engine's trouble (signed out, throttled, exhausted, model not found, an unnamed CLI failure, a provider 5xx or refused request, the network — and a batch where every conversation failed with an unrecognised error) stops the run after the batch commits what it read and is never counted against a conversation; a conversation's own (empty answer, timeout, unparseable) gets **one more try in the very next batch and is then parked** (`$CICADA_HOME/sleep/<bank>/parked.json`, released when the file changes or on `POST /sleep/parked/retry`). An empty answer from a plan CLI that exited cleanly is now `EngineProtocolError`, not "signed out". (4) **Honest live progress** on `GET /sleep/status`'s `drain` and the SSE event: `calls` (only grows; a discarded batch's calls stay counted), `elapsedMs` / `pausedMs` (measured, never a remaining time), `batchState`, per-stage `stages` (Read / Sort / Decide fill only from finished work; Notice and File carry no number), `byOrigin` (frozen = filed + read + waiting + could-not-be-read + parked + skipped, by construction), a live `arrivedSince`, `ownerPage` beliefs (start / after the first batch), `firstRun`, `startedBy`; `GET /sleep/queue` serves the per-conversation rows from frontmatter only. (5) **Pause / Continue / End this run** — a paused run is a sidecar record (`run.json`), not a state of Sleep (`status` stays `idle`, nothing is held): `POST /sleep/trigger {"continue": true}` resumes it under the same run id with its counters carried, a no-body trigger stays a fresh run, `POST /sleep/run/end` forgets it, a backend restart turns a run that was reading into a paused one (`restart`). (6) **The reserve is a soft stop** — Stage 1 stops *starting* reads at the line, what was read is filed, the rest is not an attempt; a line, not a guarantee, and a window the engine never reports is named `enforced: false`. (7) **Past nights groups by run** — `SleepHistoryEntry` carries `drainId` / `batch` / `batches` and a `run` summary from a machine-local per-run file (never summed from the visible page), and `GET /sleep/runs/{id}` sums cost by `drain_id` over every call. (8) `SleepEnginePreview.billing` (`plan \| charged \| local`) so the copy never names a provider. **Owner decisions applied:** "Read faster" is **not built** (no parallel reading, no small-model map, no "Reading model" or "At once" — SL-3 and EX-1…EX-5 stay open and now need the owner's go); the reserve is off by default and set in the engine menu; continue-after-reset is an opt-in switch, off, a narrow ruling-4 amendment (ruling 15). **Still not built:** the journal (SL-1: a cancel, a Pause or a hard plan rejection before Stage 5 still discards the batch in progress; the board's "Saved reading" section and "read and kept" wording wait for it), parallel reading (SL-3), the app half of all of the above, a spending cap for unattended metered runs, keeping the Mac awake. **Previous execution label (historical):** ◐ drain built 2026-09-29 (backend + app); Sleep page v5 built 2026-09-30, backend and app (the boards on top of the drain: Reading options, Pause / Continue / End this run, the run's counts and stages, per-conversation What's waiting with Retry, Past nights by run, the doors routing to the page while paused, Keep plan free in the engine menu); **still open: the journal (SL-1, "Saved reading") and parallel reading (SL-3 "Read faster", EX-1..EX-5 — the owner declined it 2026-09-30, it waits for his go)**; the app's Home "Last read" and `newestConsolidation` still pick the newest batch, not the run. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G164 | <a id="g164"></a>**Reading the web, part 1 — the Reader ladder: a fast, honest page reader as the default, and Settings → Reading the web** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29: "wether we should implement a low level fast scraper for websites, and that way the agents dont have to use something like browser harness ... So in parallel maybe research about extremely fast scrapers we can implement because we either need the agent's freedom to look at the page itself and even interact if needed, or the scraper that just gives what's on the site. maybe this can be different modes we can offer, and by default we set one...") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Reading settings and a public HTML fetch path exist; the full default Reader ladder is not established just by exposing its selector. Implement/verify the actual public-reader method wiring and page/citation handoff. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g164). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Answer: yes to a fast reader, as the default, and no to giving consolidation a browser** ([`2026-09-29-reading-the-web-design.md`](../specs/2026-09-29-reading-the-web-design.md), revision 2, 22 critiques adopted). **What exists (spec §3, read in code):** one fetch path, `link_enrichment.default_fetch`, on the ToS rail (4 s, ≤ 512 KB, no cookies, never behind auth) — and, found while reading, **no `robots.txt` handling at all**, an OG-plus-bs4 extractor, and a second transport (save-time `media_ingestor.enrich`, `_TIMEOUT` 5 s) the ladder must also cover. **The ladder, on the same bytes:** R0 an honest User-Agent, `Accept: text/markdown` and robots.txt; R1 metadata through one shared parser; R2 main content to markdown (extractor bench: trafilatura recommended over readability-lxml, S1's bench on the person's own saved pages to overrule published numbers); R3 a distinct `needs_js` status (never retried by the clock); R4 an optional local browser engine, later (**G167**). A metadata-tier result is written with no summarizer call. **Two axes, two defaults:** automatic reading defaults to *Reader*; reading with an agent defaults to *Off* (**G166**). **Honesty rules:** `blocked` copy says 'looks again in a month'; `fetch_reader_v` makes `blocked` and `failed:empty_body` pages eligible once after a fix; Settings copy goes through `LeavesMacNote` because a page excerpt does reach the summarizing engine. **Rulings proposed:** R-RW1…R-RW9 plus R-RW10 (robots.txt), R-RW11 (backoff by status) and R-RW12 (nothing unattended without a person's choice); each a dated TODO ruling on approval. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW1 approve R-RW1…R-RW3, R-RW10, R-RW11 and the extractor (trafilatura, pinned, today's bs4 extractor as the fallback); D-RW7 honour robots.txt (recommend yes, at the cost of some pages reading `blocked: robots` that read fine today). → extends **G102** (the metadata tier is the 'cheap slice' extended to unread pages), **G61** phase 2 (the reader and the agent queue are its rungs 1 and 2; S4's `default_fetch` reuse becomes the ladder, so G61 S4 and this row share one implementation), **G133** (`never_scraped`, the paper and video hand-offs), **G105**/**G149**. Slices S0 spec and rulings, S1 the ladder, S5 Settings. Triage: APPLY (💸 only through the existing summarizer). **Previous execution label (historical):** ❓ design written 2026-09-29, awaiting the owner's review. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G165 | <a id="g165"></a>**Reading the web, part 2 — links inside imported chats: import stays network-free, pages are minted when read** (Rodrigo 2026-09-29, in the same session: research fast readers "because we either need the agent's freedom ... or the scraper that just gives what's on the site") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Import parsing remains network-free and links can be recorded. A complete queued read-and-mint path for conversation links, with dates/quotes/consent and relation grounding, remains open. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g165). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Why (spec §3.4, §6; counts are aggregates from the owner's own exports, no link or host is recorded here).** Imported Claude and ChatGPT chats carry several hundred distinct readable links, and today none of them reaches the graph as a page. **The design:** import never fetches and mints no page; the per-turn `links` sidecar takes its offsets from the stager's own scrubbed text through a new same-body refresh path (offsets from `render`, so a scrub cannot shift them); pages are minted at read time by a new writer with named signature changes (`RawItem.source_episodes` and `defer_enrich`, `ingest_batch_results`, `attach_episode`), committed as `cicada` under trigger `capture/chat-links`, reusing the `chatgpt-export` and `claude-export` origin ids. Roles and classes decide which links are worth reading (§6.1). **A lane that is actually separate:** 10 a night, only when the person chose *A few each night*, with its own scan; `_candidates` and `scan_backfill` now exclude chat origins; every count is over the readable set, not the raw one. **Consent, stricter than the first draft:** the card offers *Read them when I say* (the default; nothing fetched until Read), *A few each night*, *Don't read links from chats*; the unattended option is never pre-selected, and any import that shows no card (the deprecated shims, an API re-import, a live conversation from the Stop hook) behaves as *Read them when I say* (R-RW12). **The catch-up job:** its own `reading_jobs` registry with a single-flight 409 (not `intake_jobs`), counts from a cached, ETagged `GET /reading/queue` and `GET /episodes/{id}/links`, a progress bar that cannot lie, routes carrying `refuse_capture_into_demo`; the Feed's 'Read' line comes from an additive `MediaSourceItem.read`. **Open questions (spec §14):** D-RW3 the chat-link default when nobody answered (`manual`, as drawn); D-RW4 chat-shared links in the main Feed list (recommend) or behind an origin filter; D-RW6 add `processed_by: cicada` (audit every reader first) or write no media episode for chat-link pages. → extends **G102**, **G164**, **G20**/G118 (the sidecar), **G133**; live conversations are slice S2b. Slices S2 sidecar, S2b live, S3 chat pages and the read job. Triage: APPLY 💸. **Previous execution label (historical):** ❓ design written 2026-09-29, awaiting the owner's review. | PARTIAL · P3 |
| G102 | <a id="g102"></a>**Site recon — read a saved page to derive RELATIONS, not to summarize it** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, restating an earlier ask: "from the bookmarks and websites is important to do some recon to see what the site is about to be able to derive relationships with other nodes") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Public link intake and source checks exist. Relation-focused site recon and personal-reason grounding still need an observed case and governed output; fetched summaries alone do not complete the row. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g102). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Partly built, and the gap is specific.** Saved links already get OpenGraph enrichment at ingest (`link_enrichment.py`, `media_ingestor.enrich`) — that is why Feed rows carry titles, sites and favicons. What does **not** exist is reading the page to derive *graph relations*: today a bookmark lands as an island with a title, and nothing connects it to the concepts, tools, people or projects it is actually about. **The framing that makes this worth building: the output is ENTITIES, not a summary.** A summary is a nicer card; extracted entities routed through Stage-2 resolution are new edges — which is what turns 789 saved links from a list into part of the graph, and is a direct prerequisite for **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval ("you saved this three months ago and it answers what you are deciding now" is impossible while saves are unlinked). **Constraints, both learned the hard way here:** (i) it is a network fetch per URL, so it belongs on the **Sleep tail** behind the same opt-out gate as connectors — never at capture time, never blocking a save; (ii) the **ToS rail** applies exactly as it did for LinkedIn and X — some sites may be fetched and parsed, some may not, and the rule is already written down: no scraping behind auth, no circumventing a block. Also relevant: **G86**'s junk-title finding (148 Google consent interstitials collapsed onto one entity) means recon must skip consent/interstitial pages or it will happily extract entities from a cookie banner. **Cheap first slice:** for already-enriched items, run extraction over the OG title+description that is *already stored* — zero new fetches, immediate edges, and it proves the resolution path before any crawling is added. → extends **G47/G71** (the saves that need relating), **G22** (media previews), **Stage 1/2** (extraction + resolution as the consumer); prerequisite for **G70** (design identity) and a direct enabler of **G79**; gated by **G86** (junk titles) and the connector network gate. **Cheap slice shipped (2026-09-02, `feat/link-summaries`).** Corrected finding first: Stage 5.57 already globbed the whole bank — the zero-`describes` result came from the pass running only after Stage 5 (never on an idle night), taking the 20 most-recent pages, and never retrying an `enrichment_attempted` page. Shipped: `link_enrichment.backfill` on the Sleep tail (`link_enrich_backfill_per_cycle`/night, oldest-first, dated fetch backoff, junk retired via `classify_page`) + `POST /maintenance/enrich-links?limit=N`; recon in `link_recon.py` — the existing Stage-1 prompt over stored title+description, 8 links per call, surface-form attribution, `entity_resolver.match_existing` (the Stage-2 judgment without `resolve()`'s creates/clarifications), `about` claims on the media page projected by Stage 5.7, unmatched mentions as pending candidates; `GET /sources` carries `description`/`about`. **Not shipped (this row stays open):** page fetching beyond OG+visible-text (JS-rendered pages, YouTube transcripts, PDFs), any `about` edge from the target's side, in-app recon controls, and relating a link to a *pending* candidate once it promotes (today the candidate carries the link's episode as its source, so promotion backfills context but not the edge — a follow-up). Final review (2026-09-03) closed four of the disclosed gaps: the endpoint now holds a process-local lock (409 on overlap, alongside the R11 Sleep guard); `GET /sources` strips the ```claims fence before excerpting (the row was carrying serialized claim YAML on every backfilled page); recon leaves an existing pending candidate untouched rather than replacing a conversation's provenance with a blurb's; and `llm_calls` counts answered calls only, so an engine abort no longer stamps a model on a zero-LLM commit. Still disclosed, not fixed: a page whose extractor returned a malformed non-engine response is stamped `recon_attempted` after one try — only engine failures (R9) leave pages unmarked. **2026-09-29:** the metadata tier of the Reader ladder (**G164** R1) is this row's 'cheap slice' extended to pages never read, with no summarizer call; links found inside imported chats (**G165**) become saved-page candidates through the same recon. The 2026-09-29 review also found the fetch path has no `robots.txt` handling (R-RW10) and that saved-link ids could collide (**G172**, fixed in PR #139). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ cheap slice shipped. **Feeds from exploring (G166, 2026-10-14):** every capture record keeps the page's JSON-LD fields (type, name, author, publisher, price, sameAs) and its h1–h3 outline in `captures/<page>/`, so recon can widen its input from "title + description" to the latest capture with no new fetch — that widening is G166 exploring's slice 2, not built yet. | PARTIAL · P3 |

## P4 — replication and selective collaboration

|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | <a id="g1"></a>**Multiple memory banks / "memory projects"** (→ **M6, committed, next-up**) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Named banks and switching exist; cross-bank references requested in the original row are not implemented. Restore the row; coordinate references with G16/G132 rather than implying bank CRUD completes it. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g1). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Several named memory banks so the live consolidated graph is never erased: a **project dropdown in the Memory/graph page** to switch the active bank; **save the current graph under a name**; **create a new (empty/seed) bank** to test against (e.g. a bank seeded from imported chat exports + the big-model M5d consolidation).
