Banks can cross-reference. → ties to D2/D4/G12. **Rodrigo confirmed: build this now, before reviewing the demo surfaces.** **Previous execution label (historical):** ✅ (M6 — `bank_registry` + `/banks` routers + app `BankSwitcher`; cc58eb5, b0f192f). | PARTIAL · P4 |
| G132 | <a id="g132"></a>**Sync across devices — a Claude Code session on the home server lands in the MacBook's bank, and can read it back** (Rodrigo 2026-09-06: "the ability to have a sync across devices … I have a home server, an old laptop I installed Ubuntu on, and I want to be able to have it so that if I have Claude Code sessions running on that server, they sync with the cicada on my MacBook") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Remote clients can reach one bank; replicated multi-device/server banks, offline edits and convergence are not implemented. Start with G181's primary writer; push-only backup is simpler and separate. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g132). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 requests multiple devices/servers. First separate a single headless bank reached by several clients (G181/G135) from replicated banks with offline writes. Do not turn git pull/merge into semantic memory sync: contradictory facts can merge cleanly. Design capture routing, conflict handling, reconnect/freshness and restore with one primary git writer per bank. Push-only private-remote backup is an optional narrower feature, never a pull/force-push mechanism; G92 settles privacy. G16 owns selective multi-person sharing. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** **What "one machine" means in the code today, so the row argues from evidence rather than from the word "sync".** The Stop hook posts to a loopback literal with only the port overridable (`api/hooks/capture.py:135`); the endpoint then opens the transcript itself against *its own* home — `harness_root` dispatches to `Path.home()/.claude/projects` (`transcript_capture.py:75`, `session_stats.py:57`) — and `validate_transcript_path` (`transcript_capture.py:94-111`) resolves the path strictly and refuses `not_a_file`/`outside_root` before any read, so a hook firing on another machine cannot be served even if it reached the backend: **a path is meaningless across machines, by construction.** The backend binds loopback everywhere it is started (`install.sh:348`, `api/config.py:127`, `BackendProcess.swift:71`), the app's base URL is a constant (`APIClient.swift:973`), and the MCP server carries three `127.0.0.1:8000` literals and no base-URL setting (`mcp/server.py:717,799,1923`). Auth is one machine-global secret, `~/.cicada/api_token`, with no per-device scoping (`auth.py:6,88`); the CORS regex (`api/main.py:146`) is a browser control, not a network one. Install is launchd-only (`install.sh:325-375`, `scripts/doctor.sh:107`, README "macOS 14+") — yet the backend imports nothing macOS-specific and every dependency is cross-platform (`api/pyproject.toml:7-22`), so a Linux node is packaging, not a port. **Nothing in the codebase runs `git fetch`, `pull` or `push`**; the one remote read is `git remote get-url origin` for an avatar (`git_service.py:380`). And no record says which machine a conversation happened on: a captured episode carries `origin`, `harness`, `session_id`, `project_dir` (`transcript_capture.py:253-264`) but no device, and `_find_session_episode` keys on `(episodes_dir, harness, session_id)` alone; the only device-id concept is `local_refs.current_device_id()` (`socket.gethostname()`, `local_refs.py:80`), used for `repos:` and, as G92 already notes, with no remap table; `resumable` is an `isfile()` on the backend's own disk (`session_stats.py:72`), the resume descriptor shells out to `claude --resume` (`routers/conversations.py:213`) and the handshake promises it to every agent (`handshake.py:72,122`) — true only on the device that holds the transcript. **Two facts decide the shape.** (1) The extractor is already pure and portable — `transcript_extract.extract` takes lines and `scrub_secrets` runs inside it (`transcript_extract.py:426,131`), with the per-turn and per-session caps — so the deterministic read can happen on the machine the transcript lives on, and only scrubbed, capped turns need ever cross a boundary. (2) **Episode ids are proven safe for one writer only**: `next_episode_id` is max-suffix+1 over the *local* `episodes/` glob (`episode_ids.py:70-85`), inbox numbers are minted the same way (`inbox_generator.py:459`), and three in-bank files are read-modify-write state a three-way merge would silently mangle (`sync_state.json` — `sync_state.py:36`; `_merge_rejected.yaml` — `merge_rejections.py:15`; the bookmark seen-set — `bookmark_seen.py:43`); `_state.md` is a projection to regenerate, never merge, and the index is gitignored (`bank_registry.py:67`). Two writable clones of one bank therefore collide on the first same-day capture — and `markdown_parser.write` overwrites on collision. **Three shapes were designed and judged against the rulings (2026-09-06, a sonnet Workflow run on this row alone: seven readers over the code and the backlog → three designers, one per framing → two judges, one on the rulings and one as the owner); both judges ranked them the same way.** **Lead — distributed Awake, one Sleep (the network is the transport).** One bank, one backend, one Sleep writer, all on the MacBook, unchanged. The satellite (the Linux box) runs the *same* extractor locally under the *same* `validate_transcript_path` checks against its own home, and POSTs turns — never a path, never raw bytes — to a new `POST /capture/transcript/turns` on the primary, bearer-authed like every route, over an overlay network (Tailscale/WireGuard). The write half of `capture_transcript` (id mint, `_find_session_episode`, hash dedup, `processed: false`) is factored into one writer both endpoints share, so **G104 and G114 hold by construction: ids are still minted on the primary, at write time, under the existing lock.** A disk spool on the satellite (`~/.cicada/capture-spool/`, FIFO, the full payload on every retry so hash-dedup makes a resend safe) drains on the next Stop or on a systemd timer, so a MacBook closed for a week loses nothing; the hook still exits 0 inside its 3 s budget. Sleep never runs on the satellite — enforced by not installing the scheduler there, not by convention. **The rail this amends, said out loud:** "the backend reads the transcript" becomes *"the transcript is read on the machine it lives on, by Cicada's own validated extractor, and only extracted turns cross a machine boundary."* The rail's purpose — no path smuggling, no arbitrary bytes, a parser decision rather than a model's, no transcript mining — survives intact; only its locality claim generalises. **Second slice — the read side.** `cicada_recall`, `cicada_handshake`, `cicada_write_claim` and `cicada_save_episode` read or write bank files directly (`mcp/server.py:954,1547,1266,2029`; `handshake.py:250`), so "point the MCP at a remote URL" is not a config change — those tools have no HTTP twin. The cheap answer is git-transport's substrate without its write side: a **read-only mirror** on the satellite (`git fetch` + `reset --hard` on a timer; never a commit, never a push) makes the file-reading tools work unmodified, with semantic recall needing the embedding model and a locally rebuilt index (keyword fallback until then); the three HTTP call sites get one `CICADA_API_BASE`; satellite-side writes (`save_episode`, `write_claim`) go to the primary over HTTP or are refused with a clear message, never committed to the mirror — a second commit-capable copy is exactly the collision and split-brain class above. **Runner-up — git as the transport** (a bare repo on the always-on box; the satellite commits episodes and pushes; the primary fetches before Stage 1 and pushes after `_finalize`; the queue is a `processed: false` filesystem walk (`sleep_cycle.py:1336`, `bank_index.py:62`), so merged-in episodes need no plumbing). Best offline and security story — no shared secret crosses machines — and the natural end state if the satellite ever becomes a full node; but its first slice is the heaviest (git networking the codebase has never had, a per-device id floor that is a G114 exception, merge handling for the read-modify-write files, a Linux install), so it stays the substrate for the mirror, not the lead. **Rejected — the server as primary** (the always-on box runs backend + Sleep; the MacBook becomes the satellite): it inverts the stated scenario, ships raw transcript bytes before scrubbing, and needs four surfaces (installer, doctor, `BackendProcess`, `APIClient`) before a single episode lands; its one insight — bytes, not paths — is already in the lead. Note for whoever revisits it: an unattended cycle on a headless box is *safe* by construction (`engine_select.py:226,254` return `byok` before the registry is touched — ruling 4), so that topology is not blocked by quota, only by the scenario. **Device identity is the small prerequisite all three share:** every episode gains `device:` (the `current_device_id()` value, the convention `repos:` already uses), `project_dir` is read relative to it, and the resume descriptor and handshake say *resumable on `<device>`* instead of promising `claude --resume` on a machine that cannot honour it — G48's conversation identity grows a fourth field. **Rails a build must keep** (both judges' must-nots, deduplicated): the satellite never mints an episode id and never runs Sleep; nothing unscrubbed crosses a machine boundary; the backend is never reachable beyond loopback without an encrypted overlay — the bearer is one unscoped secret and plaintext LAN HTTP leaks it to the whole segment; a network failure never delays the hook or drops a transcript silently; no hostname, home path or overlay address is hardcoded (env-var config only — the portability rail); and read parity is never claimed while only the write path works. **Decide before building (the owner's forks):** (a) *thin satellite forever* (capture + read-only mirror — G114's single-minter invariant untouched) or *a full second node* with its own writable clone and Sleep (git-transport's shape, needs the id-floor exception and a merge policy); (b) copy the one bearer to the server, or introduce per-device tokens now — `auth.py` has no such concept, and the recommendation is per-device before any third device or any non-loopback exposure; (c) the overlay is a prerequisite the owner provisions (an existing Tailscale/WireGuard), not something Cicada installs; (d) whether the satellite ever needs the companion app (assume no: Claude Code only); (e) whether minutes of mirror staleness is acceptable for recall, or live HTTP recall (new endpoints for every direct-file tool) is worth its plumbing. **Install:** `install.sh` and `doctor.sh` grow a Linux/systemd branch (venv, the hook registration that already exists, a user unit + the spool timer) rather than a fork — the same portability mission as G76: "other people on their own plans and harnesses" includes their own machines. → extends **G92** (a shape its three routes did not name: the person's *own* devices and no third party — route (a) stretched to a second owned machine, and the local-first substrate route (b)'s hosted E2E sync would sit on top of, which is why the E2E question there does not arise here) and **G93** (which named "continuity across devices" as chargeable *because* it is what a user cannot trivially self-host; this row makes the self-hosted half free, which narrows what that monetization case can still point to — re-read G93 against this row before pricing anything there); depends on **G105** (the hook is the capture point on every machine), **G104**/**G114** (both hold by construction for the lead shape because minting stays on the primary; the runner-up needs the explicit G114 exception noted above), **G48** (device joins session/harness/project_dir); constrains **G76** (install grows a Linux branch), **G117** (a second device is an onboarding step), **G131** (a SessionStart primer on the satellite needs the mirror fresh); relates **G16**/**D4** (many people on one bank is a different problem from one person on many machines, but the same git substrate would carry both). **DECIDE** (a)–(b), then **APPLY** slice 1 (two labels on purpose: the fork gates a slice that is already sized) — M, $0: capture is file I/O and HTTP, no LLM anywhere. **Cross-reference (2026-09-23): G135** ships per-connector capability tokens and a public, MCP-only listener for cloud AI apps — a different exposure class from this row's owned-device overlay, but the same token store serves a satellite's bearer later (fork (b)); this row's rails are unchanged. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §5: explore push-only backup separately from device sync: one primary writer pushes committed snapshots to an explicitly configured owner-owned private destination under the git writer lock; no pull/merge/rebase/force-push. Stop/report divergence, retain local capture on remote failure, show last backed-up commit. Restore is separate. Destination disclosure/encryption stays DECIDE with G92; private hosting is not E2E encryption. Existing device-sync forks remain.** **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → DECIDE · P4, after single-writer server mode. | UNBUILT · P4 |
| G16 | <a id="g16"></a>**Shared memories + shared contributors (open exploration)** | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Multiple banks and remote tokens exist; selected node/project sharing and contributor destinations, permissions, revocation and evidence exposure are not implemented. Specify a primary-writer collaboration model first. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g16). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Earlier priority review (superseded by implementation review):** Owner 2026-10-06 explicitly requests sharing selected nodes or projects with another person’s Cicada and multiple contributors. This lifts the old near-term parking for a scoped design, not for an unrestricted peer network. Define owner/contributor identity, read versus propose/write permissions, revocation, provenance, visibility of related nodes/evidence, redaction and conflict verdicts. No graph closure or raw source text is shared implicitly. Keep one primary writer per bank; collaborators submit attributed proposals or scoped writes through it. AMR speaker-to-destination routing is an input; an unclear destination asks. D4’s general human/agent/robot peer network remains research-only. **Earlier reasoning and dated progress:** Down-the-line: the ability to **share memories** between people and have **shared contributors** on a memory/bank — collaborative memory (a bank with multiple human + agent contributors, à la a shared repo). Pairs naturally with the peer/observer model (R8) and the contributors/origin provenance. **Left open for exploration later** per Rodrigo — not scoped yet. → relates to R8 (peers), M3 (contributors), G1 (banks), D4. **2026-10-06 — [`AMR comparison`](../research/2026-10-06-agent-memory-repo.md) §4: retain separate banks and ownership when composing a session; bank-qualified identities and write routing by speaker attribution plus explicit destination permission, asking when ambiguous. A speaker label is not authorization. Design input only: D4 stays research-only and this row stays parked.** **Previous execution label (historical):** RESEARCH → DECIDE · requested, P4; no longer indefinitely parked. | DECISION · P4 |
| G116 | <a id="g116"></a>**Two rulings the inbox redesign needs before its Phase 3 — who authors a rule-executed write, and whether an in-conversation resolve names its conversation** (raised by the G115 study 2026-09-02; both are contract questions, not builds; DECIDE, $0) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** User-authored resolution exists. Rule-executed authorship and optional in-conversation session linkage still require the documented decisions before Phase 3; S3 source checks cannot silently resolve a belief. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g116). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **(a) Rule execution and the author trailer.** G115's rule file lets the person write `decay(type=project): archive` once. Executing it at Stage 5 means Sleep archives an entity with no human in the loop *at that moment*, on a ruling the human wrote earlier. CLAUDE.md reserves `Cicada-Author: user` for manual/companion/media-save writes and `cicada` for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop (G85 precedent: the decay-only commit). The UI-port design proposed `user`; the buildability and friction judges both flagged it as a contract stretch. **Recommended, not yet ruled:** `cicada`, with the rule string as provenance in the manifest line (`entities/<id>.md: status archived (trigger: inbox/decay/resolved:archive, rule: decay(type=project): archive)`) so `GET /contributors` counts it as the system and `GET /entities/{id}/history` shows exactly which ruling fired; `_infer_change_type` still yields `statusChange` (R2). Until ruled, G115 Phase 2 ships `never` and `defer:N` rules only — a rule may suppress a question, not answer one. Evidence: `git_service.commit_resolution` (`:923-955`) hardcodes `authors=["user"]`; the decay split-out in `sleep_cycle._finalize` shows the `cicada` path exists. **2026-09-23:** G61 phase 2 adds a second caller — a source-settled inbox item is a check-executed write with no human in the loop at that moment; its spec recommends the same answer (`cicada`, rule string in the manifest), to be ruled once for both. **(b) `Cicada-Session:` on a user commit.** The conversation-first design proposed adding the trailer when a resolve arrives through the MCP with a `session_id`, extending "user-action commits stay session-less" with "an in-conversation resolve is a user action that has a conversation". **Recommended, not yet ruled:** keep the contract — `session_id`, `harness` and `surface` travel in the shipped `resolution` ledger event's refs (ids are allowed there; the event exists at `inbox_service.py:~456-477` with `item_id`/`entity_id`/`action`/`option_key`/`verdict`/… and these are additive keys), and `via_conversation_match` is computed from them, which answers "was this answered inside the conversation that raised it?" without giving a second meaning to a trailer every reader currently treats as "which conversations a Sleep consolidated". Revisit only if a git-only reader needs the join (e.g. `GET /entities/{id}/history` wanting to link a user resolve to a resumable conversation). **Constraint for both:** whichever way (a) goes, a rule-executed write must be attributable to its rule string in git; whichever way (b) goes, the ledger must carry the session ref. (The study's third owner question — close a silent clarification/merge question after 180 Sleep-counted days, recommended yes — is G115's, not this row's.) → gates **G115** Phase 3; touches **G85** (author contract), **G48** (session trailer contract), **G113** (ledger refs). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | DECISION · P4 |
| G202 | <a id="g202"></a>**Memory sync between two devices — the 2026-10-22 test, what works, what fails, what's unbuilt** (G181 phases 2–3, ruling 28; synthetic banks only, the owner's own services untouched) | **The test.** The Mac and a Linux server, each a scratch primary with its own bank, each the other's client over an SSH tunnel, through the real entry points (the Stop hook with Cicada's own extractor, `mcp/server.py` over stdio, `python -m api.cli --json`). T1 = the Linux server primary with the Mac its client; T2 = the Mac primary with the server its client. **Matrix** (T1 / T2): (1) a client capture lands as one episode with `device`, the timeline reads it from the client, recall finds the page after Sleep — PASS / PASS; (2) `cicada_save_episode` and `cicada_write_claim` through `/tools/call` land with author and session trailers — PASS / PASS, but no device on claims or commits (D4); (3) offline: hook captures wait in the spool and land exactly once, a replayed entry answers `unchanged` — PASS / PASS for hooks; MCP and CLI writes are refused while the server is away, never queued (D8, unbuilt); (4) 8 writes per side in the same second — unique ids, nothing lost or duplicated, linear git — PASS / PASS; (5) the same words edited on both machines at once: first wins, the second is refused, no silent overwrite; a claim written on one side withdrawn from the other (ruling 28.3, by harness label) — PASS / PASS; (6) two primaries with no link — UNBUILT / UNBUILT: two diverging banks, both mint `ep_<day>_001…` (ids collide by construction), both are called `default` with different bank ids, and nothing warns either side — that is **G132**. App path (Swift, not launched): `HeadlessAppTests` 31/31, `OutboxLanesTests` 2/2. **Defects.** **D1** (reproduced live): waiting hook captures followed the client to whichever server it pointed at next — one session ended up with its first turn on the old server and whole on the new one; the spool entry named only route, body and key. **D2** (code read): the app's `ReaderOutbox` keyed a post by bank *name* (every fresh bank is `default`), kept one folder for every server and never sent `X-Cicada-Bank-Id`. **D3**: waiting Claude Code captures were invisible (`cicada status` said nothing; the Devices page counted only the app's outbox) and flushed only when a hook next fired. **D4**: writes from a device record no device on claims or commits — G181's open item, not here. **D5**: on Linux, Sleep's search warning said "isn't on this Mac". **D6**: the handshake named the bank by its folder (`memory`) where the CLI says `default`, and never named the server, so two primaries' primers read the same to an agent. **D7**: a running MCP session switched memory silently on its next call after `api_base` changed. Observation, not filed: a Page edit's history row shows `sessions: []` though the commit carries `Cicada-Session`. | **2026-10-22 — D1, D2, D3, D5, D6, D7 fixed (branch `fix/sync-clients`).** D1: each spool entry names its target (the address and, once heard, the bank id a capture answer's `bankId` gave); a flush sends only the target's entries, a resend carries `bank_id` and `/capture/session`·`/capture/turn` refuse a bank no bank of their memory has (409 `bank_mismatch`; a bank switch on the server files it where the live capture goes); a refused entry is held for its own memory, never blocking this server's turns, and the address's remembered bank is forgotten (review of #408); entries for another server wait, never dropped, and the log says how many and for which host (`test_sync_clients.py` reproduces it with two loopback servers). D2: an outbox post names its server and bank id, a flush sends only this connection's, `X-Cicada-Bank-Id` rides every write on a server (ids learned from `/banks`). D3: `cicada status` and Settings → Devices count the hooks' spool and the outbox by server; a successful MCP call or command flushes what waits for its server. D5: "this server" off a Mac. D6: the Now line names the roster's bank and, on a client, `on <host>`. D7: the first reply after a switch says once where memory now comes from. **Open:** D4 → G181; D8 (queued MCP/CLI writes) unbuilt; row 6 → **G132**, which should also warn when a second primary exists. |
| G203 | <a id="g203"></a>**A bank commits under a neutral identity when none is configured, never one git guessed from the account** (the 2026-10-22 Hyrum audit's Follow-ups; portability) | **Problem.** `ensure_commit_identity_sync` (G181) set `Cicada <cicada@localhost>` only when `git var` failed. On a Mac with no `user.name`, git does not fail: it guesses the account's full name and `user@hostname` (reproduced 2026-10-22 on a scratch `HOME` with no `~/.gitconfig`), so every commit of a bank handed to someone else carries both. **Constraint.** The rail "a write that commits commits alone, under its true author" is the `Cicada-Author` trailer's, not git's author field; a configured identity is the person's choice and stays; history is never rewritten. **Readers of the author field (Hyrum line).** None reads `%an`/`%ae` of a bank: page history and blame (G198), contributors and their calendar, claim recovery, coexist reopen and the Sleep history all read the trailers; `repo_context` and the app's `GitRunner` read `%an` of a declared workspace repo only. | **Done 2026-10-22 (branch `agent-ready-ci`).** Each half (name, email) is asked of git with `user.useConfigOnly=true` and the other stood in, so a guessed half counts as missing and only that half is filled in the bank's config; a configured half stays (review of #416: hiding a global name in one bank gains nothing, the leak was the guess). It runs for every bank that becomes active: `scaffold_bank` (startup, new, copy) and `run_bank_migrations` (every runtime switch, before any migration commits). Older commits keep their author. `install.sh`'s first commit asks the same way. Two tests join the crucial tier, so Dev CI runs them. Tests: `test_headless_server.py` (a guessing machine commits as Cicada for author and committer; an older bank keeps its history; a configured name or `EMAIL` is kept; a bank switched to at runtime is filled; the trailer still names the writer). Live: a backend on a scratch home with no identity made its first commits as `Cicada <cicada@localhost>`, and `/contributors` still named `cicada` and `user`. |

## Later — capture extensions and conditional maintenance

| G72 | <a id="g72"></a>**Agent-skills manager page + paper-guided skill lifecycle** (WikiSkill, Tang et al., Google Research, 2026-08-27 — [arXiv:2608.27454](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27454)) | **2026-10-22 — the lifecycle half moved to [G201](#g201)** (candidate → promoted → retired, TODO ruling 35); this row keeps the manager page and inventory. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Reviewed recommendations and install detection exist. A complete installed-skills inventory, deduplication and paper-guided lifecycle/evolution manager are not implemented. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g72). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Rodrigo's global agent skills are scattered, duplicated and silently drifting across three roots — **verified 2026-08-31 by read-only listing:** `~/.claude/skills/` = 3 personal (`context7-mcp`, `macos-harness`, `langfuse` — the last a **symlink out to `~/Developer/langfuse-skills/`**), `~/.claude/plugins/cache/*/skills/` = ~61 live across 7 installed plugins **plus 14 stale files from superpowers 6.2.0 still on disk beside 6.3.0**, and `~/.codex/skills/` = **92** dirs — of which **41 collide by exact name with the compound-engineering plugin's 41, and the copies have drifted** (`ce-plan/SKILL.md` differs byte-wise between the two roots); `~/.codex/prompts/` holds a third `ce-*` copy. Meanwhile `macos-harness`/`browser-harness`/`qa` (the heavily-used ones) exist in **one harness only**. **Slice A — inventory page, key-free, no LLM, buildable now:** a `skill_inventory` service walking the three roots (+ `installed_plugins.json` for marketplace/version/gitCommitSha provenance), a new **Skills** page under the G68 sidebar showing inventory by root, per-skill provenance and mtime, a **overlap/bloat panel** (name collisions, stale plugin versions, symlinks escaping the harness root, never-modified-since-install), and **per-harness sync state** (claude-only / codex-only / identical / drifted) with a diff — reuse the `DiffView` landing in **G67**, and the `/graph`-style ETag so a 156-entry walk costs a 304. **Slice B — Sleep-side candidate detection:** a Stage-4 tail step hashes/diffs same-named `SKILL.md`s and n-gram-overlaps distinct ones, emitting **existing inbox kinds** (`merge_suggestion` for near-dupes keyed on the sorted pair; a decay-flavoured question for a superseded plugin version or a skill untouched since install) — no new discriminator, no new resolve path. **Slice C — represent skills as memory:** each discovered skill → a `skill` entity (closed 10-set, no schema change) related to a `directory` entity for its harness root, its on-disk path as a `path`-kind **G61 `sources:`** ref; the paper's `PURPOSE.md` (skill → motivating evidence) is already Cicada's `source_episodes` + wikilinks. **What the paper actually gives us:** three-layer separation (`raw/` immutable traces → `wiki/` compounding pattern pages, *never rolled back* → `skills/` reversible), which maps 1:1 onto episodes → Stage-4 patterns → `skill` entities; and the ablation that **persistent knowledge is the whole gain** (Skill Proposer with wiki access 48.7 → 63.7 avg, +15.0) — i.e. Cicada's consolidation layer is the part worth having, not the optimizer. **Explicitly speculative / NOT committed:** the WikiSkill evolution loop itself. Its acceptance rule is `R(val) > R_best` on a labelled validation split, and a *personal* memory system has **no `D_val`** — there is no ground-truth score for "did this skill make the session better." Also missing: **`skill-impact.md`'s equivalent — nothing on the machine records which skill fired on which task with a pass/fail outcome** (verified: `~/.cicada/telemetry/` logs model+cost per LLM call, not skill invocation; Claude Code transcripts and `~/.codex/logs_2.sqlite` (38 MB) are unparsed). So any Cicada loop substitutes a **human gate**, which is exactly **R2**'s already-adopted governance (failure ledger + bounded, user-confirmed rewrites) and **G55**'s "skills never self-edit" — the paper *supports* R2: it accepts only ~1 in 3 proposed skill creations and ~1 in 4 edits (Table 4). **Two findings that constrain the design:** (i) **negative transfer is real** — a 4B-evolved skill dropped Gemini-3.5-Flash on SpreadSheet 50.5% → 18.1% by encoding harness-specific workarounds, so "push `macos-harness` to Codex" must be a **proposal with a diff, never an auto-push**; (ii) giving the *executing* agent wiki access **degraded** final skill quality (63.7 → 60.9), which cuts against Cicada's instinct to surface everything via `cicada_recall` — flag as an open question, not a decision. **Not verified:** whether Codex resolves `~/.codex/skills/` the same way Claude Code resolves `~/.claude/skills/` (assumed from directory shape + `SKILL.md` presence, not from docs); whether the 41 drifted copies are user edits or version skew; whether writing into harness roots from Cicada is safe (**Slices A/B are read-only — write-back is a separate, later decision**). → extends **G55** (executable skills / R2 governance — this is its inventory + provenance prerequisite), **A4** (procedural-memory capture), **R2**; consumes **G61** (`sources:`), **G67** (DiffView), **G68** (sidebar slot), **Stage 4** (pattern detection, new input); relates to **G49** (skills shipped with the plugin). **Owner, 2026-09-03:** "a skills page — tied into the WikiSkill paper. There are skills I use a lot in Claude Code, for example the browser harness or the macOS harness; I'd like these documented in my skills too, and in that page see which skills we use the most." So the page has two halves: (a) **skills Cicada compiled about you** (G112 — the `skill` entities, each with its evidence, exportable as a SKILL.md bundle); (b) **skills you use in your harnesses** — read the *names and descriptions* of `~/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` and the plugin skills the harness exposes, plus the harness's own usage counters (Claude Code keeps `skillUsage` in `~/.claude.json`: count + last used — the same class of harness-owned data the Activity page already reads from `~/.claude/stats-cache.json`; transcripts stay unread, G48), ranked by use; a used-a-lot external skill can be *adopted* into memory as a `skill` entity with a `uses` claim and the harness as observer, so Cicada's wiki knows your toolset the way it knows your projects. Rails: names/descriptions/counts only; nothing executes a skill; the page never edits `~/.claude`. **2026-09-23 (Track O / G138):** resolved 'Not verified': Codex reads user skills from `$CODEX_HOME/skills` and `~/.agents/skills` (`openai/codex` `codex-rs/ext/skills/src/host_roots.rs`); Claude Code hot-reloads `~/.claude/skills/`; each installed skill costs listing budget, so recommend few. **Rail amended (R5 D2):** the app may write into `~/.claude/skills/` and `~/.agents/skills/` for Cicada's own bundles only — per click, marker-owned, never overwriting a changed copy; third-party skills install only through the agent's own installer after consent. Slice A (the inventory) is still open and would be a tab on the same Settings → Skills page. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G77 | <a id="g77"></a>**Voice-first Cicada + the "output packet" contract** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01, forwarding [@KingBootoshi](https://x.com/KingBootoshi)'s voice-agent build: "add this as inspiration of things to discuss as a to-do") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Voice/meeting intake exists for Wispr, not an always-available voice interface or an output-packet contract for other agents. Define the product interaction before new capture channels. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g77). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The idea worth stealing, restated:** a voice UX must be fast, fast means a small model, and a small model is not smart enough to compress an intelligent agent's work without losing the point. His fix inverts the usual one — **don't push intelligence down into the voice layer; make the intelligent layer emit a packet so plainly written that the small model cannot mistranslate it.** The voice agent becomes a reader/typist, not an interpreter, and the packet is the contract between them. **Why this lands squarely on Cicada:** Cicada already produces exactly the artifacts that would fill such packets — inbox questions (G60 already carry `question` + `options[{key,label,description}]` + `allow_other`/`allow_defer`, i.e. a *closed-choice* structure a dumb reader can voice verbatim and an ASR can map back by key), `/ask` answers with citations + gap analysis, Sleep-cycle summaries, decay nudges. The G60 question object IS a first draft of an output packet; the gap is that nothing renders one for *speech* (no plain-language rendering, no per-option pronounceable key, no "what changed since we last spoke" digest). **Sub-ideas to discuss, not committed:** (i) a `packet` projection of any inbox item / ask answer / cycle summary — short sentences, no jargon, explicit enumerated choices, a single question at the end; testable by round-trip (small model reads packet → user's spoken reply → key recovered without the big model in the loop); (ii) **the green light** — a presence/status surface saying "Cicada is awake / consolidating / has 3 things to ask you", the ambient version of the Sleep page; (iii) **speaker-verification as auth** — his neural net that recognizes only his voice, i.e. an *observer identity* signal; Cicada's claim layer already has `observer:` and `source_trust` (`user_stated` vs `mcp`), so a verified-voice channel is a trust tier, not a new schema; (iv) the FaceTime/daemon adapter pattern (answer calls only from one number) as a capture channel alongside Telegram (G62 channel registry + the G71 `ADAPTERS` seam is the shape); (v) delegation — voice states an intent, a slow smart agent (G74(b) librarian / a Hermes-style daemon) does the work, packet comes back; the `processed:false` queue is already the async hand-off contract. **Open questions:** does the packet belong in the bank as memory (it is derived — probably NOT, same argument as logos/screenshots), or is it purely a rendering? Does a spoken correction write a `user_stated` claim directly (it should — that is the highest-trust input Cicada can get)? What is the latency budget end-to-end before it stops feeling like a person? → extends **G60** (question objects = proto-packets), **G53** (state dictionary = the digest source), **G74(b)** (the slow-agent half of delegation), **G62** (a new capture channel), **G54** (the interview beat this would make voice-native); relates **G75** (the handshake is the text twin of "here is how to talk to me"). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G81 | <a id="g81"></a>**Contacts connection — real identity anchors for `person` entities** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "allow connection with contacts (name/email/phone numbers and stuff)") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Contacts reads and matching-person enrichment exist. Broader identity anchors, duplicate reconciliation and confirmation semantics remain; coordinate with G154 rather than importing every contact as a new person. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g81). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Today a `person` entity is a name and whatever a conversation said about them; there is no anchor tying "Aitor" in a chat to an actual human. Connecting the address book gives every person node a stable identity: **canonical name + email(s) + phone(s) + organization**, which unlocks (i) *dedup that actually works* — two pages for the same human collapse on a shared email rather than on fuzzy name similarity, the weakest input Stage 2 has; (ii) **G61 `sources:` for people** — an email or a LinkedIn URL is literally "where to look this fact up"; (iii) the **recorded-meetings** ambition now in `CLAUDE.md` — a transcript's speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to known humans, so this is a prerequisite, not a nicety; (iv) relationship inference the graph cannot currently make (same employer, same domain in an email address, a shared org). **Route:** macOS-native first — `CNContactStore` (Contacts framework) read-only, which is a **TCC-gated permission** the user grants once; note the app is currently unsigned (see the runtime row), and TCC prompts behave differently for an unsigned/ad-hoc-signed bundle — verify before promising a smooth grant. A one-shot vCard/CSV import is the keyless fallback and the portable path (Google Contacts export, iCloud vCard). Follow the **connector seam** that G71 established (`api/services/connectors/`, one adapter module, `ADAPTERS` registry, `base.run_sync`, `record_credentials_changed`) so this is one more adapter rather than a bespoke integration. **The hard part is not the API, it is the privacy rail — and it is different from every prior source.** Every other stream is the user's own data; a contact record is **a third party's PII** (someone else's phone number), landing in a **git-versioned, permanently-historied** store. Rails to decide before building: does the full record enter the bank at all, or only a minimal anchor (canonical name + a stable hash for matching) with the raw email/phone held outside the bank under `~/.cicada/` like logos and screenshots (the "derived/external artifact" precedent — although a contact is *source* data, not derived, so the precedent argues by analogy only)? Sensible default: **only what a fact needs** — an email used as a `sources:` ref is justified; a home address ingested "because it was in the record" is not. Also: contact records must never be swept wholesale into episodes (that would put an address book into the extraction queue); matching must be a **proposal** (`merge_suggestion` inbox kind, already exists) and never an automatic merge, because a wrong person-merge silently fuses two humans' histories — the most damaging error this graph can make. **Open questions:** whether to sync continuously (contacts change) or import once and re-check on demand; whether a contact with no conversational trace should create an entity at all (it would flood the graph with hundreds of inert people — probably NOT: a contact should *enrich* an existing person entity, not mint one); how deletion propagates when the user removes a contact. → prerequisite for the recorded-meetings half of the project goal; extends **G61** (`sources:` on people), **G71** (connector seam), **G60** (`merge_suggestion` as the proposal surface), **Stage 2** (identity resolution's best possible input); relates **G77** (voice identity/observer trust) and **G79** (curation stream). **(Absorbs G46, merged 2026-09-01)** — the original ask specifically wanted read-only AppleScript/EventKit enumeration of Contacts.app feeding entity resolution as corroborating metadata, not raw episodes, and covers most family-milestone birthdays. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G85 | <a id="g85"></a>**Decay is attributed to a model, re-charges the same interval every run, never strengthens, and its rate ignores how established a belief is** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "i see decay something changed by the model, and i belive it should be not something of the model itself but as time passes by… its decay depends on how established the fact is and it can go from evergreen ad very established to something that isnt, and vice-versa") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Watermark, outage protection, true decay author and spacing exist. Reinforcement still uses max(existing,incoming), not a frequency increment; any change must distinguish evidence strength from recall ranking. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g85). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The user is right, and the diagnosis splits into four distinct defects.** **(1) Attribution is wrong, the math is not.** The decay loop (`conflict_resolver.py:128-181`) is pure arithmetic over frontmatter and `datetime.now()` — `decay_amount = rate * days_since/7`, no LLM anywhere. But authorship is decided **once per commit for the whole batch**: `sleep_cycle.py:798-804` builds `authors` from `settings.litellm_model` regardless of any change's trigger, and `git_service.py:399-400` applies that commit-level trailer to every entity line. So a deterministic time computation is stamped with whichever model happened to run that cycle — 978 such lines across the two 2026-06-18 cycles, which also inflates that model's counts in `GET /contributors`. **Fix:** split `trigger: sleep/decay` changes into their own commit authored `Cicada-Author: cicada` — the literal already reserved for system-maintenance writes with no model and no user in the loop. **(2) Decay is not actually continuous in time — it re-charges.** `conflict_resolver.py:145-151` SUBTRACTS `rate * days_since/7` from stored confidence but the write branch (`:336-341`) never advances `last_referenced` and stamps no watermark, so the next run reads the same `last_referenced` and subtracts the same full interval again. **Confidence is a function of how many times Sleep ran, not of elapsed time** — this is the concrete defect behind the objection. Same shape in the claim engine (`claim_reconciler.py:478-483`). **Fix:** make it computed, not accumulated — keep a stable `baseline_confidence` and derive `confidence = baseline * exp(-rate * weeks_since_last_referenced)`; or stamp `decayed_through:` and measure from `max(last_referenced, decayed_through)` so each interval is charged exactly once. **(3) Rate ignores establishedness.** `decay_class` is assigned once at page creation (`:211-222`) and never revised — the update branch merges episodes, tags, aliases and body but never touches it. An entity referenced in 50 conversations decays at the rate it was born with. `source_episodes` is the natural establishedness signal and is already accumulated. **Fix:** in the update branch, promote the class one step toward `durable` when the page crosses a distinct-conversation threshold; keep `evergreen` off-limits to the agent per the existing `agent_class` rail. **(4) Bidirectionality is half-built.** Recovery exists (`:248-258`) but is a one-shot floor — `max(current, 0.6)` — gated on LLM re-extraction, so a belief reinforced ten times still lands on 0.6 and can never climb. **Fix:** make reinforcement a bounded additive increment symmetric with decay, both directions reading the same `decay_policy` resolver. → this is the row that makes "time as a signal" honest; touches **G66** (decay classes), **G80** (all four fixes are deterministic — no engine needed). **(3) answered by G147 (2026-09-24)** — not by promoting the class (that would rewrite a label the person can set, G66 §1.7, and commit on every threshold crossing) but by a derived spacing factor over distinct ISO weeks of `source_episodes`, recomputed every cycle and never stored. (4) stays open. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G89 | <a id="g89"></a>**Substack — following already works; make FEEDS first-class instead of building a connector** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "add to backlog an ability to connect substack if possible") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** RSS/ICS feeding exists, including public newsletters. First-class feed management and any authenticated Substack capture are different scopes; respect public fetch gates. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g89). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Researched against primary sources and verified live; the headline is that a Substack connector is the wrong shape.** Subscribing to a publication is already supported end-to-end — `feed_registry.py` + `feeds.yaml`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`, `media_ingestor.parse_rss` → the same dedup/ingest pipeline as bookmarks — and the app's RSS tile literally reads *"A blog or Substack Cicada checks for new posts"* (`AddSourceSheet.swift:64`), with the backend docstring saying the same (`sources.py:216`). **What Substack actually exposes (verified 2026-09-01):** `https://<pub>.substack.com/feed` is documented and live; RSS 2.0, **full body for free posts** (an ACX item ran 57k+ chars in `content:encoded`), **exactly 20 items with no pagination** — a recent window, *not* an archive, so history cannot be backfilled from it; custom-domain publications serve `/feed` on their own domain and the `*.substack.com` form 301s to it. **Paywalled posts appear with full metadata but a ~142-byte stub body** containing only a "Read more" anchor — a reliable detection heuristic, and a real ingestion hazard today (that stub would land as a normal media entity with an empty body). **There is no authenticated text feed at all**: Substack's private per-subscriber RSS is **podcast-only**, so the sanctioned machine-readable route to paid text is *none*. **A reader's own subscription list has no sanctioned route either** — no OPML export (help-centre search for "OPML" returns 0 results), no reader data export (Exports are publisher-side, for a publication you own), and no documented API. The only mechanism is `GET substack.com/api/v1/subscriptions` with a session cookie: undocumented, cookie-gated, no stable contract — **the same unsanctioned shape already ruled out for the X archive and LinkedIn enrichment**, so it stays out. Defensible alternative: the user copies their list from `substack.com/library` by hand once, and each publication is subscribed via its public feed — a user action, not automated access. **Saved posts, likes and Notes have no export or API**; Substack's own MCP server states it "cannot access profile data or Notes activity". The official **Developer API is a creator-profile lookup keyed on a LinkedIn handle** (no post content, no reader data), and the Substack MCP server is publisher analytics gated on being an Admin of a *Bestseller* publication — neither is usable here. **So the work is feed quality, and it is all currently missing:** (i) **`parse_rss` keeps only 4 fields** — link, title, body, categories (`media_ingestor.py:993-998`) — and drops `pubDate`, `dc:creator`, `<enclosure>` and the channel `<title>`, so an ingested post has **no publish date, no author, and no publication**; (ii) `RawItem.origin` is never set for feed items, so an RSS post is indistinguishable from a pasted URL in `/origins` (contrast `"chrome-bookmark"`, `"tiktok-saved"`); (iii) **the publication is not an entity** — only individual posts, so "what does this writer keep saying" is unanswerable and G79's cross-stream retrieval has nothing to hang on; (iv) **feed polling is NOT on the Sleep tail** — `sleep_cycle._poll_connectors_safely` iterates only `connectors.ADAPTERS`, so feeds refresh only when a human presses Poll (calendars share this gap); (v) a feed record is just `{url, tags, added, last_polled}` — no title, no etag/last-modified (so every poll refetches in full), no per-feed error, no enabled flag, hence no failure surfacing on the channel row; (vi) no bulk/OPML import path for adding many feeds at once; (vii) paywall-stub items need skipping (or marking) rather than ingesting. **Also inconsistent:** feeds are gated **opt-in** (`CICADA_ALLOW_FEED_FETCH == "1"`) while connectors are now opt-out — one of the two should move. → extends **G47/G71** (the ingest family and the connector seam that feeds should join), **G61** (a publication is a natural `sources:` anchor), **G79** (a followed writer is curation, and the publication entity is what makes it relatable); relates **G80** (all of this is deterministic, zero-LLM work). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ feed-following half already shipped (M4 — `feed_registry.py`, `GET/POST/DELETE /sources/feeds`, `POST /sources/poll-feeds`); the metadata-quality work (i)-(vii) above is the open remainder — see TODO Wave D.. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G91 | <a id="g91"></a>**Share-to-Cicada — a system share target for links and documents from any app** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "the importance of the companion app so that links and stuff like documents are easy to share from any app to this app") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No native system share extension was found. Paste/drop/import already exist; a macOS share target and any iOS companion require their own packaging and app-group/privacy design. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g91). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Today capture is *pull* (paste a URL, drop a file, run an import) or the Telegram bot for mobile — there is no way to be reading something anywhere on the machine and push it in. A macOS **Share Extension** makes Cicada a first-class target in every app's share menu, which is the difference between "capture when I remember to" and "capture at the moment of interest" — the moment the *reason* is still in your head, which is exactly what G71's save-with-reason wants and what an after-the-fact import can never reconstruct. **Shape:** an `.appex` inside the app bundle declaring `NSExtensionActivationRule` for `public.url`, `public.file-url`, `public.plain-text` and PDFs; the extension POSTs straight to the already-running local backend (`POST /sources/save` for links, `/sources/upload` for files) — no app-group plumbing needed because the backend is a localhost service, which is a genuine advantage of this architecture. Add a one-field "why are you saving this?" note in the extension sheet so the `saved-because` claim (G71, already built) gets populated at the only moment the answer is cheap. **Verified constraints, and they are the whole risk:** `grep NSExtension app/` returns nothing today — no extension target exists; SwiftPM does not build `.appex` targets, so this rides on `bundle.sh`'s manual assembly (already the packaging path) or needs an Xcode project; and **Launch Services only registers a share extension for an app that is installed and code-signed** — ad-hoc signing may suffice locally but this is where the unsigned-app trade-off in **G88** stops being cosmetic. Sequence it after G88 for that reason. **iOS is a separate build, not a flag** — the Telegram bot remains the mobile answer until then. → depends on **G88** (installed, signed app), completes **G71** (reason-at-capture), relates **G62** (channel registry — the share sheet is a new channel with its own origin) and **G37**. **(Absorbs G37, merged 2026-09-01)** — the ask was first flagged as "Coming soon" in `SyncSetupView`, Track D3. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** **`cicada-app` — a minimal phone capture app, started** (Rodrigo 2026-10-22: "create a new github repo called cicada-app, and start working on this (maybe expo app super minimal with the cicada mascot and just to capture and send stuff, so that we can also sync phones as devices in my network)"). Scope: Expo, the share sheet (`expo-share-intent`, which needs a dev build — Expo Go is not supported), a link or post plus an optional note, sent to the person's Cicada as a device; the device token in `expo-secure-store` (`AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`). Pairing follows G212: the Mac app shows a QR of a one-time code and the reachable URLs, the phone posts the code and receives a per-device token (`POST /devices` exists; the code that mints it is the missing piece); reach order LAN (Bonjour), the tailnet name, then a public URL. Never the cloud-facing connector. **Constraints:** capture rails apply to what it posts (scrubbed, staged, no demo bank); the repo is public, the bank is not. **Status 2026-10-22:** repository started (`app` worker); the redesign briefs cite this as G214. **Follow-up 2026-10-23 (owner: "the way we will be collecting sources to ingest from my phone"; proposal `followups-2026-10-23.md`, kept outside the repo with the redesign research, §1).** **Shipped since:** `cicada-app` v0.1 (PRs #1–#3 in that repo) — Capture, Recent and Pair; the iOS share extension (web URL, web page, text); a crash-safe outbox with an `Idempotency-Key` per item, each item stamped with the server and bank it was captured for (G202); the pairing in the Keychain — and on this side the capture-only phone token, `POST /capture/note` and the tailnet QR (#424, #432). **Recommendation: the phone stays capture-only.** Reading goes through the AI apps (G135) or an iOS Shortcut with a G213 agent token over the tailnet (`/v1` is served on the backend port too); a read tab would put a wider token on the device most easily lost, and duplicate apps that already recall well. **Missing, in order:** (1) a real-iPhone build — both targets share the app group, so it needs the owner's signing team; the camera QR and shares from the YouTube and X apps are unverified; (2) the phone's read paths written down (no code); (3) files and photos — the share extension accepts no images or files and no capture route takes bytes; proposed: one `POST /capture/file` in `CAPTURE_ROUTES` (≤ 25 MB, staged as an upload, `Idempotency-Key`, demo gate), a Hyrum mode-A change to that listed set; (4) App Intents (Shortcuts, Siri, the Action button) into the same outbox; (5) voice as text transcribed on the phone and sent as `/capture/note` — no audio leaves the phone and no model runs on the backend's capture path (keyboard dictation already works today); (6) Android, after iOS is verified. Off the tailnet the outbox waits, which is right; LAN Bonjour does not help while the backend binds 127.0.0.1 (G181). **Owner decisions — decided 2026-10-23 (TODO ruling 38 §2):** the phone stays capture-only; photos and files from the share sheet through one new capture route; voice notes transcribed on the phone and sent as text; reading memory on the phone through the Claude and ChatGPT apps over the connector, or a Shortcut calling `/v1`; the device build signed with the owner's Apple team first; Android later. **Slices:** device checklist S · read-path docs S · `/capture/file` + share activation M · App Intents M · voice note M · Android M. **Shipped 2026-10-23 (#453; cicada-app #4):** (a) **`POST /capture/file`** in `CAPTURE_ROUTES` (an addition to the listed set, Hyrum mode A): multipart `file` + `caption?` (the person's own words) + `title?` + `id?`; the type is read from the bytes (JPEG, PNG, HEIC, PDF; plain text only when declared and valid UTF-8), 415 otherwise, 413 past 25 MB (and `request_limits` refuses a bigger declared body before it is spooled); `Idempotency-Key`, demo 409 and `device` exactly like `/capture/note`. **Where the bytes live:** `$CICADA_HOME/files/<bank>/<sha256>.<ext>`, outside every bank and git repo, content-addressed, never served back — the site-capture / Messages-photo rail (pixels the scrub cannot clean never enter the bank), so no thumbnail in the bank either. The proposal's "stages exactly as an upload does, pixels where uploads keep them" did not survive reading the code: `/sources/upload` parses exports and keeps no bytes anywhere. The bank gets one episode via `episode_staging` (`user:` caption + `[image: name]`, the attachment in `turn_extras`, a `file:` record of kind/mime/name/size/sha256; a text file's words as a second `user:` turn). Images are kept as sent, HEIC included (no image library on the server, no `sips` on a Linux host, a re-encode loses the original). (b) **Voice:** `/capture/note` takes `kind: "voice"` → `note_kind: voice`, title "Voice note"; the phone transcribes with iOS's on-device recognizer only: it starts only in a language whose own recognizer is proven on-device (a per-language native check), and a patched native layer refuses rather than run a server request — `expo-speech-recognition` 58.0.0 alone silently drops `requiresOnDeviceRecognition` for a language without an on-device model and streams the audio to Apple (found in review, cicada-app #4); no recording kept, a refusal in words otherwise. (c) **App:** share activation adds one image and one file; a shared photo/PDF/text file is copied into the outbox's own folder and sent from the same stamped outbox (G202) by multipart, the copy deleted once sent or dropped; hold-to-talk on Capture. **Measured:** backend tests 15 new; the app's unit suite 75 → 93, `test:server` 10/10 against a scratch backend (a photo and a voice note each resent with one key: one episode each). **Left for later, deliberately:** *Sleep describing a picture or reading a PDF's text* — nothing reads a file's contents today but a `.txt`'s words; that is G195's business for pixels and a separate, engine-cost-measured slice for PDFs (pypdfium2 is already bundled for first-page renders, so text extraction would need no new dependency); *a bank handed over keeps the record, not the bytes* — an export that carries `$CICADA_HOME/files/<bank>/` beside the bank is open; *a device build, App Intents, Android* as listed above; *EXIF*: a photo's location stays inside the file on the Mac, never read and never in the bank — stripping it on the phone before sending is the owner's call. APPLY. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G94 | <a id="g94"></a>**Life-data streams (wearables, Spotify, GPS/saved places, Strava) — aggregate into experience, never dump the samples** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "a way where its easy to port data from other things like werables… spotify, GPS (google maps saved places), strava… CIcada should be done in such an agnostic way that it is able to ingest all this data and in turn communicate in a way thats easy for the human and agents to understand") | **Ruling 47 (owner 2026-10-28):** each stream's path follows the choosing rule (TODO ruling 47): an official API (Spotify, Strava, a wearable's own) where it gives history and metadata the harness cannot, off by default and read-only, beside its export; aggregation into experience is still this row's rule. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** No aggregate life-data pipeline for listening, wearables or GPS was found. Define rollups and provenance first; do not accumulate raw samples as memory claims. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g94). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The agnostic ingestion the user asks for is real and mostly already there** — `RawItem` + `origin` + the `ADAPTERS` connector seam + url_index dedup are source-agnostic by construction, and Google **Takeout parsing already exists** (`media_ingestor.parse_youtube_takeout*`), which is the same export vehicle that carries Maps saved places. Sanctioned routes per source: **Spotify** (real Web API, OAuth — saved tracks, top artists; plus a GDPR export for history), **Strava** (real API, OAuth), **Google Maps saved places** (Takeout), **Apple Health / wearables** (an on-device export XML, or HealthKit from a future iOS app — no server API), Oura/Whoop/Garmin (each has its own API). Each is one more adapter module in the existing registry — **that part is not the hard problem.** **The hard problem is representation, and getting it wrong would wreck the graph.** These streams are *quantitative time series*; Cicada's model is entities + claims + episodes, i.e. *semantic*. Dumping 8,000 heart-rate samples or every song play into a markdown knowledge graph produces a graph that is 99% noise and a Sleep queue that never drains — the same category error as dumping raw transcripts, which this project already refused. **The rule this row exists to fix:** ingest **aggregates and notable events, never samples** — "slept under 6h for nine days straight", "ran a personal best", "first visit to Lisbon", "listened to one album obsessively for a week". Raw samples stay outside the bank entirely (the `~/.cicada/` precedent for logos, screenshots and telemetry: derived-or-bulk artifacts of the outside world are not versioned memory), or are not retained at all. The closed 10-type set mostly absorbs the result without extension — **`location` already exists** for places visited, `media` for albums/tracks, and a workout or a sleep week is an *episode* with claims rather than an entity. Resist adding types; that instinct is what the type set is for. **Why this is worth building at all — and it is the strongest argument in the backlog for it:** the value of life data in a memory system is **correlation with experience, not the data itself**. "You were in Lisbon that week, slept five hours a night, and that is when you decided to switch projects" is exactly **G79**'s cross-stream retrieval, and no fitness app can say it because none of them hold the conversations. The summarization step — turning a numeric week into one sentence a human and an agent both read the same way — is **G77**'s output-packet idea applied to quantitative data, and it is deterministic enough to run without an engine (**G80**). **Sequence:** start with ONE source that is export-based and already half-supported (Maps saved places via Takeout → `location` entities), prove the aggregate-not-samples rule end to end, then add an OAuth adapter (Strava or Spotify) once the representation is settled. → extends **G47/G71** (ingest family + connector seam), **G79** (the north star this most directly serves), **G77** (packet-style summarization), **G80** (aggregation is engine-free), **G70** (the same "derived artifact lives outside the bank" rail). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. **2026-10-16 — the first stream built under this rule:** YouTube watch history (G71's follow-up; Takeout, Chrome's history read by the app, and the person's own agent) is one episode per platform per day of what was watched (`watch_history`), never a saved link per video and never a sample per visit; Sleep reads a day the way it reads a conversation. The other streams above remain unbuilt. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G95 | <a id="g95"></a>**Meetings and human↔human conversations as an ingestion source** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "have meetings/conversations be part of ingestion, so transcriptions from conversations can also come into this as part of human experience, wether its just a transcript, a recording, etc. useful info can come from this such as the people involved, things said, ideas discussed") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Wispr meetings preserve speaker labels and assistant-written notes. General meeting upload/recording, who-was-present semantics and other-person consent remain open; G179 handles selected message threads. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g95). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Named in `CLAUDE.md` as part of what Cicada is for, and **the one intake class the pipeline was not designed for**. Every episode today is *single-observer* — the user talking to an agent, or the user saving something. A meeting is **multi-party**, and that changes three layers, not just the parser. **What already exists and can carry it:** the ingest pipeline is source-agnostic (`RawItem` + `origin` + `processed: false`, Sleep extracts regardless of source), and — verified — the claim layer **already models third parties**: `observer: str = "agent" # agent \| rodrigo \| external:<name>` (`api/services/claims.py:68`), with three `external:*` observers live on the current graph (`eugenio-marchiori`, `konrad`, `milan`) and a per-observer trust multiplier already in the decay engine (`claim_reconciler.py:51-53`, `user_stated` fades ~3× slower). So "who said this" has a home; nothing needs inventing. **What does not exist:** no transcript parsing of any kind (no VTT/SRT/diarized-text reader — the `transcript` hits in `entity_extractor`/`session_stats` are about agent sessions), and **no audio transcription anywhere** — the Telegram voice-note transcription described in prose is **not implemented** (`grep -i whisper api/` → nothing). **Design:** (i) **Text first** — accept a transcript (`.txt`/`.md`/`.vtt`/`.srt`, or a paste) with optional speaker labels, `origin: meeting` (or per-tool: `granola`, `zoom`, `otter`, `voice-memo`), landing as one episode. This alone delivers most of the value and needs no new infrastructure. (ii) **Recordings** — transcribe **on-device** (whisper.cpp / MLX Whisper), matching the EmbeddingGemma precedent of a keyless local default; **the audio itself never enters the bank** — it is bulk binary and belongs under `~/.cicada/recordings/` or nowhere, exactly the rail that governs logos, screenshots and telemetry. Only the transcript is memory. (iii) **Speaker → observer**: a labelled speaker maps to `external:<slug>`, so a statement by someone else is stored as *theirs*, not as the user's belief — and **their** assertion about themselves is high-trust while their assertion about the world is not. This is where **G81 (contacts)** pays off: speaker labels are worthless until they resolve to real humans, and without it every meeting mints a fresh half-identified `person`. (iv) **What to extract**: attendees → `person`; ideas discussed → `concept`; decisions and commitments → claims with `valid_from` (the bi-temporal layer is already built for exactly this); action items → the existing inbox. No new entity type — a meeting is an episode, and the closed 10-type set absorbs the rest. **The rail, and it is heavier than any other source's:** recording a conversation involves **people who never opted into your memory system**. Spain (and many jurisdictions) require all-party consent for recording; beyond legality, storing another person's words in a permanently-historied git repo and letting an agent recall them is a different ethical class from saving your own chats. Non-negotiables: consent is captured per meeting and stored with the episode; a participant can be excluded or redacted after the fact (which git history makes genuinely hard — decide the redaction story *before* building, not after); third-party PII stays minimal per the **G81** rule; and nothing about a meeting is ever surfaced outside the user's own machine. **Sequence:** transcript-drop → speaker-to-observer mapping → on-device transcription → live capture (a much bigger problem: macOS system-audio capture needs ScreenCaptureKit/TCC or a virtual device, and meeting-bot integrations are a different product surface — explicitly out of scope for now). → the human↔human half of the project goal in `CLAUDE.md`; depends on **G81** (identity anchors), extends **G20/G47** (import family) and the claim layer's `external:` observers; relates **G77** (a meeting summary is the packet idea's natural home), **G94** (same aggregate-not-raw rail), **G79** (a meeting is pure experience-stream). **2026-09-23 (G134):** the first multi-party intake shipped — Wispr Flow meetings as `speaker:<label>:` turns with the per-turn `turns` sidecar, evidence kind `speaker`, `user` only by the owner's listed names; consent and the other note-takers remain here. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G111 | <a id="g111"></a>**Ingest newsletters (TLDR, TLDR AI) and answer "what landed today that matters to me"** (Rodrigo 2026-09-01: "process emails from newsletters like tldr ai, and tldr… so i could ask my agent about relevant things from today that might be relevant to me and my research") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Public RSS newsletters work. Mailbox newsletter capture and a personalized what-matters-today digest are not implemented by RSS ingestion. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g111). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Read the ask precisely: the ingestion is the easy half and it is not what was asked for.** The request is a *question* — "relevant to me and my research" — so the deliverable is a **relevance filter over a firehose**, not another capture channel. Cicada is well placed for exactly that and badly placed for nothing else here: the graph already encodes what the user works on and cares about, so scoring an incoming item against existing entities/claims is the differentiator. Raw ingestion alone produces a second inbox the user will not read. **Do the cheap path first and it may be the whole path.** TLDR publishes web archives and per-edition RSS — **VERIFIED 2026-09-01 by direct probe:** `https://tldr.tech/api/rss/ai` (TLDR AI) and `https://tldr.tech/rss` / `/api/rss/tech` (TLDR) are live RSS 2.0 feeds, 20 items each, **one item per edition** (`<link>` = `https://tldr.tech/ai/YYYY-MM-DD`, `<guid isPermaLink>`, `<pubDate>`, **no `<description>`/`content:encoded`** — the feed carries only the edition headline), and each edition page is **static server-rendered HTML** (~75 KB, no `__NEXT_DATA__`, JSON-LD present) holding **19 `<article>` blocks** per edition — one per story, each a headline suffixed "(N minute read)", a 2-4 sentence summary and exactly one outbound `href` with `utm_source=tldrai` — plus a sponsor `<article>` with a `(Sponsor)` suffix and `doubleclick` tracking links that a parser must drop. So the mailbox is never needed: the feed gives the edition stream, and the per-story split G111 needs is a ~40-line `<article>` splitter over the edition page, with the story's canonical URL (utm stripped) as the item id. No mailbox access is needed at all, because `feed_registry.subscribe_feed` (`api/services/feed_registry.py:80`) plus the existing `POST /sources/poll-feeds` and the Sleep-tail poll already do the whole capture job with **zero new consolidation code** — the module's own docstring says a poll is "identical to a manual `POST /sources/rss` paste". Verify the archive/RSS surface before writing a single line of email code. **Email is a categorically larger surface than RSS and must not be adopted casually.** An IMAP/Gmail connector reaches *everything* in the mailbox, not the newsletters — a scope the secrets posture (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, 0600) can hold but the *blast radius* of a bug cannot. If email is genuinely required, the only two acceptable shapes are (i) a **dedicated forwarding address / label-scoped** read (`label:tldr`), never a whole-mailbox grant, or (ii) the user forwarding issues by hand. Either way it is a peer adapter under `api/services/connectors/` honouring the documented `ADAPTERS` contract (`connectors/__init__.py:35,69`) — `base.run_sync` already owns the not-connected skip, the `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` background gate, error/skip recording and the chunked `ingest_batch`; do not hand-roll a second polling loop. **The parsing unit is the ITEM, not the email — this is the structural point.** One TLDR AI issue is ~8-10 *unrelated* stories, each a link plus a written summary; ingesting the email as one media item produces a page about nothing. It is the same one-source-many-items shape G71 already solved for saved-content exports, and it should reuse that machinery: a parser emitting a per-item breakdown, previewable through the identical `?preview=true` path before anything is staged. **The trap, and it is in the code today: these must NOT be evergreen.** `decay_policy.INGEST_SOURCES` (`api/services/decay_policy.py:49`) is `{media, bookmark, rss, pdf, ingest}` and everything on those paths is evergreen — decay-exempt, never archived, no decay nudge — with the stated rationale that *"a saved bookmark does not become less true by going unmentioned"*. That reasoning is sound because **saving is a deliberate act**. A newsletter arrives unbidden: the user chose the subscription, never the item. Routing 10 items/day through the `rss` path at evergreen would permanently inflate the graph with things the user never selected and would quietly break "absence of mention is a signal" — the one mechanism that keeps the graph honest. **So newsletter items need a decaying class (likely `volatile`, since news is stale in weeks) and therefore either a new writer-source tag outside `INGEST_SOURCES` or an explicit per-write override.** Decide this BEFORE the first ingest run, because retro-fixing decay classes across thousands of pages needs another one-shot migration. **Promotion is the natural filter, not a new mechanism:** an item mentioned once stays a chunk in the vector index; an item whose topic the user actually discusses crosses the existing promotion threshold on its own. That is the entity-promotion model doing precisely the job it was designed for, applied to a feed instead of a conversation — so the anti-pollution answer is mostly *already built*, provided the decay class is right. **What is genuinely new:** a daily-digest surface answering "what landed today that touches what I am working on", which is `POST /ask` (**G52**) pointed at a time-boxed slice and ranked against the graph rather than a free-text question — and worth checking whether it belongs in the Feed page or as a Sleep-time nudge rather than yet another screen. **Open:** whether unread/unscored items should ever reach the graph at all, or live only in the index until something promotes them. → extends **G71** (the connector adapter contract + the per-collection preview shape) and the shipped RSS/feeds half; bounded by **G66** (decay classes — the evergreen trap above is the binding constraint); serves **G52** (Ask is the surface the request actually names); relates **G105** (deterministic, non-agent-judgment capture — a newsletter parser is the same argument) and **G101** (what raw content is retained vs consolidated). **2026-09-29:** links inside a newsletter or an imported chat read through the same Reader ladder and read-time page writer as **G164** / **G165**, so a newsletter parser here could reuse that fetcher instead of adding its own. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G119 | <a id="g119"></a>**Other browsers — Arc, Firefox, Edge and Opera (bookmarks; open tabs where the browser exposes them)** (follow-up from the 2026-09-02 Safari track) | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Chrome/Safari plus several Chromium-family readers exist. The specifically requested Arc/Firefox/Edge/Opera support is not established for every bookmark/tab format; advertise an adapter matrix, not all browsers. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g119). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** Same shape as G30: the app reads the profile file, the backend parses bytes, `folders:` selection and the tree preview come for free via `bookmark_sync.folder_tree`. **Round 4 (C9, 2026-09-24) — the Chromium family shipped (backend and app, PR #109):** Brave, Vivaldi, Comet and Dia sync through the one Chromium parser (`bookmark_sync.CHROMIUM_BROWSERS`, `POST /sources/sync-bookmarks`'s `chromium: [{browser, dataB64}]`), each under its own origin (`<browser>-bookmark`) and channel (`<browser>-bookmarks`, listed once it has synced), **default profile only** (R-SR1: reading every `Profile N` silently would bring in a profile nobody ticked, and the removal diff has no profile scope — profile selection is this row's next slice). Bundle ids and default `Bookmarks` paths were verified from public sources, never a real profile. **What is left:** Arc (`StorableSidebar.json` — spaces/folders/tabs in one JSON, its own parser); Firefox (`places.sqlite`, `moz_bookmarks` ⋈ `moz_places`, WAL-mode — reuse `safari_tabs.load_tabs`'s temp-copy + sidecar pattern; its tab groups live in `sessionstore.jsonlz4`, not `places.sqlite` — a different reader than this row first budgeted); Edge and Opera (Chromium, but their paths are unverified until checked on a real install); and profile selection. Rail: no network, no LLM, idempotent through `url_index`. → **APPLY**, S each. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G120 | <a id="g120"></a>**Attention frequency — track who and what you keep coming back to (channels, accounts, authors, people) and let recurrence shape the graph** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03: "in social media like YouTube or Twitter there's always a certain number of channels or people who I love to see their tweets and stay informed. Do you think frequency in mentions also has to influence something in the memory graph, or at least should we track it?") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Mention-week spacing exists via G147. Attention/recurrence for evergreen curated media is a separate signal; repeated exposure must not become an inferred preference automatically. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g120). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists:** frequency already drives one thing — decay (`conflict_resolver`: confidence drops proportional to how often an entity USED to be referenced, CLAUDE.md Temporal Decay), **2026-09-24 (G147): this premise was false until G147 — decay was flat per class. Now distinct mention weeks slow entity and claim decay; the source-recurrence layers (1)–(3) below stay open.** and the promotion rule counts conversations (2+ mentions across conversations). Nothing counts **sources**: a saved video carries `media.url` and an OG title, a tweet carries an author handle in its URL, a newsletter carries a sender — but the *channel/account/author behind the item* is never made an entity, so 40 saves from one YouTube channel are 40 unrelated media pages and the person the owner follows most is invisible to the graph and to retrieval. **Design (three layers, all engine-free):** (1) **Source attribution at ingest** — `media_ingestor` derives a `source_ref` `{platform, kind: channel\|account\|author\|domain, id, display}` from the URL/metadata it already has (YouTube channel from the page's OG/`author_name`, X/Twitter handle from the path, Substack/newsletter sender from the feed row, podcast show, GitHub org, plain domain as the fallback); stored on the media episode + entity frontmatter, no fetch.
