(2) **Recurrence ledger + promotion** — a per-bank `_attention.yaml` (or a derived index — decide; must be rebuildable from the pages, never a second source of truth) counting saves/mentions per source_ref per week; when a source crosses the existing promotion threshold (≥ 2 saves across ≥ 2 weeks, or ≥ 3 saves) Sleep creates a `person`/`company`/`concept` entity for the channel/author with a `follows` claim (`observer` = user-derived, `source_trust: observed`, confidence from recurrence), `evergreen` never — it decays like any belief so a channel you stop watching fades honestly (the counter-signal is the whole point). Media pages get an `about`/`from` edge to it. (3) **Surface** — Feed gets a "You keep coming back to" strip (top sources by recent recurrence, with the count and last-seen date), the entity page shows the recurrence sparkline, `cicada_recall` weights a source's items by recurrence, and G111's "what landed today that matters" uses it as the ranking signal. **Rails:** counts only — no engagement scraping, no fetching follower lists, nothing behind auth (G102 ToS rail); ids/enums in any ledger (G113); a recurrence claim is `observed`, never `user_stated`, and the inbox may ask "still following <source>?" through the ordinary decay path rather than a new kind; portability — platform parsers are the same adapter shape as `connectors/`. **Not the same as:** G86 (feed dedup), G94 (life-data aggregates — that is samples→aggregates from wearables; this is recurrence over what you save and read), G111 (newsletters — a consumer of this signal). → feeds **G111**, **G93** (retrieval weight), **G118** (a `follows` claim cites the saves that earned it), **G81** (a recurring author is an identity anchor); consumes **G47**/**G71** (saved-content importers already carry the URLs). APPLY, M **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G121 | <a id="g121"></a>**World facts vs personal facts — a place (or company, tool) page must be anchored on why it matters to the person; encyclopedia facts are a dated cache an agent re-verifies, never truth** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03, looking at a location page whose summary and Key Facts were metro lines and court names: "what if something changes in that location and an agent in the future references the memory instead of researching the place? Should information about a place be just why it's relevant to the user?") | **2026-10-27 — ruling 44: world facts looked up and kept current; every page says why it matters (branch
`feat/web-context`).** The personal tier and the dated cache this row asked for, built: `page_relevance` derives "why it
matters to you" on every read (a stated relation or the person's own words, tense from the claim, or "No personal
relation on the record"), and `web_context` writes cited, dated `origin: web-context` world claims for public pages on
Sleep's plan runs — depth by use, re-checked by volatility × use, the newest replacing an older world fact with history
kept, never one on the person's own words, undoable under "Settled by a check". The rail above ("no fetching to refresh
world facts in this row") is lifted by ruling 44, under ruling 27's gates. Dry run on a copy of the owner's bank: 9 of 281 people public by the test (acquaintance, not consumption, keeps a
person private), 39 lookups → 76 adds, 1 replacement; the page that prompted this row now closes its stale employer
as ended. The two-tier card in the app is still not built.
**2026-10-27 — past roles written as current (PR #485).** A public figure's page read "Works at: <company-a>" while its own prose said the person had joined <company-b> the day before; one conversation (real values redacted) had said the person "worked at" <company-a>, "worked on" <project-a> and "as of yesterday, joined" <company-b>, and all three were stored open, dated the day it was said. **Why:** (1) the claims were written 2026-10-08, before #270 (2026-10-14) gave `works-at` its ending labels — the seed folded "worked at" into `works-at` (`predicates-seed.yaml` synonyms) and nothing read the tense — and no repair ever closed what was already written (`coexist_reopen` only reopens); "worked on" still has no ending today, so a past project is current even for claims read since (bank: 2 such on 2026-10-16); "lived in", "studied at", "reported to" had no vocabulary at all. (2) "joined" was no synonym and no ending (`entity_extractor.entities_to_claims` → `claim_reconciler.reconcile_stage3`), so it stayed under its own label and the card, which reads `works-at` (`PersonCard.swift` `PersonFacts.worksAt`), never saw it; Stage 3 had nothing to compare it with. **Fix:** endings for `lives-in`, `studies-at`, `reports-to`, and only endings for `works-on` (never "worked on": "I worked on alpha-project today" is ongoing work); a curated, type-gated `starts` list of unambiguous labels ("started working at", "was hired by"; never a bare "joined": a club, a team and a university are all typed `company`); Stage 1 writes a job that began as "works at" with a stated `starts` day; `past_roles_repair` for banks written before, which keeps a claim later conversations restated (owner bank, dry run over a copy after review: 17 claims to close on 11 pages — 7 on the owner's page, all ending labels — 1 start). The card itself is right once the data is; an existing "joined" claim is not folded, so such a page shows no "Works at" until re-read. **Open for the owner:** a start never closes another current place (2026-10-14 rule); "newest current place wins" would close concurrent jobs and nested homes on the owner's own page (7 groups measured: two spellings of one school, a country, a city and a district as homes, a contract and a lab as jobs), so it is not built until the owner rules. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Typed claims and dated evidence exist. Personal-reason anchoring versus refreshed encyclopedia-cache facts and visible freshness semantics are not fully implemented by a date field alone. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g121). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **Diagnosis:** the extractor fills a `location`/`company`/`tool` page with whatever the model knows — none of it observed in an episode — and writes it with the same `source_trust: agent_extracted` and confidence as things the person actually said. Two failures follow: the fact goes stale silently (a renamed venue, a closed line), and an agent reading the page cannot tell a remembered fact from a looked-up one, so it may act on the cache instead of checking. `claims.py:71` knows `user_stated\|agent_extracted\|agent_reflected\|external`; nothing marks *model knowledge*. **Ruling to adopt:** a memory is about the person's experience; the world is not Cicada's to remember. Every page has two tiers: (1) **personal claims** — the relation to the person (visited, plans, works-with, saved-because, decided) with the episode span (G118); authoritative. (2) **world facts** — convenience context; allowed only when tagged `source_trust: model_knowledge` (new value, decay multiplier ≥ `external`), `decay_class: volatile`, dated by `recorded_at`, and paired where possible with a `sources:` pointer (G61) to where the current truth lives (the venue's site, a docs URL). **Changes:** (a) the Stage-1 prompt asks for the relation first and marks anything not grounded in the text as model knowledge — a page whose only claims are world facts is a promotion-rule violation (nothing personal was observed) and stays a candidate; (b) `entity_extractor`/`claim_pipeline` accept the new trust value; existing pages get a one-shot backfill that re-tags claims without a source span as `model_knowledge` (dry-run count first on the live bank — expect most `location` Key Facts to flip); (c) the entity card renders the two tiers apart — "Why it's in your memory" above, "Context (as of <date>, from the model's knowledge — verify before relying on it)" below, with the sources link; (d) `cicada_recall` / `cicada_ask` label world facts as cached-on-<date> in their output, and the G75 handshake carries the rule in one sentence: *personal facts are authoritative; world facts are a cache — check before acting*; (e) the inbox never asks a decay question about a world fact (it simply fades). **Rails:** no fetching to refresh world facts in this row (that is G102's tail, gated); ledger ids only; the backfill's commit is `Cicada-Author: cicada` with the exact pages named. → depends on **G118** (a span is what separates the tiers), **G61** (sources), **G66** (decay classes); feeds **G75** (handshake), **G93**/**G52** (answer labelling), **G53** (state dictionary carries only personal facts). APPLY, M **2026-09-23 (G133):** the paper card is the first surface with both tiers — "Why it's in your memory" (spans in the owner's own notes) above "Context (from arXiv, as of <date>)" (an `external` `describes` claim authored by `cicada`). **2026-09-23 (G61 phase 2):** the world/personal marking lands at the predicate level first — `locus: world\|artifact\|person` in the vocabulary — and "a cache an agent re-verifies" gets its mechanism in G61's escalation ladder (a checked source can settle only a machine-held `world` fact, never a personal claim); this row's own rail still holds — the fetching lives in G61, gated, not here. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲. **2026-10-09 (G194 page quality):** change (a) in part — Stage 1's prompt now asks for the person's relation first, facts about the thing rather than about the conversation or the assistant's answer, and no entity for a detail only the assistant's answer named; promotion now needs the person's own words (`promotion`). The `model_knowledge` trust value, the two-tier card and the backfill are not built. **2026-10-28 — the person sees it, and pages have blocks (vision item 5, branch `feat/vision-page-contract-models`).** The card shows `relevance.line` under the summary (never on the owner's page). `GET /entities/{id}` carries `blocks` (`page_blocks`: relevance, where to check with each source's state, stored coordinates, looks, ongoing events, contributors with Sleep counted) and an ETag (`page_blocks.card_etag`, stats only; the app's `EntityCardCache`); history rows carry `trigger`/`via` so Sleep reads as a tag. Agents get the same blocks through `cicada_recall_detail(include=…)`; a remote connection never gets per-page history. Pinned both sides by `api/tests/fixtures/page_blocks.json`. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G126 | <a id="g126"></a>**Integrations page in Settings, by category — connect apps the way Claude Desktop and Discord do** (Rodrigo 2026-09-03: "an integrations page by category: to-do apps, calendar apps, health apps like Garmin, Strava, YouTube for entertainment, social media… a page in settings similar to how the Claude Desktop app lets you connect different apps, or Discord") | **Ingestion modularity follow-up (2026-10-06):** G188 owns registered source capabilities and common status/scope/sync/remove presentation; Integrations consumes that contract with small source-specific permission/picker views. G179 iMessage/optional Instinct detection is the first new source exercise; broader migration follows. [Requirements](../specs/2026-10-06-provider-and-ingestion-modularity.md). **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Categorized integration Settings and many readers exist. Additional cards/adapters retain their individual IDs; an available catalog tile is not proof a connector is installed or capturing. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g126). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists, scattered:** standing connections live in three places — Settings → Agents/Plans (engines and keys), the Feed's `+` catalog (imports + the three OAuth connectors: Pinterest, Reddit, X), and channel state on `GET /sources/channels` (G62). Adapters already cover Telegram, RSS feeds, ICS calendars (any calendar app that publishes ICS), Safari/Chrome bookmarks, Safari iCloud tabs, and one-shot exports for Instagram, YouTube playlists, TikTok, LinkedIn saved, Reddit GDPR, Google Takeout, ChatGPT/Claude chats. Nothing shows *the set of apps in your life* as one page. **The line between the two surfaces (ruling):** Feed's `+` catalog stays the place for a one-shot act (drop an export, sync a folder); **Settings → Integrations** is the place for a *standing* connection (sign in once, polled on the Sleep tail, disconnect here). Both are projections of the one channel registry (`channel_registry` + `connectors.ADAPTERS`), so a connector added once appears in both. **The page:** categories with one row per app and its live state (connected · last sync · items · error), logo-first: *Chat & agents* (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, ChatGPT, Codex, Cursor), *Browsers* (Safari, Chrome; G119 Arc/Firefox/Brave), *Social & saved* (X, Reddit, Pinterest, Instagram, TikTok, LinkedIn), *Entertainment* (YouTube — subscriptions/watch-later via Takeout, playlists via export; podcasts via RSS), *Calendar* (any ICS; Google/Apple via their ICS links), *To-do & notes* (Apple Reminders/Notes via export or AppleScript, Todoist/Things via export or API — new adapters, G13's intake), *Health & activity* (Strava via OAuth API, Garmin via export, Apple Health via export — **G94's rule: aggregates, never samples**; weekly summaries become claims, raw samples never enter the bank), *Feeds* (RSS/newsletters, G111), *Messaging* (Telegram). Each row: Connect (OAuth/keys/export instructions per adapter contract), what Cicada reads (one honest sentence, incl. cost — the X row already states pay-per-read), Disconnect (forgets secrets via `base.forget()`). **New adapters are the work, the page is the frame:** ship the page over the existing adapters first, then add Strava (OAuth, activities → weekly aggregate claims), Todoist/Reminders (tasks → G13 candidates), YouTube subscriptions (Takeout parser — no API key needed), Garmin/Apple Health (export parsers). **Rails:** the adapter contract in `connectors/__init__.py` (CHANNEL_ID, LABEL, FIELDS, LOGIN_MODE, SECRET_NAMES, is_connected, forget, sync) is the only way in; secrets in `~/.cicada/secrets.env`; unattended polls behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`; no scraping behind auth (G102); life data as aggregates (G94); each new app is its own small row when it starts. → frames **G94**, **G111**, **G119**, **G120** (a source's attention count is a natural column here), **G13**; extends **G71**/**G62**; feeds **G117** (onboarding step 2 is this page filtered to 'connect one thing'). The page ships over the existing registry; Strava/Todoist-Reminders/YouTube-subscriptions/Garmin-Apple-Health remain their own future rows. APPLY (page S/M; each adapter S–M) **Track L (2026-09-05) — real marks; R7 of the 2026-09-02 Safari-import plan is reversed.** That plan ruled "browser marks are drawn, not downloaded" and shipped `ChromeGlyph`/`SafariGlyph`. It was a *plan* rule, never a `TODO.md` ruling, and it named its own escape hatch ("drop `Resources/logos/chrome.png` in and flip the tile's `logoName`"); both `PlatformTile` and `OriginMark` already preferred a PNG. The hatch was taken because the drawn Chrome was wrong on four independent axes: all four wedge colours were the pre-2015 Google palette (`#DB4437/#F4B400/#0F9D58/#4285F4` vs the current `#EA4335→#D93025 / #FCC934→#FBBC04 / #34A853→#1E8E3E / #1A73E8`), the wheel was rotated ~90° (red centred at 2 o'clock instead of 11), the centre disc was ≈0.34 r against the real ≈0.45 r, and every wedge was a flat fill where the mark uses two-stop gradients. `SafariGlyph` was Apple's own compass SF Symbol tinted an invented `#00A2E8`. **The rule that replaces it:** an app whose files Cicada reads off this Mac is installed by construction, so its icon comes from `NSWorkspace` at runtime (nothing redistributed, never stale — this is also how R-L3 keeps Apple's marks out of the repo); everything else is a maintainer-fetched, licence-recorded, committed PNG (`scripts/fetch-logos.sh` + `logos.manifest.json` + `LOGOS.md`, nominative use only). **Drawn brand marks do not come back** — a wrong-coloured logo that appears only when an asset is missing looks like a logo and isn't, which is worse than an honest SF Symbol. Reserved and not yet claimed: the Firefox and Brave marks are committed for **G119**'s channels. **Track S (2026-09-05 evening) — the line this page draws is now visible on the card.** Standing vs one-shot was structural (Integrations vs the Feed's `+`) and invisible once a source had a card; `SourceLiveness` now reads the same `channel_registry` `actions` the two surfaces share and prints `Polls nightly` for a standing connection against `Imported once` for a one-shot import, so the distinction is legible on the Sources page without opening Settings. **Track O (2026-09-23):** every Integrations row is a Settings search anchor (`channel:<id>`, `harness:<id>`, `exportOnly:<id>`) — typing an app's name in Settings lands on its row; the page's content is unchanged. **DS-3b:** real harness marks; Manage and Connect are sheets. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ page shipped, adapters open. **Redesign 2026-10-22 (TODO ruling 36):** the integrations catalogue becomes the Sources and Agents pages of the connection model (**G217**). | PARTIAL · Later |
| G140 | <a id="g140"></a>**What the Instinct comparison changed — memory quality borrowed on Cicada's rails, and the video watch record** (Rodrigo 2026-09-23, on Dhravya Shah's article reverse-engineering the memory of the Instinct iMessage assistant: "I think maybe some things here can be of use. See what and see if we can work on implementing." — and on a video-watching skill: "Could match well for storing and having info about videos. Maybe getting the metadata or whatever else.") | **2026-10-27 — lean reads (branch `fix/lean-reads`; G148, G149).** A real session (values redacted) spent ~6.3k tokens over 5 calls and still grepped transcripts: one `cicada_recall` was 14,400 characters — 7 pages, 5 off-topic, the relevant page saying its facts three times — and `cicada_ask` said "the assistant" where the episode recorded a Claude Code session and its resumable id. Fix: a per-leg relevance floor (a page shows only when some leg ranks it in its first 3 and near its best; at most 5), a 1,800 / 700-character body budget with repeated bullets folded at render (never one carrying a new number, date, link or name), one-hop heads only when a single page passed, one excerpt per conversation; every printed `ep_…` id reads through `cicada_sources` (the `sources` scope, R-R28 size remotely, fenced); Ask, recall and the episode read name the other side from the episode's own fields (`episode_actor`); contract 22 / remote 15 send a factual question straight to `cicada_ask`. Measured on a 7,962-page bank copy, 25 questions: 457,788 → 150,672 characters (−67%), the answer-bearing fact in 23/25 both before and after; Ask named the actor in 2/6 "who did X" answers before, 5/6 after (haiku, plan). Still open (Sleep writes, PR 2 in the trace): the fact without its event name on the owner page, which no read can recover. **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Instinct findings are folded into provenance/video/quality rows. Watch-record infrastructure exists; preserve each named incomplete slice under its implementing ID rather than treating a comparison document as shipment. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g140). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The article, in one line.** Instinct keeps git-tracked markdown, injects a ~4.25k-token LLM-written profile (life context, autonomy calibration, communication style) plus a compaction recap, retrieves by grep over files that carry `aliases`, reconciles once a day, and forgets only explicit negations; the author's supermemory replication adds a static/dynamic profile, time-based expiry and auto-included history. Everything about Instinct is inferred from outside the product. **Adopted (Track Q, `feat/memory-quality`, plan `../plans/2026-09-23-memory-quality.md`, rulings Q-R1…Q-R17):** (1) *aliases and word-by-word matching* (Q-R1) — MCP recall's keyword leg was a whole-query substring that never read `aliases`; it is now `search_service.lexical_entity_hits` (the G136 hand-off); on a bank whose FTS file is still building, the leg answers from names, aliases and tags only, and a body- or claim-only match appears once the background build lands; (2) *relationship labels resolve* (Q-R1) — current claims are recall's third RRF leg through `claim_subject_hits`, mapped to their subject, and `mcp_tools._rrf_fuse` IS `search_service.rrf_fuse`; (3) *old versions, bounded* (Q-R2, Q-R3) — recall shows claims closed in the last 30 days as `was X until D → now Y` (≤ 2 a page, ≤ 5 in all; "withdrawn" and "ended D (its stated end)" where that is what closed them) and `cicada_get_perspective(history=true)` lists every earlier claim (≤ 20, off by default and byte-identical when off); the dead `include_superseded` on `vector_index.search_claims` is removed (it could only surface a state no writer produces); (4) *a static/dynamic profile without prose* (Q-R13) — the primer's now-view splits into Standing (the person's page and one-liner via G117's resolver, their timezone per request and never stored, "How to work with me" = standing `skill` pages by confidence alone, long-standing durable/evergreen pages) and Current (projects, pages in focus in the last 14 days, people, conversations), read off the G66 decay classes; `_state.md` schema v2; a remote connection gets the rows that describe the person (the one-liner, timezone, How to work with me, long-standing, in focus) only when it holds a `read`-scope tool — a `record`-only connection was promised "save notes, links and facts", not a profile (final review); (5) *a timeline an agent can read* (Q-R4) — `cicada_timeline(since)` reads the commit manifests on demand, ids and counts only, nothing stored; (6) *explicit negation, with provenance* (Q-R5) — `cicada_retract_claim(subject, claim_id, reason, evidence?)` lets an agent withdraw a claim IT wrote: the claim closes, a born-closed `retracts` record keeps the reason and any cited words, nothing is deleted; a human or Sleep claim is refused, a remote connection owns only its own `remote:<id>` claims, and the pre-G135 `mcp-agentic-write` placeholder counts as any local agent's only on an `mcp`-origin claim (the same placeholder lands on Telegram's user-stated claims). Records are bookkeeping, never beliefs, and every surface that lists claims drops them through one test, `claims.is_record`: MCP history, the app's claim endpoints, the `/search` claim leg (not indexed at all; search-index schema 2 rebuilds an older index) and an episode's citations; (7) *time-based expiry* (Q-R6, Q-R7) — a claim may carry `expected_end` (or be a G17 `due` date) and Sleep's engine-free tail closes it the day after, in a `cicada`-authored `Expiry <date>` commit (trigger `sleep/expiry`); a page already dirty before expiry runs is skipped that night, so neither a failed commit's restore nor a good commit can take a person's uncommitted edit. Contract v3 (local) and v2 (remote) name the new tools, and a test holds R12 for every argument either primer names (Q-R14; it found `cicada_recall_detail(id)`). **Video (R5, Q-R8…Q-R12):** `cicada_save_url` keeps a note given for an already-saved link and names the episode to cite; an agent's note is written as the agent's words; `external:<name>` is a value the observer schema accepts; `cicada_record_watch(url, summary, excerpts=[{t, quote}], chapters?)` records a watch as one episode + one `describes` claim whose spans are `assistant` (the summary) and `media` (each quote, time derived at read) — excerpts only, never a transcript, and Cicada never downloads a video; Vimeo/Loom descriptions are kept and chapters parsed from them; link enrichment's zero-LLM `describes` claim takes only the description's first paragraph, chapter stamps dropped, ≤ 400 characters (final review: a 4,433-character description had been copied into the claim whole) — the full text stays on the page. **Known gap:** a claim id is minted from its fact, so withdraw → restate reuses the closed claim's id; the withdrawal picks the open copy and mints a fresh record id, but the root fix — `write_claim` not reusing a closed claim's id — is still open. **Already better in Cicada, kept:** commit trailers naming author, engine and session vs bare reconciliation commits; typed, bi-temporal claims with trust tiers vs bullet lists of dated prose; supersede-never-delete vs prose rewrites; evidence spans with hash staleness (G118) vs no link from a fact to its sentence; temporal decay (G66) vs no automatic forgetting; one episode per session (G104) vs one document per day; capture with no intake ceiling vs "come back tomorrow"; a dated, deterministic cursor (`_state.md`) vs an unstamped profile with a measured ~2-day lag; hybrid retrieval vs grep (Instinct's own probes miss a paraphrase). **Rejected, and the rail that rejects it:** an LLM-written profile (G53: a cursor, never a copy; stale by construction; a daily cost); redundant fan-out writes (G101's stable address — Instinct itself reports stale duplicates); a "compress and remove incidental details" pass (destroys G118 spans; recall already truncates at read); read-only agent memory (the two-way port); supermemory as a backend (hosted and closed, replaces git — breaks G50/G76/G92 and the trailers); a daily-only intake with a token cap; an in-context todo board. **Open:** P9 — the article's 13-point rubric as a $0 retrieval eval on the demo bank (expected id in recall top-k for single fact, alias, paraphrase, typo, relationship label, superseded-not-current, abstention, two-hop; the LLM-judged half on demand) — the harness **G78** asks for; P3 secret and one-time-code scrubbing on every episode writer (Local-sources track, R-N3 — including the two writers this row added, `watch_record._write_episode` and `media_ingestor.write_note_episode`); P10 an open-loops cursor row; P11 a "last time in this project" pointer; typo tolerance; Stage-1 extraction of stated ends from prose (a paid prompt change); the person's own "that's off" closing a user-stated claim and entity-level forget through a `removal` item (P7's broader half); an app surface for the timeline, the watch record (▶ m:ss chips, a Watched pill, chapter seek buttons) and withdrawals; the remote primer's older rows — project one-liners, people ids and conversation titles, there since G135 — still reach a connection with no `read`-scope tool (only the G140 rows were gated). → extends **G53**, **G75**, **G93**, **G118**, **G136**, **G66**, **G17**, **G22**, **G113**; feeds **G78**. **2026-09-23:** P10 (an open-loops cursor) and P11 (a "last time in this project" pointer) are delivered by **G141** for projects: `_state.md` v3 `now`/`next`, `cicada_project`, and `GET /projects`. "An app surface for the timeline" here means this row's bank-wide `cicada_timeline`, which the Projects page does not replace; it stays open with the watch-record and withdrawal views (Tracks P/O). **2026-09-29:** the open 'app surface for the watch record (▶ m:ss chips, a Watched pill, chapter seek buttons)' is folded into **G162**: five honest states (none, transcript, watched, watched-and-transcript, recorded/method-not-given), a `basis` on `record_watch`, and Watch / Transcript enqueue in the Feed. `record_watch`'s rails (no download, excerpts only) are unchanged. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🛠️ adopted parts shipped; P9 open. | PARTIAL · Later |
| G155 | <a id="g155"></a>**Google services — Gmail, Google Calendar events and Drive, so a belief can cite a specific event or email** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "connect google services. google services can be something in the backlog tho for now, but its good to reference specific calendar events, emails, etc.") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Calendar through EventKit/ICS works. Direct Gmail/Drive OAuth ingestion and citations to specific messages/events are not implemented by that calendar reader. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g155). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **The point is citation, not intake.** The ask is that a belief can point at *the* event or *the* email it came from — G118's spans, not copies — so an agent answering "when did I agree to that" lands on the message. **What exists:** Google Calendar reaches Cicada today only through an ICS URL (`calendar_registry.py`, opt-in feed polling) or, from round 4, through macOS Calendar when the Google account is on the Mac (D2, EventKit) — neither carries a stable Google event id to cite; Google Takeout is a one-shot export the registry already names (`channel_registry.py`); there is no mail reader (**G111** argues why email must not be adopted casually: a mailbox connector reaches everything, and the blast radius of a bug is the whole inbox). **The burden to know before building:** Gmail read scopes and full Drive access are Google **restricted scopes** — an app that requests them for other people needs Google's OAuth verification and an annual third-party security assessment, which a local-first open-source app shipping one client id cannot carry lightly; a per-person client id (bring your own Google Cloud project) avoids it at a real setup cost. **The no-OAuth alternative:** a **Takeout** import (Mail as mbox, Calendar as ICS, Drive files) through the one intake — no scopes, no verification, the person's own export, re-importable (G20 in-place updates); the cost is freshness. **Constraints:** the ToS rail and the credentials rule (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, never in a bank, never logged); scrub before stage (`episode_scrub`); an email is someone else's words too — a line from a sender is `speaker` evidence, never `user`; narrowest scopes first (`calendar.events.readonly`, `drive.file`), Gmail last and only with a filter the person writes. **Rodrigo 2026-09-28: "open the backlog entry for the google direct connection"** — asked after syncing a new bank, so the direct connection is wanted, Calendar first. **The first slice, scoped:** Google Calendar only, read-only (`calendar.events.readonly` — a *sensitive* scope, not a restricted one: public use needs Google's OAuth verification, but no security assessment; until verified, a client id serves at most 100 listed test users), as a standing connector in the existing registry (`api/services/connectors/base.py`, Connect/Manage in Integrations, disconnect through `base.forget()`), signed in with PKCE in the browser the way OpenRouter's sign-in works (R-AG10). Each event staged through `episode_staging` keyed `google-calendar:<calendarId>:<eventId>` so a belief cites *the* event (its `htmlLink` as a `sources:` ref), edits rewritten in place, a cancelled event tombstoned; incremental reads through the API's `syncToken`, polled on the Sleep tail behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH`, a Sync now ungated. Attendees are `speaker`-side facts, never the person's words; descriptions scrubbed and capped like `calendar_local.py`'s. **Open for the owner:** one shared Cicada client id (verification on the maintainer) vs. bring-your-own Google Cloud project (no verification, real setup cost). Until it ships, adding the Google account under System Settings → Internet Accounts already brings its calendars in through macOS Calendar (D2) — without a citable Google id. Gmail and Drive stay RESEARCH under the burden above. → relates **G111** (newsletters, the email caution), **G126** (Integrations), **G118** (citations), round-4 D2 (calendars). **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 APPLY (Calendar slice) · RESEARCH (Gmail, Drive). | PARTIAL · Later |
| G156 | <a id="g156"></a>**Browser history sync — what the person actually read, not only what they kept** (Rodrigo 2026-09-24: "for browser's i'd give the ability to sync history, you can have that in the backlog and for handoff to the next session") | **Implementation review (2026-10-06, dev 3efba432):** Bookmarks and tab groups work; history-based reading/attention capture and its privacy/rollup controls are not implemented by bookmark polling. [Code evidence / acceptance](BACKLOG_IMPLEMENTATION_REVIEW.md#g156). **Earlier reasoning and progress (historical):** **What exists.** Browsers sync bookmarks (`bookmark_sync.py`, `bookmark_seen.py`) and Safari's iCloud tabs (`safari_tabs.py`), each channel gated on the person's own act (`cicada.browserWatch.enabled.<channel>`, the Track I T1 rule); history is read nowhere. **Where it lives:** Chrome keeps it in a per-profile SQLite `History` file (`urls`, `visits`; times in microseconds since 1601), Safari in `~/Library/Safari/History.db` (`history_items`, `history_visits`; seconds since 2001), which needs Full Disk Access like the bookmarks file does. **Privacy first — this is the most sensitive stream a browser has:** a history is every page, including the ones a person would never keep. Rails any build must respect: the **app** opens the file read-only (a copy, since the browser holds a lock) and posts a projection — the backend never opens `~/Library` (the rail); off until the person turns it on per browser, never ticked by default in onboarding; a **domain denylist** applied app-side before anything leaves the file (banking, health, adult, private-window leftovers, and the person's own additions), plus a minimum dwell or revisit count so a stray click is not a memory; titles and URLs only — never page bodies fetched from history, never behind auth (ToS rail); scrubbed (query strings carry tokens) before staging; aggregated into one episode per day per browser rather than one per visit, so the extraction queue is not flooded; deletion in the browser tombstones (G20). **Why it matters:** the north star's *experience* half — what held attention, not what was curated — and G128's "what actually held your attention". **Handoff:** next session — decide the unit (a day digest vs. per-domain threads) and the denylist's default, then plan. → extends the browser channels (**G126**, Track I T1); relates **G128**. **Previous execution label (historical):** 🔲 RESEARCH. **2026-10-23:** **G218** (activity traces) takes the live half — the frontmost tab's URL in a browser the person allowed, with dwell, held outside every bank with a retention — and proposes this row's unit for the bank: a counted day digest, index-only, written only behind the person's switch. Importing a history file stays this row's. | UNBUILT · Later |
| G218 | <a id="g218"></a>**Activity traces — what the person did on the computer, with times, so "the video I watched at 15:20" is findable later** (Rodrigo 2026-10-23, paraphrased: proactively track what I do on my computer through the day, with timestamps, maybe screenshots if needed, and take interesting information from the traces; "traces would be extremely cool") | **2026-10-28 — the traces redesign (owner's answers to the redesign, Q12; mock 11; branch `feat/traces-v2`).** Rodrigo 2026-10-28: remove the YouTube-only switch — "if we put youtube, what about all the other things I do and watch on other websites?"; manage traces by day with one screenshot per day "kind of like BeReal"; capture all connected displays; "a low screenshot frequency is probably best". Built: **(1) Learn from my day** — one switch, off (`learn_from_day`; `daily_summary`/`learn_from_summary` read aliases; a settings file migrates to on only when both old switches were on, so an index-only summary never widens into a model read). The digest is counted with no model and names no title, address or screenshot text; project time is attributed only to the person's own projects (ruling 45) through their folder/repository names as whole words in a window title, the repository on a code host, or an address tied by hand — a tie leaves it unattributed. The page previews exactly that digest (`GET /sources/traces/preview`). **Remove from memory** closes every open claim whose evidence is only digests (a `retracts` record beside each, origin `traces-remove`) as one commit, with Undo (ids kept sealed in the traces settings). **(2) What you watch and read** (`watch_and_read`, on, alias `youtube_to_watch_history`): videos of any platform the watch history knows into the watch history, every other page ≥ 30 s a day into an index-only reading day (`source: reading-history`) — never a model, never a mention; the owner accepted it as 39b amended (TODO ruling 39, 2026-10-28). **(3) Days as cards** (cover = a frame of the day's longest span, main screen; behind Touch ID), a day opened shows screenshots by moment, apps, sites and every span, each deletable, or the whole day (`POST /sources/traces/delete`). **(4) Every connected screen** behind consent sheet 2 (`CONSENT_VERSION` 2): one frame per screen, every app the policy may not describe cut out before capture (`SCContentFilter(display:excludingApplications:exceptingWindows:)`), a browser only through its vetted front window; sheet-1 people keep front-window frames until they agree. **(5) Cadence** 5 min on change / 15 min steady (was 1 / 5): measured ≤ ~0.5 MB, ~10 ms encode, ~1.1 s OCR CPU a frame → ≤ ~30 MB and ~2 CPU-min a heavy day per screen. **Speed (basis in the report):** the tick's live main-actor probes went from p50 ~6–7 ms to ~0.07–0.14 ms (the Screen Recording preflight, a TCC lookup, is now asked only when a frame is due); `/sources/traces/sync` p95 unchanged within noise (steady 10–14 ms, promoting 16–21 ms after vs 12–52 ms before, three interleaved runs); preview p95 25 ms on a 7-day synthetic store, day-cards 2 ms warm. Q13's new signals stay "not yet". **Review of #508 (fixed):** the every-screen filter is an allow-list read per
screen (helpers like Quick Look were drawn through a deny-list); Remove from memory takes back done happenings too (born
closed) and restores their dates exactly; a removal is refused (409) when traces were wiped, so it can always be undone;
the store refuses whole-screen frames under sheet 1; the recorded sheet is the one the app displayed; a one-time note for
people upgraded with the YouTube switch on; a deleted span across midnight takes its next-day screenshots. **Open:** the owner bank copy has 0 projects that are the person's by ruling 45, so attribution has nothing to match until projects are claimed; Ask lookups over reading days. **Ruling 47 (owner 2026-10-28):** traces are tier 1 of ruling 47 — consumption seen on the person's own Mac — and their consolidation (the vision map's item 7) is part of the same harness-consumption story; ruling 38's rails are unchanged. **2026-10-26 — its own page (Rodrigo 2026-10-26: "Traces are fine in Sources, but maybe it should be its own page, so we can actually see the trace of the screenshots that were taken. The user knows everything is local.").** Built in PR `feat/traces-sources-projects`: a Traces rail page (appended, no ⌘ digit — DR-22), shown only while traces are on or were ever on (`TracesPageVisibility`: enabled, consent given, or a day kept; a Wipe takes it away). The days down the side, a day's screenshots (still behind Touch ID, fetched only after it, memory only) and spans beside them, one line on where it lives ("Everything here stays on this Mac, encrypted; screenshots N days, timeline N days"), Settings… (the controls Sources held) and Wipe. Sources keeps a small Traces row linking to the page, and the switch itself until traces were first turned on. Every G218 rail unchanged. *Next:* a denser day view (an hour strip with thumbnails on it) once the owner has lived with this one. **Built (2026-10-23, PR #456, ruling 38 — screenshots from the start).** The app captures (`Services/Traces/`, `TraceRecorder` on seams; preflight-only permissions; the front window only, chosen from the window server's front-to-back list; Vision OCR on this Mac after capture; nothing on the app's disk); the backend keeps (`trace_store`, AES-256-GCM per record bound to kind and day, `$CICADA_HOME/traces/`, 30 / 7 days, Forget ≤ 15 min, Wipe shreds the key); `trace_memory` promotes — only past the 15-minute forget window — YouTube views into the watch history (`via: traces`, `dwell_s`) and, behind its switch, a counted index-only day summary; `watch_history.lookup` answers a time-of-day question ("what was that video at 15:20"). Any app registered for `https:` is a browser (described only through a verified tab). `PermissionDragHelper(kind:)` for Accessibility and Screen Recording. **Security review of #456 (BLOCK, fixed in the PR):** B1 forget now always precedes promotion and reaches spans filed under yesterday; B2 the screenshot window comes from the window server's front-to-back list and a frame is dropped when the front app or tab changed; B3 any https: handler is a browser. **Key custody is interim:** a 0600 file outside the store behind `trace_keys.KeyProvider`; the recommended ruling is a Secure Enclave–wrapped data key handed to the backend in memory once Developer ID signing lands (`storage.md`, "Activity traces"). **Watch-history switch on by default — kept, with its cost said:** a trace watch lands in the watched day, so a remote connector the person granted `sources` can read it like any watched day (Chrome's own door, G71, already does); kept on because the owner's stated goal (ruling 38) is exactly that lookup, the consent sheet names it, and it is one switch away. **Owner 2026-10-24 (TODO ruling 39b): confirmed — it stays on by default**, "turned on by default, same as having agents check your accounts and stuff (mainly read access, to update stuff)". Traces themselves stay off by default (ruling 38 §4); the switch is what a trace watch does once the person turned traces on. Its watched days record both dates like every episode (ruling 39a): the day's first watch as `timestamp` (each watch keeps its own times in the body), when Cicada first wrote the day as `received_at`. **Open slices:** the key ruling · Safari URLs (private windows unproven) · Ask/`cicada traces` lookups over raw rows (blocked by the model rail: only the summary may reach a model) · the threat-model page · Full Disk Access wiring of the drag helper · a quit-time flush of the last minute's spans · a recording dot on the menu-bar sprite · Touch ID before timeline titles and addresses (screenshot text already behind it). **Problem.** Conversations and saves are what the person said and kept; nothing records what they did. The owner's example — a video watched but never saved or mentioned — is only partly covered: G71's Chrome YouTube-history reader (off by default) indexes youtube.com visits in Chrome with their times and answers through `watch_history.lookup`, but misses every other browser, embedded and non-YouTube video, apps that are not browsers, dwell (a visit is not a watch) and what else was on screen. **Prior art (proposal `followups-2026-10-23.md`, kept outside the repo with the redesign research, §3, sources there).** Microsoft Recall's 2024 preview kept snapshots in a plain SQLite database any process running as the user could read (TotalRecall); the rework made it opt-in and removable, encrypted under TPM keys tied to Windows Hello, processed in a VBS enclave, with a sensitive-content filter that independent tests found leaky — and Signal, Brave and AdGuard still block it by default. Rewind's screen capture was switched off on 2025-12-19 after Meta bought Limitless. Screenpipe reads the Accessibility tree first and screenshots only on change (FTS5, an unauthenticated localhost API, ~20 GB a month reported, now source-available). Dayflow keeps a model-summarised timeline and deletes frames after about 3 days. ActivityWatch shows that app, title, URL and idle are enough for "what was I doing then". **macOS:** the frontmost app, idle, lock and secure input need no permission; window titles need Accessibility (window-list names are gated in a way Apple DTS calls undocumented, 2026-07); a tab's URL needs Automation per browser (Chrome's window `mode` says incognito; Safari's private windows are unverified); screenshots need Screen Recording, re-asked monthly since Sequoia. **Proposal.** Traces are a Source under This Mac, **off by default**, never ticked in onboarding. *Tier 1:* focus spans `{start, end, app, title?, url?}` — titles after the Accessibility prompt, communication apps title-off by default (their titles name other people); URLs only from browsers the person allowed, the query dropped but for content keys; every title scrubbed. *Tier 2, a separate opt-in:* event-driven screenshots (at most one a minute within a span), OCR deferred and on-device, never on the capture path. *Exclusions before any write:* private windows, password managers, secure input, the person's apps and domains, `sharingType = .none` windows, Cicada itself, a locked screen. *Controls:* a menu-bar indicator, Pause, Forget the last 15 minutes, Wipe all (the store and its key). **Where:** outside every bank (`$CICADA_HOME/traces/`), never git, never the telemetry ledger, excluded from backups; the app reads and posts spans to `POST /sources/traces/sync`, and the backend writes them with no model. **Into memory:** a YouTube span feeds the existing watch-history writer (`via: traces`, the same identity, with dwell); a day digest counted with no model (index-only, `source: traces-day`) only behind "Keep a daily summary in memory", because it puts titles into git; a model reads only that digest, only behind "Learn from traces" (💸), its claims `observed` and citing the digest's spans (no seventh evidence kind); a trace is not a mention, as a watched day is not (2026-10-18). **Agents:** Ask answers time-shaped questions from a deterministic lookup, fenced, with `sources`, on local doors only; no `traces` scope on G135 or `/v1` in v1; a client machine's traces stay on it (G181). **Security bar (the Recall lesson):** minimal before secure; encrypted at rest — key custody is RESEARCH (a Keychain item bound to the release app's signature, versus a 0600 file that protects only copies and backups), and Tier 2 waits for it; no network; no new localhost door; honest copy ("anything running as you can read what you can"). **Rails:** no model at capture; the app reads, the backend parses bytes; ruling 30's index-only; ruling 34's local day; headless; privacy (synthetic fixtures only). A ruling and one CLAUDE.md rail line come before any code. **Owner decisions — decided 2026-10-23 (TODO ruling 38 §4):** the owner chose to **include screenshots from the start**, not as a later tier. It ships off by default, outside every bank, encrypted at rest, never on the network, with one-click wipe and retention limits; the details (what is captured, the retentions, key custody, the exclusions) are this row's proposal as amended by the traces PR, which records each choice. (Recommended before the answer: Tier 1 first, screenshots a Tier 2 opt-in with 7-day retention and Touch ID to browse, 30 days for the timeline, a model reads only the digest, never remote in v1, key custody researched before Tier 2.) **Slices:** ruling + rail S · Tier 1a no-permission spans, store, controls and no-leak tests M · titles + URLs M · YouTube spans into watch history S · Ask lookups + `cicada traces` M · day digest + "Learn from traces" 💸 M · Tier 2 screenshots + OCR L · threat-model page S. → takes G156's live half; relates G128, G120, G195, G79's experience half; uses G71's writer. | APPLY · PR #456 |
| G219 | <a id="g219"></a>**Ways in from the apps where ideas used to live — Apple Reminders first, then Notion; one-way, never write-back** (Rodrigo 2026-10-23, paraphrased: first sync the Reminders app, then popular apps like Notion — "I used to keep all my project ideas in a Notion table; now I can just tell you and you'll remember") | **2026-10-23 — Reminders and the Notion export built (PR #455; ruling 38: Reminders first, then Notion, one way, never write-back).** *Reminders:* the app's `RemindersReader` (EventKit, the one full-access prompt said in words first, the person's ticked lists only, open + completed within 90 days, `CalendarReader`'s triggers, a read in flight at Disconnect never posts or turns it back on) posts to `POST /sources/reminders-local/sync`; `reminders_local.py` stages one episode per reminder (`reminders-local:<external id>`), dated by completion or creation (the due date is a fact, never the date; a later completion re-dates in place), tombstoned only for named lists and only if it could have been posted (a completion older than the window is kept), `evidence_kind: user` except a list marked "Others add to it" (`assistant`), a record like a calendar entry, one `user` commit (`capture/reminders`), demo 409. Sources: an always-listed `reminders-local` row, Off unless connected with a list ticked (#448's rule). Not on onboarding's tiles (the grid is full and Reminders needs its picker). *Notion export:* the Markdown & CSV zip on the one intake (`notion_export.py`): pages keyed `notion:<page id>`, **one episode per database row** (own Created date, an edit re-queues one row, maps one to one onto a backlog item later), CSV row joined to the row's page, part zips read, re-import skips, an edit updates in place, never tombstoned (an export can be partial); a page with no date of its own is stamped the import day but untimed (`notion-export-undated` in `episode_time.UNTIMED_SOURCES`); same-title rows match their own page by its property block, the page's properties winning; a row another person created (*Created by*) is `assistant`; reads only `.md`/`.csv`, bounded per file and in total, part zips one level down, what is left out said in the preview (review of #455, B1–B3). Evidence: `test_reminders_local.py`, `test_notion_export.py`, `RemindersReaderTests`. **Slice left — a list → a project's backlog, no model (designed, not built):** a per-list choice "Files into <project>'s backlog" kept in the bank (`backlog/_sources.json`, a REGISTRY file committed alone); after a sync commits, an ADMITTED step (refused while Sleep holds the pages, `write_admission`) files each reminder of that list as a G150 item through `backlog.add_item` with `links: [{kind: url, ref: "reminders-local:<id>"}]` as the idempotency key (looked up before minting, so a re-read never adds a second item), a completion as a signed status note `open → done` (`backlog.add_note`, author `user`), a deletion as `dropped`, never a hard delete; the same step serves a Notion database the person names (`notion:<row id>`). Not built here because it needs that registry, an admitted writer outside the capture route and a project picker in the row — more than a small change. **Notion API (slice 2) — built 2026-10-29 (`connectors/notion.py`, ruling 47 tier 2, branch `feat/connectors-tier2`):** as designed below, with three findings: Notion now serves a page as Markdown (`GET /v1/pages/{id}/markdown`, Notion-Version 2026-03-11), so one request per changed page replaces the block walk; a personal access token is refused at Save (`GET /v1/users/me` must answer a bot); and whose words (review of #519): a page is `user` only when `users/me` names the connection's owner (`bot.owner` of type `user`) and that user created the page — an internal connection is usually workspace-owned, so by default every page is `assistant` + `notion_by_other` and the Sources row says so; and no later read moves a unit stored as someone else's (an export's `notion_by_other` row included) back to `user` (`tier2._keep_others_words`). The first sync walks every shared page with Notion's own cursor, 150 a sync, and the high-water mark moves only when the walk is done; an incremental sync that cannot reach the mark in 20 listing pages walks everything instead. Each page is `notion:<id>` (the export's key: an exported page is rewritten in place, never doubled), split by H2 like the export, its stale sections and trashed pages tombstoned; a page unshared from the connection is kept as it was; at most 150 changed pages a sync, the rest a sync. The original design: **Notion API (slice 2, designed, not built):** an *internal connection* token the person creates and shares only chosen pages and databases with (least privilege; never a personal access token, never public OAuth), saved through the app into `~/.cicada/secrets.env` as `CICADA_NOTION_TOKEN` (0600, never logged, never in a bank); a connector in `connectors/` (the X/Reddit seam) polls `POST /v1/search` sorted by `last_edited_time` (newest first), stopping at a cursor kept outside the bank, then `GET /v1/blocks/{id}/children` per changed page, every request through `net_guard` and behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` (a poll Sleep's schedule starts) — the person's own Refresh is a click, not gated; pages render to the export's Markdown shape and stage through the same `notion_export` drafts keyed `notion:<page id>` (so an export and the API never double a page), a page in the trash (`in_trash`) tombstoned; the per-connection budget (180 requests a minute outside Business plans) honoured through a 429's `Retry-After`, a 401 shown as Needs sign-in. **Problem.** The memory is meant to replace the lists a person kept elsewhere, but what already sits in those lists never arrives: no Reminders reader exists (G126 left Todoist and Reminders "their own future rows"), and Notion appears only as a meeting-notes adapter idea under G134. A sync is a way in, not a second home. **Reminders (first).** The G142 path: the app's EventKit store gains a reminders reader (`requestFullAccessToReminders`, `NSRemindersFullAccessUsageDescription`; Reminders has no read-only grant, so the sheet says full access in words); the person ticks lists; incomplete reminders plus those completed within 90 days; re-read on `EKEventStoreChanged`; the whole set posted to `POST /sources/reminders-local/sync`. A `reminders_local` stager keyed `reminders-local:<calendarItemExternalIdentifier>` (title, scrubbed notes, due, priority, list, completed-at) edits in place, tombstones for named lists only, refuses a demo bank and makes one `user` commit. A reminder is the person's own words; a dated one becomes a G141 milestone and a completed one done, dated when created or completed (ruling 37B's spirit); a list the person names for a project maps straight into G150 backlog items through `backlog.py`, with no model; a shared list's items written by others are `external`. **Notion (second).** (a) An export import first — Notion's Markdown and CSV zip on the existing upload door, no credentials, re-runnable through the stager's hash. (b) Then the API with an **internal connection** token, which sees only the pages and databases the person shares with it (a personal access token acts as the person with everything; public OAuth needs a registered app); kept in `~/.cicada/secrets.env`, polled by `last_edited_time` behind `CICADA_ALLOW_CONNECTOR_FETCH` and `net_guard`, keyed `notion:<page_id>`, a trashed page tombstoned; a database marked as ideas or tasks maps to backlog items with no model. (c) The person's agent with Notion's own MCP server can already pull once (the G166 shape). **Never write-back:** one source of truth (the bank — a two-way conflict is what the claim layer solves inside it); echo loops; write scopes turning a reader into an actor in the person's other apps; and the owner's own framing, that the memory replaces the table. A one-off "export this project" or "add a reminder" happens only on request, through the agent's own tools. **Ranked by value for new users:** Reminders (S–M) · Notion (export S–M, API M) · Obsidian — a vault is a G133 watched folder; skip `.obsidian/`, read frontmatter and wikilinks as the person's links (S) · Google Docs and Drive — reach, but mostly others' words and a heavy OAuth scope; stays G155 (M–L) · Todoist — a personal token, maps onto backlog items (S–M) · Linear — mostly colleagues' items; the agent's own Linear MCP covers it (not planned). **Rails:** the app reads `~/Library`; scrub, stage, tombstone; the credentials rail; the connector gate, `net_guard` and the ToS rail (an official API, the person's own token); G150's one writer; G217's Sources row with a true status; provider-neutral copy. **Owner decisions — decided 2026-10-23 (TODO ruling 38 §3):** Reminders first (the lists the person ticks; completed reminders from the last 90 days), then Notion (export first, then its API with a token scoped to the pages the person shares); both one-way into Cicada, never writing back; then Obsidian, then Todoist. A named list mapping into the backlog with no model was not part of the answer and stays the recommendation. **Composio — a note (owner question 2026-10-23, paraphrased: could Composio be the answer for easy integrations to other apps?).** The coordinator's view, not a ruling: native first for the few apps that matter — local and private, the rows above — and Composio only as an optional, reviewed catalogue entry the person's *own agent* uses next to Cicada (the agent fetches through Composio and saves into Cicada with provenance: its `Cicada-Author`, `sources`, `external:` claims), never Cicada's own integration layer. Why: (1) a third party in the data path — hosted aggregators keep the OAuth tokens and proxy each call's content through their cloud, against the credentials rail (`~/.cicada/secrets.env`, never elsewhere) and the local-first posture; (2) it is action-oriented — tool calls that post, create and send — not a bulk-read sync engine with cursors, deletions and tombstones, which is what a one-way way in needs; (3) per-use cost and vendor dependence (`saved-content-integrations.md`, 2026-08-31, reached the same verdict for saved content: the platform APIs it wraps do not expose the personal read surfaces, and the field has already lost players). **Not verified:** current pricing and whether a self-hosted option fits one person. An entry the person's agent installs stays possible on the person's consent (the Skills rail: pinned to what they saw). **Slices:** Reminders reader + stager M · list → backlog S · Notion export S–M · Notion API M · Obsidian folder S · Todoist S–M. Proposal `followups-2026-10-23.md`, kept outside the repo with the redesign research, §4. → the intake for G13 and G150; the path of G142; relates G126, G133, G155. | APPLY · decided (ruling 38) · PR #455 (Reminders + Notion export) |
